# Visnu Purana 1

## Book One: Creation

## Chapter 1 - Maitreya asks Parāśara about the world

## \|\| 1.1.1 \|\|

*श्रीसूत उवाच  ।*  
*ॐ पराशरं मुनिवरं कृतपौर्वाह्णिकक्रियम्  ।*  
*मैत्रेयः परिपप्रच्छ प्रणिपत्याभिवाद्य च  ॥१॥*

*śrīsūta uvāca*  
*oṃ parāśaraṃ munivaraṃ kṛtapaurvāhnikakriyam \|*  
*maitreyaḥ paripapraccha praṇipatyābhivādya ca \|\| 1 \|\|*

When Parāśara, that best of sages, had finished his morning rites, Maitreya bowed, saluted him and asked: 1

## \|\| 1.1.2 \|\|

*tvatto hi vedādhyayanam adhītam akhilaṃ guro \|*  
*dharmaśāstrāṇi sarvāṇi tathāṅgāni yathākramam \|\| 2 \|\|*

‘I’ve studied all the Vedas with you, master, and all the legal texts and supplements in due order. 2

## \|\| 1.1.3 \|\|

*tvāprasādān muniśreṣṭa māmanye nākṛtaśramam \|*  
*vakṣyanti sarvaśāstreṣu prayaśo yepi vidviṣaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Thanks to your generosity, none could say that I’m remiss in any of these works, great sage, not even my adversaries. 3

## \|\| 1.1.4 \|\|

*sohamicchāmi dharmajña śrotuṃ tvatto yathā jagat \|*  
*babhūva bhūyaś ca yathā mahābhāga bhaviṣyati \|\| 4 \|\|*

You know virtue, fortunate seer, and now I’d like to hear about the origin of the world and how it will fare in future. 4

## \|\| 1.1.5 \|\|

*yanmayaṃ ca jagadbrahmanyataścautaccarācaram \|*  
*līnamāsīdyathā yatra layameṣyati yatra ca \|\| 5 \|\|*

What’s it made of, brahmin, and whence did all things, moving and unmoving, arise? How did the world end in earlier times and how will it end hereafter? 5

## \|\| 1.1.6 \|\|

*yatpramāṇāni bhūtāni devādīnāṃ ca saṃbhavam \|*  
*samudraparvatānāṃ ca saṃsthānaṃ ca yathā bhuvaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

What’s the nature of the elements? What’s the origin of gods and other beings? What’s the state of the oceans, earth and mountains? 6

## \|\| 1.1.7 \|\|

*sūryādīnāṃ ca saṃsthānaṃ pramāṇaṃ munisattama \|*  
*devādīnāṃ tathā vaṃśānmanūnmanvantarāṇica \|\| 7 \|\|*

What’s the size and nature of the sun and other luminaries, best of sages? What about the families of gods and other beings, the Manus and Manvantaras? 7

## \|\| 1.1.8 \|\|

*kalpān kalpavibhāgāṃś ca cāturyugavikalpitān \|*  
*kalpāntasya svarūpaṃ ca yugadharmāṃś ca kṛtsnaśaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

What about the aeons, how are they divided and apportioned into ages? How does each aeon end, and what traditions mark each age? I’d like to hear all this. 8

## \|\| 1.1.9 \|\|

*devarṣipārthivānāṃ ca caritaṃ yanmahāmune \|*  
*vedaśākhāpraṇayanaṃ yathāvavdyāsakartṛkam \|\| 9 \|\|*

What about the deeds of kings and heavenly seers, great sage? How did the branches of Vedic learning come about when Vyāsa had arranged them? 9

## \|\| 1.1.10 \|\|

*dharmāṃś ca brahmaṇādīnāṃ tathā cāśramavāsinām \|*  
*śretumicchāmyahaṃ sarvaṃ tvatto vāsiṣṭhanandana \|\| 10 \|\|*

What are the duties of the brahmins, the other orders and various life stages? I’d like to hear all this from you who pleased your father, Vasiṣṭha’s son. 10

## \|\| 1.1.11 \|\|

*brahmanprasādapravaṇaṃ kuruṣva mayi mānasam \|*  
*yenāhametajjānīyāṃ tvatprasādānmahāmune \|\| 11 \|\|*

Turn your gracious mind to me, brahmin, so that I may learn this through your kindness, you great sage.’ 11

## \|\| 1.1.12 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*sādhu maitreya dharmajña smāritosmi purātanam \|*  
*pituḥ pitā me bhagavān vasiṣṭho yaduvāca ha \|\| 12 \|\|*

Parāśara:

You’ve spoken well, Maitreya, and you know virtue. I recall an old account my grandsire, holy Vasiṣṭha, once told me. 12

## \|\| 1.1.13 \|\|

*viśvāmitrapuyuktena rakṣasā bhakṣitaḥ purā \|*  
*śrutastātastataḥ krodhe maitreyābhūnmamātulaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

When I learned my father had been eaten by a rākṣasa sent by Viśvāmitra, Maitreya, my fury knew no bounds. 13

## \|\| 1.1.14 \|\|

*tato 'haṃ rakṣasāṃ satraṃ vināśāya samārabham \|*  
*bhasmī bhūtāś ca śataśastasminsatre niśācarāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

I then began a major sacrifice to exterminate those demons, and hundreds of those night-ranging creatures were reduced to ashes. 14

## \|\| 1.1.15 \|\|

*tataḥ saṃkṣīyamāṇeṣu teṣu rakṣasvaśeṣataḥ \|*  
*māmuvāca mahābhāgo vasiṣṭhī matpitāmahaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

But while I was destroying them, my grandsire, eminent Vasiṣṭha, counselled me: 15

## \|\| 1.1.16 \|\|

*alamatyantakopena tāta manyumimaṃ jahi \|*  
*rākṣasā nāparādhyanti pitus te vihitaṃ hi tat \|\| 16 \|\|*

‘Don’t be angry, my dear boy. Set aside your fury. The rākṣasas haven’t wronged your father. It was his destiny. 16

## \|\| 1.1.17 \|\|

*mūḍhānāmeva bhavati krodho jñānavatāṃ kutaḥ \|*  
*hanyate tāta kaḥ kena yataḥ svakṛtabhukpumān \|\| 17 \|\|*

Only fools get angry, not the wise. Who is killed, dear boy, and who’s the killer, when a person merely suffers the outcomes of his actions? 17

## \|\| 1.1.18 \|\|

*sañcicasyāpi mahatā vatsa kleśena mānavaiḥ \|*  
*yaśasastapasaścaiva krodho nāśakaraḥ paraḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Anger is the prime undoing of fame and penance, my dear, that people strive so hard to win. 18

## \|\| 1.1.19 \|\|

*svargāpavargavyāsedhakāraṇaṃ paramarṣayaḥ \|*  
*varjayanti sadā krodhaṃ tāta mā tadvaśo bhavaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

Great seers always eschew anger, dear boy, as it blocks the way to heaven and to liberation. Don’t give in to fury. 19

## \|\| 1.1.20 \|\|

*alaṃ niśācarair dhagdhair dīnair anapakāribhiḥ \|*  
*satraṃ te viramatvetatkṣamāsārā hi sādhavaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

Don’t burn these miserable night-ranging demons; they’ve done no harm. Call off this sacrifice of yours. Good people should be merciful.’ 20

## \|\| 1.1.21 \|\|

*evaṃ tātena tenāhamanunīto mahātmanā \|*  
*upasaṃhṛtavānsatraṃ sadyastadvākyagauravāt \|\| 21 \|\|*

Thus chastised by that great sage, my grandsire, I stopped the sacrifice at once, as I respected his opinion. 21

## \|\| 1.1.22 \|\|

*tataḥ prītaḥ sa bhagavānvasiṣṭho munisattamaḥ \|*  
*saṃprāptaś ca tadā tatra pulastyo brahmaṇaḥ sutaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

This pleased that best of sages, holy Vasiṣṭha. Just then, Pulastya, Brahmā’s son, appeared. 22

## \|\| 1.1.23 \|\|

*pitāmahena dattarghyaḥ kṛtāsanaparigrahaḥ \|*  
*māmuvāca mahābhāgo maitreya pulahāgrajaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

My grandsire welcomed him and offered him a seat. That noble being, Pulaha’s elder brother, spoke to me, Maitreya. 23

## \|\| 1.1.24 \|\|

*pulāstya uvāca*  
*vaire mahati yadvākyādguroradyāśritā kṣamā \|*  
*tvayā tasmātsamastāni bhavāñśāstrāṇi vetsyati \|\| 24 \|\|*

Pulastya:

‘Because you heed your grandsire’s words and show forbearance in the midst of great hostility, you’ll comprehend all sacred texts. 24

## \|\| 1.1.25 \|\|

*saṃtaterna mamocchedaḥ kruddhenāpi yataḥ kṛtaḥ \|*  
*tvayā tasmānmahābhāga dadāmyanyaṃ mahāvaram \|\| 25 \|\|*

Even though you were enraged, you lucky man, you refrained from killing all my clan. I therefore grant you this great boon. 25

## \|\| 1.1.26 \|\|

*purāṇasaṃhitākarta bhavānvatsa bhaviṣyati \|*  
*devatāpāramārthyaṃ ca yathāvadvetsyate bhavān \|\| 26 \|\|*

You’ll be the creator of a purāṇa, dear boy, and understand precisely the nature of divinity. 26

## \|\| 1.1.27 \|\|

*pravṛtte ca nivṛtte ca karmaṇyastamalā matiḥ \|*  
*matprasādādasaṃdigdhā tava vatsa bhaviṣyati \|\| 27 \|\|*

Whether you lead a life of sacrifice or renunciation, blessed by me, your mind will be pure and unconfused, my dear.’ 27

## \|\| 1.1.28 \|\|

*tataś ca prāha bhagavānvasiṣṭho me pitāmahaḥ \|*  
*pulastyena yaduktaṃ te sarvametadbhaviṣyati \|\| 28 \|\|*

Then my grandsire, holy Vasiṣṭha, confirmed that everything would happen just as Pulastya had foretold. 28

## \|\| 1.1.29 \|\|

*iti pūrvaṃ vasiṣṭhena pulastyena ca dhīmatā \|*  
*yaduktaṃ tatsmṛtiṃ yāti tvatpraśrādakhilaṃ mama \|\| 29 \|\|*

Such was the conversation long ago with Vasiṣṭha and wise Pulastya that came to mind when you questioned me. 29

## \|\| 1.1.30 \|\|

*so 'haṃ vadāmyaśeṣaṃ te maitreya paripṛcchate \|*  
*purāmasaṃhitāṃ samyak tāṃ nibodha yathātatham \|\| 30 \|\|*

I’ll relate this whole purāṇa for you, Maitreya, seeing as you ask, so listen carefully. 30

## \|\| 1.1.31 \|\|

*viṣṇoḥ sakāśādudbhutaṃ jatattatraiva ca sthitam \|*  
*sthitisaṃyamakartāsau jagatosya jagacca saḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

The world arose from Viṣṇu and depends on him. He maintains it, he destroys it—he is the world. 31

## Chapter 2 - Parāśara praises Viṣṇu; Creation

## \|\| 1.2.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*avikārāya śuddhāya nityāya paramātmane \|*  
*sadaikarūparūpāya viṣṇave sarvajiṣṇave \|\| 1 \|\|*

I bow to Viṣṇu, unchanging, pure, eternal, supreme, always in universal form, all-conquering, 1

## \|\| 1.2.2 \|\|

*namo hiraṇyagarbhāya haraye śaṅkarāya ca \|*  
*vāsudevāya tārāya sarvasthityantakāriṇe \|\| 2 \|\|*

Who is also Brahmā, Hari, Śiva, Vāsudeva, saviour and cause of creation, preservation and destruction of the world. 2

## \|\| 1.2.3 \|\|

*ekānekasvarūpāya sthūlasūkṣmātmane namaḥ \|*  
*avyaktavyaktarūpāya viṣṇave muktihetave \|\| 3 \|\|*

I bow to Viṣṇu whose form is one and many, gross and subtle, unseen and seen, the cause of liberation. 3

## \|\| 1.2.4 \|\|

*sargasthitivināśānāṃ jagato yo jaganmayaḥ \|*  
*mūlabhūto nasamtasmai viṣṇave paramātmane \|\| 4 \|\|*

I bow to Viṣṇu, who is the world, its creator, keeper and destroyer, its root and highest spirit. 4

## \|\| 1.2.5 \|\|

*ādhārabhūtaṃ viśvasyāpyaṇīyāṃsamaṇīyasām \|*  
*praṇamya sarvabhūtasthamacyutaṃ puruṣottamam \|\| 5 \|\|*

Having bowed to Viṣṇu, who is the base of everything, smaller than the smallest, yet abiding in all beings, imperishable, the highest spirit, 5

## \|\| 1.2.6 \|\|

*jñānasvarūpamatyantanirmalaṃ paramārthataḥ \|*  
*tamevārthasvarūpeṇa bhrantidarśanataḥ sthitam \|\| 6 \|\|*

Who in the truest sense is unsullied wisdom, yet is falsely seen as having concrete form, 6

## \|\| 1.2.7 \|\|

*viṣṇu grasiṣṇuṃ viśvasya sthitau sarge tathā prabhum \|*  
*praṇamya jagatāmīśamajamakṣayamavyayam \|\| 7 \|\|*

Who consumes the world, but is also lord of creation and preservation, master of the threefold realms, unborn, unageing and unchanging, 7

## \|\| 1.2.8 \|\|

*kathayāmi yathāpūrvaṃ dakṣādyair munisattamaiḥ \|*  
*pṛṣṭaḥ provāca bhagavānabjayoniḥ pitāmahaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

I’ll tell you what was said in former times by Brahmā, my blessed lotus-born grandsire, when asked by Dakṣa and other leading sages. 8

## \|\| 1.2.9 \|\|

*taiś coktaṃ purukutsāya bhūbhuje narmadātaṭe \|*  
*sārasvatāya tenāpi mahyaṃ sārasvatena ca \|\| 9 \|\|*

They passed this tale to King Purukutsa by the river Narmadā. He related it to Sārasvata, who passed it on to me. 9

## \|\| 1.2.10 \|\|

*paraḥ parāṇāṃ paramaḥ paramātmātmasaṃsthitaḥ \|*  
*rūpavarṇādinirdeśaviśeṣaṇavivarjitaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

The highest, who is higher than the highest, the supreme spirit who abides in all, transcending colour, form, description and distinction, 10

## \|\| 1.2.11 \|\|

*apakṣayavināśābhyāṃ pariṇāmarddhijanmabhiḥ \|*  
*varjitaḥ śakyate vaktuṃ yaḥ sadāstīti kevalam \|\| 11 \|\|*

Unfettered by decline, destruction, change, development or birth; of whom alone it can be said that he exists, 11

## \|\| 1.2.12 \|\|

*sarvatrāsau samastaṃ ca vasatyatreti vai yataḥ \|*  
*tataḥ sa vāsudeveti vidvadbhiḥ paripaṭhyate \|\| 12 \|\|*

As he dwells (vasati) in all locations and everything abides in him, the wise refer to him as Vāsudeva. 12

## \|\| 1.2.13 \|\|

*tadbrahma paramaṃ nityamajamakṣayamavyayam \|*  
*ekasvarūpaṃ tu sadā heyābhāvācca nirmalam \|\| 13 \|\|*

He is the Absolute, supreme, eternal, unborn, undecaying, unchanging and universal being. Never to be abandoned, he is pure. 13

## \|\| 1.2.14 \|\|

*tadeva sarvamevaitadvyaktāvyaktasvarūpavat \|*  
*tathā puruṣarūpeṇa kālarūpeṇa ca sthitam \|\| 14 \|\|*

He is this whole world, Unseen (avyakta) and Seen (vyakta), existing in the form of Time (kāla) and Spirit (puruṣa). 14

## \|\| 1.2.15 \|\|

*parasya brahmaṇo rūpaṃ purūṣaḥ prathamaṃ dvija \|*  
*vyaktāvyakte tathaivānye rūpe kālastathāparam \|\| 15 \|\|*

The primary form of the highest Absolute is Spirit, brahmin, next are Seen and Unseen, with Time as the fourth. 15

## \|\| 1.2.16 \|\|

*pradhānapūruṣavyaktakālānāṃ paramaṃ hi yat \|*  
*paśyanti sūrayaḥ śuddhaṃ tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam \|\| 16 \|\|*

The learned know the pure and highest state of Viṣṇu is superior to primal Matter (pradhāna), Spirit, the Seen and Time. 16

## \|\| 1.2.17 \|\|

*pradhānapūruṣavyaktakālāstu pravibhāgaśaḥ \|*  
*rūpāṇi sthitisargāntavyaktisadbhāvahetavaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Matter, Spirit, the Seen and Time are in due proportion the cause of the existence of creation, preservation and destruction. 17

## \|\| 1.2.18 \|\|

*vyaktaṃ viṣṇustathavyaktaṃ purūṣaḥ kāla eva ca \|*  
*krīḍato bālakasyeva ceṣṭāṃ tasya niśāmaya \|\| 18 \|\|*

Viṣṇu is the Seen and Unseen, Time and Spirit. Understand his deeds are like the play of children. 18

## \|\| 1.2.19 \|\|

*avyaktaṃ kāraṇaṃ yattatpradhānamṛṣisattamaiḥ \|*  
*procyate prakṛtiḥ sīkṣmā nityaṃ sadasadātmakam \|\| 19 \|\|*

The best of sages say that Matter makes the Unseen possible. This primordial stuff is subtle, eternal, existent and nonexistent. 19

## \|\| 1.2.20 \|\|

*akṣayyaṃ nānyadādhāramameyamajaraṃ dhruvam \|*  
*śabdasparśavihīnaṃ tadrūpādibhir asaṃhitam \|\| 20 \|\|*

It’s durable, needs no other basis, is boundless, ageless, firm, beyond the reach of sound and touch and has no form attainable by these or other senses. 20

## \|\| 1.2.21 \|\|

*triguṇaṃ tajjagadyoniranādiprabhavāpyayam \|*  
*tenāgre sarvamevāsīvdyāptaṃ vai pralayādanu \|\| 21 \|\|*

Consisting of three qualities—purity, passion and dullness—it is the origin of the world, but has no beginning, end or middle. Between each creation and dissolution, it is all-pervading. 21

## \|\| 1.2.22 \|\|

*vedavādavido vidvanniyatā brahmavādinaḥ \|*  
*paṭhanti caitamevārthaṃ pradhānapratipādakam \|\| 22 \|\|*

Those self-restrained individuals who understand the Vedas’ words, wise sage, and who discourse on sacred topics, recite this verse about the origin of Matter: 22

## \|\| 1.2.23 \|\|

*nāho na rātrirna nabho na bhūmirnāsīttamojyotirabhūcca nānyat \|*  
*śrotrādibuddhyānupalabhyamekaṃ prādhānikaṃ brahma pumāṃstadāsīt \|\| 23 \|\|*

‘There was neither day nor night, sky nor earth, light nor darkness or any other quality. There was just a single entity: the Absolute, beyond hearing, thought and other senses, composed of Matter and of Spirit.’ 23

## \|\| 1.2.24 \|\|

*viṣṇoḥ svarūpātparatodite dve rūpe pradhānaṃ purūṣaś ca vipra \|*  
*tasyaiva te 'nyena dhṛte viyukte rūpātaraṃ taddvija kālasaṃjñam \|\| 24 \|\|*

Those two forms exist apart from Viṣṇu’s own state, brahmin. He has another that’s known as Time, by which these two are joined or parted, brahmin. 24

## \|\| 1.2.25 \|\|

*prakṛtau saṃsthitaṃ vyaktamatītapralaye tu yat \|*  
*tasmātprākṛtasaṃjñoyamucyate pratisaṃcaraḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Because the Seen returns to primal elements at the dissolution of the world, that reabsorption is known as ‘elemental’. 25

## \|\| 1.2.26 \|\|

*anādirbhagavānkālo nāntosta dvija vidyate \|*  
*avyucchinnastatastvete sargasthityantasaṃyamāḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

In the form of Time, the lord lacks start or finish, brahmin, so these rounds of creation, preservation and dissolution never cease. 26

## \|\| 1.2.27 \|\|

*guṇasāmye tatastasminpṛthakpuṃsi vyavasthite \|*  
*kālasvarūpaṃ tadviṣṇormaitreya parivartate \|\| 27 \|\|*

At the time of dissolution, these three qualities are in balance, Spirit abides alone in Matter, Maitreya, and Viṣṇu exists in the form of Time. 27

## \|\| 1.2.28 \|\|

*tatastu tatparaṃ brahma paramātmā jaganmayaḥ \|*  
*sarvagaḥ sarvabhūteśaḥ sarvātmā parameśvaraḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

The highest Absolute, the supreme spirit in whom the world abides, all-pervading master of creation, supreme lord at the heart of all, 28

## \|\| 1.2.29 \|\|

*pradhānapurūṣau cāpi praviśyātmecchayā hariḥ \|*  
*kṣobhayāmāsa saṃprāpte sargakāle vyayāvyayau \|\| 29 \|\|*

Hari, having entered Matter and Spirit, then willingly agitates these changing and unchanging elements when the moment of creation is at hand. 29

## \|\| 1.2.30 \|\|

*yathā sannidhimātreṇa gandhaḥ kṣobhāya jāyate \|*  
*manaso nopakartṛtvāttathāsau parameśvaraḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

Just as scent excites the mind by proximity alone in the absence of all contact, in that same way, the supreme lord stirs the elements in his role as benefactor. 30

## \|\| 1.2.31 \|\|

*sa eva kṣobhako brahman kṣobhyaś ca purūṣottamaḥ \|*  
*sa saṃkocavikāsābhyāṃ pradhānatvepi ca sthitaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

The highest being is indeed the agitator, brahmin, and that which is agitated subsisting in the state of matter during its contraction and expansion. 31

## \|\| 1.2.32 \|\|

*vikāsāṇusvarūpaiś ca brahmarūpādibhis tathā \|*  
*vyaktasvarūpaś ca tathā viṣṇuḥ sarveśvareśvaraḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

Viṣṇu, highest lord of lords, is manifest in the form of things composed of atoms, and in the form of Brahmā and other beings. 32

## \|\| 1.2.33 \|\|

*guṇasāmyāttatastasmātkṣetrajñādhiṣṭhitānmune \|*  
*guṇavyañjanasaṃbhūtiḥ sargakāle dvijottama \|\| 33 \|\|*

Then, at the moment of creation, from that equilibrium of qualities, overseen by Viṣṇu as a sentient being, sage, comes the property known as Greatness (mahat), which is produced by the appearance of those qualities, best of brahmins. 33

## \|\| 1.2.34 \|\|

*pradhānatattvamudbhūtaṃ mahāntaṃ tatsamāvṛṇot \|*  
*sāttviko rājasaścaiva tāmasaś ca tridhā mahān \|\| 34 \|\|*

Matter then surrounds the Greatness that arises. This Greatness is threefold—pure, passionate and dark—and is swathed in Matter, just as seeds are covered by a husk. 34

## \|\| 1.2.35 \|\|

*pradhānatattvena samaṃ tvacā bīja mivāvṛtam \|*  
*vaikārekastaijasaś ca bhūtādiścaiva tāmasaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

From Greatness, threefold individuation arises: pure, passionate and elemental. 35

## \|\| 1.2.36 \|\|

*trividhoyamahaṅkāro mahattattvādajāyata \|*  
*bhītendriyāṇāṃ hetuḥsa triguṇatvānmahāmune \|*  
*yathā pradhānena mahānmahatā sa tathā vṛtaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

It’s the cause of elements and the senses as it consists of threefold qualities, great sage. Just as Greatness is enclosed by Matter, so Matter is enclosed by Greatness. 36

## \|\| 1.2.37 \|\|

*bhūtādistu vikurvāṇaḥ śabdatanmātrakaṃ tataḥ \|*  
*sasarja śabdatanmātrādākāśaṃ śabdalakṣaṇam \|\| 37 \|\|*

Elemental individuation becomes productive and brings forth the subtle element of sound. From that arises space, characterised by that sensation. 37

## \|\| 1.2.38 \|\|

*śabdamātraṃ tathākāśaṃ bhūtādiḥ sa samāvṛṇot \|*  
*akāśastu vikurvāṇaḥ sparśamātraṃ sasarja ha \|\| 38 \|\|*

Elemental individuation envelops space, which has sound as its element. Space then becomes productive and gives rise to the element of touch. 38

## \|\| 1.2.39 \|\|

*balavānabhavadvāyustasya sparśo muṇo mataḥ \|*  
*ākāśaṃ śabdamātraṃ tu svarśamātraṃ samāvṛṇot \|\| 39 \|\|*

From that is born a mighty wind whose property is touch; and space, whose element is sound, envelops the element of touch. 39

## \|\| 1.2.40 \|\|

*tato vāyurvikurvāṇo rūpamātraṃ sasarja ha \|*  
*jyotirutpadyate vāyostadrūpaguṇamucyate \|\| 40 \|\|*

Wind then becomes productive and brings forth the element of form, and light, whose quality is form, arises from the wind. 40

## \|\| 1.2.41 \|\|

*sparśamātraṃ tu vai vāyū rūpamātraṃ samāvṛṇot \|*  
*jyotiścāpi vikurvāṇaṃ rasamātraṃ sasarja ha \|\| 41 \|\|*

Then wind, whose element is touch, envelops light, whose element is form, and light then becomes productive, bringing forth the element of taste. 41

## \|\| 1.2.42 \|\|

*sambhavanti tatoṃbhāṃsi rasādhārāṇi tāni ca \|*  
*rasamātrāṇi cāmbhāṃsi rūpamātraṃ samāvṛṇot \|\| 42 \|\|*

Next arise the waters, which are the source of taste, and the waters, whose element is taste, envelop light, whose element is form. 42

## \|\| 1.2.43 \|\|

*vikurvāṇāni cāmbhāṃsi gandhamātraṃ sasa rjire \|*  
*saṃghāto jāyate tasmāttasya gandho guṇo mataḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

The waters then become productive, bringing forth the element of scent. From that arises solid earth, whose quality is that sensation. 43

## \|\| 1.2.44 \|\|

*tasmiṃstasmiṃstu tanmātraṃ tena tanmātratā smṛtā \|\| 44 \|\|*

As each of these phenomena has its own subtle element, they are considered in terms of their elemental natures, but the subtle elements, lacking distinctive qualities, are known as ‘indistinct’. 44

## \|\| 1.2.45 \|\|

*tanmātraṇyaviśeṣāṇi aviśeṣāstato hi te \|\| 45 \|\|*

Being indistinct, they lack qualities such as terror, peace or dullness. This is the elemental creation that arises from individuation and is marked itself by dullness. 45

## \|\| 1.2.46 \|\|

*na śāntā nāpi dhorāste na mūḍhāścāviśeṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*bhūtatanmātrasargoyamahaṅkārāttu tāmasāt \|\| 46 \|\|*

They say the organs of the senses arise from individuation marked by passion, and the ten senses themselves, with mind as the eleventh, arise from individuation marked by purity. Accordingly, the senses are thought to be pure. 46

## \|\| 1.2.47 \|\|

*tejasānīndriyāṇyāhurdevā vaikārikā daśa \|*  
*ekādaśaṃ manaścātra devā vaikārikāḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

The skin, eye, nose and tongue, with ear as the fifth, brahmin, combine with the intellect to experience sound and other sensations. 47

## \|\| 1.2.48 \|\|

*tvak cakṣurnāsikā jihvā śrotramatra ca pañca mam \|*  
*śabdādīnāmavāptyarthaṃ buddhiyuktāni vai dvija \|\| 48 \|\|*

The functions of the organs of excretion and reproduction, the hands, the feet and voice, Maitreya, are said to be evacuation, production, movement and communication. 48

## \|\| 1.2.49 \|\|

*pāyūpasthau karau pādau vāk ca maitreya pañcamī \|*  
*visargaśilpagatyukti karma teṣāṃ ca kathyate \|\| 49 \|\|*

Space, wind, light, water and earth combine with each of their respective qualities of sound and so on, brahmin. 49

## \|\| 1.2.50 \|\|

*ākāśavāyutejāṃsi salilaṃ pṛthivī tathā \|*  
*śabdādibhir guṇair brahmansaṃyuktānyuttarottarai \|\| 50 \|\|*

These now exhibit terror, peace or dullness, so are said to be ‘distinct’. 50

## \|\| 1.2.51 \|\|

*śāntā ghorāś ca mūḍhāś ca viśeṣastena te smṛtāḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

Possessing energy of different kinds, but existing independently and not combining, these are incapable of creation unless completely unified. 51

## \|\| 1.2.52 \|\|

*nānāviryāḥ pṛthagbhūtāstataste saṃhatiṃ vinā \|*  
*nāśaknuvanprajāḥ sraṣṭumasamāgamya sṛtstraśaḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

Having combined with one another, blending together, they become a single mass and achieve a perfect unity. 52

## \|\| 1.2.53 \|\|

*sametyānyonyasaṃyogaṃ parasparasamāśrayāḥ \|*  
*ekasaṃghātalakṣyāś ca saṃprāpyaikyamaśeṣataḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

Overseen by Spirit, with the Unseen element’s assent, the factors beginning with Greatness and ending with the distinctive elements form an egg. 53

## \|\| 1.2.54 \|\|

*puruṣādhiṣṭhitatvācca pradhānānugraheṇa ca \|*  
*mahadādya viśeṣāntā hyaṇḍamutpādayanti te \|\| 54 \|\|*

This egg increases slowly like a bubble on the water. Consisting of the elements, wise sage, and resting on the waters, it is the natural and excellent abode of Viṣṇu when he takes the form of Brahmā. 54

## \|\| 1.2.55 \|\|

*tatkrameṇa vivṛddhaṃ sajjalabudbudavatsamam \|*  
*bhūtebhyoṇḍaṃ mahābuddhe mahattadudakeśayam \|*  
*prakṛtaṃ brahmarūpasya viṣṇoḥ sthānamanuttamam \|\| 55 \|\|*

There, Viṣṇu, universal lord, whose form is unmanifest, abides in the manifest form of Brahmā. 55

## \|\| 1.2.56 \|\|

*tatrāvyaktasvarūpo 'sau vyaktarūpo jagatpatiḥ \|*  
*viṣṇurbrahmasvarūpeṇa svayameva vyavasthitaḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

Meru and the other mountain ranges are the inner and outer membranes of the egg, and the waters inside it are the oceans. 56

## \|\| 1.2.57 \|\|

*merurulbamabhūttasya jarāyuś ca mahīdharāḥ \|*  
*garbhodakaṃ samudrāś ca tasyāsansumahātmanaḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

The mountains, lands and oceans, heavenly bodies, the threefold worlds, gods and demigods and humankind, brahmin, are all inside that egg. 57

## \|\| 1.2.58 \|\|

*sādridvīpasamudrāś ca sajyotirlokasaṃgrahaḥ \|*  
*tasminnaṇḍe 'bhavadvipra sadevāsuramānuṣāḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

The egg is wrapped in water, fire, wind and space and other elements, and by the individuation that is their source. Each layer is ten times greater than the one within, and the whole is covered by Greatness, the origin of the elements. 58

## \|\| 1.2.59 \|\|

*vārivahnyanilākāśais tato bhūtādinā bahiḥ \|*  
*vṛtaṃ daśaguṇair aṇḍaṃ bhūtādirmahatā tathā \|\| 59 \|\|*

The Greatness, together with the others, is enveloped by the Unseen, brahmin. The egg is covered by these seven natural layers, like a coconut with an inner kernel and an outer husk. 59

## \|\| 1.2.60 \|\|

*avyaktenāvṛto brahmaṃstai sarvaiḥ sahito mahān \|*  
*ebhir āvaraṇair aṇḍaṃ saptabhiḥ prakṛtair vṛtam \|*  
*nālikeraphalasyāntarbājaṃ bāhyadalair iva \|\| 60 \|\|*

Delighting in the quality of activity, the lord of all, Hari himself, becoming Brahmā, sets about creation of the world. 60

## \|\| 1.2.61 \|\|

*juṣan rajo guṇaṃ tatra svayaṃ viśveśvaro hariḥ \|*  
*brahmābhūtvāsya jagato visṛṣṭau saṃpravartate \|\| 61 \|\|*

Lord Viṣṇu, imbued with purity, whose prowess is immeasurable, preserves the world through every age until each aeon ends. 61

## \|\| 1.2.62 \|\|

*sṛṣṭaṃ ca pātyanuyugaṃ yāvatkalpavikalpanā \|*  
*sattvabhṛdbhagavānviṣṇuraprameyaparākramaḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

Filled with the quality of dullness, at the end of every aeon, Janārdana takes the wrathful form of Rudra, Maitreya, and devours the universe. 62

## \|\| 1.2.63 \|\|

*tamodrekī ca kalpānte rudrarūpī jarnādanaḥ \|*  
*maitreyākhilabhūtāni bhakṣayatyatidārūṇaḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

When he has consumed it all and reduced the world to a single ocean, the highest lord reclines on his couch, the serpent’s lap. 63

## \|\| 1.2.64 \|\|

*bhakṣayitvā ca bhūtāni jagatyekāṇavīkṛte \|*  
*nāgaparyaṅkaśayane śete ca parameśvaraḥ \|\| 64 \|\|*

When he wakes, in Brahmā’s form, he creates the world once more. 64

## \|\| 1.2.65 \|\|

*prabuddhaś ca punaḥ sṛṣṭiṃ karoti brahmarūpadhṛk \|\| 65 \|\|*

The one Lord Janārdana is known as Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva when carrying out creation, preservation and destruction of the world. 65

## \|\| 1.2.66 \|\|

*sṛṣṭisthityantakaraṇīṃ brahmaviṣṇuśivātmikām \|*  
*sa saṃjñāṃ yāti bhagavāneka eva janārdanaḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

Lord Viṣṇu, the creator, creates himself. He is both protector and protected. He is the destroyer who destroys himself at the aeon’s end. 66

## \|\| 1.2.67 \|\|

*straṣṭā sṛjati cātmānaṃ viṣṇuḥ pālyaṃ ca pāti ca \|*  
*upasaṃhriyate cānte saṃhartā ca svayaṃ prabhuḥ \|\| 67 \|\|*

Earth, water, light, wind and space, all the senses and the mind that together make the world: these are known as Spirit. 67

## \|\| 1.2.68 \|\|

*pṛthivyāpas tathā tejo vāyurākāśa eva ca \|*  
*sarvendriyāntaḥkaraṇaṃ puruṣākhyaṃ hi yajjagat \|\| 68 \|\|*

Since this alone is the universal, eternal governor of all creation, it’s also the agent that abides in all the elements, from which arise the creation, preservation and destruction of the world. 68

## \|\| 1.2.69 \|\|

*sa eva sarvabhūtātmā viśvarūpo yato 'vyayaḥ \|*  
*sargādikaṃ tu tasyaiva bhūtasthamupakārakam \|\| 69 \|\|*

He alone is creation and creator. He alone is preserver and preserved. In the form of Brahmā and the others, Viṣṇu, whose forms are endless, is the foremost, desirable bestower of desires. 69

## Chapter 3 - The divisions of time

## \|\| 1.3.1 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*nirguṇasyāprameyasya śuddhasyāpyamalātmanaḥ \|*  
*kathaṃ sargādikartṛtvaṃ brahmaṇobhyupagamyate \|\| 1 \|\|*

How are we to understand that Brahmā is the agent of creation and the rest when he’s free from qualities, measureless, pure and perfect? 1

## \|\| 1.3.2 \|\|

*śrīparāśāra uvāca*  
*śaktayaḥ sarvabhāvānāmacintyajñānagocarāḥ \|*  
*yato 'to brahmaṇastastu sargādyā bhāvaśaktayaḥ \|*  
*bhavanti tapatāṃ śreṣṭha pāvakasya yathoṣṇatā \|\| 2 \|\|*

Not all essential powers of beings are in the realm of comprehension. Powers including creativity are aspects of Brahmā’s essential nature, just as heat is fire’s aspect, best of sages. 2

## \|\| 1.3.3 \|\|

*tannibodha yathā sarge bhagavānsaṃpravartate \|*  
*nārāyaṇākhyo bhagavānbrahmā lokapitāmāhaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

You must therefore understand the function of the lord at the moment of creation. He manifests as Brahmā, also known as Nārāyaṇa, the grandsire of the world. 3

## \|\| 1.3.4 \|\|

*utpannaḥ procyate vidvannityamevopacārataḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

By convention, Brahmā is said to be arisen, wise one, even though he is eternal. 4

## \|\| 1.3.5 \|\|

*nijena tasya mānena āyurvarṣaśataṃ smṛtam \|*  
*tatparākhyaṃ tadarddha ca parārdhamabhidhiyate \|\| 5 \|\|*

He’s thought to live a hundred of his own years, a period called a para, half of which is one parārdha. 5

## \|\| 1.3.6 \|\|

*kālasvarūpaṃ viṣṇoś ca yanmayoktaṃ tavānagha \|*  
*tena tasya nibodhe tvaṃ parimāṇopapādanam \|\| 6 \|\|*

As I told you, blameless one, time is a form of Viṣṇu. Now understand how time applies to the measurement of Brahmā’s lifespan, 6

## \|\| 1.3.7 \|\|

*anyeṣāṃ caiva jantūnāṃ carāṇāmacarāś ca ye \|*  
*bhūbhūbhṛtsāgarādīnāmaśeṣāṇāṃ ca sattama \|\| 7 \|\|*

And to those of other beings, moving and unmoving, best of sages, and to the earth and mountains, seas and all the rest. 7

## \|\| 1.3.8 \|\|

*kāṣṭhā pañcadaśākhyātā nimeṣā munisattama \|*  
*kāṣṭhātriṃśatkalā triṃśatkalā mauhūrtiko vidhiḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Fifteen nimeṣas or blinks of the eye are said to make a kāṣṭhā, best of sages. Thirty kāṣṭhās make one kalā, and thirty kalās make a muhūrta, as a rule. 8

## \|\| 1.3.9 \|\|

*tāvatsaṃkhyair ahorātraṃ muhūrtair mānuṣaṃ smṛtam \|*  
*ahorātrāṇi tāvanti māsaḥ pakṣadvayātmakaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Thirty muhūrtas make one day and night for mortals. A month has thirty days and nights, divided into fortnights. 9

## \|\| 1.3.10 \|\|

*taiḥ ṣaḍbhir ayanaṃ varṣaṃ dve 'yane dakṣiṇottare \|*  
*ayanaṃ dakṣiṇaṃ rātrirdevānāmuttaraṃ dinam \|\| 10 \|\|*

Six months make an ayana, and two ayanas, south and north, comprise a year. The southern one is the deities’ night-time; the northern one, their day. 10

## \|\| 1.3.11 \|\|

*divyair varṣasahasrais tu kṛtatretādisaṃjñitam \|*  
*caturyugaṃ dvādaśabhis tadvibhāgaṃ niboda me \|\| 11 \|\|*

Each cycle of four ages—the Kṛta, Tretā and the others—lasts twelve thousand divine years. Now hear from me how they’re divided. 11

## \|\| 1.3.12 \|\|

*catvāritrīṇi dve caikaṃ kṛtādiṣu yathākramam \|*  
*dvivyābdānāṃ sahasrāṇi yugoṣvāhuḥ purāvidaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Those who understand the past have said the Kṛta and other ages last for four, three, two and one thousand divine years, respectively. 12

## \|\| 1.3.13 \|\|

*tatpramāṇaiḥ śataiḥ saṃdhyā pūrvā tatrābhidhīyate \|*  
*sandhyāṃśaścaiva tattulyo yugasyānantaro hi saḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

The period before each age is called a ‘dawn’. Each dawn lasts as many centuries as the following age has millennia. The time that follows every age is called a ‘dusk’ and is of the same duration. 13

## \|\| 1.3.14 \|\|

*sandhyāsaṃdhyāṃśayorantaryaḥ kālo munisattama \|*  
*yugākhyaḥ sa tu vijñeyaḥ kṛtatretādisaṃjñitaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

The time between each dawn and dusk is called an age or yuga, known by the names of Kṛta, Tretā and the others, best of sages. 14

## \|\| 1.3.15 \|\|

*kṛtaṃ tretā dvāparaś ca kaliścaiva caturyugam \|*  
*procyate tatsahasraṃ va brahmaṇāṃ divasaṃ mune \|\| 15 \|\|*

The Kṛta, Tretā, Dvāpara and Kali together make one fourfold cycle, sage. They say a thousand cycles make just one day for Brahmā. 15

## \|\| 1.3.16 \|\|

*brahmaṇo divase brahmanmanavastu caturdaśa \|*  
*bhavanti pariṇāmaṃ ca teṣāṃ kālakṛtaṃ śṛṇu \|\| 16 \|\|*

In each of Brahmā’s days, brahmin, fourteen Manus reign. Now you’ll hear about the length of each of these. 16

## \|\| 1.3.17 \|\|

*saptarṣayaḥ surāḥ śaktro manustatsūnavo nṛpāḥ \|*  
*ekakāle hi sṛjyante saṃhniyante ca pūrvavat \|\| 17 \|\|*

In the reign of every Manu, seven seers, deities, their king, a Manu himself and the sovereigns who are his progeny are created and absorbed again. 17

## \|\| 1.3.18 \|\|

*caturyugāṇāṃ saṃkhyātā sādhikā hyekasaptatiḥ \|*  
*manvantaraṃ manoḥ kālaḥ surādīnāṃ ca sattama \|\| 18 \|\|*

Each period is somewhat longer than seventy-one times the total years in each cycle of four ages. Called a Manvantara, it lasts for the duration of the Manu, gods and others, best of sages. 18

## \|\| 1.3.19 \|\|

*aṣṭau śata sahasrāṇi divyayā saṃkhyayā smṛtam \|*  
*dvipañcāśattathānyāni sahasrāṇyadhikāni tu \|\| 19 \|\|*

It’s thought to be as long as eight hundred and fifty-two thousand divine years. 19

## \|\| 1.3.20 \|\|

*triṃśatkoṭyastu saṃpūrṇāḥ saṃkhyātāḥ saṃkhyayā dvija \|*  
*saptaṣaṣṭistathānyāni niyutāni mahāmune \|\| 20 \|\|*

This, brahmin, is a total of three hundred and sixty-seven million and twenty thousand years for mortals, best of sages. 20

## \|\| 1.3.21 \|\|

*viṃśatistu sahasrāṇi kāloyamadhikaṃ vinā \|*  
*manvantarasya saṃkhyeyaṃ mānuṣair vatsarair dvija \|\| 21 \|\|*

That’s exactly the length of time, brahmin, for which each Manvantara endures. 21

## \|\| 1.3.22 \|\|

*caturdaśaguṇo hyeṣa kālo brahmamahaḥ smṛtam \|*  
*brahmo naimittiko nāma tasyānte pratisañcaraḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Fourteen Manvantaras are thought to be the length of one of Brahmā’s days, from which its name, one brahma, is derived. At the conclusion of each day, the universe is destroyed. 22

## \|\| 1.3.23 \|\|

*tadā hi dahyate sarva trailokyaṃ bhūrbhuvādikam \|*  
*janaṃ prayānti tāpārtā maharlokanivāsinaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

At that time, the threefold worlds—earth, space and all the rest—are consumed by fire. The inhabitants of the realm of Maharloka, oppressed by heat, retreat to Janaloka. 23

## \|\| 1.3.24 \|\|

*ekārṇave tu traitokye brahmā nārāyaṇātmakaḥ \|*  
*bhogiśayyāṃ gataḥ śete trailokyagrāsabṛṃhitaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

When all three worlds have been reduced to a single ocean, Brahmā, none other than Nārāyaṇa himself, reclines upon his serpent-couch, having grown in strength by consuming the universe. 24

## \|\| 1.3.25 \|\|

*janasthair yogibhir devaścityamānobjasambhavaḥ \|*  
*tatpramāṇāṃ hi tāṃrātriṃ tadante sṛjate punaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Ascetics who dwell in Janaloka meditate on Brahmā all through the night, which lasts as long as a brahma day. When the night is done, he creates the world again. 25

## \|\| 1.3.26 \|\|

*evaṃ tu brahmaṇo varṣamevaṃ varṣaśataṃ ca yat \|*  
*śataṃ hiṃ tasya varṣāṇāṃ paramāyurmahātmanaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

In the same way that Brahmā has his years, he has one hundred of his years. One hundred make a para, the lifespan of that great deity. 26

## \|\| 1.3.27 \|\|

*ekamasya vyatītaṃ tu parārdhaṃ brahmaṇo 'nagha \|*  
*tasyāntebhūnmahākalpaḥ pādma ityabhiviśrutaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

One-half of Brahmā’s para—a parārdha—has passed already, purest sage. The final aeon of that parārdha was the period known as Padma. 27

## \|\| 1.3.28 \|\|

*dvitīyasya parārdhasya vartamānasya vai dvija \|*  
*vārāha iti kalpoyaḥ prathamaḥ parikīrtitaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

This aeon called Vārāha, brahmin, is the first of the second parārdha, which has now begun. 28

## Chapter 4 - Brahmā creates the world anew

## \|\| 1.4.1 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*brahmanārāyaṇākhyo 'sai kalpādau bhagavānyathā \|*  
*sasarja sarvabhūtāni tadācakṣva mahāmune \|\| 1 \|\|*

Tell me, great sage, how Lord Brahmā, known as Nārāyaṇa, created all the beings when the aeon first began. 1

## \|\| 1.4.2 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*prajāḥ sasarja bhagavānbrahmā nārāyaṇātmakaḥ \|*  
*prajāpatipatirdevo yathā tanme niśāmaya \|\| 2 \|\|*

Hear from me how Brahmā, Lord Nārāyaṇa himself and heavenly master of all progenitors, made the world. 2

## \|\| 1.4.3 \|\|

*atītakalpāvasāne niśāsuptotthitaḥ prabhuḥ \|*  
*sattvodriktas tathā brahmā śūnyaṃ lokamavaikṣata \|\| 3 \|\|*

At the end of the previous aeon, Lord Brahmā, filled with purity, having slept all through the night, awoke and saw the world, bereft of living things, was empty. 3

## \|\| 1.4.4 \|\|

*nārāyaṇaḥ paro 'cintyaḥ pareṣāmapi sa prabhuḥ \|*  
*brahmasvarūpī bhagavānanādiḥ sarvasambhavaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Nārāyaṇa—supreme, unimaginable, universal master—is the beginning-less lord and all-creator in the form of Brahmā. 4

## \|\| 1.4.5 \|\|

*imaṃ codāharantyatra ślokaṃ nārāyaṇaṃ prati \|*  
*brahmasvarūpiṇaṃ devaṃ jagataḥ prabhavāpyayam \|\| 5 \|\|*

In this regard, they sing a verse about Nārāyaṇa in the heavenly form of Brahmā, the creation and destruction of the world: 5

## \|\| 1.4.6 \|\|

*āpo nārā iti proktā āpo vai narasūnavaḥ \|*  
*ayanaṃ tasya tāḥ pūrvaṃ tena nārāyaṇaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

‘The waters were known as Nāra, as they were Nara’s sons. As these were the deity’s first abode (ayana), he is known as Nārāyaṇa, “whose home was water”.’ 6

## \|\| 1.4.7 \|\|

*toyāntaḥsthāṃ mahīṃ jñātvā jagatyekārṇavīkṛte \|*  
*anumānāttaduddhāraṃ kartukāmaḥ prajāpatiḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

When the world still lay below the single ocean, the progenitor and lord, knowing that the earth was in its waters, after consideration, desired to raise it. 7

## \|\| 1.4.8 \|\|

*akarotsvatanūmanyāṃ kalpādiṣu yathā purā \|*  
*satsyakūrmādikāṃ tadvadvārāhaṃ varurāsthitaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Just as he had taken the forms of tortoise, fish and others in earlier ages, he now took another form: the boar. 8

## \|\| 1.4.9 \|\|

*vedayajñamayaṃ rūpamaśeṣajagataḥ sthitau \|*  
*sthitaḥ sthirātmā sarvātmā paramātmā prajāpatiḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

The changeless, universal, supreme progenitor took that form, which embodied all the Vedas and the sacrifice, for the preservation of the world. 9

## \|\| 1.4.10 \|\|

*janalokagataiḥ siddhaiḥ sanakādyair abhiṣṭutaḥ \|*  
*praviveśa tadā toyamātmādhāro dharādharaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Praised by Sanaka and other perfect sages who sheltered in Janaloka, he entered the waters, being the foundation of the spiritual and material worlds. 10

## \|\| 1.4.11 \|\|

*nirīkṣya taṃ tadā devī pātālātalamāgatam \|*  
*tuṣṭāva praṇatā bhūtvā bhaktinamrā vasundharā \|\| 11 \|\|*

When the goddess Earth saw that he had reached the lower realm of Pātāla, bowing with deep devotion, she praised him. 11

## \|\| 1.4.12 \|\|

*śrīpṛthivyuvāca*  
*namaste puṇḍarīkākṣa śaṅkhacakragadādhara \|*  
*māmuddharāsmādadya tvaṃ tvatto 'haṃ pūrvasutthitā \|\| 12 \|\|*

The Earth:

I bow to you who are everything, and who bear the conch and discus. Lift me up from here right now as you’ve done before. 12

## \|\| 1.4.13 \|\|

*tvayāhamuddhṛtā pūrvaṃ tvanmayāhaṃ janārdana \|*  
*tathānyāni ca bhūtāni gaganādinyaśeṣataḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

You raised me in the past, and I consist of you, Janārdana, as does the sky and all that is. 13

## \|\| 1.4.14 \|\|

*namaste para mātmātmanpuruṣātmannamostu te \|*  
*pradhānavyaktabhūtāya kālabhūtāya te namaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

I bow to you, the essence of the highest Spirit. Spirit’s essence, to you I bow. I bow to you, primal Matter, the Seen and Time itself. 14

## \|\| 1.4.15 \|\|

*tvaṃ kartā sarvabhūtānāṃ tvaṃ pātā tvaṃ vināśakṛt \|*  
*sargādiṣu prabho brahmaviṣṇurudrātmarūpadhṛk \|\| 15 \|\|*

You are creator of every being, you are protector and destroyer of creation and the other phases, Lord, when you take the forms of Brahmā, Viṣṇu and of Rudra. 15

## \|\| 1.4.16 \|\|

*sambhakṣayitvā sakalaṃ jagatyekārṇavīkṛte \|*  
*śeṣe tvameva govinda cintyamāno manīṣibhiḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

When you’ve consumed the world and it lies beneath the single ocean, you sleep, Govinda, while the wise ones contemplate you. 16

## \|\| 1.4.17 \|\|

*bhavato yatparaṃ tattvaṃ tanna jānāti kaścana \|*  
*avatāreṣu yadrūpaṃ tadarcanti divaukasaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

As no-one knows your ultimate form, the deities worship whichever form you take. 17

## \|\| 1.4.18 \|\|

*tvāmārādhya paraṃ brahma yātā muktiṃ mumukṣavaḥ \|*  
*vāsudevamanārādhya ko mokṣaṃ samavāpsyati \|\| 18 \|\|*

Revering you as the highest Absolute, those who yearn for liberation reach it. Who will reach perfection if they do not worship Vāsudeva? 18

## \|\| 1.4.19 \|\|

*yatkiñcinmanasā grāhyaṃ yadgrahyaṃ cakṣurādibhiḥ \|*  
*buddhyā ca yatparicchedyaṃ tadrūpamakhilaṃ tava \|\| 19 \|\|*

All that can be grasped by mind, by eye or other senses and all that can be known by mind are surely forms of you. 19

## \|\| 1.4.20 \|\|

*tvanmayāhaṃ tvadādhārā tvatsṛṣṭā tvatsamāśrayā \|*  
*mādhavīmiti lokoyamabhidhatte tato hi mām \|\| 20 \|\|*

I’m made of you, you are my foundation and creator, I depend on you. That’s why the whole world calls me Mādhavī. 20

## \|\| 1.4.21 \|\|

*jayākhilajñānamaya jaya sthūlamayāvyaya \|*  
*jayānanta jayāvyakta jaya vyaktamaya prabho \|\| 21 \|\|*

Be victorious! You are all knowledge. Be victorious! You are unchanging and enduring. Be victorious! You are unending. Be victorious! You are Unseen. Be victorious! You are the Seen, Lord. You are the highest supreme, you are everything. Be victorious! You are the purest lord of sacrifices. 21

## \|\| 1.4.22 \|\|

*parāparātmanviśvātmañjaya yajña pate 'nagha \|*  
*tvaṃ yajñastvaṃ paṣaṭkārastvamoṅkārastvamagnayaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

You are the sacrifice. You are the cry of vaṣaṭ. You are the syllable Oṃ. You are the sacrificial fires. You are the Vedas and their supplements. Hari, you are the spirit of the sacrifice. 22

## \|\| 1.4.23 \|\|

*tvaṃ vedāstvaṃ tadaṅgāni tvaṃ yajñapuruṣo hare \|*  
*sūryādayo grahāstārā nakṣatrāṇya khitaṃ jagat \|\| 23 \|\|*

You are the sun and other heavenly bodies, the planets, stars and all the constellations, the formed and formless, the visible and invisible, the highest spirit. 23

## \|\| 1.4.24 \|\|

*mūrtāmūrtamadṛśyaṃ ca dṛśyaṃ ca pūruṣottama \|*  
*yaccoktaṃ yacca naivoktaṃ mayātra parameśvara \|*  
*tatsarvaṃ tvaṃ namastubhyaṃ bhūyobhūyo namonamaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

You are all that I have said, highest Lord, and all that I’ve left unsaid just now. I bow to you again. To you I bow again and again. 24

## \|\| 1.4.25 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ saṃstūyamānastu pṛthivyā dharaṇīdharaḥ \|*  
*sāmasvaradhvaniḥ śrīmāñ jagarja parighargharam \|\| 25 \|\|*

Parāśara:

While the Earth was praising him like this, the glorious bearer of the world, whose voice resounded like the chanting of the Sāma Veda, bellowed loudly. 25

## \|\| 1.4.26 \|\|

*tataḥ samutkṣipya dharāṃ svadaṃṣṭrayā mahāvarāhaḥ sphuṭapadmalocanaḥ \|*  
*rasātalādutpalapatrasannibhaḥ samutthito nīla ivācalo mahān \|\| 26 \|\|*

The mighty boar then raised the earth with just one tusk. His eyes like full-blown lotuses and his body dark as lily leaves, he rose from the lower regions, towering like Mount Nīla. 26

## \|\| 1.4.27 \|\|

*uttiṣṭhatā tena mukhānilāhataṃ tatsaṃbhavāmbho janalokasaṃśrayān \|*  
*prakṣālayāmāsa hi tānmahādyutīn sanāndanādīnapakalmaṣān munīn \|\| 27 \|\|*

The water from his dive, driven by the breath he breathed as he emerged, washed away the sins of Sanandana and the other sages sheltering in Janaloka, leaving them in a purer state. 27

## \|\| 1.4.28 \|\|

*prayāniti toyāni khurāgravikṣatarasātale 'dhaḥ kṛtaśabdasantati \|*  
*śvasānilāstājha paritaḥ prayānti siddhā jane ye niyatā vasanti \|\| 28 \|\|*

Thundering unceasingly, the waters rushed down to the lower realm, trampled by his hooves. The accomplished seers in Janaloka, blasted by the wind whipped up by his breath, were scattered in all directions. 28

## \|\| 1.4.29 \|\|

*uttiṣṭhatastasya jalārdrakukṣermahāvarāhasya mahīṃ vigṛhya \|*  
*vidhunvato vedamayaṃ śarīraṃ romāntarasthā munayaḥ stuvanti \|\| 29 \|\|*

The sages, who landed in the bristles on the back of that mighty boar, praised him as he rose, bearing the earth aloft, his belly wet, as he shook his body, which was the very Vedas. 29

## \|\| 1.4.30 \|\|

*ta tuṣṭuvustoṣaparītacetaso loke jane ye nivasanti yoginaḥ \|*  
*sanandanādyā hyatinamrakandharā dharādharaṃ dhīrataroddhatekṣaṇam \|\| 30 \|\|*

Sanandana and the others in Janaloka, their hearts all filled with joy, bowing with humility, praised that bearer of the earth, whose gaze was steady: 30

## \|\| 1.4.31 \|\|

*jayeśvarāṇāṃ parameśa keśava prabho gadāśaṅkhadharāsicakradhṛk \|*  
*prasūtināśasthiheturīśvarastvameva nānyatparamaṃ ca yatpadam \|\| 31 \|\|*

‘Be victorious, Keśava, highest lord of lords! You, Lord, are the bearer of the mace and conch, sword and discus. You, Lord, are the cause of creation, preservation and destruction of the world. There is no state superior to yours. 31

## \|\| 1.4.32 \|\|

*pādeṣu dedāstava yūpadaṃṣṭrā danteṣu jajñāścitayaś ca vaktre \|*  
*hutāśajihvosi tanuruhāṇi darbhāḥ prabho yajñapumāṃstvameva \|\| 32 \|\|*

Your legs are the form of the Vedas, your tusks the sacrificial post, your teeth the offerings, your mouth the altar, your tongue the fire, the hairs on your body the blades of sacrificial grass. You, Lord, are indeed the spirit of the sacrifice. 32

## \|\| 1.4.33 \|\|

*vilocane rātryahanī mahātmansarvāśrayaṃ brahmaparaṃ śiraste \|*  
*sūktānyaśeṣāṇi saṭākalāpo ghrāṇaṃ samastāni havīṃṣi deva \|\| 33 \|\|*

Your two eyes are day and night, your head the highest Absolute, the universal state, great being. The bristles of your mane are all the Vedic hymns, Lord, your nose the offerings, 33

## \|\| 1.4.34 \|\|

*muktuṇḍasāmasvaradhīranādaprāgvaṃśakāyā khilasatrasandhe \|*  
*pūrteṣṭadharmaśravaṇosi deva sanātanātmanbhagavanprasīda \|\| 34 \|\|*

Your snout the ladle, your deep grunt the chanting of the Sāma Veda, your body the pavilion, your joints the rituals and your two ears the iṣṭa and pūrta rites conducted for oneself and others, God. Favour us, eternal Lord. 34

## \|\| 1.4.35 \|\|

*padakramākrāntabhuvaṃ bhavantamādisthitaṃ cākṣaraviśvamūrte \|*  
*viśvasya vidmaḥ parameśvaro'si prasīda nāthosi parāvarasta \|\| 35 \|\|*

We know you paced the world in just three strides, and that you are endless, but are present at the origin. You are imperishable and your form is universal. We know that you are the highest lord of all. Favour us, protector of all moving and unmoving things. 35

## \|\| 1.4.36 \|\|

*daṃṣṭrāgravinyastamaśeṣametadbhumaṇḍalaṃ nātha vibhāvyate te \|*  
*vigāhataḥ padmavanaṃ vilagānaṃ sarojinīpatramivoḍhapaṅkam \|\| 36 \|\|*

This whole world rests on the tip of your tusk, protector, like a muddy lotus leaf that sticks to you when diving in a lily pond. 36

## \|\| 1.4.37 \|\|

*dyāvāpṛthivyoratulaprabhāva yadantaraṃ tadvapuṣā tavaiva \|*  
*vyāptaṃ jagavdyāptisamarthadīpte hitāya viśvasya vibho bhava tvam \|\| 37 \|\|*

Your might is unequalled, your body fills the space between earth and heaven and your light pervades the world. Lord, abide for the good of every being. 37

## \|\| 1.4.38 \|\|

*paramārthastvamevaiko nānyosti jagataḥ pate \|*  
*tavaiṣa mahimā yena vyāptametaccarācaram \|\| 38 \|\|*

You are indeed the single highest goal, there is no other, universal Lord. Your greatness fills the universe of moving and unmoving things. 38

## \|\| 1.4.39 \|\|

*yadetaddṛśyate mūrtametajjñānātmanastava \|*  
*bhrāntijñānena paśyanti jagadrūpamayoginaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

This visible world is your body when you take the form of knowledge, but those who aren’t ascetics cannot see the world correctly. 39

## \|\| 1.4.40 \|\|

*jñānasvarūpamakhilaṃ jagadetadabuddhayaḥ \|*  
*arthasvarūpaṃ paśyanto bhrāmyante mohasaṃplave \|\| 40 \|\|*

Those unwise beings who see this world, which is in nature knowledge, as an object with reality sink in an ocean of delusion. 40

## \|\| 1.4.41 \|\|

*yetu jñānavidaḥ suddhacetasaste 'khilaṃ jagat \|*  
*jñānātmakaṃ prapaśyanti tvadrūpaṃ parameśvara \|\| 41 \|\|*

But pure-hearted beings who understand true knowledge, highest Lord, see this world as your own form comprising knowledge itself. 41

## \|\| 1.4.42 \|\|

*prasīda sarva sarvātmanbhavāya jagatāmimām \|*  
*uddharorvīmameyātmañchanno dehyabjalocana \|\| 42 \|\|*

Favour us! You are all and at the heart of all. Raise the earth to make the worlds! You are beyond all measure. Lotus-eyed deity, gratify us! 42

## \|\| 1.4.43 \|\|

*sattvodrikto 'si bhagavan govinda pṛthivīmimām \|*  
*samuddhara bhavāyeśa śanno dehyabjalocana \|\| 43 \|\|*

You are filled with goodness, Lord. Raise up this earth, Lord Govinda, to make the worlds. Lotus-eyed deity, gratify us! 43

## \|\| 1.4.44 \|\|

*sargapravṛttirbhavato jagatāmupakāriṇi \|*  
*bhavatveṣā namaste 'stu śanno dehyabjalocana \|\| 44 \|\|*

May your act of creation benefit the worlds. We bow to you. Lotus-eyed deity, gratify us!’ 44

## \|\| 1.4.45 \|\|

*śrīpāraśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ saṃstūyamānastu paramātmā mahīdharaḥ \|*  
*ujjahāra kṣitiṃ kṣipraṃ nyastavāṃś ca mahāmbhasi \|\| 45 \|\|*

Parāśara:

While the sages were praising him with these words, the highest spirit, bearer of the earth, raised the world and set it down at once amid that mighty ocean. 45

## \|\| 1.4.46 \|\|

*tasyopari jalaughasya sahatī nauriva sthitā \|*  
*vitatattvāttu dehasya na mahī yāti saṃplāvam \|\| 46 \|\|*

The earth floated on the surface of the water like some great vessel, and because it was so broad it didn’t sink. 46

## \|\| 1.4.47 \|\|

*tataḥ kṣitiṃ samāṃ kṛtvā pṛthivyāṃ socinodgirīn \|*  
*yathāvibhāgaṃ bhagavānanādiḥ parameśvaraḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

Then the lord, who has no start but is the origin of all, spread the earth and heaped up mountains on its surface place by place. 47

## \|\| 1.4.48 \|\|

*prāksargadagdhān akhilān parvatān pṛthivītale \|*  
*amoghena prabhāveṇa sasarjāmoghavāñchitaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

He whose wishes are never thwarted used his unfailing power to create again on the surface of the earth all the mountains burned at the end of the previous aeon. 48

## \|\| 1.4.49 \|\|

*bhūvibhāgaṃ tataḥ kṛtvā saptadvīpānyathātatham \|*  
*bhūrādyāṃścaturo lokānpūrvavatsamakalpayat \|\| 49 \|\|*

He divided the earth into regions, remade the seven continents in the same manner, and laid out four spheres, beginning with the land, as he had before. 49

## \|\| 1.4.50 \|\|

*brahmarūpadharo devastato 'sau rajasā vṛtaḥ \|*  
*cakāra sṛṣṭiṃ bhagavāṃścaturvakradharo hariḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

Divine Lord Hari, in the form of four-faced Brahmā, swathed in energy, then created all other things. 50

## \|\| 1.4.51 \|\|

*nimittamātramevāsau sṛjyānāṃ sargakarmaṇi \|*  
*pradhānakāraṇībhūtā yato vai sṛjyaśaktayaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

Brahmā is, however, just the instrumental cause in the creation of that which is created, since all this arises from primal matter. 51

## \|\| 1.4.52 \|\|

*nimittamātraṃ muktvaivaṃ nānyatkiñcidapekṣate \|*  
*nīyate tapatāṃ śreṣṭa svaśaktyā vastu vastutām \|\| 52 \|\|*

Apart from this single instrumental cause, no other cause is needed. One substance becomes another from its own inherent tendency, excellent ascetic. 52

## Chapter 5 - Brahmā creates living beings

## \|\| 1.5.1 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*yathā sasarja devo 'sau devarṣipitṛdāna vān \|*  
*manuṣyatiryagvṛkṣādīnbhūvyomasalilaukasaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

How did that divine being create the gods, sages, ancestral spirits, Dānavas, humankind, animals, plants and all the rest that live on land, in air or water? 1

## \|\| 1.5.2 \|\|

*yadguṇaṃ yatsvabhāvaṃ ca yadrūpaṃ ca jagaddvija \|*  
*sargādau sṛṣṭavānbrahmā tanmamācakṣva kṛtstraśaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

What are the qualities, form and nature of the world that Brahmā made at the beginning of creation? Describe all this in detail, brahmin. 2

## \|\| 1.5.3 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*maitreya kathayāmyetacchṛṣuṣva susamāhitaḥ \|*  
*yathā sasarja devosau devādīnakhilānvibhuḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Listen carefully, Maitreya, and I’ll tell you how the divine lord created the gods and all. 3

## \|\| 1.5.4 \|\|

*sṛṣṭiṃ cintaya tastasya kalpādiṣu yathā purā \|*  
*abuddhipūrvakaḥ sargaḥ prādurbhūtastamomayaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

While contemplating creation, as at the start of every previous aeon, there appeared a creation preceded by ignorance and consisting of darkness. 4

## \|\| 1.5.5 \|\|

*tamomoho mahāmohastāmistro hyandhasaṃjñitaḥ \|*  
*avidya pañcaparvaiṣā prāgurbhūtā mahātmanaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Fivefold ignorance—darkness, error, delusion, gloom and blindness—arose from that great being. 5

## \|\| 1.5.6 \|\|

*pañcadhāvasthitaḥ sargo dhyāyato pratibodhavān \|*  
*bahiranto 'prakāśaś ca saṃvṛtātmā nagātmakaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

This creation, which sprang from his meditation, is the fivefold world of plants, in which there is no perception. Outer sensations and inner emotions are absent, and objects are veiled and static. 6

## \|\| 1.5.7 \|\|

*mukhyā nagā yataḥ proktā mukhyasargastatastvayam \|\| 7 \|\|*

Because immobile things were created first, this is called foundational creation. 7

## \|\| 1.5.8 \|\|

*taṃ dṛṣvāsādhakaṃ sargamamanyadaparaṃ punaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Seeing this was not enough, Brahmā thought about creating more. 8

## \|\| 1.5.9 \|\|

*tasyābhidhyāyataḥ sargastiryakstrotobhyavartata \|*  
*yasmāttiryakpravṛttisya tiryakstrotāstataḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

While he was contemplating, the creation of animals took place. This is known as the Tiryaksrotas creation, because animals have horizontal (tiryak) digestive tracts (srotas). 9

## \|\| 1.5.10 \|\|

*paśvādayaste vikhyātā stamaḥprāyā hyavedinaḥ \|*  
*utpathagrāhiṇaścaiva te 'jñāne jñānamāninaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

They’re called beasts and other names, are generally dull by nature, unwitting, have bad habits and mistake ignorance for knowledge. 10

## \|\| 1.5.11 \|\|

*ahaṅkṛtā ahaṃmānā aṣṭāviṃśadvadhātmakāḥ \|*  
*ataḥ prakāśāste sarve āvṛtāś ca parasparam \|\| 11 \|\|*

Self-centred, proud and subject to twenty-eight faults, yet all have feelings and interact with one another. 11

## \|\| 1.5.12 \|\|

*tamapyasādhakaṃ matvā dhyāyatonyastato 'bhavat \|*  
*ūrdhvastretāstṛtīyastu sāttvikordhvamavartata \|\| 12 \|\|*

Brahmā thought that this was insufficient and, while contemplating, a further creation took place: the third, Ūrdhvasrotas, blessed with goodness, appeared above (ūrdhva) in the heavenly sphere. 12

## \|\| 1.5.13 \|\|

*te sukhaprītibahulā bahirantaś ca nāvṛtāḥ \|*  
*prakāśā bahirantaś ca ūrdhvastrotodbhavāḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Those born of the Ūrdhvasrotas creation are said to know much happiness and joy; they’re not immune from outer sensations or inner emotions, but are awake to both. 13

## \|\| 1.5.14 \|\|

*tuṣṭātmānastṛtīyastu devasargastu sa smṛtaḥ \|*  
*tasminsargebhavatprītirniṣpanne brahmaṇasta dā \|\| 14 \|\|*

Filled with contentment, this third creation is called divine, and Brahmā was pleased with all the work that he had done. 14

## \|\| 1.5.15 \|\|

*tatonyaṃ sa tadā dadhyau sādhakaṃ sargamuttamam \|*  
*asādhakāṃstu tāñjñātvāḥ mukhyasargādisambhavān \|\| 15 \|\|*

Knowing that the first and following creative acts were not enough, he then thought another feat of excellent creation would suffice. 15

## \|\| 1.5.16 \|\|

*tathābhidhyāyatastasya satyābhidhyāyinastataḥ \|*  
*prādurbabhūva cāvyaktādarvāstretāstu sādhakaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

While contemplating, as his mind was set on truth, the creation known as Arvāksrotas appeared from the Unseen element, and this he thought sufficient. 16

## \|\| 1.5.17 \|\|

*yasmādarvāgvyavartanta tatorvākstrotasastu te \|*  
*te ca prakāśabahulāstamodriktā rajodhikāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Because these creatures have vertical (arvāk) digestive tracts, they’re called Arvāksrotas. Although they are most gifted, they’re still subject to dullness and excessive energy. 17

## \|\| 1.5.18 \|\|

*tasmātte duḥkhabahulā bhūyobhūyaś ca kāriṇaḥ \|*  
*prakāśā bahirantaś ca manuṣyāḥ sādhakāstu te \|\| 18 \|\|*

They therefore suffer from much pain and are always forced to act. They also have sensations and emotions. This, then, is humanity and Brahmā thought this was sufficient. 18

## \|\| 1.5.19 \|\|

*ityete kathitāḥ sargāḥ ṣaḍatra munisattama \|*  
*prathamo mahataḥ sargo vijñeyo brahmaṇastu saḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

Six stages of creation have been explained here, best of sages. The first was the creation from the element of Greatness, that of Brahmā. 19

## \|\| 1.5.20 \|\|

*tanmātrāṇāṃ dvitīyaś ca bhūtasargo hi sa smṛtaḥ \|*  
*vaikārikastṛtīyastu sarga endriyakaḥ sma-taḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

The second was the elemental creation of rudimentary factors. The third was the subsequent creation of the senses. 20

## \|\| 1.5.21 \|\|

*ityeṣa prākṛtaḥ sargaḥ sambhūto buddhipūrvakaḥ \|*  
*mukhyasargaścaturthastu mukhyā vai sthāvarāḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

These are the primary creations and are preceded by intellect. The foundational creation was the fourth, so called because unmoving things provide a basis. 21

## \|\| 1.5.22 \|\|

*tiryakstrotāstu yaḥ proktas tairyagyonyaḥ sa ucyate \|*  
*tadūrdhvastrotasāṃ ṣaṣṭho devasargastu saṃsmṛtaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

The fifth is called Tiryaksrotas or animal creation. Next came divine creation, the sixth, or Urdhvasrotas. 22

## \|\| 1.5.23 \|\|

*tator'vākstrotasāṃ sargaḥ saptamaḥ sa tu mānuṣaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Then came Arvāksrotas, the creation of humanity, the seventh. 23

## \|\| 1.5.24 \|\|

*aṣṭamo 'nugrahaḥ sargaḥ sāttvikastāmasaś ca saḥ \|*  
*pañcaite vaikṛtāḥ sargāḥ prākṛtāstu trayaḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

The eighth is Anugraha, marked by purity and dullness. These are the five secondary creations. The primary ones are three in number. 24

## \|\| 1.5.25 \|\|

*prākṛto vaikṛtaścaiva kaumāro navamaḥ smṛtaḥ \|*  
*ityete vai samākhyātā nava sargāḥ prajāpateḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

The primary and secondary events, with Kaumāra as the ninth, are known as the nine creations of Brahmā, the creative lord. 25

## \|\| 1.5.26 \|\|

*prākṛtā vaikṛtāścaiva jagato mūlahetavaḥ \|*  
*sṛjato jagadīśasya kimanyañchrotumicchasi \|\| 26 \|\|*

These primary and secondary creations, proceeding from the universal lord, are the root cause of the universe. What further do you wish to hear? 26

## \|\| 1.5.27 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*saṃkṣepātkathitaḥ sarge devādīnāṃ mune tvayā \|*  
*vistarācchrotumicchami tvatto munivarottama \|\| 27 \|\|*

Maitreya:

You’ve described in brief the creation of the deities and the rest, master, but I’d like to hear more details from you, best of sages. 27

## \|\| 1.5.28 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*karmabhir bhāvitāḥ pūrvaiḥ kuśalāḥ kuśalais tu tāḥ \|*  
*khyātyā tayā hyanirmuktāḥ saṃhāre hyupasaṃhṛtāḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Beings are shaped by their previous actions, good and bad. They can’t escape this fact, even when they’re burned at the end of time. 28

## \|\| 1.5.29 \|\|

*sthāvarāntāḥ surādyāstu prajā brahmaścaturvidhāḥ \|*  
*brahmaṇaḥ kurvataḥ sṛṣṭiṃ jajñire mānasāstu tāḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

When Brahmā made the world, there arose four kinds of creatures, from static things to deities and others, brahmin—all products of his mind. 29

## \|\| 1.5.30 \|\|

*tato devāsurapitṝnmanuṣyāṃś ca catuṣṭayam \|*  
*sisṛkṣurambhāṃsyetāni svamātmānamayūyujat \|\| 30 \|\|*

Desiring to produce the fourfold creation of gods, demigods, ancestral spirits and humankind, he combined these four within himself. 30

## \|\| 1.5.31 \|\|

*yuktātmanastamomātrā hy udriktābhūtprajāpateḥ \|*  
*sisṛkṣorjaghanātpūrvamasurā jajñire tataḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

Thus, merged within his mind, Brahmā, lord of creation, became completely filled with dullness. Then, desiring to begin creation, from his thighs the demigods arose at first. 31

## \|\| 1.5.32 \|\|

*utsasarja tatastāṃ tu tamomātrātmikāṃ tanum \|*  
*sā tu vyaktā tanustena maitreyābhūdvibhāvarī \|\| 32 \|\|*

Brahmā cast off that body of dullness, Maitreya, and, once abandoned, it became the night. 32

## \|\| 1.5.33 \|\|

*sisṛkṣuranyadehasthaḥ prītimāpa tataḥ surāḥ \|*  
*sattvodriktāḥ samudbhutā mukhato brahmaṇo dvijaḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

Still filled with that creative urge and taking on another body, Brahmā was pleased, brahmin, and the deities, imbued with purity, appeared from his mouth. 33

## \|\| 1.5.34 \|\|

*tyaktā sāpi tanustena sattvaprāyamabhūddinam \|*  
*tato hi balino rātrāvasurā devatā divā \|\| 34 \|\|*

That body was also shed by Brahmā and became the day that’s filled with light. That’s why demigods are strong by night and deities are strong by day. 34

## \|\| 1.5.35 \|\|

*sattvamātrātmikāmeva tatonyāṃ jagṛhe tanum \|*  
*pitṛvanmanyamānasya pitarastasya jajñire \|\| 35 \|\|*

Brahmā then took another body imbued with purity and, thinking of himself as sire, the ancestors of humankind appeared. 35

## \|\| 1.5.36 \|\|

*utsasarja tatastāṃ tu pitṝnsṛṣṭvāpi sa prabhuḥ \|*  
*sā cotsṛṣṭābhavatsaṃdhyā dina naktāntarasthitā \|\| 36 \|\|*

After the lord had made the ancestors, he cast off that body as well and, being abandoned, it became the dusk. 36

## \|\| 1.5.37 \|\|

*rajomātrātmikāmanyāṃ jagṛhe sa tanuṃ tataḥ \|*  
*rajomātrotkaṭā jātā manuṣyā dvijasattama \|\| 37 \|\|*

He then took another form imbued with energy, best of brahmins, and humankind, which abounds in vigour, was born. 37

## \|\| 1.5.38 \|\|

*tāmapyāśu sa tatyāja tanuṃ sadyaḥ prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*jyotstrā samabhavatsāpi prāksandhyā yābhidhīyate \|\| 38 \|\|*

At that very moment, the creative lord cast off that form, and it became the glimmer known as dawn. 38

## \|\| 1.5.39 \|\|

*jyotstrāgame tu balino manuṣyāḥ pitaras tathā \|*  
*maitreya saṃdhyāsamaye tasmādete bhavanti vai \|\| 39 \|\|*

That’s why folk are more active at the break of day and the ancestors more powerful at dusk, Maitreya. 39

## \|\| 1.5.40 \|\|

*jyotsnā rātryahani saṃdhyā catvāryatāni vai prabhoḥ \|*  
*brahmaṇastu śarīrāṇi triguṇopāśrayāṇi tu \|\| 40 \|\|*

Night and day, dawn and dusk—these four are bodies of Lord Brahmā imbued with threefold qualities of dullness, purity and energy. 40

## \|\| 1.5.41 \|\|

*rajomātrātmikāmeva tatonyāṃ jagṛhe tanum \|*  
*tataḥ kṣudbrahmaṇo jātā jajñe kāmastayā tataḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

Brahmā then took another form infused with energy, and hunger was born from him, with the anger it engenders. 41

## \|\| 1.5.42 \|\|

*kṣutkṣāmānandhakāretha sosṛjadbhagavāṃstataḥ \|*  
*vurūpāḥ śmaśrulā jātāstebhyadhāvaṃ stataḥ prabhum \|\| 42 \|\|*

Then in darkness filled with hunger, Lord Brahmā created beings, hairy monsters, who rushed towards him. 42

## \|\| 1.5.43 \|\|

*maivaṃ bho rakṣyatāmeṣa yair uktaṃ rākṣasāstu te \|*  
*ūcuḥ khādāma ityanye ye te yakṣāstu jakṣaṇāt \|\| 43 \|\|*

Those who cried, ‘Don’t do this, Lord. Protect us! (rakṣyatām)’, became the demons known as rākṣasas. Others cried, ‘We want to eat!’, and they became the yakṣas because they needed food (jakṣaṇa). 43

## \|\| 1.5.44 \|\|

*apriyeṇa tu tāndṛṣṭvā keśāḥ śīryantavedhasaḥ \|*  
*hīnāś ca śiraso bhūyaḥ samārohanta tacchiraḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

When Brahmā beheld these awful beings, the hairs dropped from his head, but having fallen, they climbed up again. 44

## \|\| 1.5.45 \|\|

*sarpaṇātte 'bhavan sarpā hīnatvādahayaḥ smṛtāḥ \|*  
*tataḥ kruddho jagatstraṣṭā krodhātmāno vinirmame \|*  
*varṇena kapiśenograbhūtāste piśitāśanāḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

Because they slipped (sarpaṇa) from his scalp, they became the serpents (sarpa), and because they left (hīna) his head behind, they’re also known as snakes (ahi). The enraged creator of the world then brought forth flesh-eating bhūta-spirits, red in colour, furious and terrifying. 45

## \|\| 1.5.46 \|\|

*dhyāyatoṅgātsamutpannā gandharvāstasya tatkṣaṇāt \|*  
*pibanto jajñire vācaṃ gandharvāstena te dvija \|\| 46 \|\|*

While he meditated, the gandharvas arose from his body at that time, brahmin, so-called as they were born while drinking speech (gandharva). 46

## \|\| 1.5.47 \|\|

*etāni sṛṣṭvā bhagavānbrahma tacchakticoditaḥ \|*  
*tataḥ svacchandatonyāni vayāṃsi vayaso 'sṛjat \|\| 47 \|\|*

After Lord Brahmā had made these beings, spurred by the energy of that endeavour, he then produced other beings at will, and with that energy made the birds. 47

## \|\| 1.5.48 \|\|

*apayo vakṣasaścakre mukhatojāḥ sa sṛṣṛvān \|*  
*sṛśṛvānudarādgāś ca pārśvabhyāṃ ca prajāpatiḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

The lord of creation made sheep from his chest, goats from his mouth and cattle from his belly and flanks. 48

## \|\| 1.5.49 \|\|

*padbhyāṃ cāśvānsamātaṅgānrāsabhāngavayānmṛgān \|*  
*uṣṭrānaśvatarāṃścaiva nyaṅkūnanyāś ca jātayaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

From his feet came elephants and horses, donkeys, wild oxen, deer, camels, mules, antelopes and beasts of other kinds. 49

## \|\| 1.5.50 \|\|

*oṣadhyaḥ phalamūlinyo romabhyastasya jatrire \|*  
*tretāyugamukhe brahmā kalpasyādau dvijottama \|*  
*sṛṣṭvāpaścauṣadhīḥ samyagyuyojasa tadādhvare \|\| 50 \|\|*

Those plants that bring forth fruit and roots arose from the hairs of his head. Having made all the plants and animals at the onset of the aeon, best of brahmins, Brahmā employed them in sacrifices at the beginning of the Tretā age. 50

## \|\| 1.5.51 \|\|

*gaurajaḥ puruṣo meṣaścāśvāśvataragardabhāḥ \|*  
*etāngrāmyānpaśūnāhurāraṇyāṃś ca nibodha me \|\| 51 \|\|*

Cattle, goats, buffalo, sheep, horses, mules and donkeys—these they call domestic animals. Now learn about wild animals from me: 51

## \|\| 1.5.52 \|\|

*śvāpadā dvikhurā hastī vānarāḥ pakṣipañcamāḥ \|*  
*audakāḥ paśavaḥ ṣaṣṭhā saptamāstu sarīsṛpāḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

Those with claws or cloven hooves, elephants, monkeys, birds are fifth, those that live in water sixth, and those that creep and crawl are the seventh kind. 52

## \|\| 1.5.53 \|\|

*gāyatraṃ ca ṛcaścaiva trivṛtsomaṃ rathantaram \|*  
*agniṣṭomaṃ ca yajñānāṃ nirmame prathamānmukhāt \|\| 53 \|\|*

The Gāyatrī metre, the Ṛg Veda, the Trivṛtsāma, the Rathantara and, of the sacrifices, the Agniṣṭoma, arose from Brahmā’s eastern mouth. 53

## \|\| 1.5.54 \|\|

*yajūṃṣi traiṣṭubhaṃ chandaḥ stomaṃ pañcadaśaṃ tathā \|*  
*bṛhatsāma tathokthaṃ ca dakṣiṇādasṛjanmu khāt \|\| 54 \|\|*

The Yajur Veda, the Triṣṭubh metre, the Pañcadaśa hymns, the Bṛhatsāman and the Uktha issued from his southern mouth. 54

## \|\| 1.5.55 \|\|

*sāmāni jagatīchandaḥ stomaṃ saptadaśaṃ tathā \|*  
*vairūpamatirātraṃ ca paścimādasṛjanmukhāt \|\| 55 \|\|*

The Sāma Veda, the Jagatī metre, the Saptadaśa hymns, the Vairūpa and the Atirātra sacrifice issued from his western mouth. 55

## \|\| 1.5.56 \|\|

*ekaviṃśamatharvāṇamāptoryāmāṇameva ca \|*  
*anuṣṭubhaṃ ca vairājamuttarādasṛjanmukhāt \|\| 56 \|\|*

The Ekaviṃśa hymns, the Atharva Veda, the Āptoryāma, the Anuṣṭubh metre and the Vairāja issued from his northern mouth. 56

## \|\| 1.5.57 \|\|

*uccāvacāni bhūtāni gātrebhyastasya jajñire \|*  
*devāsurapitṝn sṛṣṭvā manuṣyāṃś ca prajāpatiḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

Creatures great and small arose from his limbs after the lord of creation made the gods, demigods, ancestors and humankind. 57

## \|\| 1.5.58 \|\|

*tataḥ punaḥ sasarjādau saṃkalpasya pitāmahaḥ \|*  
*yakṣān piśācāngandharvān tathaivāpsarasāṃ gaṇān \|\| 58 \|\|*

At the beginning of the aeon, the universal grandsire made the yakṣas, piśācas, gandharvas and hosts of apsarases, 58

## \|\| 1.5.59 \|\|

*narakinnararakṣāṃsi vayaḥ paśumṛgoragān \|*  
*avyayaṃ ca vyayaṃ caiva yadidaṃ sthāṇujaṅgamam \|\| 59 \|\|*

Naras, kiṃnaras and rākṣasas, birds, animals of village and forest, snakes—everything in the world, changing and unchanging, moving and unmoving. 59

## \|\| 1.5.60 \|\|

*tatsasarja tadā brahma bhagavānādikṛtprabhuḥ \|*  
*teṣāṃ ye yāni karmāṇi praksṛṣṭyāṃ pratipedire \|*  
*tānyeva te prapadyante sṛjyamānāḥ punaḥpunaḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

Lord Brahmā, original creator, made all this. The roles all these beings had filled in previous aeons, they undertook once more, being made again. 60

## \|\| 1.5.61 \|\|

*histrāhiṃstre mṛdukrūre dharmadharmāvṛtānṛte \|*  
*tadbhāvitāḥ prapadyante tasmāttattasya rocate \|\| 61 \|\|*

Cruel and kind, harsh and gentle, right and wrong, true and false—beings undertook and delighted in the roles they were assigned. 61

## \|\| 1.5.62 \|\|

*indriyārtheṣu bhūteṣu śarīreṣu ca sa prabhuḥ \|*  
*nānātvaṃ viniyogaṃ ca dhātaivaṃ vyasṛjatsvayam \|\| 62 \|\|*

The lord creator made the differences among the objects of the senses, beings, bodies and their functions. 62

## \|\| 1.5.63 \|\|

*nāma rūpaṃ ca bhūtānāṃ kṛtyānāṃ ca prapañcanam \|*  
*vedaśabdebhya evādau devādīnāṃ cakāra saḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

He gave names and forms to all the creatures and fixed the range of tasks for gods and other beings in the beginning, following the Vedas’ words. 63

## \|\| 1.5.64 \|\|

*ṛṣīṇāṃ nāmadheyāni yathā vedaśrutāni vai \|*  
*tathā niyogayogyāni hyanyeṣāmapi so 'karot \|\| 64 \|\|*

He provided names and fitting roles to all the sages in accordance with the Vedas. 64

## \|\| 1.5.65 \|\|

*yathartuṣvṛtuliṅgāni nānārūpāṇi parya ye \|*  
*dṛsyante tāni tānyeva tathā bhāvā yugādiṣu \|\| 65 \|\|*

Just as varied signs of every season appear in turn, so, too, are the characteristics of each age. 65

## \|\| 1.5.66 \|\|

*karotyevaṃvidhāṃ sṛṣṭiṃ kalpādau sa punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*sisṛkṣāśaktiyuktosau sṛjyaśaktipracoditaḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

Thus, Brahmā undertakes creation once more at the start of every aeon, filled with the power of creative urges and driven by the energies of those to be created. 66

## Chapter 6 - Human society

## \|\| 1.6.1 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*arvākstrotāstu kathito bhavatā yastu mānuṣaḥ \|*  
*brahmanvistarato brūhi brahma tamasṛjadyathā \|\| 1 \|\|*

You’ve mentioned the Arvāksrotas creation, the origin of humankind, brahmin, but tell me in more detail how Brahmā accomplished this. 1

## \|\| 1.6.2 \|\|

*yathā ca varṇānasṛjadyadguṇāṃ ś ca prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*yacca teṣāṃ smṛtaṃ karma viprādīnāṃ taducyatām \|\| 2 \|\|*

Tell me how he made the four communities, the varṇas, with their respective qualities, great sage, as well as the duties of the brahmins and the others. 2

## \|\| 1.6.3 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*satyābhidhyāyinaḥ pūrvaṃ sisṛkṣerbrahmaṇo jagat \|*  
*ajāyanta dvijaśreṣṭha sattvodriktā mukhātprajāḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

When Brahmā wished to create the world, while contemplating purity, best of brahmins, beings invested with that quality first issued from his mouth. 3

## \|\| 1.6.4 \|\|

*vakṣamo rajasodriktās tathā vai brahmaṇo 'bhavan \|*  
*rajasā tamasā caiva samudriktāstathorutaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

From Brahmā’s chest came others filled with energy, and from his thighs came beings with both energy and dullness. 4

## \|\| 1.6.5 \|\|

*padbhyāmanyāḥ prajā brahmā sasarja dvijasattama \|*  
*tamaḥpradhānāstāḥ sarvaścāturvarṇyamidaṃ tataḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Brahmā brought forth others from his feet, best of brahmins, all overcome by dullness. These comprise the four communities. 5

## \|\| 1.6.6 \|\|

*brahmaṇāḥ kṣatriyā veśyāḥ śūdrāś ca dvijasattama \|*  
*pādoruvakṣasthalato mukhataś ca samudgatāḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Brahmins, kṣatriyas, vaiśyas and śūdras arose from his mouth, chest, thighs and feet, best of brahmins. 6

## \|\| 1.6.7 \|\|

*yajñaniṣpattaye sarvametadbrahyā cakāra vai \|*  
*cāturvarṇyaṃ mahābhāga yajñasādhanamuttamam \|\| 7 \|\|*

Brahmā did this so that sacrifices could be undertaken, blessed sage, and these four orders are the perfect means for doing so. 7

## \|\| 1.6.8 \|\|

*yajñair āpyāyitā devā vṛṣṭyutsargeṇa vai prajāḥ \|*  
*āpyāyayante dharmajña yajñāḥ kalyāṇahetavaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Deities are sustained by sacrifices, and folk are nourished when the gods send rain. Sacrifices are the source of welfare—you understand this, dharma. 8

## \|\| 1.6.9 \|\|

*niṣpādyante narais tais tu svadharmābhirataiḥ sadā \|*  
*viśuddhācaraṇaipetaiḥ sadbhiḥ sanmārgagāmibhiḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Sacrifice is carried out by pious men delighting in their duties and pure in their behaviour as they walk the path of virtue. 9

## \|\| 1.6.10 \|\|

*svargāpavargau mānuṣyātprāpnuvanti narā mune \|*  
*yaccābhir ucitaṃ sthānaṃ tadyānti manujā dvija \|\| 10 \|\|*

By means of sacrifice, men reach heaven or liberation by adhering to their duties, sage, or else they’ll reach a realm where they’ll be happy, brahmin. 10

## \|\| 1.6.11 \|\|

*prajāstā brahmaṇā sṛṣṭāścāturvarṇyavyavasthi tāḥ \|*  
*samyakchraddhāḥ samācārapravaṇā munisattama \|\| 11 \|\|*

People blessed with perfect faith and filled with virtue were made in four communities by Brahmā, best of sages. 11

## \|\| 1.6.12 \|\|

*yathecchāvāsaniratāḥ sarvabādhāvivarjitāḥ \|*  
*śuddhāntaḥ karaṇāḥ śuddhāḥ karmānuṣṭhā nanirmalāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Residing happily where they wished, they were free from troubles, pure of heart, blameless and virtuous in all their actions. 12

## \|\| 1.6.13 \|\|

*śuddhe ca tāsāṃ manasi śuddhentaḥsaṃsthite harau \|*  
*suddhajñānaṃ prapaśyanti bilvākhyaṃ yena tatpadam \|\| 13 \|\|*

When Hari in pure form dwelled within them, perfect knowledge filled their stainless minds and, on this account, they saw that state called Viṣṇu. 13

## \|\| 1.6.14 \|\|

*tataḥ kālātma ko yo 'sau sa cāṃśaḥ kathito hareḥ \|*  
*sa pātayatyaghaṃ ghoram alpam alpālpasāravat \|\| 14 \|\|*

Then Hari’s aspect known as Time caused wickedness to come among them. It was frightening but petty, of little substance, 14

## \|\| 1.6.15 \|\|

*adharmabījamudbhutaṃ tamolobhasamudbhavam \|*  
*prajāsu tāsu maitreya rāgādikamasādhakam \|\| 15 \|\|*

Misconduct’s seed, born of greed and darkness. It took the form of anger and other negative emotions and impeded their success. 15

## \|\| 1.6.16 \|\|

*tataḥ sā sahajā siddhastāsāṃ nātīva jāyate \|*  
*rasollāsādayaścānyāḥ siddhayoṣṭau bhavanti yāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Then innate perfection, consisting of eight accomplishments, spontaneous satisfaction and the others, arose no more to the same extent among them. 16

## \|\| 1.6.17 \|\|

*tāsu kṣīṇāsvaśeṣāsu vardhamāne ca pātake \|*  
*dvandvābhibhavaduḥkhārtāstā bhavanti tataḥ prajāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

While all were weakened and evil grew apace, they were beset by strife, defeat and suffering. 17

## \|\| 1.6.18 \|\|

*tato durgāṇi tāścakrurdhānvaṃ pārvatamaudakam \|*  
*kṛtrimaṃ ca tathā durgaṃ purakharvaṭakādikam \|\| 18 \|\|*

Seeking refuge in the forests, in the mountains and on water, there they built their strongholds, towns and cities. 18

## \|\| 1.6.19 \|\|

*gṛhīṇi ca yathānyāyaṃ teṣu cakuḥ purādiṣu \|*  
*śītātapādibādhānāṃ praśamāya mahāmate \|\| 19 \|\|*

They duly made their dwellings in those cities and elsewhere, wise sage, to shelter from the cold, the heat and other troubles. 19

## \|\| 1.6.20 \|\|

*pratīkāramimaṃ katvā śītādestāḥ prajāḥ punaḥ \|*  
*vārtopāyaṃ tataścakrurhastasiddhiṃ ca karmajām \|\| 20 \|\|*

Warding off the cold and other elements, folk adopted manual labour as their livelihood. 20

## \|\| 1.6.21 \|\|

*vrīhayaś ca yavāścaiva godhūmāścaṇavastilāḥ \|*  
*priyaṅgavo hyudā rāś ca koradūṣāḥ satīnakāḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

Rice, barley, wheat, millet, sesame, foxtail millet, sorghum, ditch millet, peas, 21

## \|\| 1.6.22 \|\|

*māṣā mudgā masūrāś ca niṣpāvāḥ sakulātthakāḥ \|*  
*āḍhakyaścaṇakāścaiva śaṇāḥ saptadaśa smṛtāḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Beans, black and brown lentils, cowpeas, horse gram, pigeon peas, chickpeas and hemp—these are the seventeen crops they grew, sage. 22

## \|\| 1.6.23 \|\|

*ityetā oṣadhīnāṃ tu grāmyānāṃ jātayo mune \|*  
*oṣadhyo yajñiyāścaiva grāmyāraṇyāścaturdaśa \|\| 23 \|\|*

Fourteen plants, both cultivated and wild, are suitable for sacrifice: 23

## \|\| 1.6.24 \|\|

*vrīhayaḥsayavā māṣā godhūmāścāṇava stilāḥ \|*  
*priyaṅgusaptamā hyete aṣṭamāstu kulatthakāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Rice, barley, beans, wheat, millet, sesame, with foxtail millet as the seventh and horse gram as the eighth, barnyard millet, 24

## \|\| 1.6.25 \|\|

*śyāmākāstvatha nīvārā jartilāḥ sagavedhukāḥ \|*  
*tathā veṇuyavāḥ proktas tathā markaṭakā mune \|\| 25 \|\|*

Wild rice, wild sesame, mallow and bamboo seeds are mentioned, sage, along with corn. 25

## \|\| 1.6.26 \|\|

*grāmyāraṇyāḥ smṛtā hyetā oṣadhyastu caturdaśa \|*  
*yajñaniṣpattaye yajñastathāsāṃ heturuttamaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

These fourteen, wild and cultivated, are used in the performance of the sacrifice, and yet it is the sacrifice that enables them to grow. 26

## \|\| 1.6.27 \|\|

*etāś ca saha yajñena prajānāṃ kāraṇaṃ param \|*  
*parāvaravidaḥ prājñāstato yajñānvitanveta \|\| 27 \|\|*

These plants and sacrifice are humanity’s first foundation. That’s why the wise who understand both causes and effects perform these rites. 27

## \|\| 1.6.28 \|\|

*ahanyahanyanuṣṭhānaṃ yajñānāṃ munisattama \|*  
*upakārakaraṃ puṃsāṃ kriyamāṇā ghaśāntidam \|\| 28 \|\|*

Daily performance of the sacrifice benefits humanity, best of sages, and cleanses all impurities from those who do so. 28

## \|\| 1.6.29 \|\|

*yeṣāṃ tu kālasṛṣo 'sau pāpabandurmahāmune \|*  
*cetaḥsu vavṛdhe cakruste na yajñeṣu mānasam \|\| 29 \|\|*

The seed of evil sown by Time grew in some men’s hearts, wise sage, and their thoughts began to wander from the sacrifice. 29

## \|\| 1.6.30 \|\|

*vedavādāṃs tathā vedānyajña karmādikaṃ ca yat \|*  
*tatsarvaṃ nindayāmāsuryajñavyāsedhakāriṇaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

Making light of Vedic discourse, the Vedas and the rites, they opposed the sacrificial act. 30

## \|\| 1.6.31 \|\|

*pravṛttimārgavyucchittikāriṇo vedanindakāḥ \|*  
*durātmāno durācārā babhūvuḥ kuṭilāśayāḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

These wicked, devious miscreants mocked the Vedas and blocked the path of sacrificial action. 31

## \|\| 1.6.32 \|\|

*saṃsiddhāyāṃ tu vārtāyāṃ prajāḥ sṛṣṭvā prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*saryādāṃ sthāpayāmāsa yathāsthānaṃ yathāguṇam \|\| 32 \|\|*

After the lord of creation had established people in the livelihoods prepared for them, he laid down limits suited to the status and the qualities 32

## \|\| 1.6.33 \|\|

*varṇā nāmāśramāṇāṃ ca dharmadharmabhṛtāṃ vara \|*  
*lokāṃś ca sarvavarṇānāṃ samyagdharmānupālinām \|\| 33 \|\|*

Of each order and stage of life, best guardian of duty, and also their responsibilities. He also made the heavenly realms for each community who adhered perfectly to their obligations. 33

## \|\| 1.6.34 \|\|

*prajāpatyaṃ brahmaṇānāṃ smṛtaṃ sthānaṃ kriyāvatām \|*  
*sthānamaindraṃ kṣatriyāṇāṃ saṃgrameṣvanivartatām \|\| 34 \|\|*

The realm for brahmins who carry out the sacrifice is that of Brahmā. The realm for kṣatriyas who never retreat in battle is Indra’s. 34

## \|\| 1.6.35 \|\|

*vaiśyānāṃ mārutaṃ sthānaṃ svadharmamanuvartinām \|*  
*gāndharvaṃ śūdrajātīnāṃ.paricaryānuvartinām \|\| 35 \|\|*

The realm for vaiśyas who delight in duty is the Maruts’, while that for śūdras who rejoice in service is the gandharvas’. 35

## \|\| 1.6.36 \|\|

*aṣṭāśītisahasrāṇi munīnāmūrdhvaretasām \|*  
*smṛtaṃ teṣāṃ tu yatsthānaṃ tadeva guruvāsinām \|\| 36 \|\|*

The realm for those who lived in their guru’s home is that of the eighty-one thousand sages who never shed their semen. 36

## \|\| 1.6.37 \|\|

*saptarṣiṇāṃ tu yatsthānaṃ smṛtaṃ tadvai vanaukasām \|*  
*prājāpatyaṃ gṛhasthānāṃ nyāsināṃ brahmasaṃjñitam \|\| 37 \|\|*

The realm for those who retire to the forest is that of the Seven Sages. The progenitors’ realm is for householders, and Brahmā’s is for renunciants. 37

## \|\| 1.6.38 \|\|

*yogināmamṛtaṃ sthānaṃ svātmasaṃtoṣakāriṇām \|\| 38 \|\|*

The immortal realm for ascetics is the highest state of Viṣṇu. 38

## \|\| 1.6.39 \|\|

*ekāntinaḥ sadā brahmadhyāyino yoginaś ca ye \|*  
*teṣāṃ tu paramaṃ sthānaṃ yattatpaśyanti sūrayaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

Those ascetics who always contemplate the Absolute alone achieve this, the highest realm perceived by the wise. 39

## \|\| 1.6.40 \|\|

*gatvāgatvā nivartante candrasūryādayo grahāḥ \|*  
*adyāpi na nivartante dvādaśākṣaracintakāḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

After every dissolution, the moon, the sun and planets are all re-created, but those who contemplate the mantra of twelve syllables, even now, need never be reborn. 40

## \|\| 1.6.41 \|\|

*tāmistramandhatāmistraṃ mahārauravarauravau \|*  
*asipatravanaṃ ghoraṃ kālasūtramavīcikam \|\| 41 \|\|*

The hell realms of Tāmistra, Andhatāmisra, Mahāraurava, Raurava, Asipatravana, Ghora, Kālasūtra and Avīci 41

## \|\| 1.6.42 \|\|

*vinindakānāṃ vedasya yajñavyāghātakāriṇām \|*  
*sthānametatsamākhyātaṃ svadharmatyāginaś ca ye \|\| 42 \|\|*

Are said to wait for those who insult the Vedas, obstruct the sacrifice or shirk their communal duties. 42

## Chapter 7 - The first patriarchs

## \|\| 1.7.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tatobhidhyāyatastasya jajñire mānasāḥ prajāḥ \|*  
*taccharīrasamutpannaiḥ kāryais taiḥ karaṇaiḥ saha \|*  
*kṣetrajñāḥ samavartanta gātrebhyasya syaḥ dhīmataḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*
