While Brahmā was engaged in contemplation, his mind-born progeny arose. These conscious beings, whose forms and senses came from Brahmā’s body, appeared from the limbs of that wise deity. 1

## \|\| 1.7.2 \|\|

*te sarve samāvartanta ye mayā prāgudāhatāḥ \|*  
*devādyāḥ sthāvarāntāś ca traiguṇyaviṣaye sthitāḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

All the creatures I’ve described, from gods to static objects, occupy a world infused with threefold qualities. 2

## \|\| 1.7.3 \|\|

*evaṃbhūtāni sṛṣṭāni carāṇi sthāvarāṇi ca \|\| 3 \|\|*

All creatures, moving and unmoving, were created in this way. 3

## \|\| 1.7.4 \|\|

*yadāsya tāḥ prajāḥ sarvā na vyavardhanta dhīmataḥ \|*  
*athānyānmānasānputrānsadṛśānātmano 'sṛjat \|\| 4 \|\|*

But as these beings, arising from that wise deity, failed to multiply, he then brought forth more sons from his own mind in the image of himself: 4

## \|\| 1.7.5 \|\|

*bhṛguṃ pulastyaṃ pulahaṃ kratumaṅgirasaṃ tathā \|*  
*marīciṃ dabhamatriṃ ca vasiṣṭhaṃ caiva mānasān \|\| 5 \|\|*

Bhṛgu, Pulastya, Pulaha, Kratu, Aṅgiras, Marīci, Dakṣa, Atri and Vasiṣṭha. 5

## \|\| 1.7.6 \|\|

*nava brahmāṇa ityete purāṇe niścayaṃ gatāḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

These are the nine mind-born sons of Brahmā named in the purāṇas. 6

## \|\| 1.7.7 \|\|

*khyātiṃ bhūtiṃ ca saṃbhūtiṃ kṣamāṃ prītiṃ tathaiva ca \|*  
*sannatiṃ ca tathaivorjamanasūyāṃ tathaiva ca \|\| 7 \|\|*

Sanandana and the others whom Brahmā brought forth earlier had neither worldly ties nor an urge for procreation. 7

## \|\| 1.7.8 \|\|

*prasūtiṃ ca tataḥ sṛṣṭvā dadau teṣāṃ mahātmanām \|*  
*patnyo bhavadhva mityuktvā teṣāmeva tu dattavān \|\| 8 \|\|*

All had perfect knowledge and were therefore free from envy and desire, but as they showed indifference to the creation of the world, a terrible fury, able to burn the universe, arose in that great deity. 8

## \|\| 1.7.9 \|\|

*sanandanādayo ye ca pūrvasṛṣṭāstu vedhasā \|*  
*na te lokeṣvasajjanta nirapekṣāḥ prajāsu te \|\| 9 \|\|*

All three worlds were set ablaze in a ring of fire sparked by Brahmā’s anger, sage. 9

## \|\| 1.7.10 \|\|

*sarve te 'bhyā gatajñānā vītarāgā vimatsarāḥ \|*  
*teṣvevaṃ nirapekṣeṣu lokasṛṣṭau mahātmanāḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

From his forehead, furrowed with burning fury, sprang wrathful Rudra, blazing like the midday sun, his vast form half-man, half-woman. 10

## \|\| 1.7.11 \|\|

*brahmaṇobhūnmahān krodhastrailokyadahanakṣamaḥ \|*  
*tasya krodhātsa mudbhūtajvālāmālātidīpitam \|*  
*brahmaṇobhūttadā sarvaṃ trailokyamakhilaṃ mune \|\| 11 \|\|*

Brahmā ordered Rudra to divide himself, then withdrew from sight. 11

## \|\| 1.7.12 \|\|

*bhrakuṭīkuṭilāttasya lalāṭāt krodhadīpitāt \|*  
*samutpanna stadā rudro madhyāhnārkasamaprabhaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Thus, addressed by Brahmā, Rudra split his male and female halves apart, then divided his male half eleven ways. 12

## \|\| 1.7.13 \|\|

*ardhanārīnaravapuḥ pracaṇḍo 'tiśarīravān \|*  
*vibhajātmānamityuktvā taṃ brahmāntardadhe tataḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Some forms were pleasant, some hideous, some mild and others fierce. That lordly being then split his female half in many forms, both black and white. 13

## \|\| 1.7.14 \|\|

*tathoktosau dvidhā strītvaṃ puruṣatvaṃ tathākarot \|*  
*vibhedapuruṣatvaṃ ca daśadhā caikadhā punaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Brahmā then manifested himself as Manu, Lord Svāyaṃbhuva, brahmin, the patriarch of humanity. 14

## \|\| 1.7.15 \|\|

*saumyāsaumyostadāśāntāśāntaiḥ strītvaṃ ca sa prabhuḥ \|*  
*vibheda bahudhā devāḥ svarūpair asitaiḥ sitaiḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

That divine and lordly Manu took to wife the woman Śatarupā, who was purified of sins through penance. 15

## \|\| 1.7.16 \|\|

*tato brahmātmasaṃbhūtaṃ pūrvaṃ svāyaṃbhuvaṃ prabhu \|*  
*ātmānameva kṛta vānprajāpālye manuṃ dvija \|\| 16 \|\|*

With him, divine Śatarūpa bore two sons, Priyavrata and Uttānapāda, and two daughters, named Prasūti and Ākūti, endowed with beauty, nobility and virtue—you know this, dharma. 16

## \|\| 1.7.17 \|\|

*śatarūpāṃ ca tāṃ nārīṃ taponirdhūtakalpaṣām \|*  
*svāyaṃbhuvo manurdevaḥ patnītve jagṛhe prabhuḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Svāyaṃbhuva gave Prasūti to Dakṣa and Ākūti to Ruci in those far-off times. The patriarch Ruci accepted Ākūti, and they had a son called Sacrifice and a daughter, Recompense, who became a couple, blessed sage. 17

## \|\| 1.7.18 \|\|

*tasmāttu puruṣāddevī śatarūpā vyajāyata \|*  
*priyavratottānapādau prasūtyākūtisaṃjñitam \|\| 18 \|\|*

Sacrifice and Recompense had twelve sons, known as the divine Yāmas in the age of Manu Svāyaṃbhuva. 18

## \|\| 1.7.19 \|\|

*kanyādvayaṃ ca dharmajña rūpaudāryaguṇānvitam \|*  
*dadau prasūtiṃ dakṣāya ākūtiṃ rucaye purā \|\| 19 \|\|*

Similarly, Dakṣa had twenty-four daughters with Prasūti. Listen carefully to their names: 19

## \|\| 1.7.20 \|\|

*prajāpatiḥ sa jagrāha tayorjajñe sadakṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*putro yajñe mahābhāga dampatyormithunaṃ tataḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

Faith, Fortune, Constance, Contentment, Comfort, Prudence, Ritual, Intellect, Modesty, Beauty, Peace and Felicity, with Fame as the thirteenth. 20

## \|\| 1.7.21 \|\|

*yajñasya dakṣiṇāyāṃ tu putrā dvādaśa jajñire \|*  
*yāmā iti samākhyātā devāḥ svāyaṃbhuve manau \|\| 21 \|\|*

The lord Virtue took these daughters of Dakṣa as his wives. The eleven remaining younger fair-eyed daughters were: 21

## \|\| 1.7.22 \|\|

*prasūtyāṃ ca tathā dakṣaścatastro viṃśatis tathā \|*  
*sasarja kanyāstāsāṃ ca samyaṅnāmāni me śṛṇu \|\| 22 \|\|*

Renown, Truth, Increase, Memory, Joy, Patience, Humility, Kindness, Energy, Svāhā and Svadhā. 22

## \|\| 1.7.23 \|\|

*śraddhā lakṣmīrdhṛtistuṣṭirmedhā puṣṭis tathā kriyā \|*  
*buddhirlajjā vapuḥ śāntiḥ siddhiḥ kīrtistrayodaśī \|\| 23 \|\|*

Bhṛgu, Bhava, Marīci, the sage Aṅgiras, Pulastya, Pulaha, the excellent seer Kratu, 23

## \|\| 1.7.24 \|\|

*patnyarthaṃ pratijagrāha dharmo dākṣāyaṇīḥ prabhuḥ \|*  
*tābhyaḥ śiṣṭayavīyasya ekādaśa sulocanāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Atri, Vasiṣṭha, Vahni and Pitara—these seers duly wedded Renown and her sisters, best of sages. 24

## \|\| 1.7.25 \|\|

*khyātiḥ satyatha saṃbhūtiḥ smṛtiḥ pītiḥ kṣamā tathā \|*  
*santatiścānasūyā ca ūrjā svāhā svadhā tathā \|\| 25 \|\|*

The sons of Virtue were as follows: Faith bore Desire, Fortune Pride, Constance a son, Restraint. Contentment bore Satisfaction and Comfort Greed, 25

## \|\| 1.7.26 \|\|

*bhṛturbhavo marīciś ca tathā caivāṅgirā muniḥ \|*  
*pulastyaḥ pulahaścaiva kratuścarṣivaras tathā \|\| 26 \|\|*

Prudence bore Knowledge and Ritual bore Correction, Conduct and Decency. Intellect bore Understanding, Modesty Propriety, the son of Beauty was Resolve, Peace bore Security, 26

## \|\| 1.7.27 \|\|

*atrirvasiṣṭho vahniś ca pitaraś ca yathākramama \|*  
*khyātyādya jagṛhuḥ kanyā munayo muni sattama \|\| 27 \|\|*

Felicity bore Happiness and Fame Glory. Such were the sons of Virtue. With Desire, Love gave birth to Rapture, the grandson of Virtue. 27

## \|\| 1.7.28 \|\|

*śraddhā kāmaṃ calā darpaṃ niyamaṃ dhṛtirātmajam \|*  
*santoṣaṃ ca tathā tuṣṭirlobhaṃ puṣṭirasūyata \|\| 28 \|\|*

Vice’s wife was Violence. They gave birth to a son, Falsehood, and a daughter, Deceit, who became a couple and bore two sons, Fear and the hell realm Naraka. 28

## \|\| 1.7.29 \|\|

*medhā śrutaṃ kriyā daṇḍaṃ nayaṃ vinayameva ca \|\| 29 \|\|*

Their two daughters, Delusion and Pain, became their brothers’ wives. Fear and Delusion gave birth to Death, who carries off all beings. 29

## \|\| 1.7.30 \|\|

*bodhaṃ buddhis tathā lajjā vinayaṃ vapurātmajam \|*  
*vyavasāyaṃ prajajñe vai kṣemaṃ śāntirasūyatā \|\| 30 \|\|*

Pain and Fear had a son, Suffering. The children of Death were Disease, Decrepitude, Sorrow, Avidity and Anger. 30

## \|\| 1.7.31 \|\|

*sukhaṃ siddhiryaśaḥ kīrtirityete dharmasūnavaḥ \|*  
*kāmādratiḥ sutaṃ harṣaṃ dharmapautramasūyata \|\| 31 \|\|*

These all knew pain and were marked by vice. None had a wife, a son or retained his semen. 31

## \|\| 1.7.32 \|\|

*hiṃsā bhāryā tvadharmasya tato jajñe tathānṛtam \|*  
*kanyā ca nikṛtistābhyāṃ māyā jajñe ca vedanā \|\| 32 \|\|*

Such are the terrible forms of Viṣṇu, best sage’s son. They went forth as the eternal causes of destruction in this world. 32

## \|\| 1.7.33 \|\|

*māyā ca vedanā caiva mithunaṃ tvidametayoḥ \|*  
*tayorjajñetha vai māyā mṛtyuṃ bhūtāpahāriṇam \|\| 33 \|\|*

Dakṣa, Marīci, Atri, Bhṛgu and the other patriarchs, blessed sage, are the eternal causes of creation here. 33

## \|\| 1.7.34 \|\|

*vedanā svasūtaṃ cāpi duḥkhaṃ jajñe 'tha rauravāt \|*  
*mṛtyorvyādhijarāśokatṛṣṇakrodhāś ca jajñire \|\| 34 \|\|*

The Manus and their sons, brave protectors of the earth, are heroes who rejoice in the path of truth and are the ever-present causes of the preservation of the world. 34

## \|\| 1.7.35 \|\|

*duḥkhottarāḥ smṛtā hyete sarve cādharmalakṣaṇāḥ \|*  
*naiṣāṃ putrosti vai bhāryā te sarve hyūrdhvaretasaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

Tell me, brahmin, what is the nature of this perpetual process of creation, preservation and destruction? 35

## \|\| 1.7.36 \|\|

*raudrāṇyetānirūpāṇi viṣṇormunivarātmaja \|*  
*nityapralayahetutvaṃ jagatosya prayānti vai \|\| 36 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Lord Madhusūdana, who is beyond conception, in all three varied forms, brings about the never-ending cycle of creation, preservation and destruction. 36

## \|\| 1.7.37 \|\|

*dakṣo marīciratriś ca bhṛgvādyāś ca prajeśvarāḥ \|*  
*jagatyatra mahābhāga nityasargasya hetavaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

The destruction of all beings is fourfold, brahmin: occasional, foundational, continual and perpetual. 37

## \|\| 1.7.38 \|\|

*manavo manuputrāś ca bhūpā vīryadharāś ca ye \|*  
*sanmārganiratāḥ śūrāste sarve sthitikāriṇaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

Occasional or Brāhmic destruction takes place when the universal lord is sleeping. In foundational destruction, the world is broken into fundamental elements. 38

## \|\| 1.7.39 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*yeyaṃ nityā sthitirbrahmannityasargastatheritaḥ \|*  
*nityābhāvaś ca teṣāṃ vai svarūpaṃ mama kathyatām \|\| 39 \|\|*

Continual destruction is said to be that of the ascetic who, through spiritual knowledge, abides in the Supreme. Perpetual destruction is the unrelenting death, day and night, of every being ever born. 39

## \|\| 1.7.40 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*sargasthitivināśāṃś ca bhagavānmadhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*tais tai rūpair acintyātmā karotyavyāhato vibhuḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

The production of fundamental elements is thought to be foundational creation. The creation that follows the intermediate destruction of the world is called ephemeral. 40

## \|\| 1.7.41 \|\|

*naimittikaḥ prākṛtikastathaivātyantiko dvija \|*  
*nityaś ca sarvabhūtānāṃ pralayo 'yaṃ caturvidhaḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

The creation in which beings are born each day is called perpetual creation by those who understand the meaning of purāṇas, best of sages. 41

## \|\| 1.7.42 \|\|

*brahmo naimittikastatra ccheteyaṃ jagatīpatiḥ \|*  
*prayāti prākṛtaṃ caiva brahmaṇḍaṃ prakṛtau layam \|\| 42 \|\|*

That’s how Lord Viṣṇu, creator of beings abiding in all bodies, undertakes creation, preservation and destruction. 42

## \|\| 1.7.43 \|\|

*jñānādātyantikaḥ prokto yoginaḥ paramātmani \|*  
*nityaḥ sadaiva bhūtānāṃ yo vināśo divāniśam \|\| 43 \|\|*

The powers of Viṣṇu to create, preserve and to destroy, Maitreya, are always present in all bodies, day and night. 43

## \|\| 1.7.44 \|\|

*prasūtiḥ prakṛteryā tu sā sṛṣṭiḥ prākṛtā smṛtā \|*  
*dainandinī tathā proktā nāntarapralayādanu \|\| 44 \|\|*

One who transcends this great threefold power consisting of all qualities, brahmin, proceeds to the highest state, never to return. 44

## Chapter 8 - The eight forms of Rudra

## \|\| 1.8.1 \|\|

*śrīpāraśara uvāca*  
*kathitastāmasaḥ sargo brahmaṇaste mahāmune \|*  
*rudrasargaṃ pravakṣyāmi tanme nigadataḥ śṛṇu \|\| 1 \|\|*

Parāśara:

I’ve described for you the creation undertaken by Brahmā that has the quality of dullness, great sage. Now I’ll speak about the creation of Rudra. Listen while I explain. 1

## \|\| 1.8.2 \|\|

*kalpādāvātmanastulyaṃ sutaṃ pradhyāyatastataḥ \|*  
*prādurāsītprabhoraṅke kumāro nīlalohitaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

While Brahmā was meditating at the beginning of the aeon, a son, his equal, manifested in his lap, a boy of ruddy complexion. 2

## \|\| 1.8.3 \|\|

*ruroda susvaraṃ sotha prādravaddi jasattama \|*  
*kiṃ tvaṃ rodiṣi taṃ brahma rudantaṃ pratyuvāca ha \|\| 3 \|\|*

He ran about while wailing loudly (rudan), best of brahmins. When Brahmā asked the weeping child why he wept, ‘I want a name!’ was his reply. 3

## \|\| 1.8.4 \|\|

*nāma dehīti taṃ so 'tha pratyuvāca prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*rudrastvaṃ devanāmnāsi māro dīrdhairyamāvaha \|*  
*evamuktaḥ punaḥ so 'tha saptakṛtvo ruroda vai \|\| 4 \|\|*

The lord of creation answered, ‘Deity, your name is Rudra, but do not cry. Be firm!’ But even after Brahmā had spoken, the child cried out another seven times. 4

## \|\| 1.8.5 \|\|

*tatonyāni dadau tasmai sapta nāmāni vai prabhuḥ \|*  
*sthānāni caiṣāmaṣṭānāṃ patnīḥ putrāṃś ca sa prabhuḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Each time the lord gave him another name. To each of these eight aspects the lord assigned stations, wives and sons. 5

## \|\| 1.8.6 \|\|

*bhavaṃ śarvamatheśānaṃ tathā paśupatiṃ dvija \|*  
*bhīmamugraṃ mahādevamuvāca sa pitāmahaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

The universal grandsire gave him all these names, brahmin: Bhava, Śarva, Īśāna, Paśupati, Bhīma, Ugra and Mahādeva. 6

## \|\| 1.8.7 \|\|

*cakre nāmānyathaitāni sthānānyeṣāṃ cakāra saḥ \|*  
*sūryo jalaṃ mahī vāyurvahnirākāśameva ca \|*  
*dīkṣito brahmaṇaḥ soma ityetāstanavaḥ kramāt \|\| 7 \|\|*

He gave these names, then fixed a place for each: the sun, water, earth, wind, fire, air, the consecrated brahmin and the moon—these are the eight stations in order. 7

## \|\| 1.8.8 \|\|

*suvarcalā tathaivoṣā vikeśī cāparā śivā \|*  
*svāhā diśas tathā dīkṣā rohiṇī ca yathākramam \|\| 8 \|\|*

Suvarcalā, Uṣā, Vikeśī, Śivā, Svāhā and Diśas, Dīkṣā and Rohiṇī— 8

## \|\| 1.8.9 \|\|

*sūryādīnāṃ dvijaśreṣṭha rudrādyair nāmabhiḥ saha \|*  
*patnyaḥ smṛtā mahābhāga tadapatyāni me śṛṇu \|\| 9 \|\|*

These are the respective wives of the sun and other forms, which are known by the names of Rudra and the others, best of men. Next, you’ll hear from me about their offspring, blessed sage. 9

## \|\| 1.8.10 \|\|

*eṣāṃ sūtiprasūtibhyāmidamāpūritaṃ jagat \|\| 10 \|\|*

Their descendants filled this world: 10

## \|\| 1.8.11 \|\|

*śanaiścaras tathā śukro lohitāṅgo manojavaḥ \|*  
*skandaḥ sargo 'tha santāno budhaścānukramātsutāḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

The sons in order were tardy Saturn, brilliant Venus, ruddy Mars, Manojava, Skanda, Svarga, Saṃtāna and wise Mercury. 11

## \|\| 1.8.12 \|\|

*evaṃprakāro rudro 'sau satīṃ bhāryāmaninditām \|*  
*upayeme duhitaraṃ rakṣasyaiva prajāpateḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

In the form of Rudra, he took Satī as his wife. She later sacrificed her body when enraged by Dakṣa. 12

## \|\| 1.8.13 \|\|

*dakṣakopācca tatyāja sa satī svakalevaram \|*  
*himavadduhita sābhūnmenāyāṃ dvijasattama \|\| 13 \|\|*

Satī was reborn the daughter of the mountain-deity Himālaya and Menā, best of brahmins, and, as peerless Umā, Lord Śiva married her again. 13

## \|\| 1.8.14 \|\|

*upayeme punaścomāmananyāṃ bhagavānharaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

The divine sons Dhātṛ and Vidhātṛ were born to the sage Bhṛgu and Renown, as was Śrī, the consort of Nārāyaṇa, god of gods. 14

## \|\| 1.8.15 \|\|

*devau dhātṛvidhātārau bhṛgoḥ khyātirasūyata \|*  
*śriyaṃ ca devadevasya patnī nārāyaṇasya yā \|\| 15 \|\|*

It’s said that glorious Śrī arose from the waters at the churning of the nectar of immortality. How can you now say that she was born to Bhṛgu and Renown? 15

## \|\| 1.8.16 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*kṣīrābdhau śrīḥ samutpannā śrūyate 'mṛtamanthane \|*  
*bhṛgoḥ khyātyāṃ samutpannetyetadāha kathaṃ bhavāt \|\| 16 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Śrī, the mother of the universe, is eternal and never leaves the side of Viṣṇu, best of brahmins. Just as Viṣṇu is everywhere, so indeed is she. 16

## \|\| 1.8.17 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*nityaivaiṣā jaganmātā viṣṇoḥ śrīranapāyini \|*  
*yathā sarvagato viṣṇustathaiveyaṃ dvijottama \|\| 17 \|\|*

Viṣṇu is meaning and she is word. She is policy and Hari is principle. Viṣṇu is perception and she is intellect. He is virtue and she is conduct. 17

## \|\| 1.8.18 \|\|

*artho viṣṇuriyaṃ vāṇī nītireṣā nayo hariḥ \|*  
*bodho viṣṇuriyaṃ buddhirdharmo 'sau satkriyā tviyam \|\| 18 \|\|*

Viṣṇu is creator and she is creation. Śrī is earth and Hari is support. The lord is satisfaction and Lakṣmī is eternal contentment, Maitreya. 18

## \|\| 1.8.19 \|\|

*straṣṭā viṣṇuriyaṃ sṛṣṭiḥ śrīrbhūmirbhūdharo hariḥ \|*  
*santoṣo bhagavāllaṅkṣmīstuṣṭirmaitreya śāśvatī \|\| 19 \|\|*

Śrī is inclination and the lord is desire. He is sacrifice and she, the fee. The goddess is the oblation of clarified butter and Janārdana, the prayer that accompanies it. 19

## \|\| 1.8.20 \|\|

*icchā śrīrbhagavānkāmo yajño 'sau dakṣiṇā tviyam \|*  
*ājyāhutirasau devī puroḍāśo janārdanaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

Lakṣmī is the pavilion for the sacrificer’s wives, sage, and Madhusūdana, the pavilion for the men of the family. Lakṣmī is the altar and Hari is the sacrificial post. Śrī is the kindling and the lord is the sacred grass. 20

## \|\| 1.8.21 \|\|

*patnīśālā mune lakṣmīḥ prāgvaṃśau madhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*citirlakṣmīrhariryūpa idhmā śrīrbhagavānkuśaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

The lord takes the form of the Sāma Veda and the lotus-throned goddess is the chanting. Lakṣmī is the invocation svāhā, and Vāsudeva, master of the universe, is the fire that consumes the offering. 21

## \|\| 1.8.22 \|\|

*sāmāsvarūpī bhagavānudgītiḥ kamalālayā \|*  
*svāhā lakṣmīrjagannātho vāsudevo hutā śanaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Lord Viṣṇu is Śiva and Lakṣmī is Gaurī, his wife, best of brahmins. Keśava is the sun and lotus-throned Śrī its brilliance, Maitreya. 22

## \|\| 1.8.23 \|\|

*śaṅkaro bhagavāñchaurirgaurīlakṣmīrdvijottama \|*  
*maitreya keśavaḥ sūryastatprabhā kamalālayā \|\| 23 \|\|*

Viṣṇu is the host of ancestral spirits and Padmā is the invocation svadhā, which provides their eternal sustenance. Śrī is heaven and Viṣṇu, the universal lord, is boundless space. 23

## \|\| 1.8.24 \|\|

*viṣṇuḥ pitṛgaṇaḥ padmā svadhā śāśvatapuṣṭidā \|*  
*dyauḥ śrīḥ sarvātmako viṣṇuravakāśotivistaraḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Śrī’s husband is the hare-marked moon and Śrī its ever-present loveliness. Lakṣmī is the firmness that girds the world and Hari is the all-pervading wind. 24

## \|\| 1.8.25 \|\|

*śaśāṅkaḥ śrīdharaḥ kāntiḥ śrīstathaivānapāyinī \|*  
*dhṛtirlakṣmīrjagacceṣṭā vāyuḥ sarvatrago hariḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Govinda is the ocean and Śrī its shore, wise brahmin. Indrāṇī, wife of Indra, is a form of Lakṣmī, and the king of gods is Madhusūdana. 25

## \|\| 1.8.26 \|\|

*jaladhirdvijagovindastadvelā śrīrmahāmune \|*  
*lakṣmīsvarūpamindrāṇī devendro madhusūdanaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

Viṣṇu who bears the discus is Yama himself, the lord of the underworld, and the lotus-throned Śrī is Yama’s wife, Dhūmorṇā. Śrī is wealth and her husband is the deity Kubera, lord of riches. 26

## \|\| 1.8.27 \|\|

*yamaścakradharaḥ sākṣāddhūmorṇā kamalālayā \|*  
*ṛddhiḥ śrīḥ śrīdharo devaḥ svayameva dhaneśvaraḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

Fortunate Lakṣmī is Gaurī and Keśava is Varuṇa, god of oceans. Śrī is the army of the gods and Hari its commander, best of brahmins. 27

## \|\| 1.8.28 \|\|

*gaurī lakṣmīmaṃhābāgā keśavo varuṇaḥ svayam \|*  
*śrīrdevasenā viprendra devasenāpatirhariḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Viṣṇu who wields the mace is support and Lakṣmī is power, best of brahmins. Lakṣmī is the unit of time known as kāṣṭhā and he is the nimeṣa. He is the muhūrta and she the kalā. 28

## \|\| 1.8.29 \|\|

*avaṣṭaṃbho gadāpāṇiḥ śaktirlakṣmīr dvijottama \|*  
*kāṣṭhā lakṣmīrnimeṣo 'sau muhūrto 'sau kalā tviyam \|\| 29 \|\|*

Lakṣmī is light and Hari, lord of all, the shining sun. Śrī, the mother of the world, takes the form of a vine and Viṣṇu, the tree it clings to. 29

## \|\| 1.8.30 \|\|

*jyotsnā lakṣmīḥ pradīpo 'sau sarvaḥ sarveśvaro hariḥ \|*  
*latābhūtā jaganmātā śrīrviṣṇurdrumasaṃjñitaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

Śrī is night and the god who bears the mace and discus is the day. Wish-granting Viṣṇu is the groom and she who dwells in the lotus grove his bride. 30

## \|\| 1.8.31 \|\|

*vibhāvarī śrīrdivaso devacakragadādharaḥ \|*  
*varaprado varo viṣṇurvadhūḥ padmavanāla yā \|\| 31 \|\|*

The lord takes the form of all masculine rivers and Śrī the feminine ones. Lotus-eyed Viṣṇu is the banner and lotus-throned Śrī the pennant. 31

## \|\| 1.8.32 \|\|

*nadasvarūpī bhagavāñchrīrnadīrūpasaṃsthitā \|*  
*dhvajaś ca puṇḍarīkākṣaḥ patākā kamalālayā \|\| 32 \|\|*

Lakṣmī is yearning and supreme Nārāyaṇa, master of the world, is greed. Lakṣmī is love and Govinda is sex—you understand this, dharma. 32

## \|\| 1.8.33 \|\|

*tṛṣṇā lakṣmīrjagannātho lobho nārāyaṇaḥ paraḥ \|*  
*ratī rāgaś ca maitreya lakṣmīrgovinda eva ca \|\| 33 \|\|*

Why say more when it can be said so simply? 33

## \|\| 1.8.34 \|\|

*kiṃ cātibahunoktena saṃkṣepeṇedamucyate \|\| 34 \|\|*

All things masculine among the animals, gods and humankind are Lord Hari, while everything feminine is Lakṣmī, Maitreya. There is nothing apart from them. 34

## Chapter 9 - The sage Durvāsas curses Indra; Churning of the ocean

## \|\| 1.9.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*idaṃ ca śṛṇu maitreya yatpṛṣṭohamiha tvayā \|*  
*śrīsambandhaṃ māyāpyetacchrutamāsīnmarīcitaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Now listen to this account of Śrī, about whom you asked me, Maitreya, as I heard it from the seer Marīci. 1

## \|\| 1.9.2 \|\|

*durvāsāḥ śaṅkarasyāṃśaścāra pṛthivimimāma \|*  
*sa dadarśa srajaṃ divyāmṛṣirvidyādharīkare \|\| 2 \|\|*

The sage Durvāsas, a partial manifestation of Śiva, was wandering this world when he saw a female vidyādhara holding a divine garland. 2

## \|\| 1.9.3 \|\|

*saṃtānakānāmakhilaṃ yasyā gandhena vāsitam \|*  
*atisevyamabhūdbrahyan tadvanaṃ vanacāriṇām \|\| 3 \|\|*

The perfume of the garland’s santānaka flowers filled the forest, brahmin, and the scent brought great delight to those who roamed there. 3

## \|\| 1.9.4 \|\|

*unmattavratadhṛgviprastāṃ dṛṣṭvā śobhanāṃ srajam \|*  
*tāṃ yayāce varārohāṃ vidyādharavadhūṃ tataḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

That brahmin, who held vows of holy craziness, saw the splendid garland and asked the fair-hipped vidyādhara woman to give it to him. 4

## \|\| 1.9.5 \|\|

*yācitā tena tanvaṅgī mālāṃ vidyādharāṅganā \|*  
*dadau tasmai viśālākṣī sādaraṃ praṇipatya tam \|\| 5 \|\|*

At his request, the slender, wide-eyed vidyādharī bowed graciously and handed him the flowers. 5

## \|\| 1.9.6 \|\|

*tāmādāyātmano mūrdhni srajamunmattarūpadhṛk \|*  
*kṛtvā sa vipro maitreya paribabhrāma medinīm \|\| 6 \|\|*

That wild brahmin took the garland and placed it on his head, Maitreya, as he wandered through the world. 6

## \|\| 1.9.7 \|\|

*sa dadarśa tamāyāntamunmattairāvate sthitam \|*  
*trailokyādhipatiṃ devaṃ saha devaiḥ śacīpatim \|\| 7 \|\|*

One day, Durvāsas beheld the deity Indra, overlord of the threefold worlds, Śacī’s husband, approaching on his raging elephant Airāvata, accompanied by the other gods. 7

## \|\| 1.9.8 \|\|

*tāmātmanaḥ sa śirasaḥ srajamunmatta ṣaṭpadām \|*  
*ādāyāmararājāya cikṣeponmattavanmuniḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

The sage took the garland, along with its frenzied bees, from his head and, like a madman, tossed it to the king of gods. 8

## \|\| 1.9.9 \|\|

*gṛhītvāmararājena sragairāvatamūrdhani \|*  
*nyastā rarāja kailāsaśikhare jāhnavī yathā \|\| 9 \|\|*

Indra caught the garland and placed it on the elephant’s brow, where it shone like the river Gaṅgā, Jahnu’s daughter, on the summit of Mount Kailāsa. 9

## \|\| 1.9.10 \|\|

*madāndhakāritākṣosau gandhākṛṣṭena vāraṇaḥ \|*  
*kareṇāghrāya cikṣepa tāṃ srajaṃ dharaṇītale \|\| 10 \|\|*

The elephant, whose eyes were blinded by intoxication, was attracted by the scent and sniffed the garland with its trunk but tossed it to the ground. 10

## \|\| 1.9.11 \|\|

*tataścukrodha bhagavāndurvāsā muni sattamaḥ \|*  
*maitreya devarājānaṃ kruddhaścaitaduvāca ha \|\| 11 \|\|*

The best of sages, holy Durvāsas, was enraged, Maitreya, and angrily addressed the king of gods: 11

## \|\| 1.9.12 \|\|

*durvāsā uvāca*  
*aiśvaryamadaduṣṭātmannatistabdhosi vāsava \|*  
*śriyo dhāma srajaṃ yastvaṃ maddattāṃ nābhinandasi \|\| 12 \|\|*

Durvāsas:

Your heart has been corrupted by the intoxication of your power. You’re worse than arrogant, Indra. The garland that I gave you is the dwelling place of Śrī, but you didn’t really like it. 12

## \|\| 1.9.13 \|\|

*prasāda iti noktaṃ te praṇipātapuraḥ saram \|*  
*harṣotphullakapolena na cāpi śirasā dhṛtā \|\| 13 \|\|*

You didn’t thank me with a bow. You didn’t place it on your head, your cheeks aglow with pleasure. 13

## \|\| 1.9.14 \|\|

*mayā dattāmi māṃ mālaṃ yasmānna bahu manyase \|*  
*trailokyaśrīrato mūḍha vināśamupayāsyati \|\| 14 \|\|*

Because you shunned the garland that I offered you, fool, you’ll lose your sovereignty over all three worlds. 14

## \|\| 1.9.15 \|\|

*māṃ manyase tvaṃ madṛśaṃ nūnaṃ śakrataradvijaiḥ \|*  
*atovamā namasmāsu māninā bhavatā kṛtam \|\| 15 \|\|*

You clearly think that I’m like other brahmins, Indra, and in doing so, you arrogantly insult me. 15

## \|\| 1.9.16 \|\|

*maddatta bhavatā yasmātkṣiptā mālā mahītale \|*  
*tasmātpraṇaṣṭalakṣmīkaṃ trailokyaṃ te bhaviṣyati \|\| 16 \|\|*

Because you tossed the garland that I gave you to the ground, the threefold worlds will lose their splendour. 16

## \|\| 1.9.17 \|\|

*yasya saṃjātakopasya bhayameti carācaram \|*  
*taṃ tvaṃ māmatigarveṇa devarājavamanyase \|\| 17 \|\|*

This whole world of moving and unmoving things fears me when I’m angry, but you, king of gods, full of pride, disrespect me. 17

## \|\| 1.9.18 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*mahendro vāraṇaskandhādavatīrya tvarānvitaḥ \|*  
*prasādayāmāsa muniṃ durvāsasamakalmaṣam \|\| 18 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Mighty Indra quickly climbed down from the elephant’s back and apologised to the unstained seer Durvāsas. 18

## \|\| 1.9.19 \|\|

*prasādyamānaḥ sa tadā praṇipātapuraḥsaram \|*  
*ityuvāca sahasrākṣaṃ durvāsā munisattama \|\| 19 \|\|*

Being appeased, that best of sages then replied to the god of a thousand eyes, who bowed respectfully before him: 19

## \|\| 1.9.20 \|\|

*durvāsā uvāca*  
*nāhaṃ kṛpāluhṛdayo na camāṃ bhajate kṣamā \|*  
*anye te munayaḥ śakra durvāsasamavehi māma \|\| 20 \|\|*

Durvāsas:

Unlike other sages, I’m neither kind nor patient, mighty Indra. Remember that I’m Durvāsas! 20

## \|\| 1.9.21 \|\|

*gautamādibhir anyais tvaṃ garvamāropito mudhā \|*  
*akṣāntisārasarvasvaṃ durvāsasamavehi mām \|\| 21 \|\|*

You’ve been flattered by sages such as Gautama for no good reason. Remember that I’m Durvāsas, the very essence of impatience! 21

## \|\| 1.9.22 \|\|

*vasiṣṭhādyair dayāsāraiḥ stotraṃ kurvadbhir uccakaiḥ \|*  
*garvaṃ gato si yenaivaṃ māmapyadyāvamanyase \|\| 22 \|\|*

Because Vasiṣṭha and other sweet-natured seers loudly sang your praises, you’ve grown proud and disrespect me. 22

## \|\| 1.9.23 \|\|

*jvalajjaṭākalāpasya bhṛkuṭīkuṭilaṃ mukham \|*  
*nirīkṣya kastribhuvane mama yo na gato bhayam \|\| 23 \|\|*

My matted locks are a blazing mass and my brow is deeply furrowed. Seeing me, who in all three worlds isn’t fearful? 23

## \|\| 1.9.24 \|\|

*nāhaṃ kṣamiṣye bahunā kimuktena śatakrato \|*  
*viḍaṃbanāmimāṃ bhūyaḥ karoṣyanunayātmikām \|\| 24 \|\|*

Why waste words, god of a hundred sacrifices? I won’t accept any further semblance of respect you try to show me. 24

## \|\| 1.9.25 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvā prayayai vipro devarājopi taṃ punaḥ \|*  
*āruhyairāvataṃ brahman prayayāvamarāvatīm \|\| 25 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, the brahmin left, and Indra, king of gods, mounted on Airāvata, returned to his city of Amarāvatī. 25

## \|\| 1.9.26 \|\|

*tataḥ prabhṛti niḥśrīkaṃ saśakraṃ bhuvanatrayam \|*  
*maitreyāsīdapadhvastaṃ saṃkṣā ṇauṣadhivīrudham \|\| 26 \|\|*

From that moment, brahmin, all three worlds, along with mighty Indra, lost their splendour, Maitreya, and grew wretched. Herbs and other plant life withered, 26

## \|\| 1.9.27 \|\|

*na yajñāḥ samavartante na tapasyanti tāpasāḥ \|*  
*na ca dānādidharmeṇu manaścakre tadā janaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

Sacrifices ceased, ascetics gave up penance and folk no longer turned their minds to charity or other virtues. 27

## \|\| 1.9.28 \|\|

*niḥsattvāḥ sakalā lokā lobhādyupahatendriyāḥ \|*  
*svalpepi hi babhūvuste sābhilāṣā dvijottama \|\| 28 \|\|*

All the people suffered, their senses overcome by greed, best of brahmins, and they began to covet trifles. 28

## \|\| 1.9.29 \|\|

*yataḥ sattvaṃ tato lakṣmīḥ sattvaṃ bhūtyanusāri ca \|*  
*niḥśrīkāṇāṃ kutaḥ sattvaṃ vinā tena guṇāḥ kutaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

Where vitality exists, one also finds prosperity, as they follow one another. Where is vitality for those who lose prosperity? Without vitality, where are virtues? 29

## \|\| 1.9.30 \|\|

*balaśauryādyabhāvaś ca puruṣāṇāṃ guṇair vinā \|*  
*lavanāyaḥ samastasya balaśauryavivarjitaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

Without virtues, where are people’s strength, courage and other noble sentiments? Bereft of strength and courage, a person will be shunned by everyone, and a man rejected will lose his mind. 30

## \|\| 1.9.31 \|\|

*bhavatyapadhvastamatirlaghitaḥ prathitaḥ pumān \|*  
*evamatyantaniḥśrīke trailokye sattvavarjite \|\| 31 \|\|*

When all three worlds thus lost prosperity and vitality, the Daityas and the Dānavas made war against the deities. 31

## \|\| 1.9.32 \|\|

*davān prati balodyogaṃ cakrurdaiteyadānavāḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

Overcome by greed, and without wellbeing and energy themselves, the Daityas fought against the gods who also lacked those qualities. 32

## \|\| 1.9.33 \|\|

*lobhābhibhūtā niḥśrīkā daityāḥ sattvavivarjitāḥ \|*  
*śripāvihīnair niḥsattvair devaiś cakrus tato raṇam \|\| 33 \|\|*

After they’d been worsted by the demons, Indra and the thirteen deities led by Agni sought protection from Brahmā, the fortunate grandsire of the world. 33

## \|\| 1.9.34 \|\|

*vijitāstridaśā daityair indrādyāḥ śaraṇaṃ yayuḥ \|*  
*pitāmahaṃ mahābhāgaṃ hutāśanapurogamāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

The gods relayed all this to Brahmā. ‘Go to Viṣṇu for protection, lord of high and low,’ he said, ‘for he’s the scourge of demigods. 34

## \|\| 1.9.35 \|\|

*yathāvatkathito devair brahmā prāha tataḥ surān \|*  
*parāvareśaṃ śaraṇaṃ vrajadhvamasurārdanam \|\| 35 \|\|*

He’s the lord who has no cause, and origin of creation, preservation and destruction of the world. Viṣṇu is lord of all progenitors, unending and unconquerable. 35

## \|\| 1.9.36 \|\|

*utpattisthitināśānāmahetuṃ hetumīśvaram \|*  
*prajā patipatiṃ viṣṇumanantamaparājitam \|\| 36 \|\|*

He’s the cause of unborn primal matter and of spirit, which are transformed by his energy. Viṣṇu, who eases the pain of those who bow before him, will restore your splendour.’ 36

## \|\| 1.9.37 \|\|

*pradhānapuṃsorajayoḥ kāraṇaṃ kāryabhūtayoḥ \|*  
*praṇatārtiharaṃ viṣṇuṃ sa vaḥ śreyo vidhāsyati \|\| 37 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Having thus addressed them, Brahmā, universal grandsire, set off with all the gods for the Milky Ocean’s northern shore. 37

## \|\| 1.9.38 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evamuktvā surānsarvān brahmā lokapitāmahaḥ \|*  
*kṣīrodasyottaraṃ tīraṃ tair eva sahito yayau \|\| 38 \|\|*

Arriving with the thirteen deities, Brahmā gratified Hari, lord of high and low, with these words that pleased him: 38

## \|\| 1.9.39 \|\|

*sa gatvā tridaśaiḥ sarvaiḥ samavetaḥ pitāmahaḥ \|*  
*tuṣṭāva vāgbhir iṣṭābhiḥ parāvarapatiṃ harim \|\| 39 \|\|*

Brahmā:

I bow to the universal lord of all, unending, unarising, unchanging, the abode and foundation of the world, invisible, indivisible, 39

## \|\| 1.9.40 \|\|

*brahmovāca*  
*namāmi sarvaṃ sarveśamanantamajamavyayam \|*  
*lokadhāma dharādhāramaprakāśamabhedinam \|\| 40 \|\|*

Nārāyaṇa, smallest of the small, largest of the large, including all the world and all things in it, 40

## \|\| 1.9.41 \|\|

*nārāyaṇamaṇīyāṃsamaśeṣaṇāmaṇīyasām \|*  
*samastānāṃ gariṣṭhaṃ ca bhūrādīnāṃ garīyasām \|\| 41 \|\|*

In whom all things abide, from whom all things including me arise, the deity who is all things, higher than the highest, 41

## \|\| 1.9.42 \|\|

*yatra sarvaṃ yataḥ sarvamutpannaṃ matpuraḥsaram \|*  
*sarvabhūtaś ca yo devaḥ parāṇāmapi yaḥ paraḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

Who is beyond the highest spirit, who takes the form of the Supreme itself, and on whom ascetics who yearn for liberation meditate as the source of freedom. 42

## \|\| 1.9.43 \|\|

*paraḥ parasmātpuruṣātparamātmasvarūpadhṛk \|*  
*yogibhiścintyate yo 'sau suktihetormumukṣubhiḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

May the lord beyond vitality and other basic qualities, purest of the pure, primal spirit, favour us. 43

## \|\| 1.9.44 \|\|

*sattvādayo na santīśe yatra ca prākṛtā guṇāḥ \|*  
*sa śuddhaḥ sarvaśuddhebhyaḥ pumānādyaḥ prasīdatu \|\| 44 \|\|*

May that pure being, Hari, whose power lies beyond the passage of time that’s made of kalās, kāṣṭhās, nimeṣas and so on, favour us. 44

## \|\| 1.9.45 \|\|

*kalā kāṣṭhā muhūrtādikālāsūtrasya gocare \|*  
*yasya śaktirna śuddhasya sa no viṣṇuḥ prasīdatu \|\| 45 \|\|*

May Viṣṇu, known as the pure supreme lord because of his favours, and who is at the heart of all embodied beings, favour us. 45

## \|\| 1.9.46 \|\|

*procyate parameśo hi yaḥ śuddho 'pyupacārataḥ \|*  
*prasīdatu sa no viṣṇurātmā yaḥ sarvadehinām \|\| 46 \|\|*

May Hari, the cause and the effect, the cause of the cause itself, and the effect of the effect itself, favour us. 46

## \|\| 1.9.47 \|\|

*yaḥ kāraṇaṃ ca kāryaṃ ca kāraṇasyāpi kāraṇam \|*  
*kārya syāpi ca yaḥ kāryaṃ prasīdatu sa no hariḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

Who himself is also the effect, which is the effect of the effect of the effect, and who has become the effect of that effect, I bow to him. 47

## \|\| 1.9.48 \|\|

*kāryakāryasya yatkāryaṃ tatkāryasyāpi yaḥ svayam \|*  
*tatkāryakāryabhūto yastataś ca praṇatāsma tam \|\| 48 \|\|*

Who is the cause of the cause, and the cause of the cause of that cause, and is the cause of all these causes—I bow to that lord of gods. 48

## \|\| 1.9.49 \|\|

*kāraṇaṃ kāraṇasyāpi tasya kāraṇakāraṇam \|*  
*tatkāraṇānāṃ hetuṃ taṃ praṇatā sma pareśvaram \|\| 49 \|\|*

The enjoyer and the enjoyed, the creator and the creation, the deed and the doer—I bow to that highest state. 49

## \|\| 1.9.50 \|\|

*bhoktāraṃ bhogyabhūtaṃ ca sraṣṭāraṃ sṛjyameva ca \|*  
*kāryakartṛsvarūpaṃ taṃ praṇatā sma paraṃ padam \|\| 50 \|\|*

That highest state of Viṣṇu consists of purest understanding and is unending, unborn, undying, undecaying, unmanifest and unchanging. 50

## \|\| 1.9.51 \|\|

*viśuddhabodhavannityamajamakṣayamavyayam \|*  
*avyaktamavikāraṃ yattadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam \|\| 51 \|\|*

I bow to that highest state of Viṣṇu, which is neither gross nor subtle, but lies beyond description and is forever pure. 51

## \|\| 1.9.52 \|\|

*na sthulaṃ na ca sūkṣmaṃ yanna viśeṣaṇagocaram \|*  
*tatpadaṃ paramaṃ viṣṇoḥ praṇamāma sadāmalam \|\| 52 \|\|*

The power to create all things abides in one ten-thousandth part of one ten-thousandth part of that being in the form of the Absolute supreme. I bow to that undecaying entity. 52

## \|\| 1.9.53 \|\|

*yasyā yutāyutāṃśāṃśe viśvaśaktiriyaṃ sthitā \|*  
*parabrahmasvarūpaṃ yatpraṇamāmastamavyayam \|\| 53 \|\|*

Neither gods, nor sages, nor I, nor Śiva know that state that is the ultimate condition of the highest lord, Viṣṇu. 53

## \|\| 1.9.54 \|\|

*yadyoginaḥ sadodyuktāḥ puṇyapāpakṣayekṣayam \|*  
*paśyanti praṇave cintyaṃ tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam \|\| 54 \|\|*

That indestructible state, which ever-striving ascetics perceive in the syllable Oṃ, and which is the destroyer of both merit and demerit, is the highest state of Viṣṇu that should be contemplated. 54

## \|\| 1.9.55 \|\|

*yanna devā na munayo na cāhaṃ na ca śaṅkaraḥ \|*  
*jānanti parameśa sya tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam \|\| 55 \|\|*

That is the highest state of Viṣṇu, the deity whose powers are at the heart of Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva, and before whom nothing else existed. 55

## \|\| 1.9.56 \|\|

*śaktayo yasya devasya brahmaviṣṇuśivātmikāḥ \|*  
*bhavantyabhūtapūrvasya tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam \|\| 56 \|\|*

Universal Lord who is at the heart of everything, who is everything, the everlasting refuge of everything—favour us, your devotees, Viṣṇu, and reveal yourself. 56

## \|\| 1.9.57 \|\|

*sarveśa sarvabhūtātmansarva sarvāśrayācyuta \|*  
*prasīda viṣṇo bhaktānāṃ vraja no dṛṣṭigocaram \|\| 57 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing Brahmā’s supplication, the thirteen deities also bowed and said, ‘Favour us and reveal yourself. 57

## \|\| 1.9.58 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityudīritamākarṇya brahmaṇastridaśāstataḥ \|*  
*praṇamyocuḥ prasīdeti vraja no dṛṣṭi gocaram \|\| 58 \|\|*

We bow down to that highest state of yours, which even Brahmā cannot comprehend. You are the domicile of the world, all-pervasive and imperishable.’ 58

## \|\| 1.9.59 \|\|

*yannāyaṃ bhagavān brahma jānāti paramaṃ padam \|*  
*tannatāḥ sma-jagaddhāma tava sarvagatācyuta \|\| 59 \|\|*

After Brahmā and the gods had spoken, all the heavenly sages, led by Bṛhaspati, said: 59

## \|\| 1.9.60 \|\|

*ityante vacasasteṣāṃ devānāṃ brahmaṇasta thā \|*  
*ūcurdevarṣayaḥsarva bṛhaspatipurogamāḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

‘The primal sacrifice, primal and praiseworthy spirit, born before the first of beings: we bow to him, creator of the world’s creator, beyond description. 60

## \|\| 1.9.61 \|\|

*adyo yajñaḥ pumānīḍyaḥ pūrveṣāṃ yaś ca pūrvajaḥ \|*  
*tannatāḥ sma jagatstraṣṭuḥ sraṣṭārama viśeṣaṇam \|\| 61 \|\|*

Lord, you are the master of all that’s been and all that is to be, you embody the sacrifice and are undying; favour all those who bow to you and show yourself to us. 61

## \|\| 1.9.62 \|\|

*bhagavanbhūtabhavyeśa yajñamūrtidharāvyaya \|*  
*prasīda praṇatānāṃ tvaṃ sarveṣāṃ dehi darśanam \|\| 62 \|\|*

Here is Brahmā, and here is three-eyed Śiva accompanied by the Rudras. Pūṣan is here with all the Ādityas, as is Agni and the fire gods. 62

## \|\| 1.9.63 \|\|

*eṣa brahma sahāsmābhiḥ saha rudrais trilocanaḥ \|*  
*sarvādityaiḥ samaṃ pūṣā pāvako 'yaṃ sahāgnibhaḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

These are the Aśvins and Vasus, and these, the hosts of Maruts. All the Sādhyas are here as well. These are the gods and this, their sovereign, Indra. 63

## \|\| 1.9.64 \|\|

*aśvinau vasavaśceme sarve caite marudgaṇāḥ \|*  
*sādhyā viśve tathā devā devendraścāyamīśvaraḥ \|\| 64 \|\|*

Routed by the Daitya army, they bow deeply, protector. All these hosts of gods have come for your protection.’ 64

## \|\| 1.9.65 \|\|

*praṇāmapravaṇā nātha daityasainyaiḥ parājitāḥ \|*  
*śaraṇaṃ tvāmanuprāptāḥ samastā devatāgaṇāḥ \|\| 65 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus praised, Maitreya, the holy bearer of conch and discus, the highest lord, appeared before them. 65

## \|\| 1.9.66 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ saṃstūyamānastu bhagavāñchaṃkhacakradhṛk \|*  
*jagāma darśanaṃ teṣāṃ maitreya parame śvaraḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

Beholding the bearer of these weapons of incomparable appearance, a mass of splendour and magnificence, 66

## \|\| 1.9.67 \|\|

*taṃ dṛṣṭvā te tadā devāḥ śaṅkhacakragadādharam \|*  
*apūrvarūpasaṃsthānaṃ tejasāṃ rāśimūrjitam \|\| 67 \|\|*

Having first bowed down, the reverent deities’ eyes were filled with anxious tears and, led by Brahmā, grandsire of the world, they praised lotus-eyed Viṣṇu. 67

## \|\| 1.9.68 \|\|

*praṇamya praṇatāḥ sarve saṃkṣobha stimitekṣaṇāḥ \|*  
*tuṣṭuvuḥ puṇḍarīkākṣaṃ pitāmahapurogamāḥ \|\| 68 \|\|*

The deities:

We bow to you again and again, lord of all. You are Brahmā and Śiva, who wields the bow Pināka. You are Indra, Agni, purifying Wind, Varuṇa, Savitṛ and Yama. You are the Vasus, Maruts, Sādhyas and the hosts of Viśvedevas. 68

## \|\| 1.9.69 \|\|

*devā ūcuḥ \|*  
*namonamo viśeṣastvaṃ tvaṃ brahma tvaṃ pinākadhṛk \|*  
*indrastvama gniḥ pavano varuṇaḥ savitā yamaḥ \|\| 69 \|\|*

You, Lord, are indeed this host of deities that has come before you, creator of the world, because you are everywhere. 69

## \|\| 1.9.70 \|\|

*vasavo marutaḥ sādhyā viśvedevagaṇā bhavān \|*  
*yo 'yaṃ tavāgrato deva samīpaṃ devatāgaṇaḥ \|*  
*sa tvameva jagatstraṣṭā yataḥ sarvagato bhavān \|\| 70 \|\|*

You are the sacrifice, the cry of vaṣaṭ, the syllable Oṃ, lord of creation, knowledge and knower. You are at the heart of everything, and all the world consists of you. 70

## \|\| 1.9.71 \|\|

*tvaṃ yajñastvaṃ vaṣaṭkārastvamoṅkāraḥ prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*vidyā vedyaṃ ca sarvātmaṃstvanmayaṃ cākhilaṃ jagat \|\| 71 \|\|*

Defeated by the Daityas, we’re miserable and come to you for refuge, Viṣṇu, so favour us. You are at the heart of everything. Please defend us with your might. 71

## \|\| 1.9.72 \|\|

*tvamārtāḥ śaraṇaṃ viṣṇo prayātā daityanirjitāḥ \|*  
*vayaṃ prasīda sarvātmaṃstejasāpyāyayasva naḥ \|\| 72 \|\|*

Until a being comes to you for refuge, dispeller of all impurities, they’ll have pain and longing, delusion and unhappiness. 72

## \|\| 1.9.73 \|\|

*tāvadārtis tathā vāñchā tāva nmohas tathā mukham \|*  
*yāvanna yāti śaraṇaṃ tvāmaśeṣāghanāśanam \|\| 73 \|\|*

You are grace itself, so be gracious to us, your devotees. With power arising from all your energy, protector, protect us. 73

## \|\| 1.9.74 \|\|

*tvaṃ prasādaṃ prasannatman prapannānāṃ kuruṣva naḥ \|*  
*tejasāṃ nātha sarveṣāṃ svaśaktyāpyāyanaṃ kuru \|\| 74 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus praised by the reverent immortals, Lord Hari, creator of the universe, whose eyes were filled with grace, addressed them: 74

## \|\| 1.9.75 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ saṃstūyamānastu praṇatair amarair hariḥ \|*  
*prasannadṛśṭirbhagavānidamāha sa viśvakṛt \|\| 75 \|\|*

The Lord:

I’ll restore your energy, deities, and tell you what to do. 75

## \|\| 1.9.76 \|\|

*tejaso bhavatāṃ devāḥ kariṣyāmyupabṛṃhaṇam \|*  
*vadāmyahaṃ yatkriyatāṃ bhavadbhis tadidaṃ surāḥ \|\| 76 \|\|*

In collaboration with the Daityas, cast every kind of healing herb into the Milky Ocean, take the sacred mountain Mandara as your churning-stick and the nāga Vāsuki as the cord with which to turn it. Then, with my help, you deities will churn forth the nectar of immortality. 76

## \|\| 1.9.77 \|\|

*ānīya sahitā daityaiḥ kṣīrābdhau saralau ṣadhīḥ \|*  
*prakṣipyātramṛtārthaṃ tāḥ sakalā daityadānavaiḥ \|*  
*manthānaṃ mandaraṃ kṛtvā netraṃ kṛtvā ca vāsukim \|\| 77 \|\|*

First speak kindly to the Daityas in this regard, and tell them they’ll receive an equal portion from this shared labour. 77

## \|\| 1.9.78 \|\|

*mathyatāmamṛte devāḥ sahāye mayyavasthite \|\| 78 \|\|*

By drinking nectar from the churning of the ocean, they’ll gain power and immortality. 78

## \|\| 1.9.79 \|\|

*sāmapūrvaṃ ca daiteyāstatra sāhāyyakarmaṇi \|*  
*sāmānyaphalabhoktāro yūyaṃ vācyā bhaviṣyatha \|\| 79 \|\|*

But I’ll ensure your enemies, you thirteen deathless gods, receive no nectar and gain nothing from their efforts. 79

## \|\| 1.9.80 \|\|

*mathyamāne ca tatrābdhau yatsamutpatsyate 'mṛtam \|*  
*tatpānādbalino yūyamamarāś ca bhaviṣyatha \|\| 80 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus addressed by Viṣṇu, god of gods, all the deities made a compact with the Daityas and set about the churning of the nectar. 80

## \|\| 1.9.81 \|\|

*tathā cāhaṃ kariṣyāmi te yathā tridaśadviṣaḥ \|*  
*na prāpsyantyamṛtaṃ devāḥ kevalaṃ kleśabhāginaḥ \|\| 81 \|\|*

The deities, Daityas and Dānavas gathered varied herbs and cast them on the waters of the Milky Ocean, pure and splendid like the clouds in autumn. 81

## \|\| 1.9.82 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktā devadevena sarva eva tadā surāḥ \|*  
*sandhānamasūraiḥ kṛtvā yatnavanto 'mṛte 'bhavan \|\| 82 \|\|*

Taking Mount Mandara as their churning-stick and using Vāsuki as the cord, they then began to churn the nectar vigorously, Maitreya. 82

## \|\| 1.9.83 \|\|

*nānauṣadhīḥ samānīya devadaiteyadānavāḥ \|*  
*kṣiptvā kṣīrābdhipayasi śara dabhrāmalatviṣi \|\| 83 \|\|*

Viṣṇu sent the assembled gods to the tail of Vāsuki, and the Daityas to the fore part of that serpent’s body. 83

## \|\| 1.9.84 \|\|

*manthānaṃ mandaraṃ kṛtvā netraṃ kṛtvā ca vāsukim \|*  
*tato mathitumārabdhā maitreya tarasāmṛtam \|\| 84 \|\|*

Scorched by fiery blasts from Vāsuki’s hood, best of sages, all the Daityas lost their vigour and grew weak. 84

## \|\| 1.9.85 \|\|

*vibudhāḥ sahitāḥ sarve yataḥ pucchaṃ tataḥ kṛtāḥ \|*  
*kṛṣṇena vāsukerdaityāḥ pūrvakāye niveśitāḥ \|\| 85 \|\|*

But the deities at the serpent’s tail were refreshed by rain that fell from clouds whipped up by gusts of breath from Vāsuki’s maw. 85

## \|\| 1.9.86 \|\|

*te tasya mukhaniśvāsavahnitāpahatatviṣaḥ \|*  
*nistejaso 'surāḥ sarve babhūvuramitaujasaḥ \|\| 86 \|\|*

In the middle of the Milky Ocean, Lord Hari himself in the form of a tortoise served as a support for Mount Mandara as it turned, great sage. 86

## \|\| 1.9.87 \|\|

*tenaiva mukhaniśvāsavāyunāstabalāhakaiḥ \|*  
*pucchapradeśe varṣadbhis tadā cāpyāyitāḥ surāḥ \|\| 87 \|\|*

Taking a second form among the gods, the bearer of the mace and discus drew on that serpent king and, in yet another form, he did the same among the Daityas. 87

## \|\| 1.9.88 \|\|

*kṣīro damadhye bhagavānkūrmarūpī svayaṃ hariḥ \|*  
*manthanādreradhiṣṭhānaṃ bhramato 'bhūnmahāmune \|\| 88 \|\|*

Keśava ascended to the mountain top in third gigantic form, Maitreya, invisible to both gods and Daityas. 88

## \|\| 1.9.89 \|\|

*rūpeṇānyena devānāṃ madhye cakragadādharaḥ \|*  
*cakarṣa nāga rājānaṃ daityamadhye 'pareṇa ca \|\| 89 \|\|*

With one portion of his energy, Hari sustained the serpent king and, with another, reinforced the deities. 89

## \|\| 1.9.90 \|\|

*uparyākrāntavāñśailaṃ bṛhadrūpeṇa keśavaḥ \|*  
*tathāpareṇa maitreya yanna dṛṣṭaṃ surāsuraiḥ \|\| 90 \|\|*

As the gods and Dānavas churned the ocean, there first arose the sacred cow Surabhi, source of all milk, worshipped by the deities. 90

## \|\| 1.9.91 \|\|

*tejasā nāgarā jānaṃ tathāpyāyitavānhariḥ \|*  
*anyena tejasā devānupabṛṃhitavānprabhuḥ \|\| 91 \|\|*

Both gods and Dānavas were delighted, sage, their minds held captive and their eyes agleam. 91

## \|\| 1.9.92 \|\|

*mathyamāne tatastasminkṣirābdho devadānavaiḥ \|*  
*havirdhāmābhavatpūrvaṃ surabhiḥ surapūjītā \|\| 92 \|\|*

When next the goddess of wine, Vāruṇī, arose, her eyes rolling with drunkenness, the siddhas in the heavens asked themselves, ‘What’s this?!’ 92

## \|\| 1.9.93 \|\|

*jagmurmudaṃ tato devā dānavāś ca mahāmune \|*  
*vyākṣiptacetasaścaiva babhūvustimitekṣaṇāḥ \|\| 93 \|\|*

From the Milky Ocean as it turned, there then appeared the Pārijāta tree, filling all the worlds with perfume and delighting the deities’ womenfolk. 93

## \|\| 1.9.94 \|\|

*kimetaditisiddhānāṃ divi cintayatāṃ tataḥ \|*  
*babhūva vāruṇī devī madāghūrṇitalocanā \|\| 94 \|\|*

And next a marvellous host of apsarases, endowed with beauty, nobility and virtue, Maitreya, emerged from the Milky Ocean. 94

## \|\| 1.9.95 \|\|

*kṛtāvartāttatastasmātkṣīrodādvāsayañjagat \|*  
*gandhena pārījātobhūddevastrīnandanastaruḥ \|\| 95 \|\|*

The cool-rayed moon that next came forth was seized by Śiva, and the nāgas took the poison arising from the ocean. 95

## \|\| 1.9.96 \|\|

*rūpaudāryaguṇopetas tathā cāpsarasāṃ gaṇaḥ \|*  
*kṣīrodadheḥ samutpanno maitreya paramādbhutaḥ \|\| 96 \|\|*

Finally, the gods’ physician, the white-clad deity Dhanvantari himself, appeared with a jar of nectar. 96

## \|\| 1.9.97 \|\|

*tataḥ śītāṃśurabhavajjagṛhe taṃ maheśvaraḥ \|*  
*jagṛhuś ca viṣaṃ nāgāḥ kṣīrodābdhisamutthitam \|\| 97 \|\|*

The hearts of all those Daityas and Dānavas were filled at once with joy and they exulted, Maitreya, along with all the sages. 97

## \|\| 1.9.98 \|\|

*tato dhanvantarirdevaḥ śvetāmbaradharasvayam \|*  
*bibhratkamaṇḍaluṃ pūrṇamamṛtasya samutthitaḥ \|\| 98 \|\|*

From the milk then arose the goddess Śrī, radiant with beauty, standing on an open lotus, with another in her hand. 98

## \|\| 1.9.99 \|\|

*tataḥ svasthamanaskāste sarve daitaiyadānavāḥ \|*  
*babhūvurmuditāḥ sarve maitreya munibhiḥ saha \|\| 99 \|\|*

Great sages filled with joy then praised her with the paean called Śrī-sūkta. 99

## \|\| 1.9.100 \|\|

*tataḥ sphuratkāntimatī vikāsikamale sthitā \|*  
*śrīrdevī payasastasmādudbhūtā dhṛtapaṅkajā \|\| 100 \|\|*

Gandharvas with Viśvāvasu at their head sang before her, brahmin, as hosts of apsarases led by Ghṛtācī danced. 100

## \|\| 1.9.101 \|\|

*tāṃ tuṣṭuvurmudā yuktāḥ śrīsūktena maharṣayaḥ \|\| 101 \|\|*

Gaṅgā and the other rivers came forward to bathe her with their waters. The guardian elephants of four quarters brought pure water in golden vessels and poured it on the goddess, sovereign of all the worlds. 101

## \|\| 1.9.102 \|\|

*viśvāvasumukhāstasyā gandharvāḥ purato jaguḥ \|*  
*ghṛtācīpramukhāstatra nanṛtuścāpsarogaṇāḥ \|\| 102 \|\|*

The Milky Ocean, appearing as a deity, offered her a garland of fresh waterlilies, and the gods’ own artificer, Viśvakarman, crafted jewellery to adorn her. 102

## \|\| 1.9.103 \|\|

*gaṅgādyāḥ saritastoyaiḥ snānārthamupatasthire \|*  
*diggajā hemapātrasthamādāya vimalaṃ jalam \|*  
*strāpayāñcakrire devīṃ sarvalokamaheśvarīm \|\| 103 \|\|*

Clad in heavenly wreathes and garments, bathed and wearing jewels, Śrī pressed herself to Hari’s bosom while all the gods looked on. 103

## \|\| 1.9.104 \|\|

*kṣīrodo rūpadhṛktasyai mālāmamlānapaṅkajām \|*  
*dadau vibhūṣaṇānyaṅge viśvakarmā cakāra ha \|\| 104 \|\|*

The deities, observed by Lakṣmī as she clung to Hari’s bosom, Maitreya, attained at once the highest state of bliss. 104

## \|\| 1.9.105 \|\|

*divyamālyāmbaradharāstrātā bhūṣaṇabhūṣitā \|*  
*paśyatāṃ sarvadevānāṃ yayau vakṣasthalaṃ hareḥ \|\| 105 \|\|*

But the Daityas led by Vipracitti, shunned by Viṣṇu and ignored by Lakṣmī, blessed sage, descended into deep despair. 105

## \|\| 1.9.106 \|\|

*tayā vilokitā devā harivakṣasthalasthayā \|*  
*lakṣmyā maitreya sahasā parāṃ nirvṛtimāgatāḥ \|\| 106 \|\|*

Those mighty Daityas, brahmin, then seized the pitcher with the nectar from the hands of Dhanvantari. 106

## \|\| 1.9.107 \|\|

*udvegaṃ paramaṃ jagmurdaityā viṣṇuparāṅmukhāḥ \|*  
*tyaktā lakṣmyā mahābhāga vipracittirurogamāḥ \|\| 107 \|\|*

But Viṣṇu, appearing in a female form, tricked them with an apparition. Seizing back the pitcher from the demons, he returned it to the gods. 107

## \|\| 1.9.108 \|\|

*tataste jagṛhurdaityā dhanvantarikarasthitam \|*  
*kamaṇḍaluṃ mahāvīryā yatrā'ste 'mṛtamuttamam \|\| 108 \|\|*

Indra and the hosts of gods then drank that nectar, but the Daityas, wielding weapons, assailed them without mercy. 108

## \|\| 1.9.109 \|\|

*māyayā mohayitvā tānviṣṇuḥ strīrūpasaṃsthitaḥ \|*  
*dānavebhyastadādāya devebhyaḥ pradadau prabhuḥ \|\| 109 \|\|*

As the mighty gods had drunk the draft, they destroyed the demon army, which was scattered in all directions and fled to the lower realm of Pātāla. 109

## \|\| 1.9.110 \|\|

*tataḥ papuḥ suraguṇāḥ śakrādyāstattadāmṛtam \|*  
*udyatāyudhanistraṃśā daityāstāṃś ca samābhyayuḥ \|\| 110 \|\|*

Then the deities, filled with joy, bowed to Viṣṇu, bearer of the discus, conch and mace, and ruled once more the realm of Triviṣṭapa. 110

## \|\| 1.9.111 \|\|

*pīte 'mṛte ca balibhir devair daityacamūstadā \|*  
*vadhyamānā diśo bheje pātālaṃ ca viveśa vai \|\| 111 \|\|*

The sun of shining brilliance resumed his usual course, best of sages, and the other luminaries proceeded on their proper paths. 111

## \|\| 1.9.112 \|\|

*tato devā mudā yuktāḥ śaṅkhacakragadābhṛtam \|*  
*praṇipatya yathāpūrvamāśāsattattriviṣṭapam \|\| 112 \|\|*

The lovely light of sacred fires blazed brightly, and the minds of every being turned once more to virtue. 112

## \|\| 1.9.113 \|\|

*tataḥ prasannabhāḥ sūryaḥ prayayau svena vartmanā \|*  
*jyotīṃṣi ca yathāmārgaṃ prayayurmunisattama \|\| 113 \|\|*

All three worlds now prospered, best of brahmins, and mighty Indra, chief of thirteen deities, regained his splendour. 113

## \|\| 1.9.114 \|\|

*jajvāla bhagavāṃścoccaiś cārudīptirvibhāvasuḥ \|*  
*dharme ca sarvabhūtānāṃ tadā matirajāyata \|\| 114 \|\|*

Seated on his lion throne, Indra, having gained again the threefold worlds and ruling over the gods once more, praised the goddess who holds a lotus in her hand. 114

## \|\| 1.9.115 \|\|

*trailokyaṃ ca śriyā juṣṭaṃ babhūva dvijasattama \|*  
*śakraś ca tridaśaśreṣṭhaḥ punaḥ śrīmā najāyata \|\| 115 \|\|*

Indra:

I bow to you, Śrī, mother of the world, born from a lotus, with eyes like wakeful waterlilies, pressed to Viṣṇu’s bosom. 115

## \|\| 1.9.116 \|\|

*siṃhāsanagataḥ śakraḥsaṃprāpya tridivaṃ punaḥ \|*  
*devarājye stito devīṃ tuṣṭāvābjakarāṃ tataḥ \|\| 116 \|\|*

You are success personified. You are the cries of svadhā and svāhā. You are the nectar that purifies the world. You are the twilight at dawn and dusk, prosperity, faith and wisdom. You are Sarasvatī, the deity of speech. 116

## \|\| 1.9.117 \|\|

*indra uvāca*  
*namasye sarvalokānāṃ jananīmabjasambhavām \|*  
*śriyamunnidrapadmākṣīṃ viṣṇuvakṣasthalasthitām \|\| 117 \|\|*

You are knowledge of the sacrifice, knowledge that is great and secret, fairest goddess, and the knowledge of the self that yields the prize of liberation. 117

## \|\| 1.9.118 \|\|

*padmālayāṃ padmakarāṃ padmapatranibhekṣaṇām \|*  
*vande padmasukhīṃ devīṃ padmanābhapriyāmaham \|\| 118 \|\|*

You are logic, the threefold Vedas, the professions and the art of governance. This world is filled with all your forms, both mild and wrathful, goddess. 118

## \|\| 1.9.119 \|\|

*tvaṃ siddhistvaṃ svadhā svāhā sudhā tvaṃ lokapāvanī \|*  
*sandhyā rātriḥ prabhā bhūtirmedhā śraddhā sarasvatī \|\| 119 \|\|*

Who, apart from you, goddess, dwells in the bosom of the god of gods, the embodiment of all the sacrifice, the object of ascetics’ meditation, the bearer of the mace? 119

## \|\| 1.9.120 \|\|

*yajñavidyā mahāvidya guhyavidya ca śobhane \|*  
*ātmavidyā ca devi tvaṃ visuktiphaladāyinī \|\| 120 \|\|*

Bereft of you, all three worlds were on the brink of ruin, goddess, but now you’ve rescued them. 120

## \|\| 1.9.121 \|\|

*ānvīkṣikītrayīvārta daṇḍanītistvameva ca \|*  
*saimyāsaumyair jagadrūpais tvayaittaddevi pūritam \|\| 121 \|\|*

Whenever you are looking on, goddess of good fortune, men will always have a wife, sons, a house, companions, sustenance, money and all the rest. 121

## \|\| 1.9.122 \|\|

*kā tvanyā tvāmṛte devi sarvayajñamayaṃ vapuḥ \|*  
*adhyāste devadevasya yogicintyaṃ gadābhṛtaḥ \|\| 122 \|\|*

Health, power, victory and happiness, goddess, aren’t hard to find for those on whom you cast your gaze. 122

## \|\| 1.9.123 \|\|

*tvāyā devi parityaktaṃ sakalaṃ bhuvanatrayam \|*  
*vinaṣṭaprāyamabhavattvayedānīṃ samedhitam \|\| 123 \|\|*

You are the mother of every being and Hari god of gods their sire. You and Viṣṇu, mother, pervade this world of moving and unmoving things. 123

## \|\| 1.9.124 \|\|

*dārāḥ putrāstathāgārasuhṛddhānyadhanādikam \|*  
*bhavatyetanmahābhāge nityaṃ tvadvīkṣaṇāntṛṇām \|\| 124 \|\|*

Don’t forsake our storehouse, byre, our home, our goods, our bodies or our wives—you, who confers purity on everything. 124

## \|\| 1.9.125 \|\|

*śarīrārogyamaiśvaryam aripakṣakṣayaḥ sukham \|*  
*devi tvaddṛṣṭidṛṣṭānāṃ puruṣāṇāṃ na durlabham \|\| 125 \|\|*

Don’t forsake my sons, my circle of companions, my cattle or adornments—you, whose place is in the bosom of lordly Viṣṇu. 125

## \|\| 1.9.126 \|\|

*tvaṃ mātā sarvalokānāṃ devadevo hariḥ pitā \|*  
*tvayaitadviṣṇunā cāmba jagavdyāptaṃ carācaram \|\| 126 \|\|*

Those whom you reject, stainless goddess, are bereft at once of goodness, truth and purity and of the virtues like morality and the rest. 126

## \|\| 1.9.127 \|\|

*mā naḥ kośaṃ tathā goṣṭhaṃ mā gṛhaṃ mā paricchadam \|*  
*mā śarīraṃ kalatraṃ ca tyajethāḥ sarvapāvani \|\| 127 \|\|*

Under your gaze, even the wicked are filled at once with every wished-for quality, including morality, and their kinsfolk prosper. 127

## \|\| 1.9.128 \|\|

*mā putrānmā suhṛrdvaga mā paśunmā vibhūṣaṇam \|*  
*tyajethā mama devasya viṣṇorvakṣaḥsthalālaye \|\| 128 \|\|*

Under your gaze, goddess, a person becomes praiseworthy, virtuous, wealthy, noble, wise, courageous and victorious. 128

## \|\| 1.9.129 \|\|

*sattvena satyaśaucābhyāṃ tathā śīladibhir guṇaiḥ \|*  
*tyajyante te narāḥ sadyaḥ saṃtyaktā ye tvāyāmale \|\| 129 \|\|*

But when you, supporter of the world, beloved of Viṣṇu, turn your face away, a person’s entire store of virtues starting with morality is lost at once. 129

## \|\| 1.9.130 \|\|

*tvayā vilokitāḥ sadyaḥ śīlādyair akhilair guṇaiḥ \|*  
*kulaiśvaryaiś ca yujyante puruṣā nirguṇā api \|\| 130 \|\|*

Even the tongue of Brahmā can’t describe your virtues. Favour us, lotus-eyed goddess, and never forsake us. 130

## \|\| 1.9.131 \|\|

*sar ślāghyaḥ sa guṇī dhanyaḥ sa kulīnaḥ sa buddhimān \|*  
*sa śūraḥ sa ca vikrānto yastvayā devi vīkṣitaḥ \|\| 131 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Śrī, abiding in all beings, thus praised, was perfectly delighted, brahmin, and replied to Indra, deity of a hundred sacrifices, while all the other gods attended. 131

## \|\| 1.9.132 \|\|

*sadyo vaiguṇyamāyānti śīlādyaḥ sakalā guṇāḥ \|*  
*parāṅmukhī jagaddhātrī yasya tvaṃ viṣṇuvallabhe \|\| 132 \|\|*

Śrī:

Indra, king of gods, your song has pleased me. Ask for anything you desire, for I am here to grant your wish. 132

## \|\| 1.9.133 \|\|

*na te varṇayituṃ śaktā guṇāñcihvāpi bedhasaḥ \|*  
*prasīda devi padmākṣi māsmāṃstyākṣīḥ kadāṭana \|\| 133 \|\|*

Indra:

If you’ll grant my wish, goddess, and if I’m worthy of such a favour, then never forsake this threefold world. Let this be my first request. 133

## \|\| 1.9.134 \|\|

*śrīparaśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ śrīḥ saṃstutā samyak prāha devī śatakratum \|*  
*śṛṇvatāṃ sarvadevānāṃ sarvabhūtasthitā dvija \|\| 134 \|\|*

Never forsake the person who lauds you with this song, ocean-born goddess. Let this be my second wish. 134

## \|\| 1.9.135 \|\|

*śrīruvāca*  
*parituṣṭāsmi deveśe stotreṇānena te hare \|*  
*varaṃ vṛṇīṣva yastviṣṭo vara dāhaṃ tavāgatā \|\| 135 \|\|*

Śrī:

I’ll not forsake the threefold worlds, Indra, chief of thirteen gods. I grant this favour as I’m pleased with the adoration of your song. 135

## \|\| 1.9.136 \|\|

*indra uvāca*  
*varadā yadi me devi vārarhe yadi vāpyaham \|*  
*trailokyaṃ na tvayā tyājyameṣa mestu varaḥ paraḥ \|\| 136 \|\|*

Likewise, I’ll never turn my back on one who lauds me with this paean at dawn and dusk. 136

## \|\| 1.9.137 \|\|

*stotreṇa yastathaitena tvāṃ stoṣyatyabdhisambhave \|*  
*sa tvayā na parītyājyo dvitīyostu varo mama \|\| 137 \|\|*

Parāśara:

That’s how the blessed goddess Śrī, pleased with Indra’s song of praise, Maitreya, granted two wishes to the king of gods in former times. 137

## \|\| 1.9.138 \|\|

*śrīruvāca*  
*trailokyaṃ trida śaśreṣṭha na santyakṣyāmi vāsava \|*  
*datto varo mayāyante stautrārādhanatuṣṭayā \|\| 138 \|\|*

Śrī was originally born the daughter of Bhṛgu and Khyāti and arose again from the ocean when the gods and demons churned the nectar. 138

## \|\| 1.9.139 \|\|

*yaś ca sāyaṃ tathā prātaḥ stotreṇānena mānavaḥ \|*  
*māṃ stoṣyati na tasyāhaṃ bhaviṣyāmi parāṅmukhī \|\| 139 \|\|*

When Janārdana, master of the world and god of gods, manifests on earth, Śrī arises as his consort. 139

## \|\| 1.9.140 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ dadau varaṃ devī devarājāya vai purā \|*  
*maitreya śrīrmahābhāgā stotrārādhanatoṣitā \|\| 140 \|\|*

Later, when Hari was born the son of Aditi in the form of a dwarf, Śrī was born as Padmā, and when he was born as Paraśurāma, Bhṛgu’s scion, she was born as Dharaṇī. 140

## \|\| 1.9.141 \|\|

*bhṛgoḥ khyātyāṃ samutpannā śrīḥ pūrvamudadheḥ punaḥ \|*  
*devadānavayatnena prasūtāmṛtamanthane \|\| 141 \|\|*

When he appeared as Rāma, scion of Raghu, she was Sītā. When he was Kṛṣṇa, she was Rukmiṇī, and during the other manifestations of Viṣṇu, she was always his companion. 141

## \|\| 1.9.142 \|\|

*evaṃ yadā jagatsvāmī devadevo janārdanaḥ \|*  
*avatāraṃ karotyeṣā tadā śrīstatmahāyini \|\| 142 \|\|*

When he appears as a deity, she also takes a heavenly form, and when he appears in mortal form, she always does the same; Śrī adopts a form to complement that of Viṣṇu. 142

## \|\| 1.9.143 \|\|

*punaś ca padmādutpannā ādityobhūdyadā hariḥ \|*  
*yadā tu bhārgavo rāmastadābhūddharaṇī tviyam \|\| 143 \|\|*

Prosperity will never wane for three generations in the house of one who hears or recites this account of Lakṣmī’s birth. 143

## \|\| 1.9.144 \|\|

*rāghavatve 'bhavatsītā rukmiṇī kṛṣṇajanmani \|*  
*anyeṣu cāvatāreṣu viṣṇoreṣānapāyini \|\| 144 \|\|*

Misfortune, the source of woe, sage, never falls on homes in which this song of Śrī is sung. 144

## \|\| 1.9.145 \|\|

*devatve devadeheyaṃ manuṣyatve ca mānuṣī \|*  
*viṣṇordehānurūpāṃ vai karotyeṣātmanastanum \|\| 145 \|\|*

This is the answer to your question, brahmin, as to how Śrī, previously born as Bhṛgu’s daughter Satī, arose from the Milky Ocean. 145

## \|\| 1.9.146 \|\|

*yaścaitacchṛṇuyājjanma lakṣmyā yaś ca paṭhennaraḥ \|*  
*śriyo na vicyutistasya gṛhe yāvatkulatrayam \|\| 146 \|\|*

Calamity will never visit those who recite this song in praise of Lakṣmī daily, as it’s the key to all good fortune, and originated with the deities led by Indra. 146

## Chapter 10 - Descendants of the first patriarchs and Dakṣa’s daughters

## \|\| 1.10.1 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*kathitaṃ me tvāyā sarvaṃ yatpṛṣṭosi mayā mune \|*  
*bhṛgusargātprabhṛtyeṣa sargo me kathyatāṃ punaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

You’ve told me everything I asked about, great sage, but tell me more about creation. How did Bhṛgu beget offspring, for a start? 1

## \|\| 1.10.2 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*bhṛgoḥ khyātyāṃ samutpannā lakṣmīrviṣṇuparigrahaḥ \|*  
*tathā dhātṛvidhātārau khyātyāṃ jātau sutau bhṛgoḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Just as Viṣṇu’s consort Lakṣmī was born to Bhṛgu and Khyāti, they also had two sons, Dhātṛ and Vidhārtṛ. 2

## \|\| 1.10.3 \|\|

*āyatirniyatiścaiva meroḥ kanye mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*bhārye dhātṛvidhātroste tayorjātau sutāvubhau \|\| 3 \|\|*

Two daughters of great Mount Meru, Āyati and Niyati, became the wives of Dhātṛ and Vidhārtṛ, and each one bore a son, 3

## \|\| 1.10.4 \|\|

*prāṇaścaiva mṛkaṇḍuś ca mārkaṇḍeyo mṛkaṇḍutaḥ \|*  
*tato vedaśirā jajñe prāṇasyāpi sutaṃ śṛṇu \|\| 4 \|\|*

Prāṇa and Mṛkaṇḍu, respectively. The sage Mārkaṇḍeya was descended from the latter. Mṛkaṇḍu also had a son named Vedaśiras, but now you’ll hear about the son of Prāṇa: 4

## \|\| 1.10.5 \|\|

*prāṇasya dyutimānputro rājavāṃś ca tato 'bhavat \|*  
*tato vaṃśo mahābhāga vistaraṃ bhārgavo gataḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Prāṇa’s son was Dyutimant, and his son was Rājavant, fortunate sage. The lengthy lineage of Bhārgavas were his descendants. 5

## \|\| 1.10.6 \|\|

*patnī marīceḥ saṃbhūtiḥ paurṇamāsamasūyatā \|*  
*virajāḥ parvataścaiva tasya putro mahātmanaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Marīci’s wife, Saṃbhūti, bore Paurṇamāsa, and Virajas and Parvata were that great being’s two sons. 6

## \|\| 1.10.7 \|\|

*vaṃśasaṃkīrtane putrānvadiṣyehaṃ tato dvija \|*  
*smṛtiścāṅgīrasaḥ patnī prasūtā kanyakās tathā \|*  
*sinīvālī kuhūścaiva rākā cānumatī tathā \|\| 7 \|\|*

I’ll tell you more about them when I describe this lineage in more detail, brahmin. Aṅgiras’s wife, Smṛti, bore daughters, Sinīvālī, Kuhū, Rākā and Anumati. 7

## \|\| 1.10.8 \|\|

*anasūyā tathaivātrerjajñe niṣkalmaṣānsutān \|*  
*somaṃ durvāsasaṃ caiva dattātreyaṃ ca yoginam \|\| 8 \|\|*

Atri’s wife, Anasūyā, had three pure sons, Soma, Durvāsas and the ascetic Dattātreya. 8

## \|\| 1.10.9 \|\|

*prītyāṃ pulastyabhāryāyāṃ dattolistatsuto 'bhavat \|*  
*pūrvajanmani yo 'gastyaḥ smṛtaḥ svāyaṃbhuventare \|\| 9 \|\|*

Pulastya’s wife, Prīti, had a son Dattoli, who, in Svāyaṃbhuva’s reign, was previously born as Agastya. 9

## \|\| 1.10.10 \|\|

*kardamaścārvarīvāṃś ca sahiṣṇuś ca sutāstrayaḥ \|*  
*kṣamā tu suṣave bhāryā pulahasya prajāpateḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Kardama, Arvarīvant and Sahiṣṇu were the three sons born to Kṣamā, wife of the patriarch Pulaha. 10

## \|\| 1.10.11 \|\|

*kratoś ca saṃtatirbhāryā vālakhilyānasūyata \|*  
*ṣaṣṭiputrasahasrāṇi munīnāmūrdhvaretasām \|*  
*aṅguṣṭhaparvamātrāṇāṃ jvaladbhāskaratejasām \|\| 11 \|\|*

Kratu’s wife, Sannati, gave birth to sixty thousand Vālakhilya sages who retained their semen, and were no bigger than your thumb, but each had the energy of the blazing sun. 11

## \|\| 1.10.12 \|\|

*ūrjāyāṃ tu vasiṣṭhasya saptājāyanta vai sutāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Vasiṣṭha’s seven sons were born to Ūrjā: 12

## \|\| 1.10.13 \|\|

*rajo gotrordhvabāhuś ca savanaścānaghas tathā \|*  
*sutapāḥ śukra ityete sarve saptarṣayo 'malāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Rajas, Gotra, Ūrdhvabāhu, Savana, Anagha, Sutapas and Śukra, all of whom were stainless sages. 13

## \|\| 1.10.14 \|\|

*yo 'sāvagnyabhimānī syāt brahmaṇastanayo 'grajaḥ \|*  
*tasmātsvāhā sutāṃllebhe trīnudāraujaso dvija \|\| 14 \|\|*

Brahmā’s first-born son, the fire god Agni, also known as Abhimānin, had three sons of brilliant energy with Svāhā, brahmin: 14

## \|\| 1.10.15 \|\|

*pāvakaṃ pavamānaṃ tu śuciṃ cāpi jalāśinam \|\| 15 \|\|*

Pāvaka, Pavamāna and Śuci who lived on water. 15

## \|\| 1.10.16 \|\|

*teṣāṃ tu santatāvanye catvāriṃśacca pañca ca \|*  
*kathyante vahnayaścaite pitāputratrayaṃ ca yat \|\| 16 \|\|*

Together they had another forty-five descendants. Along with their father and his three sons, they were known together as the Vahnaya fire gods. 16

## \|\| 1.10.17 \|\|

*evamekonapañcaśadvahnayaḥ parikīrtitāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

They say the Vahnayas are forty-nine in all. 17

## \|\| 1.10.18 \|\|

*pitaro brahmaṇā sṛṣṭā vyākhyātā ye mayā dvija \|*  
*āgniṣvātta barhiṣado 'nagnayaḥ sāgnayaś ca ye \|\| 18 \|\|*

These patriarchs whom I’ve described to you, brought forth by Brahmā, include both Agniṣvāttas who maintained no sacrificial fires, and the Barhiṣads, who did. 18

## \|\| 1.10.19 \|\|

*tebhyaḥ svadhā sute jajñe menāṃ vai dhārīṇīṃ tathā \|*  
*te ubhe brahmavādinyau yoginyāvapyubhe dvija \|\| 19 \|\|*

Svadhā bore the Vahnayas two daughters, Menā and Dhāriṇī, brahmin, both ascetics who expounded on the Vedas. 19

## \|\| 1.10.20 \|\|

*uttamajñānasaṃpanne sarvaiḥ samudirtair guṇaiḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

These two were blessed with highest knowledge and every noble virtue. 20

## \|\| 1.10.21 \|\|

*ityeṣā dakṣakanyānāṃ kathitā satyasaṃtatiḥ \|*  
*śraddhāvānsaṃsmarannetāmanapatyo na jāyate \|\| 21 \|\|*

Recalling this account of Dakṣa’s daughters’ offspring, a faithful person never remains childless. 21

## Chapter 11 - The birth of Dhruva

## \|\| 1.11.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*priyavratottānapādau manoḥ svāyaṃbhuvasya tu \|*  
*dvau putrau tu mahāvīryau dharmajñau kathitau tava \|\| 1 \|\|*

Parāśara:

I’ve spoken of Priyavrata and Uttānapāda, Manu Svāyaṃbhuva’s noble and heroic sons. 1

## \|\| 1.11.2 \|\|

*tayoruttānapādasya surucyāmuttamaḥ sutaḥ \|*  
*abhīṣṭāyāmabhūdbrahyanpituratyantavallabhaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

One of these, Uttānapāda, had a favourite wife, Suruci, who had a son, Uttama, on whom his father doted, brahmin. 2

## \|\| 1.11.3 \|\|

*sunītirnāma yā rājñastasyāsīn mahiṣī dvija \|*  
*sa nāti prītimāṃstasyāmabhūdyasyā dhruvaḥ sutaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

King Uttānapāda had a second wife, Sunīti, whom he didn’t love so deeply, but she also bore a son, named Dhruva. 3

## \|\| 1.11.4 \|\|

*rājāsanasthitasyāṅkaṃ piturbhrataramāśritam \|*  
*dṛṣṭvottamaṃ dhruva ścakre tamāroḍhuṃ manoratham \|\| 4 \|\|*

The king was seated on his throne one day when Dhruva saw his brother Uttama nestled in his father’s lap and tried to climb up too. 4

## \|\| 1.11.5 \|\|

*pratyakṣaṃ bhūpatistasyāḥ surucyā nābhyanandata \|*  
*praṇayenāgataṃ putramutsaṃgārohaṇotsukam \|\| 5 \|\|*

Because Uttama’s mother, Suruci, was watching, the king discouraged Dhruva, although the lad had come to him so eagerly. 5

## \|\| 1.11.6 \|\|

*sapatnī tanayaṃ dṛṣṭvā tamaṅkārohaṇotsukam \|*  
*svaputraṃ ca tathārūḍhaṃ surucirvākyamabravīt \|\| 6 \|\|*

When Suruci saw her rival’s son clambering on his father’s lap where Uttama was sitting, she spoke these words to him: 6

## \|\| 1.11.7 \|\|

*kriyate kiṃ vṛthā vatsa mahāneṣa manorathaḥ \|*  
*anyastrīgarbhajātena hy asaṃbhūya mamodare \|\| 7 \|\|*

‘Why hanker after things you cannot have, dear child? Don’t you know that you were born from another mother, not from me? You want a thing that’s just for Uttama, who’s special. 7

## \|\| 1.11.8 \|\|

*uttamottamamaprāpyamaviveko hi vāñchasi \|*  
*satyaṃ sutastvamapy asya kiṃ tu na tvaṃ mayā dhṛtaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

It’s true that you’re a prince also, but you are not my child. 8

## \|\| 1.11.9 \|\|

*etadrājāsanaṃ sarva bhūbhṛtsaṃśrayaketanam \|*  
*yogyaṃ mamaiva putrasya kimātmā kliśyatetvayā \|\| 9 \|\|*

This throne is just for princes like Uttama, my son. Don’t trouble yourself on its account. 9

## \|\| 1.11.10 \|\|

*uccairmanorathasteyaṃ matputrasyeva kiṃ vṛthā \|*  
*sunītyāmātmano janma kiṃ tvayā nāvagamyate \|\| 10 \|\|*

Why have such hopes for things that cannot be, as if you were my boy? Don’t you understand Sunīti is your mother?’ 10

## \|\| 1.11.11 \|\|

*śrīparāśara vāca*  
*utsṛjya pitaraṃ bālastac chrutvā mātṛbhāṣitam \|*  
*jagāma kupito māturnijāyā dvijamandiram \|\| 11 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After Dhruva heard what Uttama’s mother said, brahmin, the angry child then left his father and retreated to his mother’s chambers. 11

## \|\| 1.11.12 \|\|

*taṃ dṛṣṭvā kupitaṃ putramīṣatprasphuritādharam \|*  
*sunītiraṅkamāropya maitreyedamabhāṣata \|\| 12 \|\|*

When Sunīti saw her furious son, his lower lip atremble, Maitreya, she placed him on her lap and spoke these words to him: 12

## \|\| 1.11.13 \|\|

*vatsa kaḥ kopahetuste kaś ca tvāṃ nābhi nandati \|*  
*kovajānāti pitaraṃ vatsa yasteparādhyati \|\| 13 \|\|*

Sunīti:

Who’s made you angry, darling boy? Who’s upset you? Who offends your father by hurting your feelings so? 13

## \|\| 1.11.14 \|\|

*śrīparāśara vāca*  
*ityuktaḥ sakalaṃ mātre kathayāmāsa tadyathā \|*  
*suruciḥ prāha bhūpālapratyakṣamatagarvitā \|\| 14 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, Dhruva told his mother all that proud Suruci had said in the presence of the king. 14

## \|\| 1.11.15 \|\|

*viniśvasyeti kathite tasminputreṇa durmanāḥ \|*  
*śvasakṣāmekṣaṇā dīnā sunītirvākyamabravīt \|\| 15 \|\|*

When her son relayed all this, heavy-hearted Sunīti groaned, her eyes ablaze with angry sighs. The miserable woman made this reply: 15

## \|\| 1.11.16 \|\|

*sunītiruvāca*  
*suruciḥ satyamāhedaṃ mandabhāgyosiḥputraka \|*  
*na hi puṇyavatāṃ vatsa sapatnair eva mucyate \|\| 16 \|\|*

Sunīti:

What Suruci said is true. You’re not so privileged, my son. The rivals of more fortunate folk would never speak like that, dear boy. 16

## \|\| 1.11.17 \|\|

*nodvegastāta kartavyaḥ kṛtaṃ yadbhavatā purā \|*  
*tatkopahartu śaknoti dātuṃ kaścākṛtaṃ tvayā \|\| 17 \|\|*

Don’t be upset, my love. Who can undo the deeds of previous lives, and who can hold you to account for deeds as yet undone? 17

## \|\| 1.11.18 \|\|

*tattvayā nātra kartavyaṃ duḥkhaṃ tadvākyasambhavam \|\| 18 \|\|*

One who’s blessed with a royal throne and umbrella, excellent horses and fine elephants—these are his alone. Remember this and calm yourself, my boy. 18

## \|\| 1.11.19 \|\|

*rājāsanaṃ rājacchatraṃ varāścāvaravāraṇāḥ \|*  
*yasya puṇyāni tasyaite matvaitacchāṃmya putraka \|\| 19 \|\|*

On account of merit earned in former lifetimes, the king takes great delight (suruci) in Suruci, but others like me who are not so lucky are his wives in name alone. 19

## \|\| 1.11.20 \|\|

*anyajanmakṛtaiḥ puṇyaiḥ surucyā surucirnṛpaḥ \|*  
*bhāryeti procyate cānyāṃ madvidhā puṇyavarjitā \|\| 20 \|\|*

Her son Uttama is blessed with heaps of merit, but you, my son, Dhruva, were not born to privilege. 20

## \|\| 1.11.21 \|\|

*puṇyopacayasaṃpannastasyāḥ putrastathottamaḥ \|*  
*mama putras tathā jātaḥ svalpapuṇyo dhruvo bhavān \|\| 21 \|\|*

But don’t be sad, my boy. The wise are always happy with their lot. 21

## \|\| 1.11.22 \|\|

*tathāpi duḥkhaṃ na bhavān kartumarhasi putraka \|*  
*yasya yāvatsa tenaiva svena tuṣyati mānavaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

If you’re really pained by what Suruci said, then try to win some merit, which will bring you all rewards. 22

## \|\| 1.11.23 \|\|

*yadi te duḥkhamatyarthaṃ surucyā vacasābhavat \|*  
*tatpuṇyopacaye yatnaṃ kuru sarvaphalaprade \|\| 23 \|\|*

Be polite, virtuous, friendly and kind to animals, for just as water flows downhill, good fortune comes to those who earn it. 23

## \|\| 1.11.24 \|\|

*suśīlo bhava dharmātmā maitraḥ prāṇihite rataḥ \|*  
*nimnaṃ yathāpaḥ pravaṇāḥ pātramāyānti sampadaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Dhruva:

Mother, the words you’ve spoken to comfort me find no place within my heart, which was broken by her insults. 24

## \|\| 1.11.25 \|\|

*dhruva uvāca*  
*amba yattvamidaṃ prāttha praśamāya vaco mama \|*  
*naitaddurvācasā bhinne hṛdaye mama tiṣṭhati \|\| 25 \|\|*

I’ll strive to reach that highest, peerless state, honoured by all the worlds. 25

## \|\| 1.11.26 \|\|

*so 'haṃ tathā yatiṣyāmi yathā sarvottamottamam \|*  
*sthānaṃ prāpsyāmyaśoṣāṇāṃ jagatāmabhi pūjitam \|\| 26 \|\|*

Suruci is the royal favourite and, although I’m not her son, you’ll see my glory, mother, even though I lay inside your womb. 26

## \|\| 1.11.27 \|\|

*surucirdayitā rājñastasyā jātosmi nodarāt \|*  
*prabhāvaṃ paśya meṃba tvaṃ vṛddhasyāpi tavodare \|\| 27 \|\|*

She bore my brother Uttama in her belly, so let him have the throne our father gave him. That’s how matters ought to be. 27

## \|\| 1.11.28 \|\|

*uttamaḥ sa mama bhrātā yo garbheṇa dhṛtastayā \|*  
*sa rājāsanamāpnotu pitrā dattaṃ tathāstu tat \|\| 28 \|\|*

I don’t desire a place that’s promised to another. Through my efforts, mother, I want a state that even father hasn’t reached. 28

## \|\| 1.11.29 \|\|

*nānyadattamabhīpsyāmi stānamamba svakarmaṇā \|*  
*icchāmi tadahaṃ sthānaṃ yatra prāpa pitā mama \|\| 29 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, Dhruva left his mother’s rooms and left the city for a grove beyond the walls. 29

## \|\| 1.11.30 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*nirjagāma gṛhānmāturityuktvā mātaraṃ dhruvaḥ \|*  
*purācca nirgamya tatastadbāhyopavanaṃ yayo \|\| 30 \|\|*

There he saw the Seven Sages, who’d arrived before him, seated on their deer-skin cloaks spread upon the ground. 30

## \|\| 1.11.31 \|\|

*sa dadarśa munīṃs tatra saptapūrvāgatāndhruvaḥ \|*  
*kuṣmājinottarīyeṣu viṣṭareṣu samāsthitān \|\| 31 \|\|*

The prince duly greeted them, bowed respectfully and, while stooping with humility, addressed them. 31

## \|\| 1.11.32 \|\|

*sa rājaputrastānsarvānpraṇipatyābhya bhāṣata \|*  
*praśrayāvanataḥ samyagabhivādanapūrvakam \|\| 32 \|\|*

Dhruva:

Best of sages, know me to be Uttānapāda’s son, born to Sunīti. I’ve come to you because I’m disenchanted with the world. 32

## \|\| 1.11.33 \|\|

*dhruva uvāca*  
*uttānapādatanayaṃ māṃ nibodhata sattamāḥ \|*  
*jātaṃ sunītyāṃ nirvedādyuṣmākaṃ prāptamantikam \|\| 33 \|\|*

The sages:

Prince, you’re just a boy of four or five. There cannot yet be any reason for your disillusion. 33

## \|\| 1.11.34 \|\|

*ṛṣaya ūcuḥ*  
*catuḥpañcā bdasaṃbhūto bālastvaṃ tṛpanandana \|*  
*nirvedakāraṇaṃ kiñcittava nādyāpi vartate \|\| 34 \|\|*

You’ve no cause for worry while your royal father lives. We can’t foresee that you will want for anything, dear boy. 34

## \|\| 1.11.35 \|\|

*na cintyaṃ bhavataḥ kiñciddhrayate bhūpatiḥ pitā \|*  
*na caivaiṣṭa viyogādi tava paśyāma bālaka \|\| 35 \|\|*

You don’t look sick. What’s causing your unhappiness? If there’s a problem, you should tell us. 35

## \|\| 1.11.36 \|\|

*śarīre na ca te vyādhirasmābhir upalakṣyate \|*  
*nirvedaḥ kinnimittaste kathyatāṃ yadi vidyate \|\| 36 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Dhruva then repeated Surūci’s words and, hearing this, the sages said to one another, 36

## \|\| 1.11.37 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tataḥ sa kathayāmāsa surucyā yadudāhṛtam \|*  
*tanniśamya tataḥ procurmunayaste parasparam \|\| 37 \|\|*

‘Aha! Even though he’s just a child, he’s got real warrior spirit. His irritation at his mother’s rival’s words hasn’t left his heart. 37

## \|\| 1.11.38 \|\|

*aho kṣātraṃ paraṃ tejo bālasyāpi yadakṣamā \|*  
*sapatnyā māturuktaṃ yaddhṛdayānnāpasarpati \|\| 38 \|\|*

Come, come, you warrior’s son. Tell us, if you like, what you plan to do on account of disenchantment. 38

## \|\| 1.11.39 \|\|

*bhobhoḥ kṣatriyadāyādaḥ nirvedādyattvāyādhunā \|*  
*kartuṃ vyavasitaṃ tannaḥ kathyatāṃ yadi rocate \|\| 39 \|\|*

Tell us what we can do to help. You’re clearly very bright, and we see you have something that you want to say.’ 39

## \|\| 1.11.40 \|\|

*yacca kāryaṃ tavāsmābhiḥ sāhāyyamamitadyute \|*  
*taducyatāṃ vivakṣustvamasmābhir upalakṣyase \|\| 40 \|\|*

Dhruva:

I don’t want riches or a kingdom, best of brahmins. I only want to know that state that none has reached before. 40

## \|\| 1.11.41 \|\|

*dhruva uvāca*  
*nāha marthamabhīpsāmi na rājyaṃ dvijasattamāḥ \|*  
*tatsthānamekamicchāmi bhuktaṃ nānyena yatpurā \|\| 41 \|\|*

Tell me, best of sages, what I must do to reach that situation, which is superior to all others. 41

## \|\| 1.11.42 \|\|

*etanme kriyatāṃ samyakkathyatāṃ prāpyate yathā \|*  
*sthānamagryaṃ samastebhyaḥ sthānebhyo munisattamāḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

That foremost state cannot be reached by those who do not praise Govinda. You must therefore venerate the everlasting one. 42

## \|\| 1.11.43 \|\|

*marīciruvāca*  
*anārādhitagovindair naraiḥ sthānaṃ nṛpātmaja \|*  
*na hi saṃprāpyate śreṣṭhaṃ tasmādārādhayācyutam \|\| 43 \|\|*

One with whom Janārdana, highest of the high, the primal spirit, is pleased, will reach the everlasting state. I speak the truth. 43

## \|\| 1.11.44 \|\|

*atriruvāca*  
*paraḥ parāṇāṃ puruṣo yasya tuṣṭo janārdanaḥ \|*  
*saṃprāpnotyakṣayaṃ sthānametatsatyaṃ mayoditam \|\| 44 \|\|*

Worship eternal, changeless Govinda, within whom all this world abides, if you wish to reach that highest situation. 44

## \|\| 1.11.45 \|\|

*aṅgirā uvāca*  
*yasyāntaḥ sarvamevedamacyutasyāvyayātmanaḥ \|*  
*tamārādhaya govindaṃ sthānamagryaṃ yadīcchasi \|\| 45 \|\|*

A person who has venerated Hari, the highest Absolute, the supreme abode, who is both supreme and Absolute, reaches liberation, even though it’s difficult to attain. 45

## \|\| 1.11.46 \|\|

*pulastya uvāca*  
*paraṃ brahma paraṃ dhāma yo 'sau brahma tathā param \|*  
*tamārādhya hariṃ yāti muktimapyati durlabhām \|\| 46 \|\|*

Janārdana is the spirit of the sacrifice when a ritual is performed, and the highest spirit reached through yoga practice. When he is pleased, what is unattainable? 46

## \|\| 1.11.47 \|\|

*pulaha uvāca*  
*aindramindraḥ paraṃ sthānaṃ yamārādhya jagatpatim \|*  
*prāpa yajñapatiṃ viṣṇuṃ tamārādhaya suvrata \|\| 47 \|\|*

After Indra praised the universal lord, he attained the highest state of sovereignty. You must also worship Viṣṇu, lord of sacrifices, you who are loyal to your vows. 47

## \|\| 1.11.48 \|\|

*kraturuvāca*  
*yo yajñapuruṣo yajño yogeśaḥ paramaḥ pumān \|*  
*tasmiṃstuṣṭe yadaprāpyaṃ kintadasti janārdane \|\| 48 \|\|*

When Viṣṇu is venerated, you’ll obtain your heart’s desires, dear boy, even that state that is higher than the highest in all three worlds. 48

## \|\| 1.11.49 \|\|

*vasiṣṭha uvāca*  
*prāpnoṣyārādhite viṣṇau manasā yadyadicchati \|*  
*trelokyāntargataṃ sthānaṃ kimu vatsottamottamam \|\| 49 \|\|*

Dhruva:

As I bow before you, you’ve told me that the deity must be worshipped. Now tell me which prayer I should offer to gratify that being, 49

## \|\| 1.11.50 \|\|

*dhruva uvāca*  
*ārādhyaḥ kathito devo bhavadbhiḥ praṇatasya me \|*  
*mayā tatparītoṣāya yajjaptavyaṃ taducyatām \|\| 50 \|\|*

And tell me how to venerate the greatest lord, kind and gracious sages. 50

## \|\| 1.11.51 \|\|

*yathā cārādhanaṃ tasya mayā kāryaṃ mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*prasādasumukhāstanme kathayantu maharṣayaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

The sages:

Prince, you deserve to hear from us exactly how the praise of Viṣṇu is undertaken by those who are devoted to it. 51

## \|\| 1.11.52 \|\|

*ṛṣaya ūcuḥ*  
*rājaputra yathā viṣṇorārādhanaparair naraiḥ \|*  
*kāryamārādhanaṃ tanno yathāvacchrotumarhasi \|\| 52 \|\|*
