First, you should rid your mind of all external worries. Next, you should fix your thoughts on the god in whom the world abides. 52

## \|\| 1.11.53 \|\|

*bāhyārthādakhilāccittaṃ tyājayetprathamaṃ naraḥ \|*  
*tasminneva jagaddhāmni tataḥ kurvita niścalam \|\| 53 \|\|*

When your mind is focused on that single entity, conscious of the fact that you consist of the deity and holding yourself firm, prince, you should then recite this mantra that you’ll hear from us: 53

## \|\| 1.11.54 \|\|

*evamekāgracittena tanmayena dhṛtātmanā \|*  
*japtavyaṃ yanni bodhaitattanraḥ pārthivanandana \|\| 54 \|\|*

‘Oṃ. I bow to Vāsudeva, who takes the forms of Brahmā born from the golden egg, Spirit, Matter and the Unseen element, and whose essential nature is pure knowledge.’ 54

## \|\| 1.11.55 \|\|

*hiraṇyagarbhapuruṣapradhānavyaktarūpiṇe \|*  
*oṃ namo vāsudevāya śuddhajñānasvarūpime \|\| 55 \|\|*

Because Manu, Lord Svāyaṃbhuva, your grandsire, recited this mantra in former times, Janārdana was gratified by him. 55

## \|\| 1.11.56 \|\|

*etajjajāpa bhagavān japyaṃ svāyaṃbhuvo manuḥ \|*  
*pitāmahastava purā tasya tuṣṭo janārdanaḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

The deity granted him all the wealth that he desired, unequalled in all three worlds. You should also please Govinda with continual recitation of this mantra. 56

## Chapter 12 - The trials of Dhruva

## \|\| 1.12.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*niśamyaitadaśeṣeṇa maitreya nṛpate sutaḥ \|*  
*nirjagāma vanāttasmātpraṇipatya sa tānṛṣīn \|\| 1 \|\|*

Hearing this, Maitreya, the prince bowed deeply to the sages and departed from the forest. 1

## \|\| 1.12.2 \|\|

*kṛtakṛtyamivātmānaṃ manyamānastato dvija \|*  
*madhusaṃjñaṃ mahāpuṇyaṃ jagāma yamunātaṭam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Thinking what he had to do, brahmin, Dhruva proceeded to the sacred ford called Madhu on the Yamunā River’s banks. 2

## \|\| 1.12.3 \|\|

*punaś ca madhusaṃjñena daityenādhiṣṭhitaṃ yataḥ \|*  
*tato madhuvanaṃ nāmnā khyātamatra mahītale \|\| 3 \|\|*

It’s known in all the world as Madhu’s Forest, because a Daitya of that name had once resided there. 3

## \|\| 1.12.4 \|\|

*hatvā ca lavaṇaṃ rakṣo madhuputraṃ mahābalam \|*  
*śatrughno madhurāṃ nāma purīṃ yatra cakāra vai \|\| 4 \|\|*

It’s where Śatrughna slew Madhu’s son, the mighty rākṣasa Lavaṇa, and built the city known as Madhurā. 4

## \|\| 1.12.5 \|\|

*yatra vai devadevasya sānnidhyaṃ harimedhasaḥ \|*  
*sarvapāpahare tasmiṃstapastīrthe cakāra saḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

At that sacred ford, which removes all sins, Dhruva undertook austerities in Hari’s presence, god of gods. 5

## \|\| 1.12.6 \|\|

*marīcimukhyair munibhir yathoddiṣṭamabhūttathā \|*  
*ātmanyaśepadeveśaṃ sthitaṃ viṣṇumamanyata \|\| 6 \|\|*

As instructed by Marīci and the other sages, Dhruva contemplated Viṣṇu, lord of all the deities, abiding in his heart. 6

## \|\| 1.12.7 \|\|

*ananyacetasastasya dhyāyato bhagavānhariḥ \|*  
*sarvabhūtagato vipra sarvabhāvagato 'bhavat \|\| 7 \|\|*

While Dhruva set his mind on nothing else, brahmin, Lord Hari, who occupies all creation, pervaded Dhruva’s being. 7

## \|\| 1.12.8 \|\|

*manasyavasthite tasminviṣṇau maitreya yoginaḥ \|*  
*na śaśāka dharā bhāramudvoḍhuṃ bhūtadhāriṇī \|\| 8 \|\|*

With Viṣṇu dwelling in his ascetic heart, Maitreya, the earth, support of all creation, could no longer bear its burden. 8

## \|\| 1.12.9 \|\|

*vāmapādasthite tasminnanāmārdhena medinī \|*  
*dvitīyaṃ ca nanāmārdhaṃ kṣiterdakṣiṇataḥ sthite \|\| 9 \|\|*

When Dhruva stood on his left foot, half the world sank beneath him, and when he stood on his right foot, the other half then sank as well. 9

## \|\| 1.12.10 \|\|

*pādāṃsuṣṭhena saṃpīḍya yathā sa vasudhāṃ sthitaḥ \|*  
*tadā samastā vasudhā cacāla saha parvataiḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

When he stood on just one toe, brahmin, the whole world with all its mountains shook. 10

## \|\| 1.12.11 \|\|

*nadyo nadāḥ samudrāś ca saṃkṣobhaṃ paramaṃ yayuḥ \|*  
*tatkṣobhādamarāḥ kṣobhaṃ paraṃ jagmurmahāmune \|\| 11 \|\|*

The rivers and oceans trembled, great sage, and the gods were deeply troubled. 11

## \|\| 1.12.12 \|\|

*yāmā nāma tadā devā maitreya paramākulāḥ \|*  
*indreṇa saha saṃmantrya dhyānabhaṅgaṃ pracakramuḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

The deities known as Yāmas were most alarmed, Maitreya, and, having consulted Indra, set out to disrupt Dhruva’s meditation. 12

## \|\| 1.12.13 \|\|

*kūṣmāṇḍā vividhai rūpair mahendreṇa mahāmune \|*  
*samādhibhaṅgamatyantamārabdhāḥ kartumāturāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

The anxious Kūṣmāṇḍa demigods, taking various forms and accompanied by their king, great sage, also tried to break his concentration. 13

## \|\| 1.12.14 \|\|

*sunītirnāma tanmātā sāstrā tatpurataḥ sthitā \|*  
*putreti karuṇāṃ vācamāha māyāmayī tadā \|\| 14 \|\|*

They created an illusion of Sunīti Dhruva’s mother, who stood in tears before him, weeping piteously, 14

## \|\| 1.12.15 \|\|

*putrakāsmānnivartasva śarīrātyayadāruṇāt \|*  
*nirbandhato mayā labdho bahubhis tvaṃ manorathai \|\| 15 \|\|*

‘Son, son, stop this cruel penance at the cost of your body. I only conceived you by persevering with much hope. 15

## \|\| 1.12.16 \|\|

*dīnāmekāṃ parityaktumanāthāṃ na tvamarhasi \|*  
*sapatnīvacanādvatsa agatestvaṃ gatirmama \|\| 16 \|\|*

Don’t forsake me—miserable, alone and vulnerable—because of something that my rival said, my dear. You’re my refuge and I have no other. 16

## \|\| 1.12.17 \|\|

*kva ca tvaṃ pañcavarṣīyaḥ kvacaitaddārūṇaṃ tapaḥ \|*  
*nivartatāṃ manaḥ kaṣṭānnirbandhātphalavarjitāt \|\| 17 \|\|*

You’re only five years old, and these are terrible austerities. Turn your mind away from this foolish, fruitless whim. 17

## \|\| 1.12.18 \|\|

*kālaḥ krīḍanakānānte tadante 'dhyayanasya te \|*  
*tataḥ samastabhogānāṃ tadante ceṣyate tapaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Now’s the time for toys, and later you can go to school. Then comes the age for lots of fun. Only after that comes penance. 18

## \|\| 1.12.19 \|\|

*kālaḥ krīḍanakānāṃ yastava bālasya putraka \|*  
*tasmiṃstvamicchasi tapaḥ kiṃ nāśāyātmano rataḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

Now’s the time for toys for a child like you, son, but you’re wasting it in austerities. How could you enjoy such self-destruction? 19

## \|\| 1.12.20 \|\|

*matprītiḥ paramo dharmo vayovasthākriyākramam \|*  
*anuvartasva mā mohānnivartāsmādadharmataḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

Your first duty is to be nice to me. Life’s a series of stages and of duties. Don’t follow this delusion. Stop this nonsense. 20

## \|\| 1.12.21 \|\|

*parityajati vatsādhya yadhyetanna bhavāṃstapaḥ \|*  
*tyakṣyāmyahamiha prāṇāṃstato vai paśyatastava \|\| 21 \|\|*

If you don’t give up austerities now, my dear, I’ll kill myself before your very eyes!’ 21

## \|\| 1.12.22 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tāṃ pralāpavatīmevaṃ bāṣpākulavilocanām \|*  
*samāhitamanā viṣṇau paśyannapi na dṛṣṭavān \|\| 22 \|\|*

Parāśara:

But Dhruva’s mind was fixed on Viṣṇu, and, even though his eyes were open, he didn’t see her tearful lamentations. 22

## \|\| 1.12.23 \|\|

*vatsa vatsa sughorāṇi rakṣāṃsyetāni bhīṣaṇe \|*  
*vane 'bhyudyataśastrāṇīḥ samāyāntyapagamyatām \|\| 23 \|\|*

‘Son, son! Frightful demons wielding weapons are gathering in this awful forest. We must flee at once!’ 23

## \|\| 1.12.24 \|\|

*ityuktvā prayayau sātha rakṣāṃsyāvirbabhustataḥ \|*  
*abhyudyatograśastrāṇi jvālāmālākulair mukhaiḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

With these words, she ran away and, at that very moment, rākṣasas appeared, holding frightful weapons and belching wreaths of flame. 24

## \|\| 1.12.25 \|\|

*tato nādānatīvogranrājaputrasya te puraḥ \|*  
*mumucurdīptaśastrāṇi bhrāmayanto niśācarāḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Those night-ranging demons roared their terrible roars before the prince and waved their glinting weapons. 25

## \|\| 1.12.26 \|\|

*śivāś ca śataśo neduḥ sajvālāḥ kavalair mukhaiḥ \|*  
*trāsāya tasya bālasya yogayuktasya sarvadā \|\| 26 \|\|*

Jackals by the hundred with flaming maws howled to terrify the boy, but he remained most perfectly absorbed in yoga. 26

## \|\| 1.12.27 \|\|

*hanyatāṃ danyatāmeṣa chidyatāṃ chidyatāmayam \|*  
*bhakṣyatāṃ bhakṣyatāṃ cāya mityucuste niśācarāḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

‘Kill him, kill him! Chop him up, chop him up! Eat him, eat him!’ cried those demons of the night. 27

## \|\| 1.12.28 \|\|

*tato nānāvidhānnādān siṃhoṣṭramakarānanāḥ \|*  
*trāsāya rājaputrasya neduste rajanīcarāḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Rākṣasas with heads of lions, camels and crocodiles made all sorts of cries to frighten that young prince. 28

## \|\| 1.12.29 \|\|

*rakṣāṃsi tāni te nādāḥ śivāstānyāyudhāni ca \|*  
*govindāsaktacittasya yayurnandriyagocaram \|\| 29 \|\|*

But neither demons, roars, nor jackals, nor the weapons impinged on Dhruva’s senses, as his mind was fixed on Govinda alone. 29

## \|\| 1.12.30 \|\|

*ekāgracetāḥ satataṃ viṣṇumevātmasaṃśrayam \|*  
*dṛṣṭavān pṛthivīnāthaputro nānyaṃ kathañcana \|\| 30 \|\|*

With singular concentration, the prince perceived nothing other than Viṣṇu himself abiding in his heart. 30

## \|\| 1.12.31 \|\|

*tataḥ sarvāsu māyāsu vilīnāsu punaḥ surāḥ \|*  
*saṃkṣobhaṃ paramaṃ jagmustatparābhavaśaṅkitāḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

When all these phantoms disappeared, the deities, alarmed by failure, grew even more concerned. 31

## \|\| 1.12.32 \|\|

*te sametya jagadyonimanādinidhanaṃ harim \|*  
*śaraṇyaṃ śaraṇaṃ yātāstapasā tasya tāpitāḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

Worried by Dhruva’s austerities, they went in a group to Hari, the beginningless and endless origin of the world and refuge of all tormented beings who seek it. 32

## \|\| 1.12.33 \|\|

*devā ūcuḥ*  
*devadeva jagannātha pareśa puruṣottama \|*  
*dhruvasya tapasā taptāstvāṃ vayaṃ śaraṇaṃ gatāḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

The deities:

God of gods, protector of the world, highest lord and ultimate spirit: we’re worried by Dhruva’s austerities and have come for your protection. 33

## \|\| 1.12.34 \|\|

*dine dine kalāleśaiḥ śaśāṅka pūryate yathā \|*  
*tathāyaṃ tapasā deva prayātyṛddhim aharniṃśam \|\| 34 \|\|*

Just as the hare-marked moon waxes day by day, so do his austerities increase his power, Lord. 34

## \|\| 1.12.35 \|\|

*autānapāditapasā vayamitthaṃ janārdana \|*  
*bhītāstvāṃ śaraṇaṃ yātāstapasastaṃ nivartaya \|\| 35 \|\|*

We fear the practices of Uttānapāda’s son and come to you for refuge, Janārdana. Please make him stop. 35

## \|\| 1.12.36 \|\|

*navidmaḥ kiṃ sa śakratvaṃ sūryatvaṃ kimabhīpsati \|*  
*vittapāmbupasomānāṃ sābhilāṣaḥ padeṣu kim \|\| 36 \|\|*

We do not understand his purpose—to be like Indra or the sun? Does he want to be the equal of the god of wealth, the lord of oceans or the moon? 36

## \|\| 1.12.37 \|\|

*tadasmākaṃ prasīdeśa hṛdayācchalyamuddhara \|*  
*uttānapādatanayaṃ tapasaḥ sannivartaya \|\| 37 \|\|*

Favour us, Lord, take this thorn from our hearts and cause Uttānapāda’s son to abandon his austerities. 37

## \|\| 1.12.38 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*nendratvaṃ na ca sūryatvaṃ naivāmbupadhaneśatām \|*  
*prārthayatyeṣa yaṃ kāmaṃ taṃ karomyakhilaṃ surā \|\| 38 \|\|*

The Lord:

Dhruva has no desire to be like Indra, the sun, Kubera or Varuṇa, gods, and I’ll grant his wish in full. 38

## \|\| 1.12.39 \|\|

*yāta devā yathākāma svasthānaṃ vigatajvarāḥ \|*  
*nivartayāmyahaṃ bālaṃ tapasyāsaktamānasam \|\| 39 \|\|*

Return to your own realms at leisure, gods, but do not worry, as I’ll cause that boy whose heart is set on austerities to desist. 39

## \|\| 1.12.40 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktā devadevena praṇamya tridaśāstataḥ \|*  
*prayayuḥ svāni dhiṣṇyāni śatakratupurogamāḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus addressed by the god of gods, the thirteen deities bowed and set out for their respective realms, led by Indra, deity of a hundred sacrifices. 40

## \|\| 1.12.41 \|\|

*bhagavānapi sarvātmā tanmayatvena toṣitaḥ \|*  
*gatvā dhruvamuvācedaṃ caturbhujavapur hariḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

Then Lord Hari, heart of all, pleased that Dhruva was absorbed in him, appearing to the boy in four-armed form, spoke these words: 41

## \|\| 1.12.42 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*auttānapāde bhadraṃ te tapasā paritoṣitaḥ \|*  
*varadohamanuprāpto varaṃ varaya suvrata \|\| 42 \|\|*

The Glorious Lord:

Well done, son of Uttānapāda! I’m pleased with your austerities, and I’ve come to grant your wish. Ask for anything you desire, as you’ve upheld your vows. 42

## \|\| 1.12.43 \|\|

*brāhyārthanirapekṣaṃ te mayi cittaṃ yadā hitam \|*  
*tuṣṭohaṃ bhavatastena tadvṛṇīṣva varaṃ param \|\| 43 \|\|*

I’m pleased with you because your mind is set on me alone, excluding other matters. You may therefore ask your dearest wish. 43

## \|\| 1.12.44 \|\|

*parāśara uvāca*  
*śrutvetthaṃ gaditaṃ tasya devadevasya bālakaḥ \|*  
*unmīlitākṣo dadṛśe dhyānadṛṣṭaṃ hariṃ puraḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing these words spoken by the god of gods, the boy opened his eyes and beheld Hari before him, just as he’d perceived him in his meditation. 44

## \|\| 1.12.45 \|\|

*śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgavarāsidharamacyutam \|*  
*kirīṭinaṃ samālokya jagāma śirasā mahīm \|\| 45 \|\|*

Seeing the imperishable being with discus, conch, mace, bow and brilliant sword, and wearing a diadem on his head, Dhruva pressed his forehead to the ground. 45

## \|\| 1.12.46 \|\|

*romāñcitāṅgaḥ sahasā sādhvasaṃ paramaṃ gataḥ \|*  
*stavāya devadevasya sa cakre mānasaṃ dhruvaḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

The hairs on the prince’s limbs arose at once and, filled with awe, he set his heart on praising the god of gods. 46

## \|\| 1.12.47 \|\|

*kiṃ vadāmi stutāvasya kenoktenāsya saṃstutiḥ \|*  
*ityākula matirdevaṃ tameva śaraṇaṃ yayau \|\| 47 \|\|*

‘What should I say to praise him? What words can express my adoration?’ Such thoughts filled Dhruva’s mind as he took refuge in the deity. 47

## \|\| 1.12.48 \|\|

*dhruva uvāca*  
*bhagavanyadi me toṣaṃ tapasā paramaṃ gataḥ \|*  
*stotuṃ tadahamicchāmi varamenaṃ prayaccha me \|\| 48 \|\|*

Dhruva:

Lord, if you’re truly pleased with my austerities, then grant my wish: I only want to praise you. 48

## \|\| 1.12.49 \|\|

*(brahmādyauryasya vedajñair jñāyate yasya no gatiḥ \|*  
*taṃ tvāṃ kathamahaṃ deva stotuṃ śakromi bālakaḥ \|*  
*tvadbhaktipravaṇaṃ hyetatparameśvara me manaḥ \|*  
*stotuṃ pravṛttaṃ tvatpādau tatra prajñāṃ prayacca me)*  
*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*śaṅkhaprāntena govindastaṃ pasparśa kṛtāñjalim \|*  
*uttānapādatanayaṃ dvijavarya jagatpatiḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

But how can a child like me adore you, Lord, whose state is unknown even to Brahmā and to those who comprehend the Vedas? 49

## \|\| 1.12.50 \|\|

*atha prasannavadanaḥ sa kṣaṇānnṛpanandanaḥ \|*  
*tuṣṭāva praṇato bhūtvā bhūtadhātāramacyutam \|\| 50 \|\|*

This heart of mine is filled with faith in you, highest Lord, and is ready to worship at your feet; only grant me wisdom to do so. 50

## \|\| 1.12.51 \|\|

*dhruva uvāca*  
*bhūmirāponalo vāyuḥ khaṃ mano buddhireva ca \|*  
*bhūtādirādi prakṛtiryasya rūpaṃ natosmi tam \|\| 51 \|\|*

Parāśara:

With the tip of his conch shell, the universal Lord Govinda touched the son of Uttānapāda, best of brahmins, whose palms were joined respectfully. 51

## \|\| 1.12.52 \|\|

*śuddhaḥ sūkṣmokhilavyāpī pradhānātparataḥ pumān \|*  
*yasya rūpaṃ namastasmai puruṣāya guṇātmane \|\| 52 \|\|*

The prince’s face began at once to shine and, bowing with devotion, he praised the eternal basis of all creation. 52

## \|\| 1.12.53 \|\|

*bhūtādīnāṃ samastānāṃ gandhādīnāṃ ca śāśvataḥ \|*  
*budhyādīnāṃ pradhānasya puruṣasya ca yaḥ paraḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

Dhruva:

I bow to him who takes the form of earth, water, fire, wind, sky, mind, intellect, primal elements and matter. 53

## \|\| 1.12.54 \|\|

*taṃ brahmabhūtamātmānamaśeṣajagataḥ patim \|*  
*prapadye śaraṇaṃ śuddhaṃ tvadrūpaṃ parameśvara \|\| 54 \|\|*

I bow to him whose form is pure, subtle and all-pervading; the spirit which transcends elemental substance; the spirit at the heart of every quality; 54

## \|\| 1.12.55 \|\|

*bṛhattvādbṛṃhaṇatvācca yadrūpaṃ brahmasaṃjñitam \|*  
*tasmai namaste sarvātmanyogicintyāvikāriṇe \|\| 55 \|\|*

The eternal one who lies beyond creation, starting with the earth, the sense-objects starting with scent, the senses starting with intellect and spirit as well as matter. 55

## \|\| 1.12.56 \|\|

*sahasraśīrṣā puruṣaḥ sahasrākṣaḥ sahasrapāt \|*  
*sarvavyāpī bhuvaḥ sparśādatyatiṣṭhaddaśāṅgulam \|\| 56 \|\|*

I take refuge in that pure form of yours, which is the Absolute, creation itself, supreme Lord, and master of the universe. 56

## \|\| 1.12.57 \|\|

*yadbhūtaṃ yacca vai bhavyaṃ purūṣottama tadbhavān \|*  
*tvattau virāṭ svarāṭ samrāṭ tvattaścapyadhipūruṣaḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

I bow to that changeless form contemplated by ascetics and known as the Absolute because of its greatness and because it shapes the world—you who are at the heart of everything. 57

## \|\| 1.12.58 \|\|

*atyaricyata sodhaś ca tiryagūrdhvaṃ ca vai bhuvaḥ \|*  
*tvatto viśvamidaṃ jātaṃ tvattau bhūtaṃ bhaviṣyati \|\| 58 \|\|*

The all-pervasive being with a thousand heads, a thousand eyes and a thousand feet extends ten inches beyond the furthest limit of the universe. 58

## \|\| 1.12.59 \|\|

*tvadrūpadhāriṇaścāntaḥ sarvabhūtamidaṃ jagat \|*  
*tvatto yajñaḥ sarvahutaḥ pṛṣadājyaṃ paśurdvidhā \|\| 59 \|\|*

You, supreme spirit, are all that was and all that is to be. From you arise Virāj, Svarāj, Samrāj, and from you arises Adhipūruṣa. 59

## \|\| 1.12.60 \|\|

*tvatto ṛcotha sāmāni tvattaśchandāṃsi jajñire \|*  
*tvatto yajūṃṣyajāyanta tvattośvāścaikato dataḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

You extend above, below and beyond the universe. All this is born from you, as is all that has existed and all that’s still to come. 60

## \|\| 1.12.61 \|\|

*gāvastvattaḥ samudbhūtāstva ttojā avayo mṛgāḥ \|*  
*tvamanmukhādbrāhmaṇā bāhvos tvatto kṣatramajāyata \|\| 61 \|\|*

This whole world is in you when you take this form. The sacrifice with all the offerings, the clarified butter spotted with curds and both kinds of sacrificial animals arose from you. 61

## \|\| 1.12.62 \|\|

*vaiśvāstavorujāḥ śūdrāstava padbhyāṃ samudgatāḥ \|*  
*ākṣṇoḥ sūryo 'nilaḥ prāṇāccandramā manasastava \|\| 62 \|\|*

The Ṛg and Sāma Vedas arose from you, the Vedic metres were born from you, and from you the Yajur Veda came. From you arose the horses and those animals which have no teeth in their upper jaw. 62

## \|\| 1.12.63 \|\|

*prāṇontaḥ muṣirājjāto mukhādagnirajāyata \|*  
*nābhito gaganaṃ dyauś ca śirasaḥ samavartata \|\| 63 \|\|*

Cattle arose from you, and from you arose the goats, the sheep and deer. From your mouth arose the brahmins, and from your arms the kṣatriyas. 63

## \|\| 1.12.64 \|\|

*diśaḥ śrotrātkṣitiḥ padbhyāṃ tvattaḥ sarvamabhūdidim \|\| 64 \|\|*

The vaiśyas were born from your thighs, and the śūdras from your feet. The sun arose from your eyes, the wind from your breath and the moon from your mind. 64

## \|\| 1.12.65 \|\|

*nyagrodhaḥ sumahānalpe yathā bīje vyavasthitaḥ \|*  
*saṃyame visvamakhilaṃ bījabhūte tathātvāyi \|\| 65 \|\|*

Breath was born from your inner channel and fire arose from your mouth. From your navel came the sky, and heaven was born from your head. The ten directions arose from your ears, the earth was born of your feet. All this arose from you! 65

## \|\| 1.12.66 \|\|

*bījādaṅkurasambhūte nyagrodhastu samutthitaḥ \|*  
*vistāraṃ ca yathā yāti tvattaḥ sṛṣṭau tathā jagat \|\| 66 \|\|*

Just as a mighty banyan tree is inherent in a tiny seed, at the time of its destruction, all the world inheres in you in that same way. 66

## \|\| 1.12.67 \|\|

*yathā hi kadalī nānyā tvakpatrādapi dṛsyate \|*  
*evaṃ viśvasya nānyastvaṃ tvasthayīśvara dṛśyeta \|\| 67 \|\|*

Just as a banyan sprouts from that seed and grows both tall and wide, so, too, does the world that you emit. 67

## \|\| 1.12.68 \|\|

*hlādinī saṃdhinī saṃvittvayyekā sarvasaṃsthitau \|*  
*hlādatāpakarī miśrā tvayi no guṇavarjite \|\| 68 \|\|*

Just as a banana palm has no reality distinct from its trunk and leaves, in the same way, protector, Lord, you are no different from the universe that is seen to abide in you. 68

## \|\| 1.12.69 \|\|

*pṛthagbhūtaikabhūtāya bhūtabhūtāya te namaḥ \|*  
*prabhūtabhūtabhūtāya tubhyaṃ bhūtātmane namaḥ \|\| 69 \|\|*

Unified consciousness, the source of joy, dwells as one with all within you, but the individual origins of pain and pleasure do not exist in you, as you are free from all these qualities. 69

## \|\| 1.12.70 \|\|

*vyaktaṃ pradhānapuruṣau virāṭ samrāṭ svarāṭ tathā \|*  
*vibhāvyatentaḥkaraṇe puruṣeṣvakṣayo bhavān \|\| 70 \|\|*

I bow to you, both one and many, the essence of all beings. I bow to you, the cause of primary elements and the heart of all creation. 70

## \|\| 1.12.71 \|\|

*sarvasminsarvabhūtastvaṃ sarvaḥ sarvasvarūpadhṛk \|*  
*sarvaṃ tvattastataś ca tvaṃ namaḥ sarvātmane 'stu te \|\| 71 \|\|*

You are the Seen, Matter and Spirit. You are perceived as indestructible and as Virāj, Samrāj and Svarāj in the hearts of men. 71

## \|\| 1.12.72 \|\|

*sarvātmakosi sarveśa sarvabhūtasthito yataḥ \|*  
*kathayāmi tataḥ kiṃ te sarvaṃ vetsi hṛti sthitam \|\| 72 \|\|*

You consist of all and exist in all. You are all. All this is a form of you and comes from you yet is separate from you. I bow to you at the heart of all. 72

## \|\| 1.12.73 \|\|

*sarvātmansarvabhūteśa sarvasattvasamudbhava \|*  
*sarvabhūto bhavānvetti sarvasattvamanoratham \|\| 73 \|\|*

You form the nature of everything, Lord of all, because you abide in all creation. But what’s the use of saying this, when you know all that’s in my heart? 73

## \|\| 1.12.74 \|\|

*yo me manoratho nātha saphalaḥ sa tvayā kṛtaḥ \|*  
*tapaś ca taptaṃ saphalaṃ yaddṛṣṭo 'si jagatpate \|\| 74 \|\|*

You who are at the heart of everything, Lord of all creation, origin of all beings, you consist of everything and know the desire of every heart. 74

## \|\| 1.12.75 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*tapasastatphalaṃ yaddṛṣṭo 'haṃ tvayā dhruva \|*  
*madṛrśanaṃ hi viphalaṃ rājaputra na jāyate \|\| 75 \|\|*

My heart’s desire has been fulfilled by you, protector, as the austerities I undertook succeeded when I saw you, universal Lord. 75

## \|\| 1.12.76 \|\|

*varaṃ varaya tasmāttvaṃ yathābhimatamātmanaḥ \|*  
*sarvaṃ saṃpadyate puṃsāṃ mayi dṛṣṭipathaṃ gate \|\| 76 \|\|*

The Glorious Lord:

Your austerities have succeeded as you’ve beheld me, Dhruva, and the sight of me is never fruitless, prince. 76

## \|\| 1.12.77 \|\|

*dhruva uvāca*  
*bhagavanbhūtabhavyeśa sarvasyāste bhavān hṛdi \|*  
*kimajñātaṃ tava brahmanmanasā yanmayekṣitam \|\| 77 \|\|*

Make a wish, therefore, in accord with your heart’s desire, as everything is possible for those to whom I manifest. 77

## \|\| 1.12.78 \|\|

*tathāpi tubhyaṃ deveśa kathayiṣyāmi yanmayā \|*  
*prārthyate durvinītena hṛdayenātidurlabham \|\| 78 \|\|*

Dhruva:

Lord, master of all creation, you abide in the heart of everything. Lord, how could my heart’s desire remain unknown to you? 78

## \|\| 1.12.79 \|\|

*kiṃ vā sarvajagatstraṣṭaḥ prasanne tvayi durlabham \|*  
*tvatprasādaphalaṃ bhuṅkte trailokyaṃ magha vānapi \|\| 79 \|\|*

Nevertheless, Lord of gods, I’ll describe to you the single thing, most difficult to attain, that my stubborn heart desires. 79

## \|\| 1.12.80 \|\|

*naitadrājāsanaṃ yogyamajātasya mamodarāt \|*  
*iti garvadavocanmāṃ sapatnīmāturuccakaiḥ \|\| 80 \|\|*

What, on the other hand, is difficult to attain when you are pleased, creator of the universe? With your blessing even mighty Indra enjoys the threefold worlds. 80

## \|\| 1.12.81 \|\|

*ādhārabhūtaṃ jagataḥ sarveṣāmuttamottamam \|*  
*prārthayāmi prabho sthānaṃ tvatprasādādatovyayam \|\| 81 \|\|*

On account of her arrogance, my mother’s rival shouted that the royal throne was unsuited to a child born from another woman’s womb. 81

## \|\| 1.12.82 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*yattvayā prārthyate sthānametatprāpsyati vai bhavān \|*  
*tvayāhaṃ toṣitaḥ pūrvamanyajanmani vālaka \|\| 82 \|\|*

By your grace, I ask you, Lord, for that state, supporter of the world, higher than the highest of all, which endures forever. 82

## \|\| 1.12.83 \|\|

*tvamāsīrbrahmaṇaḥ pūrvaṃ mayyekāgramatiḥ sadā \|*  
*mātāpitroś ca śuśraṣurnijadharmānupālakaḥ \|\| 83 \|\|*

The Glorious Lord:

You will attain the state that you requested as you pleased me previously in another life, my boy. 83

## \|\| 1.12.84 \|\|

*kālena gacchatā mitraṃ rājaputrastavābhavat \|*  
*yovanekhilabhogāḍhyo darśanīyojjvalākṛtiḥ \|\| 84 \|\|*

In former times, you were a brahmin whose mind was ever set on me alone. You served your mother and your father and did your duty. 84

## \|\| 1.12.85 \|\|

*tatsaṅgāttasya tāmṛddhimavalokyādidurlabhām \|*  
*bhaveyaṃ rājaputrohamiti vāñchā tvayā kṛtā \|\| 85 \|\|*

In time, you befriended a certain prince who partook in all those youthful pastimes and cut a dashing, handsome figure. 85

## \|\| 1.12.86 \|\|

*tato yathābhilaṣitā prāptā te rājaputratā \|*  
*uttānapādasya gṛhe jātosi dhruva durlabhe \|\| 86 \|\|*

Through your friendship with him, you saw his rare wealth and formed a wish to be a prince yourself. 86

## \|\| 1.12.87 \|\|

*anyeṣāṃ durlabhaṃ sthānaṃ kule svāyambhuvasya yat \|\| 87 \|\|*

Then, in accordance with that desire, Dhruva, you were duly born a royal son in the lofty house of Uttānapāda. 87

## \|\| 1.12.88 \|\|

*tasyaitadaparaṃ bāla yenāhaṃ paritoṣitaḥ \|*  
*māmārādhya naro muktimavāpnotyavilambitām \|\| 88 \|\|*

But birth in the lineage of Svāyaṃbhuva, which for others is a blessing, 88

## \|\| 1.12.89 \|\|

*mayyarpitamanā bāla kimu svargādikaṃ padam \|\| 89 \|\|*

Was not so good for you, my boy, which pleases me, as those who worship me will win liberation without delay. 89

## \|\| 1.12.90 \|\|

*trailokyādadhike sthāne sarvatārāgrahāśrayaḥ \|*  
*bhaviṣyati na saṃdeho matprasādādbhāvandhruva \|\| 90 \|\|*

What’s a place in heaven to one whose heart is set on me, my son? In a place above the threefold worlds, with my blessing, you will surely be the pivot of all the stars and planets, 90

## \|\| 1.12.91 \|\|

*sūryātsomāttathā bhaumātsomaputrādbṛhaspateḥ \|*  
*sitārkatanayādīnāṃ sarvarkṣāṇāṃ tathā dhruvaḥ \|\| 91 \|\|*

Beyond the sun and moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus and the other luminaries, the stars, 91

## \|\| 1.12.92 \|\|

*saptarṣiṇāmaśeṣāṇāṃ ye ca vaimānikāḥ surāḥ \|*  
*sarveṣāmupari sthānaṃ tava dattaṃ mayā dhruva \|\| 92 \|\|*

And the Seven Sages without exception, as well as the deities in their celestial carriages. I’ll give you, Dhruva, a station above all these. 92

## \|\| 1.12.93 \|\|

*keciccaturyugaṃ yāvatkecinmanvantaraṃ surāḥ \|*  
*tiṣṭhanti bhavato dattā mayā vai kalpasaṃsthitiḥ \|\| 93 \|\|*

Some gods endure for all four ages, some for a Manvantara, but I’ll give you the lifespan of one whole aeon. 93

## \|\| 1.12.94 \|\|

*sunītirapi te mātā tvadāsannātinirmalā \|*  
*vimāne tārakā bhūtvā tāvatkālaṃ nivatsyati \|\| 94 \|\|*

Your mother, Sunīti, will also become a brilliant star always dwelling in a mansion by your side. 94

## \|\| 1.12.95 \|\|

*ye ca tvāṃ mānavāḥ prātaḥ sāyaṃ ca susamāhi tāḥ \|*  
*kīrtayiṣyanti teṣāṃ ca mahatpuṇyaṃ bhaviṣyati \|\| 95 \|\|*

All those who praise you with deep devotion at dawn and dusk will win great merit. 95

## \|\| 1.12.96 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ pūrvaṃ jagannāthāddevadevājjanārdanāt \|*  
*varaṃ prāpya dhruvaḥ sthāna madhyāste sa mahāmate \|\| 96 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So it was in former times that Janārdana, protector of the world and god of gods, granted Dhruva’s wish, and this wise sage is now forever fixed (dhruvam) in that position. 96

## \|\| 1.12.97 \|\|

*svayaṃ śuśrūṣaṇāddharmyānmātāpitroś ca vai tathā \|*  
*ddhādaśākṣaramāhatmyāttapasaś ca prabhāvataḥ \|\| 97 \|\|*

Seeing Dhruva’s greatness, pride and wealth, Uśanas, preceptor of gods and demigods, sang these verses: 97

## \|\| 1.12.98 \|\|

*tasyābhimānamṛddhiṃ ca mahimānaṃ nirikṣya hi \|*  
*devāsurāṇāmācāryaḥ ślokamatrośanā jagau \|\| 98 \|\|*

‘Ah! Such was the power of his austerities. Ah! Such was the reward of his great penance, that Dhruva stands above the Seven Sages. 98

## \|\| 1.12.99 \|\|

*ahosya tapaso vīryamahosya tapasaḥ phalam \|*  
*yadenaṃ purataḥ kṛtvā dhruvaṃ saptarṣayaḥ sthitāḥ \|\| 99 \|\|*

And this is Dhruva’s mother, Sunīti, also called Sūnṛtā. Who on earth can describe her majesty? 99

## \|\| 1.12.100 \|\|

*dhruvasya jananī ceyaṃ sunītirnāma sūnṛtā \|*  
*asyāś ca mahimānaṃ kaḥ śakto varṇayituṃ bhuvi \|\| 100 \|\|*

She who carried Dhruva in her womb now occupies a lofty station, resort of all three worlds, and attains the highest state of Viṣṇu.’ 100

## \|\| 1.12.101 \|\|

*trailokyaśrayatāṃ prāptaṃ paraṃ sthānaṃ stirāyati \|*  
*sthānaṃ prāptā paraṃ dhṛtvā yā kukṣivivare dhruvam \|\| 101 \|\|*

Those who always honour Dhruva’s rise to heaven will be free from all misdeeds, while dwelling with joy in paradise. 101

## \|\| 1.12.102 \|\|

*yaścaitatkīrtayennityaṃ dhruvasyārohaṇaṃ divi \|*  
*sarvapāpavinirmuktaḥ svargaloke mahīyate \|\| 102 \|\|*

Such a being will never slip from his place in heaven or on earth and, filled with every happiness, will lead a long and joyous life. 102

## Chapter 13 - Dhruva’s descendants

## \|\| 1.13.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*dhruvācchiṣṭiṃ ca bhavyaṃ ca bhavyācchambhurvyajāyata \|*  
*śiṣṭorādhatta succhāyā pañcaputrānakalmaṣān \|\| 1 \|\|*

Dhruva had two sons, Śiṣṭi and Bhavya. Bhavya’s son was Śaṃbhu. Śiṣṭi had five pure sons with his wife, Succhā: 1

## \|\| 1.13.2 \|\|

*ripuṃ ripuñjayaṃ vipraṃ vṛkalaṃ vṛkatejasam \|*  
*riporādhatta bṛhatī cākṣuṣaṃ sarvatejasam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Ripu, Ripuñjaya, Vipra, Vṛkala and Vṛkatejas. Ripu’s son with his wife, Bṛhatī, was the glorious Cākṣusa. 2

## \|\| 1.13.3 \|\|

*ajījanatpuṣkariṇyāṃ vāruṇyāṃ cākṣuṣo manum \|*  
*prajāpaterātmajāyāṃ vīraṇasya mahātmanaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Cākṣusa had a son, Manu, with his wife, Puṣkariṇī, a descendant of Varuṇa and daughter of the great patriarch Vīraṇa. 3

## \|\| 1.13.4 \|\|

*manorajāyanta daśa naḍvalāyāṃ mahaujasaḥ \|*  
*kanyāyāṃ tapatāṃ śreṣṭha vairājasya prajāpateḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Manu had ten mighty sons with his wife, Naḍvalā, peerless ascetic, daughter of the patriarch Vairāja. 4

## \|\| 1.13.5 \|\|

*kuruḥ puruḥ śata dyumnastapasvī satyavāñchuciḥ \|*  
*agniṣṭomotirātraś ca sudyumnaśceti te nava \|*  
*abhimanyuś ca daśamo naḍvalāyāṃ mahaujasaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Kuru, Puru, Śatadyumna, Tapasvin, Satyavant, Śuci, Agniṣṭut, Atirātra and Sudyumna—these nine, with mighty Abhimanyu as the tenth, were born to Naḍvalā. 5

## \|\| 1.13.6 \|\|

*kurorajanayatputrān ṣaḍāgnoyī mahāprabhān \|*  
*aṅgaṃ sumanasaṃ khyātiṃ kratumaṅgirasaṃ śibim \|\| 6 \|\|*

Kuru’s wife, Āgneyī, had six brilliant sons: Aṅga, Sumanas, Khyāti, Kratu, Aṅgiras and Śibi. 6

## \|\| 1.13.7 \|\|

*aṅgātsunīthāpatyaṃ vai venamekamajāyata \|*  
*prajārthamṛṣayastasya mamanthurdakṣiṇaṃ karam \|\| 7 \|\|*

Aṅga’s wife, Sunīthā, had one son, Vena, whose right hand was massaged by the sages to bring forth a son. 7

## \|\| 1.13.8 \|\|

*venasya pāṇau mathite sambabhūva mahāmune \|*  
*vainyo nāma mahīpālo yaḥ pṛthuḥ parikīrtitaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

When Vena’s hand was massaged, sage, a son named Vainya arose from it, and became the king called Pṛthu. 8

## \|\| 1.13.9 \|\|

*yena dugdhā mahī pūrvaṃ prajānāṃ hitakāraṇāt \|\| 9 \|\|*

It was he, in former times, who milked the earth for the benefit of his subjects. 9

## \|\| 1.13.10 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*kimarthaṃ mathitaḥ pāṇirvenasya paramarṣibhiḥ \|*  
*yatra jajñe mahāvīryaḥ sa pṛthṛrmunisattama \|\| 10 \|\|*

Why did seers massage Vena’s hand, best of sages, leading to heroic Pṛthu’s birth? 10

## \|\| 1.13.11 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*sunīthā nāma yā kanyā kṛtyo prathamato 'bhavat \|*  
*aṅgasya bhāryā sā dattā tasyāṃ veno vyajāyata \|\| 11 \|\|*

Parāśara:

A girl named Sunīthā, first-born daughter of Mṛtyu (‘Death’), was given in marriage to Aṅga and then gave birth to Vena. 11

## \|\| 1.13.12 \|\|

*sa mātāmahadoṣeṇa tena mṛtyoḥ sutātmajaḥ \|*  
*nisargādeṣa maitreya duṣṭa eva vyajāyata \|\| 12 \|\|*

This son of Mṛtyu’s daughter, Maitreya, born under his grandsire’s evil sway, was wicked by his nature. 12

## \|\| 1.13.13 \|\|

*abhiṣikto yadā rājye sa venaḥ paramarṣibhiḥ \|*  
*ghoṣa yāmāsa sa tadā pṛthivyāṃ pṛthivīpatiḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

When Vena was anointed king by the best of sages, he proclaimed to the all world, 13

## \|\| 1.13.14 \|\|

*na yaṣṭavyaṃ na dātavyaṃ na hotavyaṃ kathañcana \|*  
*bhoktā yajñasya kastvanyo 'hyahaṃ yajñapatiḥ prabhuḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

‘There shall be neither sacrifice, nor offerings, nor sacrificial gifts, for who apart from me, lord of the sacrifice and king, is fit to enjoy these rituals?’ 14

## \|\| 1.13.15 \|\|

*tatastamṛṣayaḥ pūrva saṃpūjyaṃ pṛthivīpatim \|*  
*ūcuḥ sāmākalaṃ vākyaṃ maitreya samapasthitāḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

The sages came before the king, Maitreya, and, having paid respects, tried to soothe him with these words: 15

## \|\| 1.13.16 \|\|

*ṛṣaya ūcuḥ*  
*bhobho rājan śṛṇuṣva tvaṃ yadvadāma mahīpate \|*  
*rājyaṃ dehopakārāya prajānāṃ ca hitaṃ param \|\| 16 \|\|*

The sages:

Come, come, your majesty, listen to what we say to you for the benefit of your kingdom, your own wellbeing and for the greatest good of all your subjects. 16

## \|\| 1.13.17 \|\|

*dīrghasatreṇa deveśaṃ sarvayajñeśvaraṃ harim \|*  
*pūjayiṣyāma bhadraṃ te tasyāṃśaste bhaviṣyati \|\| 17 \|\|*

We will worship Hari, the lord of gods and master of all sacrifices, with a ritual of long duration. May you enjoy good fortune! A portion of the fruit of that event will come to you. 17

## \|\| 1.13.18 \|\|

*yajñena yajñapuruṣo viṣṇuḥ saṃprīṇite nṛpa \|*  
*asmābhir bhavataḥ kāmānsarvāneva pradāsyati \|\| 18 \|\|*

Viṣṇu, spirit of the sacrifice, will be gratified by the ritual we conduct, sire, and will grant your every wish. 18

## \|\| 1.13.19 \|\|

*yajñair yajñeśvaro yeṣāṃ rāṣṭre saṃpūjyate hariḥ \|*  
*teṣāṃ sarvepsitāvāptiṃ dadāti nṛpa bhūbhṛtām \|\| 19 \|\|*

In those kingdoms where Hari, lord of sacrifices, is honoured with oblations, he grants those kings fulfillment of all desires, your majesty. 19

## \|\| 1.13.20 \|\|

*vena uvāca*  
*mattaḥ ko 'bhyadhikonyosti kaścārādhyo mamāparaḥ \|*  
*koyaṃ haririti khyāto yo vo yajñaśvaro mataḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

Vena:

Who is superior to me, and who is honoured apart from me? Who is this being known as Hari, whom you regard as lord of rites? 20

## \|\| 1.13.21 \|\|

*brahmā janārdanaḥ śambhurindro vāyuryamo raviḥ \|*  
*hutabhugvaruṇo dhātā pūṣā bhūmirniṃśākaraḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

Brahmā, Janārdana, Śiva, Indra, Vāyu, Yama, the Sun, Agni, consumer of the offerings, Varuṇa, Dhātṛ, Pūṣan, the Earth and the Moon, who brings the night; 21

## \|\| 1.13.22 \|\|

*ete cānye ca ye devāḥ śāpānugrahakāriṇaḥ \|*  
*nṛpasyaite śarīrasthāḥ sarvadevamayo nṛpaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

These and other deities, who both curse and bless, all abide in the person of the king. The king embodies all these gods. 22

## \|\| 1.13.23 \|\|

*evaṃ jñāsvā mayājñaptaṃ yadyathā kriyatāṃ tathā \|*  
*na dātavyaṃ na yaṣṭavyaṃ na hotavyaṃ ca bho dvijāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Knowing this, you’ll do as I command. There shall be no sacrifice, brahmins, no gifts or offerings. 23

## \|\| 1.13.24 \|\|

*bhartṛśuśrūṣaṇaṃ dharmo yathā strīṇāṃ paro mataḥ \|*  
*mamājñāpālanaṃ dharmo bhavatāṃ ca tathā dvijāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Just as women’s highest duty is to serve their husbands, brahmins, your duty is to carry out my wishes. 24

## \|\| 1.13.25 \|\|

*ṛṣaya ūcuḥ*  
*dehyanujñāṃ mahārāja mā dharmo yātu saṃkṣayam \|*  
*haviṣāṃ pariṇāmo 'yaṃ yadetadakhilaṃ jagat \|\| 25 \|\|*

The sages:

Great king, command us to continue with the sacrifice so virtue won’t decline, as all the world depends on sacrificial offerings. 25

## \|\| 1.13.26 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*iti vijñāpyamāno 'pi sa venaḥ paramarṣibhiḥ \|*  
*yadā dadāti nānujñaṃ proktaḥ proktaḥ punaḥ punaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Even though the best of sages gave Vena this advice and implored him many times, the wicked king ignored them. 26

## \|\| 1.13.27 \|\|

*tataste munayaḥ sarve kopāmarṣasamanvitāḥ \|*  
*hanyatāṃ hanyatāṃ pāpa ityūcuste parasparam \|\| 27 \|\|*

Then all the seers, filled with anger and indignation, said to one another, ‘This wicked ruler must be killed. He must be killed. 27

## \|\| 1.13.28 \|\|

*yo yajñapuruṣaṃ viṣṇumanādinidhanaṃ prabhum \|*  
*vinindatyadhamācāro na sa yogyo bhuvaḥ patiḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Any wretch who denigrates the spirit of the sacrifice, the lord divine, who has no start or end, is unfit to rule the earth.’ 28

## \|\| 1.13.29 \|\|

*ityuktvā mantrapūtais taiḥ kuśair munigaṇānṛpam \|*  
*nijaghnurnihataṃ purvaṃ bhagavannindanādinā \|\| 29 \|\|*

So saying, the host of sages beat the king to death with kuśa-grass that had been purified with mantras, but as he’d insulted Viṣṇu, he was dead already. 29

## \|\| 1.13.30 \|\|

*tataś ca munayo reṇuṃ dadṛśuḥ sarvato dvija \|*  
*kimetaditi cāsannātpapracchuste janāstadā \|\| 30 \|\|*

The sages later saw great clouds of dust billowing in all directions, brahmin. ‘What’s that?’ they asked the folk nearby. 30

## \|\| 1.13.31 \|\|

*ākhyātaṃ ca janais teṣāṃ corībhūtair arājake \|*  
*rāṣṭre tu lokair ārabdhaṃ parasvādānamāturaiḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

‘Now the kingdom has no king,’ they said, ‘those who suffer have turned to theft and are seizing others’ goods. 31

## \|\| 1.13.32 \|\|

*teṣāmudīrṇavegānāṃ corāṇāṃ munisattamāḥ \|*  
*sumahaddṛśyate reṇuḥ paravittāpahāriṇām \|\| 32 \|\|*

The clouds of dust you see, good sages, are raised by thieves who rush about while stealing people’s things.’ 32

## \|\| 1.13.33 \|\|

*tataḥ saṃmantrya te sarve sunayastasya bhūbhṛtaḥ \|*  
*mamanthurūruṃ putrārthamanapatyasya yatnataḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

After consulting one another, all the sages tightly squeezed the dead king’s thigh to bring forth progeny, as he had died without a son. 33

## \|\| 1.13.34 \|\|

*mathyamānātsamuttasthau tasyoroḥ puruṣaḥ kila \|*  
*dagdhasthūṇāpratīkāśaḥ khalvāṭāsyotihrasvakaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

As they did so, a dwarfish man with flattened features, looking like a blackened stump, appeared from it. 34

## \|\| 1.13.35 \|\|

*kiṃ karomīti tānsarvānsa viprānāha cāturaḥ \|*  
*niṣīdeti tamūcuste niṣādastena so 'bhavat \|\| 35 \|\|*

This being then asked the brahmins what he should do, to which they said, ‘Sit down—niṣīda.’ That’s why he’s called Niṣāda. 35

## \|\| 1.13.36 \|\|

*tatastatsaṃbhavā jātā vindhyaśailanivāsinaḥ \|*  
*niṣādā muniśārdūla pāpakarmopalakṣaṇāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

His descendants are Niṣādas who inhabit the Vindhya mountains and are noted for their deeds of cruelty, tiger of a sage. 36

## \|\| 1.13.37 \|\|

*tena dvāreṇa tatpāpaṃ niṣkrāntaṃ tasya bhūpateḥ \|*  
*niṣādāste tato jātā venakalmaṣanāśanāḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

By these means, King Vena’s misdeeds were expunged, as Niṣādas came to shoulder them. 37

## \|\| 1.13.38 \|\|

*tasyaiva dakṣiṇaṃ hastaṃ mamanthuste tato dvijāḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

Those best of sages then began to squeeze the king’s right hand. 38

## \|\| 1.13.39 \|\|

*mathyamāne ca tatrābhūtpṛthurvainyaḥ pratāpavān \|*  
*dīpyamānaḥ svavapuṣā sākṣādagniriva jvalan \|\| 39 \|\|*

And as they did so, Vena’s son, the mighty ruler known as Pṛthu, appeared. His body blazed with its own light, shining like a second Agni. 39

## \|\| 1.13.40 \|\|

*ādyamājagavaṃ nāma khātpapāta tato dhanuḥ \|*  
*śarāś ca divyā nabhasaḥ kavacaṃ ca papāta ha \|\| 40 \|\|*

At that moment, the primeval bow called Ājagava descended from the sky, along with heavenly shafts and armour. 40

## \|\| 1.13.41 \|\|

*tasmin jāte tu bhūtāni saṃprahṛṣṭāni sarvaśaḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

Beings everywhere rejoiced at Pṛthu’s birth. 41

## \|\| 1.13.42 \|\|

*satputreṇaiva jātena venopi tridivaṃ yayo \|*  
*punnāmno narakāt trātaḥ sutena sumahātmanā \|\| 42 \|\|*

Because Vena brought forth such a noble son, he was rescued from the hell realm known as Put by mighty Pṛthu and rose to heaven. 42

## \|\| 1.13.43 \|\|

*taṃ samudrāś ca nadyaś ca ratnānyādāya sarvaśaḥ \|*  
*toyāni cābhiṣekārthaṃ sarvāṇyevopatasthire \|\| 43 \|\|*

The Oceans and the Rivers came from all directions, bringing jewels and water for Pṛthu’s consecration. 43

## \|\| 1.13.44 \|\|

*pitāmahaś ca bhagavāndevair āṅgirasaiḥ saha \|*  
*sthāvarāṇi ca bhūtāni jaṅgamāni ca sarvaśaḥ \|*  
*samāgamya tadā vainyamabhyasiñcannarādhipam \|\| 44 \|\|*

Lord Brahmā, grandsire of the world, the deities accompanied by Aṅgiras’s descendants and beings mobile and immobile gathered from all sides for Vena’s son’s anointing. 44

## \|\| 1.13.45 \|\|

*haste tu dakṣiṇe cakraṃ dṛṣṭvā tasya pitāmahaḥ \|*  
*viṣṇoraṃśaṃ ṣṛthuṃ matvā paritoṣaṃ paraṃ yayai \|\| 45 \|\|*

When Brahmā saw that Pṛthu’s right hand bore the discus mark, he recognised him as Viṣṇu’s partial manifestation and was overjoyed. 45

## \|\| 1.13.46 \|\|

*viṣṇucakra kare cihnaṃ sarveṣāṃ cakravartinām \|*  
*bhavatyavyāhato yasya prabhāvastridaśerapi \|\| 46 \|\|*

(This sign of Viṣṇu marks the hand of every universal sovereign. Even the thirteen deities cannot thwart the rule of such a being.) 46

## \|\| 1.13.47 \|\|

*mahatā rājarājyena pṛthurvainyaḥ pratāpavān \|*  
*sobhiṣikto mahātejā vidhivaddharmakovidai \|\| 47 \|\|*

Pṛthu, Vena’s mighty shining son, was anointed universal ruler in a rite performed by experts. 47

## \|\| 1.13.48 \|\|

*pitrā parājitāstasya prajāstenānurañcitāḥ \|*  
*anurāgāttatastasya nāma rājetyajāyata \|\| 48 \|\|*

He gratified the subjects who’d been slighted by his father and, because of their affection (anurāga), the title ‘king’ (rājan) came into use. 48

## \|\| 1.13.49 \|\|

*āpastastaṃbhire cāsya samudramabhiyāsyataḥ \|*  
*parvatāś ca dadurmārgaṃ dhvajabhaṅgaś ca nābhavat \|\| 49 \|\|*

The waters solidified for him when he went to cross the ocean, the mountains opened paths for him and his banner was never torn. 49

## \|\| 1.13.50 \|\|

*akṛṣṭapacyā pṛthivī siddhantyannāni cintayā \|*  
*sarvakāmadughā gāvaḥ puṭake puṭake madhu \|\| 50 \|\|*

The earth untouched by ploughs bore crops, food appeared with just a thought, cows fulfilled each wish and honey dripped from every hollow. 50

## \|\| 1.13.51 \|\|

*tasya vai jātamātrasya yajñe paitāmahe śubhe \|*  
*sūtaḥ sūtyāṃ samutpannaḥ sautye 'hani mahāmatiḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

As soon as Pṛthu was born, at the glorious sacrifice held by Brahmā, grandsire of the world, a wise bard (sūta) arose at the pressing (sūti) of the soma on the day that rite was held. 51

## \|\| 1.13.52 \|\|

*tasminneva mahāyajñejajñe prājñe 'tha māgadhaḥ \|*  
*proktau tadā munivarais tāvubhau sūtamāgadhau \|\| 52 \|\|*

At that same great sacrifice, a gifted panegyrist was born, and the best of sages then addressed him and that bard: 52

## \|\| 1.13.53 \|\|

*stūyatāmeṣa nṛpatiḥ pṛthurvainyaḥ pratāpavān \|*  
*karmaitadanurūpaṃ vāṃ pātraṃ stotrasya cāparam \|\| 53 \|\|*

‘Praise this king, Pṛthu, the mighty son of Vena, for it’s your special role and he’s a worthy subject for your acclaim.’ 53

## \|\| 1.13.54 \|\|

*tatastāvūcaturviprānsarvāneva kṛtāñjalī \|*  
*adya jātasya no karma jñāyatesya mahīpateḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

With folded palms, they then replied to all those brahmins, ‘The king was only born today, and his exploits are as yet unknown. 54

## \|\| 1.13.55 \|\|

*guṇā na cāsya jñāyante na cāsya prathitaṃ yaśaḥ \|*  
*stotraṃ kimāśrayaṃ tvasya kāryamasmābhir ucyatām \|\| 55 \|\|*

We haven’t witnessed his qualities, and his fame is yet to spread. Say which virtues should be eulogised.’ 55

## \|\| 1.13.56 \|\|

*kṛṣaya ūcuḥ*  
*kariṣyatyeṣa yatkarma cakravarti mahābalaḥ \|*  
*guṇā bhaviṣyā ye cāsya tair ayaṃ stūyatāṃ nṛpaḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

The sages:

Praise this mighty universal sovereign for the deeds he will perform, and for his future qualities. 56

## \|\| 1.13.57 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tataḥ sa nṛpatistoṣaṃ tacchratvā paramaṃ yayau \|*  
*sadguṇaiḥ ślāghyatāmeti tasmācchlāghyā guṇā mama \|\| 57 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, King Pṛthu, filled with joy, exclaimed, ‘Because a person is commended for good qualities, the bard and the panegyrist will acclaim my virtues. 57

## \|\| 1.13.58 \|\|

*tasmādyadadya stotreṇa guṇanirvarṇanaṃ tvimau \|*  
*kariṣyete kariṣyāmi tadevāhaṃ samāhitaḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

I shall therefore diligently practise all those virtues commended in their paean, 58

## \|\| 1.13.59 \|\|

*yadimau varjanīyaṃ ca kiñcidatra vadiṣyataḥ \|*  
*tadahaṃ varjayiṣyāmītyevaṃ cakre matiṃ nṛpaḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

And I’ll avoid those things they say are best avoided.’ Such was the resolution of the king. 59

## \|\| 1.13.60 \|\|

*atha tau cakratuḥ stotraṃ pṛthorvainyasya dhīmataḥ \|*  
*bhaviṣyaiḥ karmabhiḥ samyaksusvarau sūtamāgadhau \|\| 60 \|\|*

Then the sweet-voiced bard and panegyrist composed this paean in praise of Pṛthu’s future deeds, Vena’s sagacious son: 60

## \|\| 1.13.61 \|\|

*satyavāgdānaśīloyaṃ satyasandho nareśvaraḥ \|*  
*hrīmānmaitraḥ kṣamāśīlo vikrānto duṣṭaśānaḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

‘Truthful, generous and honest, this prudent king is patient with his allies, but remains the doughty bane of foe. 61

## \|\| 1.13.62 \|\|

*dharmajñaś ca kṛtajñaś ca dayāvān priyabhāṣakaḥ \|*  
*mānyānmānayitā yajvā brahmaṇyaḥ sādhusaṃmataḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

He knows virtue and is grateful, kind and gently spoken, bestowing honour where it’s due and undertaking sacrifices. He’s devout and cherishes the good. 62

## \|\| 1.13.63 \|\|

*samaḥ śatrau ca mitre ca vyavahārasthitau nṛpaḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

This king fairly metes out justice to friend and foe alike.’ 63

## \|\| 1.13.64 \|\|

*sūtenoktān guṇānitthaṃ sa tadā māgadhena ca \|*  
*cakāra hṛdi tādṛk ca karmaṇā kṛtavānasau \|\| 64 \|\|*

Pṛthu then held in his heart the virtues thus praised by the bard and the panegyrist, and always acted in accordance with their words. 64

## \|\| 1.13.65 \|\|

*tatastu pṛthivīpālaḥ pālayanpṛthivīmimām \|*  
*iyāja vividhair yajñair mahadbhir bhūridakṣiṇaiḥ \|\| 65 \|\|*

He then performed various major sacrifices, accompanied by generous donations, while protecting all the world. 65

## \|\| 1.13.66 \|\|

*taṃ prajāḥ pṛthivīnāthamupatasthuḥ kṣudhārditāḥ \|*  
*oṣadhīṣu praṇaṣṭāsu tasminkāle hyarājake \|*  
*tamūcuste natāḥ pṛṣṭāstatrāgamanakāraṇam \|\| 66 \|\|*

In the absence of a ruler, plant life had perished, and the people, beset by hunger, approached the king. Asked by Pṛthu why they came, they replied as follows: 66

## \|\| 1.13.67 \|\|

*prajā ūcuḥ*  
*arājake nṛpaśraṣṭha dharitryā sakalauṣadhīḥ \|*  
*grastāstataḥ kṣayaṃ yānti prajāḥ sarvāḥ prajeśvara \|\| 67 \|\|*

The subjects:

Your most excellent majesty, without a king, the Earth withheld all vegetation and, as a result, the entire population is heading to destruction, Lord of men. 67

## \|\| 1.13.68 \|\|

*tvanno vṛttiprado dhātrā prajāpālo nirūpitaḥ \|*  
*dehi naḥ kṣutparītā nāṃ prajānāṃ jīvanauṣadhīḥ \|\| 68 \|\|*

You provide subsistence and are our support; you are regarded as protector of the people. Give us, your famine-stricken subjects, some vegetables to live on. 68

## \|\| 1.13.69 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tatastu napatirdivyamādāyajagavaṃ dhanuḥ \|*  
*śarāṃś ca divyānkupitaḥ sonvadhāvadvasundharām \|\| 69 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The furious king took up the heavenly bow called Ājagava and some arrows, and set off searching for the Earth. 69

## \|\| 1.13.70 \|\|

*tato nanāśa tvarītā gaurbhūtvā ca vasundharā \|*  
*sā lokānbrahmalokādīnsaṃtrāsādagamanmahī \|\| 70 \|\|*

But the Earth took the appearance of a cow and, filled with fear, she fled to Brahmā’s heaven and other divine realms. 70

## \|\| 1.13.71 \|\|

*yatra yatra yayau devī sā tadā bhūtadhāriṇī \|*  
*tatra tatra tu sā vainyaṃ dadṛśe 'bhyudyatāyudham \|\| 71 \|\|*

Wherever that heavenly support of all creation went, there she saw the son of Vena with his weapon at the ready. 71

## \|\| 1.13.72 \|\|

*tatastaṃ prāha vasudhā pṛthuṃ rājaparākramam \|*  
*pravepamānā tadbāṇaparitrāṇaparāyaṇā \|\| 72 \|\|*

The trembling Earth cried out to Pṛthu of great prowess, hoping to be spared his arrows. 72

## \|\| 1.13.73 \|\|

*pṛthivyuvāca*  
*strīvadhe tvaṃ mahāpāpaṃ kiṃ narendra na paśyasi \|*  
*yena māṃ hantumatyarthaṃ prakaroṣi nṛpodyamam \|\| 73 \|\|*

The Earth:

You’re endeavouring to slay me, sire, but don’t you know it’s a dire sin to kill a female? 73

## \|\| 1.13.74 \|\|

*pṛthuruvāca*  
*ekasmin yatra nidhanaṃ prāpite duṣṭakāriṇi \|*  
*bahūnāṃ bhavati kṣemaṃ tasya puṇyaprado vadhaḥ \|\| 74 \|\|*

Pṛthu:

When the death of a single evildoer yields security for many, then that death is meritorious. 74

## \|\| 1.13.75 \|\|

*pṛthuvyuvāca*  
*prajānāmupakārāya yadi māṃ tvaṃ haniṣyasi \|*  
*ādhāraḥ kaḥ prajānāṃ te nṛpaśreṣṭha bhaviṣyati \|\| 75 \|\|*

The Earth:

If you kill me for your subjects’ sake, who’ll support them, best of sovereigns? 75

## \|\| 1.13.76 \|\|

*pṛthuruvāca*  
*tvāṃ hatvā vasudhe bāṇair macchāsanaparāṅmukhīm \|*  
*ātma yogabalenemā dhārayiṣyāmyahaṃ prajāḥ \|\| 76 \|\|*

Pṛthu:

Having slain you with these arrows, Earth, who turned your back on my commands, I’ll support my subjects through the power of my yoga. 76

## \|\| 1.13.77 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tataḥ vasudhā taṃ bhūyaḥ taṃ bhūyaḥ prāha pārthivam \|*  
*pravepitāṅgī paramaṃ sādhvasaṃ samupāgatā \|\| 77 \|\|*

Parāśara:

At this, the Earth grew even more afraid, her every limb atremble, and, bowing down, she made the king this offer. 77

## \|\| 1.13.78 \|\|

*pṛthivyuvāca*  
*upāyataḥ samārabdhāḥ sarve siddhyantyupakramāḥ \|*  
*tasmādvadāmyupāyaṃ te taṃ kuruṣva yadīcchasi \|\| 78 \|\|*

The Earth:

All undertakings will succeed if proper steps are taken. I’ll therefore tell you what to do, if only you are willing. 78

## \|\| 1.13.79 \|\|

*samastā yā mayā jīrṇā naranātha mahauṣadhīḥ \|*  
*yadīcchasi pradāsyāmi tāḥ kṣīrapariṇāminīḥ \|\| 79 \|\|*

If your majesty desires, with my milk, I shall restore the vegetation that I’d allowed to wither. 79

## \|\| 1.13.80 \|\|

*tasmātprajāhitārthāya mama dharmabhṛtāṃ vara \|*  
*taṃ tu vatsaṃ kuruṣva tvaṃ kṣareyaṃ yena vatsalā \|\| 80 \|\|*

For the welfare of your subjects, virtue’s best defender, bestow on me a calf so I, affectionate mother that I am, may bring forth milk for him. 80

## \|\| 1.13.81 \|\|

*samāṃ ca kuru sarvatra yena kṣīraṃ samantataḥ \|*  
*varauṣadhībījabhūtaṃ bījaṃ sarvatra bhāvaye \|\| 81 \|\|*

Level the surface of the world so I may cause my milk, which will become the source of finest vegetation, to flow in all directions. 81

## \|\| 1.13.82 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tata utsārayāmāsa śailān śatasahasraśaḥ \|*  
*dhanuṣkoṭyā padā vainyastena śaulā vivardhitāḥ \|\| 82 \|\|*

Parāśara:

With his bow-tip, Vena’s son then dragged the mountains by the hundreds and the thousands into massive heaps. 82

## \|\| 1.13.83 \|\|

*na hi pūrvavisarge vai viṣame pṛthivītale \|*  
*pravībhāgaḥ purāṇāṃ vā grāmāṇāṃ vā purābhavāt \|\| 83 \|\|*

(Before he did so, the surface of the world was rough and undivided into villages or towns. 83

## \|\| 1.13.84 \|\|

*na sasyāni na gorakṣyaṃ na kṛṣirna vaṇikpathaḥ \|*  
*vainyātprabhṛti maitreya sarvasyaitasya sambhavaḥ \|\| 84 \|\|*

There were no crops of grain, nor cattle-rearing, nor ploughing, nor paths for merchants. All these began with Vena’s son, Maitreya.) 84

## \|\| 1.13.85 \|\|

*yatrayatra samaṃ tvasya bhūmerāsīdddvajottama \|*  
*tatratatra prajāḥ sarvā nivāsaṃ samarocayant \|\| 85 \|\|*

The king deemed all the level places of the world fit for his subjects’ habitations. 85

## \|\| 1.13.86 \|\|

*āhāraḥ phalamūlāni prajānāmabhavattadā \|*  
*kṛcchreṇa mahatā so 'pi praṇaṣṭāsvaiṣadīṣu vai \|\| 86 \|\|*

Before this time, people lived on roots and berries, but life grew hard when all the vegetation died. 86

## \|\| 1.13.87 \|\|

*sa kalpayitvā vatsaṃ tu manuṃ svāyaṃbhuvaṃ prabhum \|*  
*svapāṇau pṛtivīnātho dudoha pṛthivīṃ pṛthuḥ \|*  
*sasyājātāni sarvāṇi prajānāṃ hitakāmyayā \|\| 87 \|\|*

Pṛthu gave the Earth a calf, which was in fact Lord Manu Svāyaṃbhuva. From his desire to benefit his subjects, King Pṛthu then milked all kinds of grain from the Earth into his own two hands. 87

## \|\| 1.13.88 \|\|

*tenānnena prajāstāta vartantedyāpi nityaśaḥ \|\| 88 \|\|*

The people thrived on that nutrition, my dear, as they do today and always will. 88

## \|\| 1.13.89 \|\|

*prāṇapradātā sa pṛthuryasmādbhumerabhītpitā \|*  
*tatastu pṛthivīsaṃjñāmavāpākhilādhāriṇī \|\| 89 \|\|*

Pṛthu gave the Earth the gift of life, and so became her father. That’s why the Earth, support of every living thing, is known as Pṛthivī, ‘Pṛthu’s daughter’. 89

## \|\| 1.13.90 \|\|

*tataś ca devair munibhir daityai rakṣobhir adribhiḥ \|*  
*gandharvair uragair yakṣaiḥ pitṛbhis tarubhis tathā \|\| 90 \|\|*

The deities, sages, Daityas, rākṣasas, mountains, gandharvas, nāgas, yakṣas, ancestral spirits and trees 90

## \|\| 1.13.91 \|\|

*tattatpātramupādāya tattaddugdhaṃ mune payaḥ \|*  
*vatsado gdhṛviśeṣāś ca teṣāṃ tadyonayo 'bhavan \|\| 91 \|\|*

All took a bowl and received some milk, sage. Each had their own specific calf and milker, the source of their vitality. 91

## \|\| 1.13.92 \|\|

*saiṣā dhatrī vidhātrī ca dhāriṇī poṣaṇī tathā \|*  
*sarvasya tu tataḥ pṛthvī viṣṇupādatalodbhāvā \|\| 92 \|\|*

This Earth—supporter, creator, bearer and nurturer of the world—was born from Viṣṇu’s sole. 92

## \|\| 1.13.93 \|\|

*evaṃ prabhāvaḥsa pṛthuḥ putro vainyasya vīryavān \|*  
*jajñe mahīpatiḥ pūrvo rājābhūjjanarañjanāt \|\| 93 \|\|*

Such was Pṛthu’s prowess, Vena’s mighty son, born to rule the world in former times, and who was known as rājā because he pleased (rañjana) his subjects. 93

## \|\| 1.13.94 \|\|

*ya idaṃ janma vainyasya pṛthoḥ saṃkīrtarennaraḥ \|*  
*na tasya duṣkṛtaṃ kiñcitphaladāyi prajāyate \|\| 94 \|\|*

One who tells this tale of Pṛthu’s birth will never endure the consequence of any sin. 94

## \|\| 1.13.95 \|\|

*duḥsvapnopaśamaṃ nṝṇāṃ śṛṇvatāmetaduttamam \|*  
*pṛthorjanma prabhāvaś ca karoti satataṃ nṛṇām \|\| 95 \|\|*

This account of Pṛthu’s noble origin and prowess will always drive away the evil dreams of all who hear it. 95

## Chapter 14 - Pṛthu’s descendants

## \|\| 1.14.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*pṛtoḥ putro tu dharmajño jajñātentardhivādinau \|*  
*śikhaṇḍinī havirdhānamantardhānāvdyajāyata \|\| 1 \|\|*

Pṛthu had two heroic sons, Antardhi and Vādin. Antardhana’s wife, Śikhaṇḍinī, had a son, Havirdhāna. 1

## \|\| 1.14.2 \|\|

*havirdhānāt ṣaḍāgnoyī dhiṣaṇājayatsutānā \|*  
*prācīnabarhiṣaṃ śukraṃ gayaṃ kṛṣṇaṃ vṛjājinau \|\| 2 \|\|*

Havirdhāna had six sons with his wife, Dhiṣaṇā, a descendant of Agni. They were Prācīnabarhis, Śukra, Gaya, Kṛṣṇa, Vṛja and Ajina. 2

## \|\| 1.14.3 \|\|

*pracīnabarhirbhagavānmahānāsītprajāpatiḥ \|*  
*havirdhānānmahābhāga yena saṃvardhitāḥ prajāḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Havirdhāna’s son holy Prācīnabarhis was an important patriarch and mighty ruler who caused the human race to multiply. 3

## \|\| 1.14.4 \|\|

*prācīnāgrāḥ kuśāstasya pṛthivyāṃ viśrutā mune \|*  
*prācīnavarhirabhavatkhyāto bhuvi mahābalaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

It was said that he placed sacrificial kuśa-grass (barhis) on the ground with its tips pointing east (prācīna), sage, and that’s why that mighty king was known in the world as Prācīnabarhis. 4

## \|\| 1.14.5 \|\|

*samudratanayāyāṃ tu kṛtadāro mahīpatiḥ \|*  
*mahatastamasaḥ pāre savarṇāyāṃ mahāmate \|\| 5 \|\|*

At the conclusion of severe austerities, Prācīnabarhis married Savarṇā, the daughter of the Ocean. 5

## \|\| 1.14.6 \|\|

*savarṇādhatta sāmudrī daśa prācīnabarhiṣaḥ \|*  
*sarve prācetaso nāma dhanurvedasya pāragāḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

The Ocean’s daughter, Savarṇā, bore the king ten sons known as the Pracetases, who mastered the science of archery. 6

## \|\| 1.14.7 \|\|

*apṛthagdharmacaraṇāste tapyanta mahattapaḥ \|*  
*daśavarṣasahasrāṇi samudrasalileśayaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Together they practised virtue and undertook great austerities, living in the waters of the ocean for ten thousand years. 7

## \|\| 1.14.8 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*yadarthaṃ te mahātmānastapastepurmahāmune \|*  
*prācetasaḥ samudrāmbhasyetadākhyātumarhasi \|\| 8 \|\|*

Please explain, great sage, why the noble Pracetases undertook austerities below the ocean’s waters. 8

## \|\| 1.14.9 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*pitrā pracetasaḥ proktāḥ prajārthamamitātmanā \|*  
*prajāpatiniyuktena bahumānapuraḥsaram \|\| 9 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Respecting the command of the lord of creation, Brahmā, their noble-minded father, Prācīnabarhis, told the Pracetases that they needed progeny. 9

## \|\| 1.14.10 \|\|

*prācīnabarhiruvāca*  
*brahmaṇā devadevena samādiṣṭosmyahaṃ sutāḥ \|*  
*prajāḥ saṃvardhanīyāste mayā coktaṃ tatheti tat \|\| 10 \|\|*

Prācīnabarhis:

Brahmā, god of gods, commanded me to raise the population, sons, and I’ve agreed to do so. 10

## \|\| 1.14.11 \|\|

*tanmama prītaye putrāḥ prajāvṛddhimatandritāḥ \|*  
*kurudhvaṃ mānanīyā vaḥ samyagājñā prajāpateḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

Therefore, to please me, sons, spare no effort in this regard and duly follow Brahmā’s orders, which deserve respect. 11

## \|\| 1.14.12 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tataste tatpituḥ śrutvā vacanaṃ nṛpanandanāḥ \|*  
*tathetyuktvā ca taṃ bhūyaḥ papracchuḥ pitaraṃ mune \|\| 12 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing their father’s words, those princes all agreed, sage, but asked a further question. 12

## \|\| 1.14.13 \|\|

*pracetasa ūcuḥ*  
*yena tāta prajāvṛddhau samarthā karmaṇā vayam \|*  
*bhavema tat samastaṃ naḥ karma vyākhyātumarhasi \|\| 13 \|\|*

The Pracetases:

Father, tell us exactly what we must do to raise the population. 13

## \|\| 1.14.14 \|\|

*pitovāca*  
*ārādhya varadaṃ viṣṇum iṣṭaprāptim asaṃśayam \|*  
*sameti nānyathā martyaḥ kima nyatkathayāmi vaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Their father:

By worshipping Viṣṇu, who grants all wishes, a mortal will surely attain his goal. As there is no other way, what else can I tell you? 14

## \|\| 1.14.15 \|\|

*tasmātprajā vivṛddhyarthaṃ sarvabhūtaprabhuṃ harim \|*  
*ārādhayata govindaṃ yadi siddhimabhīpsathaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

To raise the population, you must therefore worship Hari, Govinda, lord of all creation, if you want success. 15

## \|\| 1.14.16 \|\|

*dharmamarthaṃ ca kāmaṃ ca mokṣaṃ cānvicchatāṃ sadā \|*  
*ārādhanīyo bhagavānanādipuruṣottamaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

The beginningless lord, the highest spirit, should always be revered by one who yearns for virtue, wealth, love or liberation. 16

## \|\| 1.14.17 \|\|

*yasminnārādhite sargaṃ cakārādau prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*tamārādhyācyutaṃ vṛddhiḥ prajānāṃ vo bhaviṣyati \|\| 17 \|\|*

In the beginning, Brahmā worshipped Hari, then made the world. When you’ve praised the everlasting lord, you’ll raise the population. 17

## \|\| 1.14.18 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityevamuktāste pitrā putrāḥ prācetaso daśa \|*  
*magnāḥ payodhisalile tapasteṣuḥ samāhitāḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After Prācīnabarhis spoke, his ten sons, the Pracetases, dived into the Milky Ocean, where they resolutely undertook austerities. 18

## \|\| 1.14.19 \|\|

*daśavarṣasahasrāṇi nyastacittā jagatpatau \|*  
*nārāyaṇe muniśreṣṭha sarvalokaparāyaṇe \|\| 19 \|\|*

For ten thousand years, their minds were set on Nārāyaṇa, best of sages, the universal lord and refuge of the world. 19

## \|\| 1.14.20 \|\|

*tatraivāvasthitā devamekāgramanaso harim \|*  
*tuṣṭuvuryaḥstutaḥ kāmān stoturiṣṭānprayacchati \|\| 20 \|\|*

There they remained, meditating solely on the deity and praising Hari, who grants the wishes of all who worship him. 20

## \|\| 1.14.21 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*stavaṃ pracetaso viṣṇoḥ samudrāmbhasi saṃsthitāḥ \|*  
*cakrustanme muniśreṣṭha supuṇyaṃ vaktumarhasi \|\| 21 \|\|*

Maitreya:

Repeat for me the wondrous song of praise the Pracetases sang for Viṣṇu while they lived in the ocean’s waters, best of sages. 21

## \|\| 1.14.22 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*śṛṇu maitreya govindaṃ yathāpūrvaṃ pracetasaḥ \|*  
*tuṣṭuvus tanmayībhūtāḥ samudrasalileśayāḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Now listen to the paean sung by the Pracetases, Maitreya, who were in truth composed of Govinda, while living underwater long ago. 22

## \|\| 1.14.23 \|\|

*pracetasa ūcuḥ*  
*natāḥ sma sarvavacasāṃ pratiṣṭhā yatra śāśvatī \|*  
*tamādyantamaśeṣasya jagataḥ paramaṃ prabhum \|\| 23 \|\|*

The Pracetases:

We bow to him whose eternal glory is the subject of all speech, the start and finish, the highest universal lord, 23

## \|\| 1.14.24 \|\|

*jyotirādyamanaupamyamaṇvanantamapāravat \|*  
*yonibhūtamaśeṣasya sthāvarasya carasya ca \|\| 24 \|\|*

Who is primal light, incomparable, indivisible, unending and unlimited; the origin of all that moves and all that’s static. 24

## \|\| 1.14.25 \|\|

*yasyāhaḥ prathamaṃ rūpamarūpasya tathā niśā \|*  
*saṃdhyā ca parameśasya tasmai kālātmane namaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

We bow to the formless one whose initial form is day, then night and twilight; the highest lord, who is Time itself. 25

## \|\| 1.14.26 \|\|

*bujyate 'nudinaṃ devaiḥ pitṛbhiś ca sudhātmakaḥ \|*  
*bījabhūtaṃ samastasya tasmai somātmanena maḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

We bow to him who, as the moon, is imbibed each day by gods and ancestors in the form of nectar, and is the life of all. 26

## \|\| 1.14.27 \|\|

*yastamāsyatti tīvrātmā prabhābhir bhāsayannabhaḥ \|*  
*gharmaśītāmbhasā yonistasmai sūryatmane namaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

We bow to him who, as the sun, dispels the dark, burns bright, illuminates the sky with his own rays and is the source of heat and cold and rain. 27

## \|\| 1.14.28 \|\|

*kāṭhinyavān yo bibharti jagadetadaśeṣataḥ \|*  
*śabdādisaṃśrayo vyāpī tasmai bhūmyātmane namaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

We bow to him who, as the earth, is imbued with firmness, supports the world, is the realm of sound and all sensations and is all-pervading. 28

## \|\| 1.14.29 \|\|

*yadyonibhūtaṃ jagato vījaṃ yatsarvadehinām \|*  
*tattoyarūpamīśasya namāmo harimedhasaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

We bow with joy to blessed Hari in the form of water, which is the universal womb and seed of every living being. 29

## \|\| 1.14.30 \|\|

*yo mukhaṃ sarvadevānāṃ havyabhukkavyabhuk tathā \|*  
*pitṝṇāṃ ca namastasmai viṣṇave pāvakātmane \|\| 30 \|\|*

We bow to Viṣṇu in the form of fire, which is the mouth of all the gods and ancestors, and which consumes the havya and kavya offerings. 30

## \|\| 1.14.31 \|\|

*pañcadhāvasthito dehe yaśceṣṭāṃ kurute 'niśam \|*  
*ākāśayonirbhagavāṃstasmai vāyvātmane namaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

We bow to the lord in the form of wind, existing in the body in five different modes, constant source of motion, whose origin is the sky. 31

## \|\| 1.14.32 \|\|

*avakāśamaśeṣāṇāṃ bhūtānāṃ yaḥ prayacchati \|*  
*anantamūrtimāñdhuddhastasmai vyomātmane namaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

We bow to him in the form of air, which constitutes the space between all beings and is endless, pure and formless. 32

## \|\| 1.14.33 \|\|

*samastondriyasargasya yaḥ sadā sthānamuttamam \|*  
*tasmai śabdādirūpāya namaḥ kṛṣṇāya vedhase \|\| 33 \|\|*

We bow to the creator Kṛṣṇa, in the form of sensations such as sound, who is always the ultimate object of all the senses. 33

## \|\| 1.14.34 \|\|

*gṛhṇāti viṣayānnityamindriyātmākṣarākṣaraḥ \|*  
*yastasmai jñānamūlāya natāsma harimedhase \|\| 34 \|\|*

We bow to Hari, who in the form of senses always apprehends their objects, changing and unchanging, the root of understanding. 34

## \|\| 1.14.35 \|\|

*hṛhītānindriyair arthānātmane yaḥ prayacchati \|*  
*antaḥkaraṇarūpāya tasmai viśvatmane namaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

We bow to him in the form of intellect, at the heart of all, who carries to the mind that which the senses apprehend. 35

## \|\| 1.14.36 \|\|

*yasminnanante sakalaṃ viśvaṃ yasmāttathodgatam \|*  
*layasthānaṃ ca yastasmai namaḥ prakṛtidharmiṇe \|\| 36 \|\|*

We bow to the endless being in whom all things abide, from whom all things arise, in whom all things dissolve, who is marked by primal nature. 36

## \|\| 1.14.37 \|\|

*śuddhaḥ saṃllakṣyate bhrāntyā guṇavāniva yo 'guṇaḥ \|*  
*tamātmarūpiṇaṃ deva natāsma puruṣottamam \|\| 37 \|\|*

We bow to that deity whose own form is the highest primal spirit, who is pure and has no qualities, but is misperceived to have them. 37

## \|\| 1.14.38 \|\|

*avikāramajaṃ śuddhaṃ nirguṇaṃ yannirajanam \|*  
*natāḥsmatatparaṃ brahma viṣṇoryatparamaṃ padam \|\| 38 \|\|*

We bow to that supreme state of Viṣṇu, the highest Absolute, changeless, unborn, pure, unqualified and unsullied. 38

## \|\| 1.14.39 \|\|

*adīrghahrasvamasthūlaṇvanamaśyāmalohitam \|*  
*asnehacchāyamatanumasaktamaśarīrīṇam \|\| 39 \|\|*

That state has neither length, coarseness, fineness, peer, affection, splendour, shape, passion nor movement. 39

## \|\| 1.14.40 \|\|

*anākāśamasaṃsparśamagandhamarasaṃ ca yat \|*  
*acakṣuśrotramacalamavākpāṇimamānisam \|\| 40 \|\|*

It lies beyond space, touch, smell, taste, eyes and ears. It’s unmoving, having neither voice, nor hands, nor mind. 40

## \|\| 1.14.41 \|\|

*anāmagomatrasukhamatejaskamahetukam \|*  
*abhayaṃ bhrāntirahitama nidramajarāmaram \|\| 41 \|\|*

It has neither name, lineage, comfort, glory, cause, fear, doubt, sleep nor waking. 41

## \|\| 1.14.42 \|\|

*arajośabdamamṛtamaplutaṃ yadasaṃvṛtam \|*  
*pūrvāpareṇa vai yasmistadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam \|\| 42 \|\|*

Without passion, sound, death, decline or limit, nothing exists before or after Viṣṇu’s highest state. 42

## \|\| 1.14.43 \|\|

*parameśatvaguṇavatsarvabhūtama saṃśayam \|*  
*natāsma tatpadaṃ viṣṇorjihvādṛggocaraṃ na yat \|\| 43 \|\|*

We bow to that state which has the quality of highest supremacy, which is in all beings but depends on none, and which exists beyond the range of tongue and eye. 43

## \|\| 1.14.44 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ pracetaso viṣṇuṃ stuvantastatsamādhayaḥ \|*  
*daśavarṣasahasrāṇi tapaścerurmahārṇave \|\| 44 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus praising Viṣṇu while contemplating him, the Pracetases undertook austerities on the ocean floor for ten thousand years. 44

## \|\| 1.14.45 \|\|

*tataḥ prasanno bhagavāṃsteṣāmantarjale hariḥ \|*  
*dadau darśanamunnidranīlotpaladalacchaviḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

Lord Hari, complexioned like a fully opened azure lotus petal, was gratified and appeared before them underwater. 45

## \|\| 1.14.46 \|\|

*patatri rājamārūḍhamavalokya pracetasaḥ \|*  
*praṇipetuḥ śirobhis taṃ bhaktibhārāvanāmitaiḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

Seeing Viṣṇu mounted on Garuḍa, king of birds, the Pracetases fell down before him, bowing their heads in reverence. 46

## \|\| 1.14.47 \|\|

*tatastānāha bhagavānvrayatāmīpsato varaḥ \|*  
*prasāda sumukhohaṃ vo varadaḥ samupasthitaḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

The lord then said to them, ‘Ask for anything you desire, as I’m inclined to favour you and have come to grant your wish.’ 47

## \|\| 1.14.48 \|\|

*tatastamūcurvaradaṃ praṇipatya pracetasaḥ \|*  
*yathā pitrā samādiṣṭaṃ prajānāṃ vṛddhikāraṇam \|\| 48 \|\|*

The Pracetases, bowing before the wish-granting lord, reported that their father had instructed them to raise the population. 48

## \|\| 1.14.49 \|\|

*sa cāpi devastaṃ dattvā yathabhilaṣitaṃ varam \|*  
*antardhānaṃ jagāmāśu te ca niścakramurjalāt \|\| 49 \|\|*

The deity allowed the boon they requested, then withdrew from sight. The Pracetases then left behind the waters of the ocean. 49

## Chapter 15 - The Pracetases, Māriṣā, the Ādityas and Prahlāda

## \|\| 1.15.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tapaścaratsu pṛthivīṃ pracetaḥsu mahīruhāḥ \|*  
*arakṣyamāṇāmāvavrurbabhūvātha prajākṣayaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Parāśara:

While the Pracetases were undertaking their austerities, the unprotected earth was overgrown with vegetation and the people suffered. 1

## \|\| 1.15.2 \|\|

*nāśakanmaruto vātuṃ vṛtaṃ khamabhavaddrumaiḥ \|*  
*daśavarṣasahasrāṇi na śakuśceṣṭituṃ prajāḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

The winds had ceased to blow, the sky was filled with clouds and for ten thousand years folk could do no work. 2

## \|\| 1.15.3 \|\|

*tāndṛṣṭvā jalaniṣkrāntāḥ sarve kruddhāḥ pracetasaḥ \|*  
*mukhebhyo vāyumagniṃ ca te 'sṛjan jātamanyavaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

The Pracetases, emerging from the ocean, saw all this and were enraged. From their mouths blew wind and fire born of their anger. 3

## \|\| 1.15.4 \|\|

*unmūlānatha tānvṛkṣānkṛtvā vāyuraśoṣayat \|*  
*tānagniradahad ghoras tatrābhūd drumasaṃkṣayaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

The wind uprooted trees and dried them out. The terrible fire then burned them, consuming all the vegetation. 4

## \|\| 1.15.5 \|\|

*drumakṣayam atho dṛṣṭvā kiñcicchiṣṭeṣu śākhiṣu \|*  
*upagamyābravīdetānrājā somaḥ prajāpatīn \|\| 5 \|\|*

Seeing the ruin of the forests with so little left behind, Soma, the regal deity of the moon, approached the patriarchs and said, 5

## \|\| 1.15.6 \|\|

*kopaṃ yacchata rājānaḥ śṛṇudhvañca vaco mama \|*  
*sandhānaṃ vaḥ kariṣyāmi saha kṣitiruhair aham \|\| 6 \|\|*

‘Don’t be angry, your majesties, and listen to my words. I’ll make a deal with you about these trees. 6

## \|\| 1.15.7 \|\|

*ratnabhūtā ca kanyeyaṃ vārkṣeyī varavarṇinī \|*  
*bhaviṣyajjānatā pūrvaṃ mayā gobhir vivardhitā \|\| 7 \|\|*

Because I’m able to foresee the future, with my light I raised this precious girl, the fair-faced daughter of the forest. 7

## \|\| 1.15.8 \|\|

*māriṣā nāma nāmnaiṣā vṛkṣāṇāmiti nirmitā \|*  
*bhāryā vo 'stu mahābhāgā dhruvaṃ vaṃśavivardhinī \|\| 8 \|\|*

Māriṣā by name, she is indeed the offspring of the trees. Why don’t you marry this lucky girl, as she will doubtless bring about the increase of your race? 8

## \|\| 1.15.9 \|\|

*yuṣmākaṃ teja sordhena mama cārdhena tejasaḥ \|*  
*asyāmutpatsyate vidvāndakṣonāma prajāpatiḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Māriṣā will bear a son with half your splendour and half of mine, and he’ll become the prudent patriarch known as Dakṣa. 9

## \|\| 1.15.10 \|\|

*mama cāṃśena saṃyukto yuṣmattejomayena vai \|*  
*tejasāgnisamo bhūyaḥ prajāḥ saṃvardhayiṣyati \|\| 10 \|\|*

Endowed with a part of me and blessed with your energy, he’ll blaze like fire, causing humanity to thrive. 10

## \|\| 1.15.11 \|\|

*kaṇḍurnāma muniḥ pūrvamāsīdvedavidāṃ varaḥ \|*  
*muramye gomatītīre ma tepe paramaṃ tapaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

In former times, there was a sage named Kaṇḍu, an outstanding Vedic expert, who undertook extreme austerities on the Gomatī River’s pleasant banks. 11

## \|\| 1.15.12 \|\|

*tatkṣobhāya surendreṇa pramlocākhyā varāpsarāḥ \|*  
*prayuktā kṣobhayāmāsa tamṛṣiṃ sā śucismitā \|\| 12 \|\|*

To distract him, Indra, king of gods, engaged a lovely apsaras named Pramlocā, and the sweetly smiling woman succeeded in diverting that sagacious man. 12

## \|\| 1.15.13 \|\|

*kṣobhitaḥ sa tayā sārdhaṃ varṣāṇāmadhikaṃ śatam \|*  
*atiṣṭhanmandaradroṇyāṃ viṣayāsaktamānasaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Distracted by Pramlocā, Kaṇḍu lived with her for a hundred years or more in a valley on Mount Mandara’s slopes, his heart engrossed in sensual pleasures. 13

## \|\| 1.15.14 \|\|

*taṃ sā prāha mahābhāga gantumicchāmyahaṃ divam \|*  
*prasādasumukho brahmannanujñāṃ dātumarhasi \|\| 14 \|\|*

One day, Pramlocā said to that fortunate sage, ‘I want to return to heaven. Look kindly on me, brahmin, and grant that I may leave.’ 14

## \|\| 1.15.15 \|\|

*tayaivamuktaḥ sa munistasyāmāsaktamānasaḥ \|*  
*dināni katicidbhadre sthīyatāmityabhāṣata \|\| 15 \|\|*

After she had spoken, the sage, whose heart was ever set on her, replied, ‘Just stay a few more days, my dear.’ 15

## \|\| 1.15.16 \|\|

*evamuktā tatastena sāgraṃ varṣaśataṃ punaḥ \|*  
*bubhuje viṣayāṃstanvī tena sākaṃ mahātmanā \|\| 16 \|\|*

Thus addressed by that great sage, the slender girl spent another century enjoying herself with him. 16

## \|\| 1.15.17 \|\|

*anujñāṃ dehi bhagavan vrajāmi tridaśālayam \|*  
*uktastatheti sa punaḥ sthīyatāmityabhāṣata \|\| 17 \|\|*

‘Please permit me, master, to return to the realm of the thirteen gods,’ she begged the sage, but again he begged her to remain. 17

## \|\| 1.15.18 \|\|

*punargate varṣaśate sādhika sā śubhānanā \|*  
*yāmītyāha divaṃ brahmanpraṇayasmitaśobhanam \|\| 18 \|\|*

When another hundred years had passed, that fair-faced girl said with a bright and loving smile, ‘Brahmin, I’m going back to heaven.’ 18

## \|\| 1.15.19 \|\|

*uktastayaivaṃ sa munirupaguhyāyate kṣaṇām \|*  
*ihāsyatāṃ kṣaṇaṃ subhu cirakālaṃ gamiṣyasi \|\| 19 \|\|*

Hearing this, the sage embraced the apsaras with almond eyes. ‘Stay a while,’ said he, ‘as you’ll be gone so long, and your eyebrows are so lovely.’ 19

## \|\| 1.15.20 \|\|

*sā krīḍamānā suśroṇī saha tenarṣiṇā punaḥ \|*  
*śatadvayaṃ kiñcidūnaṃ varṣaṇāmanvatiṣṭhata \|\| 20 \|\|*

Fearing the sage’s curse, fair-hipped Pramlocā stayed with him for another two hundred years or so. 20

## \|\| 1.15.21 \|\|

*gamanāya mahābhāga devarājaniveśanam \|*  
*proktaḥ proktastayā tanvyā sthīyatāmityabhāṣata \|\| 21 \|\|*

Again and again, the slender girl told the lucky sage that she was leaving for the home of Indra, king of gods, but he always talked her out of it. 21

## \|\| 1.15.22 \|\|

*tasya śāpabhayādbhītā dākṣiṇyena ca dakṣiṇā \|*  
*proktā praṇayabhaṅgartivedinī na jahau munim \|\| 22 \|\|*

Still fearing the sage’s curse, the respectful woman spoke sweetly and stayed with him, because she understood the pain of separation that lovers feel. 22

## \|\| 1.15.23 \|\|

*tayā ca ramatastasya paramarṣeraharniśam \|*  
*navaṃnavamabhūtprama manmathāviṣṭacetasaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

While the best of sages enjoyed himself with Pramlocā by day and night, his love for her was constantly renewed and his heart was filled with passion. 23

## \|\| 1.15.24 \|\|

*ekadā tu tvarāyukto niścakrāmoṭajānmuniḥ \|*  
*niṣkrāmantaṃ ca kutreti gamyate prāha sā śubhā \|\| 24 \|\|*

One day, the sage was hurrying from his hut and, as he did so, the splendid woman asked where he was going. 24

## \|\| 1.15.25 \|\|

*ityuktaḥ sa tayā prāha parivṛttamahaḥ śubhe \|*  
*sandhyopāstiṃ kariṣyāmi kriyāloponyathā bhavet \|\| 25 \|\|*

‘The day is done, my dear, so I must perform my twilight rituals,’ he replied, ‘otherwise an essential rite will be neglected.’ 25

## \|\| 1.15.26 \|\|

*tataḥ prahasya sudadī taṃ sā prāha mahāmunim \|*  
*kimadya sarvadharmajña parivṛttamahastava \|\| 26 \|\|*

With a laugh, the cheerful girl replied to him, ‘You know all about virtue, but is just one day really done for you? 26

## \|\| 1.15.27 \|\|

*bahūnāṃ vipra varṣāṇāṃ parivṛttama hastava \|*  
*gatametanna kurute vismayaṃ kasya kathyatām \|\| 27 \|\|*

One day for you appears to last for many years, brahmin. Who wouldn’t be amazed by this? Please explain your meaning.’ 27

## \|\| 1.15.28 \|\|

*muniruvāca*  
*prātastvamāgatā bhadre nadītīramidaṃ śubham \|*  
*mayā dṛṣṭāsi tanvaṅgi praviṣṭāsi mamāśramam \|\| 28 \|\|*

The sage:

This morning you came to this lovely riverbank, my dear. I saw you there, so slender, and you came back to my ashram. 28

## \|\| 1.15.29 \|\|

*iyaṃ ca vartate sandhyā pariṇāmamahargatam \|*  
*upahāsaḥ kimarthoyaṃ sadbhāvaḥ kathyatāṃ mama \|\| 29 \|\|*

Now it’s dusk and the day is done. Why are you laughing? Tell me truly. 29

## \|\| 1.15.30 \|\|

*pramlocovāca*  
*pratyūṣasyāgatā brahman satyametanna tanmṛṣā \|*  
*nanvasya tasya kālasya gatānyabdaśatāni te \|\| 30 \|\|*

Pramlocā:

It’s true I came at dawn, brahmin—there is no doubt of that. But many hundred years have passed since then! 30

## \|\| 1.15.31 \|\|

*soma uvāca*  
*tataḥ sa sādhvaso viprastāṃ papracchāyatekṣaṇām \|*  
*kathyatāṃ bhīru kaḥ kālas tvayā me ramataḥ saha \|\| 31 \|\|*

Soma:

Then the brahmin, filled with consternation, asked the girl with almond eyes, ‘Tell me, timid one, how long have we been enjoying ourselves together?’ 31

## \|\| 1.15.32 \|\|

*pramlocovāca*  
*saptottarāmyatītāni navavarṣaśatāni te \|*  
*māsāś ca ṣaṭtathaivānyatsamatītaṃ dinatrayam \|\| 32 \|\|*

Pramlocā:

Nine hundred and seven years, six months and three days. 32

## \|\| 1.15.33 \|\|

*ṛṣiruvāca*  
*satyaṃ bhīru vadasy etat parihāso 'thavā śubhe \|*  
*dinamekamahaṃ manyetvayā sārdhamihāsitam \|\| 33 \|\|*

The sage:

Is this the truth or some kind of joke, you lovely timid girl? I thought we’d spent a single day together. 33

## \|\| 1.15.34 \|\|

*pramlocovāca*  
*vadiṣyāmyanṛtaṃ brahmankathamatra tavāntike \|*  
*viśeṣeṇādya bhavatā pṛṣṭā mārgānuvartinā \|\| 34 \|\|*

Pramlocā:

How could I mislead you, brahmin, especially as you, a sage on virtue’s path, inquired of me. 34

## \|\| 1.15.35 \|\|

*soma uvāca*  
*niśamya tadvacaḥ satyaṃ sa munirnṛpanandanāḥ \|*  
*dhikdhik māmityatīvetthaṃ ninindātmānamātmanā \|\| 35 \|\|*

Soma:

When the sage heard her explanation, princes, he cursed himself and cried, ‘Shame on me! Shame on me!’ 35

## \|\| 1.15.36 \|\|

*muniruvāca*  
*tapāṃsi mama naṣṭani hataṃ brahmavidāṃ dhanam \|*  
*hato vivekaḥ kenāpi yoṣinmohāya nirmitāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

The sage:

‘My austerities have been wasted, my store of Vedic knowledge lost, and my judgement’s been impaired. Someone must have sent this woman to distract me. 36

## \|\| 1.15.37 \|\|

*ūrmiṣaṭkātigaṃ brahma jñeyamātmajayena me \|*  
*matireṣā hṛtā yena dhik taṃ kāmaṃ mahāgraham \|\| 37 \|\|*

I’d conquered my own impulses and was about to grasp the Absolute, which lies beyond the sixfold waves of pain. Curse that greatest evil, desire, which robbed me of my resolution! 37

## \|\| 1.15.38 \|\|

*vratāni vedavedyāptikārāṇānyakhilāni ca \|*  
*narakagrāmamargeṇa saṅgenāpahṛtāni me \|\| 38 \|\|*

All my vows, which would have yielded Vedic knowledge, have been stolen by a tryst that leads to hell.’ 38

## \|\| 1.15.39 \|\|

*vinindyetthaṃ sa dharmajñaḥ svayamātmānamātmanā \|*  
*tāmapsarasamāsīnāmidaṃ vacanamabravīt \|\| 39 \|\|*

Uttering these curses, the sage, who apprehended virtue, shouted at the apsaras, who sat nearby: 39

## \|\| 1.15.40 \|\|

*gaccha pāpe yathākāmaṃ yatkāryaṃ tatkṛtaṃ tvayā \|*  
*devarājasya matkṣobhaṃ kurvantyā bhāvaceṣṭitaiḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

‘Get out, you wicked woman. You distracted me by making love, just as the king of gods intended. 40

## \|\| 1.15.41 \|\|

*na tvāṃ karoṇyahaṃ bhasma krodhatīvreṇa vahninā \|*  
*satāṃ saptapadaṃ maitrumuṣi tohaṃ tvayā saha \|\| 41 \|\|*

I should burn you to ashes with the fire of my anger. On the other hand, like virtuous people, we took the seven steps of marriage and lived together as a couple. 41

## \|\| 1.15.42 \|\|

*atha vā tava ko doṣaḥ kiṃ vā kupyāmyahaṃ tava \|*  
*mamaiva doṣo nitarāṃ yenāhamajitendriyaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

You’ve done no wrong. Why should I be angry with you? It’s my own fault because I couldn’t curb my senses. 42

## \|\| 1.15.43 \|\|

*yayā śakrapriyārthinyā kṛto me tapaso vyayaḥ \|*  
*tvayā dhiktāṃ mahāmohamañjṛṣāṃ sujugupsitām \|\| 43 \|\|*

You wanted Indra’s favour and disrupted my austerities. Shame on you! You’re a bundle of distractions best avoided!’ 43

## \|\| 1.15.44 \|\|

*soma uvāca*  
*yāvaditthaṃ sa viprarṣis tāṃ bravīti samudhyamām \|*  
*tāvadgalatsvedajalā sābabhūvātivepathuḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

Soma:

While that sagacious brahmin abused the girl with the lovely waist, she began to sweat and tremble. 44

## \|\| 1.15.45 \|\|

*pravepamānāṃ satataṃ khinnagātralatāṃ satīm \|*  
*gaccha gaccheti sakrodhamuvāca munisattamaḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

The poor lass shook as sweat rolled down her slender limbs. ‘Get out! Get out!’ the best of sages shouted angrily. 45

## \|\| 1.15.46 \|\|

*sā tu nirbhartsitā tena vināṣkramya tadāśramāt \|*  
*ākāśagāminī svedaṃ mamārja tarupallavaiḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

Thus abused by Kaṇḍu, the sky-going apsaras fled the ashram and, as she flew, the forest’s tender branches brushed away her perspiration. 46

## \|\| 1.15.47 \|\|

*nirmārjamānā gātrāṇi galatsvedajalāni vai \|*  
*vṛkṣāddhṛkṣaṃ yayau balā tadagraruṇapallavaiḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

As she passed from tree to tree, the delicate shoots that grew on each brushed against her sweaty limbs. 47

## \|\| 1.15.48 \|\|

*ṛṣiṇā yastadā garbhastasyā dehe samāhitaḥ \|*  
*nirjagāma sa romāñcasvedarūpī tadaṅgataḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

The child within her womb, which she’d conceived with Kaṇḍu, emerged from her limbs in the form of droplets, such as attend excitement. 48

## \|\| 1.15.49 \|\|

*taṃ vṛkṣā jagṛhurgarbhamekaṃ cakre tu mārutaḥ \|*  
*mayā cāpyāyito gobhiḥ sa tadā vavadhe śanaiḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

The trees collected all the drops, and the wind combined them in a single body. I nurtured that body with my rays, and in this way the infant slowly grew. 49

## \|\| 1.15.50 \|\|
