*vṛkṣāgragarbhasambhūtā māriṣākhyā varānanā \|*  
*tāṃ pradāsyanti vo vṛkṣāḥ kopa eṣa praśāmyatām \|\| 50 \|\|*

That baby, begotten among the branches, became this fair-faced girl, Māriṣā. The trees now offer her to you, so set aside your anger. 50

## \|\| 1.15.51 \|\|

*kaṇḍorapatyamevaṃ sā vṛkṣebhyaś ca samudgatā \|*  
*mamāpatyaṃ tathā vāyoḥ pramlocātanayā ca sā \|\| 51 \|\|*

Māriṣā, child of Kaṇḍu, brought forth by trees, is also daughter of the wind, of Pramlocā and of me, the Moon. 51

## \|\| 1.15.52 \|\|

*sa cāpi bhagavān kaṇḍuḥ kṣīṇe tapasi sattamaḥ \|*  
*puruṣottamākhyaṃ maitreya viṣṇorāyatanaṃ yayau \|\| 52 \|\|*

When holy Kaṇḍu’s austerities had been reduced to nothing, best of princes, he retired to the lofty mountain known as Puruṣottama, abode of Viṣṇu. 52

## \|\| 1.15.53 \|\|

*tatraikāgramatirbhūtvā cakārārādhanaṃ hareḥ \|*  
*brahmapāramayaṃ kurvañjapamekāgramānasaḥ \|*  
*ūrdhvabāhurmahāyogī sthitvāsau bhūpanandanāḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

There, the great ascetic stood, his arms above his head, praising Hari and reciting a prayer that encompasses the furthest reaches of the Absolute, princes, his heart set on the deity alone. 53

## \|\| 1.15.54 \|\|

*pracetasa ūcuḥ*  
*brahmapāraṃ mune śrotumicchāmaḥ paramaṃ stavam \|*  
*japatā kaṇḍunā devo yenārādhyata keśavaḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

The Pracetases:

We’d like to hear that sage’s brilliant song of praise, which reached the Absolute’s outer limits, which Kaṇḍu sang to worship heavenly Keśava. 54

## \|\| 1.15.55 \|\|

*soma uvāca*  
*pāraṃ paraṃ viṣṇurapārapāraḥ paraḥ parebhyaḥ pāmāratharūpī \|*  
*sabrahpāraḥ parapārabhūtaḥ paraḥ parāṇāmapi pārapāraḥ \|\| 55 \|\|*

Soma:

Viṣṇu is beyond the furthest limit, further than the furthest, higher than the highest and is the form of the utmost destination. He is the furthest reach of the Absolute, he is beyond the furthest limit, highest of the high, furthest of the far. 55

## \|\| 1.15.56 \|\|

*sa kāraṇaṃ kāraṇatastatopi tasyāpi hetuḥ \|*  
*kāryeṣu caivaṃ saha karmakartṛrūpair aśeṣair avatīha sarvam \|\| 56 \|\|*

He is the cause of the universe and that cause’s cause. He is that cause’s cause, and the cause of the highest cause. Thus, in every act, in every form of action and of agent, he protects the universe. 56

## \|\| 1.15.57 \|\|

*brahma prabhurbrahma sa sarvabhūto brahma prajānāṃ patiracyuto 'sau \|*  
*brahmāvyayaṃ nityamajaṃ sa viṣṇurapakṣayādyair akhilair asaṅgi \|\| 57 \|\|*

He is the Absolute, the lord and the Absolute that is everything. He is the Absolute, Acyuta, unfailing father of creation. He is the changeless Absolute, Viṣṇu, eternal and unborn, free from all beginnings and all endings. 57

## \|\| 1.15.58 \|\|

*brahmākṣaramajaṃ nityaṃ yathāsau puruṣottamaḥ \|*  
*tathā rāgādayo doṣaḥ prayāntu praśamaṃ mama \|\| 58 \|\|*

Just as the highest spirit, the Absolute, is imperishable, unborn and everlasting, so may all my passions and other faults be overcome. 58

## \|\| 1.15.59 \|\|

*soma uvāca*  
*etadbrahma parākhyaṃ vai saṃstavaṃ paramaṃ japan \|*  
*avāpa paramāṃsiddhiṃ sa tamārādhya keśavam \|\| 59 \|\|*

Soma:

Reciting this excellent song of praise, known as the ‘Prayer of the Supreme Absolute’, Kaṇḍu worshipped Keśava and reached the highest goal. 59

## \|\| 1.15.60 \|\|

*iyaṃ ca mārīṣā pūrvamāsīdya tāṃ bravīmi vaḥ \|*  
*kāryagauravametasyāḥ kathane phaladāyi vaḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

Now I’ll tell you who Māriṣā was in her previous life, because the significance of her deeds in this account will benefit you. 60

## \|\| 1.15.61 \|\|

*aputrā prāgiyaṃ viṣṇuṃ mṛte bhartari sattamāḥ \|*  
*bhīpapatnī mahābhāgā toṣayāmāsa bhaktitaḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

In earlier times, outstanding princes, she was a fortunate queen. After her husband’s death and being childless, she gratified Lord Viṣṇu with her devotion. 61

## \|\| 1.15.62 \|\|

*ārādhitastayā viṣṇuḥ prāha pratyakṣatāṃ gataḥ \|*  
*varaṃ vṛṇīṣveti śubhe sā ca prāhātmavāñchitam \|\| 62 \|\|*

Worshipped by her, Viṣṇu, appearing to that splendid woman, said, ‘Make a wish.’ She then revealed her heart’s desire: 62

## \|\| 1.15.63 \|\|

*bhagavanbālavaidhavyādvṛthājanmāhamīdṛśī \|*  
*mandabhāgyā samudbhutā viphalā ca jagatpate \|\| 63 \|\|*

‘Widowed at a tender age, Lord, my birth has been in vain. Father of creation, I was born amid misfortune and futility. 63

## \|\| 1.15.64 \|\|

*bhavantu patayaḥ ślāghyā mama janmani janmani \|*  
*tvatprasādāttathā putraḥ prajāpatisamostu me \|\| 64 \|\|*

May I therefore have a worthy husband in every birth. Similarly, by your mercy, may I have a son who’s equal to the patriarchs. 64

## \|\| 1.15.65 \|\|

*kulaṃ śīlaṃ vayaḥ satyaṃ dākṣiṇyaṃ kṣiprakāritā \|*  
*avisaṃvāditā sattvaṃ vṛddhasevā kṛtajñatā \|\| 65 \|\|*

May I have both wealth and beauty, may I be agreeable in the sight of all, Viṣṇu, and may I not be issued from a womb.’ 65

## \|\| 1.15.66 \|\|

*rūpasampatsamāyuktā sarvasya priyadarśanā \|*  
*ayonijā ca jāyeyaṃ tvanprasādādādhekṣaja \|\| 66 \|\|*

Soma:

After she had spoken, Hṛṣīkeśa, chief of gods, highest lord and wish-bestower, lifted up the deeply bowing woman and addressed these words to her: 66

## \|\| 1.15.67 \|\|

*soma uvāca*  
*tayaivamukto deveśo hṛṣīkeśa uvāca tām \|*  
*praṇāmanamrāmutthāpya varadaḥ parameśvaraḥ \|\| 67 \|\|*

The Lord:

‘In a single lifetime, you will have ten husbands, heroic and renowned for noble deeds. 67

## \|\| 1.15.68 \|\|

*deva uvāca*  
*bhaviṣyanti mahāvīryā ekasminneva janmani \|*  
*prakhyātodārakarṇāṇo bhavatyāḥ patayo daśa \|\| 68 \|\|*

And you, splendid woman, will have a mighty son, courageous, strong and blessed with the qualities of a patriarch. 68

## \|\| 1.15.69 \|\|

*putrañca sumahāvīryaṃ mahābalaparākramam \|*  
*prajāpatiguṇair yuktaṃ tvamavāpsyasi śobhane \|\| 69 \|\|*

He will be the founder of many lineages on this earth, and his progeny will fill the threefold worlds. 69

## \|\| 1.15.70 \|\|

*vaṃśānāṃ tasya kartṛtvaṃ jagatyasminbhaviṣyati \|*  
*trailokyamakhilā sūtistasya cāpūrayiṣyati \|\| 70 \|\|*

You will not be issued from a womb, but will be a virtuous woman endowed with beauty, nobility and other wished-for qualities, and, by my grace, you’ll delight the hearts of men.’ 70

## \|\| 1.15.71 \|\|

*tvaṃ cāpyayonijā sādhvī rūpaudāryaguṇānvitā \|*  
*manaḥ prītikarī nṝṇāṃ matprasādādbhaviṣyasi \|\| 71 \|\|*

Having spoken to that girl with almond eyes, the lord withdrew from sight and she was born Māriṣā and will be a helpmeet for you, royal princes. 71

## \|\| 1.15.72 \|\|

*ityuktvāntardadhe devastāṃ viśālavilocanām \|*  
*sā ceyaṃ māriṣā jātā yuṣmatpatnī nṛpātmajāḥ \|\| 72 \|\|*

Parāśara:

In accord with Soma’s orders, the Pracetases shed their anger and accepted Māriṣā from the forest as their spouse. 72

## \|\| 1.15.73 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tataḥ somasya vacanājjagṛhuste pracetasaḥ \|*  
*saṃhṛtya kopaṃ vṛkṣebhyaḥ patnīdharmoṇa mārīṣām \|\| 73 \|\|*

With the ten Pracetases, Māriṣā gave birth to Dakṣa, that mighty patriarch, who was born as Brahmā’s son in a previous life. 73

## \|\| 1.15.74 \|\|

*daśabhyastu pracetobhyo mārīṣāyāṃ prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*jajñe dakṣo mahābhāgo yaḥ pūrvaṃ brahmaṇo 'bhavat \|\| 74 \|\|*

This fortunate Dakṣa brought forth sons for the peopling of the world, wise sage, and to provide himself with offspring. 74

## \|\| 1.15.75 \|\|

*sa tu dakṣo mahābhāgaḥsṛṣṭyarthaṃ sumahāmate \|*  
*putrānutpādayāmāsa prajāsṛṣṭyarthamātmanaḥ \|\| 75 \|\|*

Obeying Brahmā’s orders, Dakṣa established moving and unmoving things, creatures with two feet and those with four, to further procreation. 75

## \|\| 1.15.76 \|\|

*avarāṃś ca varāṃścaiva dvipadotha catuṣpadān \|*  
*ādeśaṃ brahmaṇaḥ kurvan sṛṣṭyarthaṃ samupasthitaḥ \|\| 76 \|\|*

Dakṣa brought forth fifty women with his mental power, gave ten to Dharma, thirteen to Kaśyapa and twenty-seven to the Moon, all of whom were charged with regulating time. 76

## \|\| 1.15.77 \|\|

*sa sṛṣṭvā manasā dakṣaḥ paścādasṛjata striyaḥ \|*  
*dadau sa daśa dharmāya kaśyapāya trayodaśa \|*  
*kālasya nayane yuktāḥ saptaviṃśatimindave \|\| 77 \|\|*

These women then gave rise to gods, Daityas, nāgas, cattle, birds, gandharvas, apsarases, Dānavas and the rest. 77

## \|\| 1.15.78 \|\|

*tāsu devās tathā daityā nāgā gāvas tathā khagāḥ \|*  
*gandharvāpsarasaścaiva dānavādyāś ca jajñire \|\| 78 \|\|*

Since then, Maitreya, all creatures have been born of sexual union. The offspring of beings who arose before that time were born of willpower, sight or touch, or were brought forth by the various extreme austerities of those who undertook them. 78

## \|\| 1.15.79 \|\|

*tataḥ prabhṛti maitreya prajā maithunasambhavāḥ \|*  
*saṃkalpāddarśanātsparśātpūrveṣāmabhavan prajāḥ \|*  
*tapoviśeṣaiḥ siddhānāṃ tadātyantatapasvinām \|\| 79 \|\|*

Maitreya:

I’d heard before that Dakṣa had been born from the thumb of Brahmā’s right hand, sage. How could he be born a second time as the son of the Pracetases? 79

## \|\| 1.15.80 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*aṅguṣṭhāddakṣiṇāddakṣaḥ pūrvaṃ jāto mayā śrutaḥ \|*  
*kathaṃ prācetaso bhūyaḥ samutpanno mahāmune \|\| 80 \|\|*

This grave doubt weighs on my mind, brahmin, because Dakṣa was Soma’s grandson, but at the same time was his father-in-law! 80

## \|\| 1.15.81 \|\|

*eṣa me saṃśayo brahmansumahānhṛdi vartate \|*  
*yaddaihitraś ca somasya punaḥ śvaśuratāṃ gataḥ \|\| 81 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Beings are always subject to both birth and death, you best of sages. The wise are not confused by this, nor are those endowed with special insight. 81

## \|\| 1.15.82 \|\|

*śrīrāśara uvāca*  
*utpattiś ca nirodhaś ca nityo bhūteṣu sarvāda \|*  
*ṛṣayotra na suhyanti ye cānye divyacakṣuṣaḥ \|\| 82 \|\|*

Dakṣa and the other patriarchs exist in every era, foremost seer, and they also cease to be. This should not perplex the wise. 82

## \|\| 1.15.83 \|\|

*yugeyuge bhavantyete dakṣādyā munisattama \|*  
*punaścaivaṃ niruddhyante vidvāṃstatra na muhyati \|\| 83 \|\|*

In former times, there was no concept of young or old among them, best of brahmins. The severity of their austerities and their power were the only standards of comparison. 83

## \|\| 1.15.84 \|\|

*kāniṣṭhyaṃ jyaiṣṭhyamapyeṣāṃ pūrvaṃ nābhūdddvijottama \|*  
*tapa eva garīyobhūtprabhā vaścaiva kāraṇam \|\| 84 \|\|*

Maitreya:

Brahmin, describe to me in detail the origin of the deities, Dānavas, gandharvas, nāgas and rākṣasas. 84

## \|\| 1.15.85 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*devānāṃ dānavānāṃ ca gandharvoragarakṣasām \|*  
*utpattiṃ vistareṇeha mama brahmanprakīrtaya \|\| 85 \|\|*

Parāśara:

In former times, Brahmā, the self-arisen deity, commanded Dakṣa to bring forth living things, wise sage. Listen to how he did so. 85

## \|\| 1.15.86 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*prajāḥ sṛjeti vyādiṣṭaḥ pūrvaṃ dakṣaḥ svayaṃbhuvā \|*  
*yathā sasarja bhūtāni tathā śṛṇu mahāmune \|\| 86 \|\|*

Dakṣa created living beings before—including deities, sages, gandharvas, demigods and nāgas—with his mental energy. 86

## \|\| 1.15.87 \|\|

*mānasānyeva bhūtāni pūrvaṃ dakṣo 'sṛjattadā \|*  
*devānṛṣīnsagandharvānasurānpannagāṃs tathā \|\| 87 \|\|*

But the beings that creative lord brought forth did not increase in number, and, after much reflection, 87

## \|\| 1.15.88 \|\|

*yadāsya sṛjamānasya na vyavardhanta tāḥ prajāḥ \|*  
*tataḥ saṃcintya sa punaḥ sṛṣṭihetoḥ prajāpatiḥ \|\| 88 \|\|*

Desiring the procreation of varied kinds of offspring, he determined sexual union should become the normal means of reproduction. Accordingly, he wedded Asiknī, the daughter of the patriarch Vīraṇa, who had undertaken dire austerities and was a great supporter of the world. 88

## \|\| 1.15.89 \|\|

*maithunenaiva dharmeṇa sisṛkṣurvividhāḥ prajāḥ \|*  
*asaknīmāvahatkanyāṃ vīraṇasya prajāpateḥ \|*  
*sutāṃ sutapasā yuktāṃ mahatīṃ lokadhāriṇīm \|\| 89 \|\|*

Following this, the mighty patriarch Dakṣa had five thousand sons with her to raise the population. 89

## \|\| 1.15.90 \|\|

*atha putrasahasrāṇai vairuṇyāṃ pañca vīryavān \|*  
*asiknyāṃ janayāmāsa sargahetoḥ prajāpatiḥ \|\| 90 \|\|*

Seeing these sons were keen on procreation, brahmin, Nārada, that heavenly sage, appeared to them and spoke these honeyed words. 90

## \|\| 1.15.91 \|\|

*tāndṛṣṭvā nārado vipra saṃvivardhayiṣūnprajāḥ \|*  
*saṃgamya priyasaṃvādo devarṣiridamabravīt \|\| 91 \|\|*

Nārada:

You mighty Haryaśvas will have progeny—your wishes have been noted—but hear what I have to say: 91

## \|\| 1.15.92 \|\|

*he haryaśvā mahāvīryāḥ prajā yūyaṃ kariṣyatha \|*  
*īdṛśo dṛśyate yatno bhavatāṃ śrūyatāmīdam \|\| 92 \|\|*

It’s a pity that you’re so naive and do not know the limits of the earth—above, below or on all sides. How will you populate it otherwise? 92

## \|\| 1.15.93 \|\|

*bāliśa bata yūyaṃ vai nāsyā jānīta vai bhuvaḥ \|*  
*antarūrdhvamadhaścaiva kathaṃ sṛkṣyatha vai prajāḥ \|\| 93 \|\|*

Only when the upper, lower and sideward paths have all been cleared will you innocents behold the limits of the world. 93

## \|\| 1.15.94 \|\|

*ūrdhvaṃ tiryagadhaścaiva yadāpratihatā gatiḥ \|*  
*tadā kasmādbhuvo nāntaṃ sarve drakṣyatha bāliśāḥ \|\| 94 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the Haryaśvas set off in all directions, but to this day they’ve not returned, just as rivers don’t flow backwards from the sea. 94

## \|\| 1.15.95 \|\|

*te tu tadvacanaṃ śrutvā prayātāḥ sarvato diśam \|*  
*adyāpi no nivartante samudrebhya ivāpagāḥ \|\| 95 \|\|*

After they had disappeared, holy Dakṣa, son of the Pracetases, had another thousand sons with Vīraṇa’s daughter. 95

## \|\| 1.15.96 \|\|

*harya śveṣvatha naṣṭoṣu dakṣaḥ prācetasaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*vairuṇyāmatha putrāṇāṃ sahasramasṛjatprabhuḥ \|\| 96 \|\|*

These, the Śabalāśvas, also intent on procreation, brahmin, were dispatched by Nārada with the same instructions as before. 96

## \|\| 1.15.97 \|\|

*vivardhayiṣavaste tu śabalāśvāḥ prajāḥ punaḥ \|*  
*pūrvoktaṃ vacanaṃ brahmannāradenaiva noditāḥ \|\| 97 \|\|*

‘This sage has spoken truly,’ they said to one another. ‘We should doubtless take the paths our brothers took. 97

## \|\| 1.15.98 \|\|

*anyonyamūcuste sarve samyag āha mahāmuniḥ \|*  
*bhratṝṇāṃ padavī caiva gantavyā nātra saṃśayaḥ \|\| 98 \|\|*

Once we know the limits of the world, we’ll be able to populate it.’ 98

## \|\| 1.15.99 \|\|

*jñātvā pramāṇaṃ pṛthvyāś ca prajāḥsṛjyāmahe tataḥ \|\| 99 \|\|*

Off they went in all directions along those very paths, but to this day they’ve not returned, just as rivers don’t flow backwards from the sea. 99

## \|\| 1.15.100 \|\|

*tepi tenaiva mārgeṇa prayātāḥ sarvatomukham \|*  
*adyāpi na nivartante samudrebhya ivāpagāḥ \|*  
*tataḥ prabhṛti vai bhrātā bhrāturanveṣaṇe dvija \|*  
*prayāto naśyati tathā tanna kāryaṃ vijānatā \|\| 100 \|\|*

Ever since that time, one brother who searches for another is lost, brahmin, and that’s why a prudent brother never does so. 100

## \|\| 1.15.101 \|\|

*tāṃ ścāpi naṣṭān vijñāya putrān dakṣaḥ prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*krodhaṃ cakre mahābhāgo nāradaṃ sa śaśāpa ca \|\| 101 \|\|*

When the great patriarch Dakṣa learned those sons had disappeared as well, he was furious and cursed Nārada. 101

## \|\| 1.15.102 \|\|

*sargakāmastato vidvānsa maitreya prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*ṣaṣṭiṃ takṣo 'sṛjatkanyā vairuṇyāmiti naḥ śrutam \|\| 102 \|\|*

But still wishing to populate the earth, Maitreya, Dakṣa had sixty daughters with Vīraṇa’s daughter, so we heard. 102

## \|\| 1.15.103 \|\|

*dadau sa daśa dharmāya kaśyapāya trayodaśa \|*  
*saptaviṃśati somāya catastro 'riṣṭanemine \|\| 103 \|\|*

He gave ten girls to Dharma, thirteen to Kaśyapa, twenty-seven to Soma and four to Ariṣṭanemi, 103

## \|\| 1.15.104 \|\|

*dve caiva bahuputrāya dve caivāṅgirase tathā \|*  
*dvekṛśāśvāya viduṣe tāsāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu \|\| 104 \|\|*

Two to Bahuputra, two to Aṅgiras and two to wise Kṛśāśva. Listen while I tell you all their names: 104

## \|\| 1.15.105 \|\|

*arundhatī vasurjāmirlaṅghā bhānumarutvatī \|*  
*saṃkalpā ca muhūrtā ca sādhyā viśvā ca tādṛśī \|*  
*dharmapatnyo daśatvetāstāsvapatyāni me śṛṇu \|*  
*viśvedevāstu viśvāyāḥ sādhyā sādhyānajāyata \|*  
*marutvatyāṃ sarutvanto vasoś ca vasavaḥ smṛtā \|\| 105 \|\|*

Arundhatī, Vasu, Jāmī, Lambā, Bhānu, Marutvatī, Saṃkalpā, Muhūrtā, Sādhyā and Viśvā: these ten were the wives of Dharma. Now listen while I tell you of their progeny. 105

## \|\| 1.15.106 \|\|

*bhānostu bhānavaḥ putrā muhūrtāyāṃ muhūrtajāḥ \|\| 106 \|\|*

Viśvā gave birth to the divine Viśvedevas, Sādhyā bore the Sādhyas, Marutvatī bore the Maruts, the Vasus are regarded as the progeny of Vasu. 106

## \|\| 1.15.107 \|\|

*laṅghāyāścaiva ghoṣotha nāgavīthī tu jāmitā \|\| 107 \|\|*

Bhānu bore the Bhānus (the Suns), and Muhūrtā bore the Muhūrtas (the Moments). Lambā bore Ghoṣa (Sound), and Nāgavīthī was born of Jāmī. 107

## \|\| 1.15.108 \|\|

*pṛthivīviṣayaṃ sarvamarundhatyāma jāyata \|*  
*saṃkalpāyāstu sarvātmā jajñe saṃkalpa eva hi \|\| 108 \|\|*

All the region of the earth was born from Arundhatī. Saṃkalpā gave birth to Saṃkalpa (Resolve), which is at the heart of everything. 108

## \|\| 1.15.109 \|\|

*ye tvanekavasuprāṇadevā jyotiḥ purogamāḥ \|*  
*vasavoṣṭau samākhyātāsteṣāṃ vakṣyāmi vistaram \|\| 109 \|\|*

I’ll now describe in detail the deities called the Eight Vasus, led by Jyotis (Light), filled with radiance and energy: 109

## \|\| 1.15.110 \|\|

*āpo dhruvaś ca somaś ca dharmaścaivānilo 'nalaḥ \|*  
*pratyūṣaś ca prabhāsaś ca vasavo nāmabhiḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 110 \|\|*

Āpa (Water), Dhruva (Pole Star), Soma (Moon), Dhara (Earth), Anila (Wind), Anala (Fire), Pratyūṣa (Dawn) and Prabhāsa (Splendour): this is how the Vasus are recalled by name. 110

## \|\| 1.15.111 \|\|

*āpasya putro vaitaṇḍaḥ śramaḥ śānto 'dhvanis tathā \|*  
*dhruvasya putro bhagavānkālo lokaprakālanaḥ \|\| 111 \|\|*

Āpa’s sons were Vaitaṇḍa, Śrama (Toil), Śrānta (Fatigue) and Adhuni. Dhruva’s son was holy Kāla (Time), who impels the world. 111

## \|\| 1.15.112 \|\|

*somasya bhagavānvarco varcasvī yena jāyate \|\| 112 \|\|*

Soma’s son was holy Varcas (Power), whose son was Varcasvin (Energy). 112

## \|\| 1.15.113 \|\|

*dharmasya putro draviṇo hutahavyavahas tathā \|*  
*manoharāyāṃ śiśiraḥ prāṇotha ravaṇas tathā \|\| 113 \|\|*

Dhara’s sons with his wife, Manoharā (Attraction), were Draviṇas (Offerings), Hutahavyavaha (Sacrificial Fire), Śiśira (Cold), Prāṇa (Breath) and Ramaṇa (Pleasure). 113

## \|\| 1.15.114 \|\|

*anilasya śivā bhāryā tasyāḥ putro manojavaḥ \|*  
*avijñātagatiścaiva dvau putrāvanilasya tu \|\| 114 \|\|*

Anila’s wife was Śivā. Her sons were Purojava and Avijñātagati. These were the sons she bore with Anila. 114

## \|\| 1.15.115 \|\|

*agniputraḥ kumārastu śarastambe vyajāyata \|*  
*tasya śākho viśākhaś ca naigameyaś ca pṛṣṭhajāḥ \|\| 115 \|\|*

Agni’s son Kumāra was born in a clump of reeds. Śākha, Viśākha and Naigameya were his younger brothers. 115

## \|\| 1.15.116 \|\|

*apatyaṃ kṛttikānāṃ tu kārttikeya iti smṛtaḥ \|\| 116 \|\|*

The son of the Kṛttikās (the Pleiades) was Kārttikeya, the god of war. 116

## \|\| 1.15.117 \|\|

*pratyūṣasya viduḥ putraṃ ṛṣi nāmnātha devalam \|*  
*dvau putrau devalasyāpi kṣamāvantau manīṣiṇau \|\| 117 \|\|*

They called Pratyuṣa’s son the sage Devala. Devala had two wise and patient sons. 117

## \|\| 1.15.118 \|\|

*bṛhaspatestu bhaginī varastrī brahmacāriṇī \|*  
*yogasiddhā jagatkṛtsnamasaktā vicaratyuta \|*  
*prabhāsasya tu sā bhāryā vasūnāmaṣṭamasya tu \|\| 118 \|\|*

Bṛhaspati’s sister, the brilliant celibate woman Yogasiddhā, traversed the whole world without attachment, and became the wife of Prabhāsa, the eighth Vasu. 118

## \|\| 1.15.119 \|\|

*viśvakarmā mahābhāgastasyāṃ jajñe prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*kartā śilpasahasrāṇāṃ tridaśānāṃ ca vārdhakiḥ \|\| 119 \|\|*

Yogasiddhā gave birth to the fortunate patriarch Viśvakarman, the inventor of a thousand arts and craftsman of the thirteen gods. 119

## \|\| 1.15.120 \|\|

*bhūṣaṇānāṃ ca sarveṣāṃ kartāśilpavatāṃ varaḥ \|*  
*yaḥ sarvaiṣāṃ vimānāni devatānāṃ cakāra ha \|*  
*manuṣyāścopajīvanti yasya śilpaṃ mahātmanaḥ \|\| 120 \|\|*

He was creator of every ornament and foremost artisan who made the carriages for all the gods. Humankind depends on skills perfected by that great being. 120

## \|\| 1.15.121 \|\|

*ajaikapād ahirbudhnyas tvaṣṭā rudraś ca vīryavān \|*  
*tvaṣṭuścāpyātmajaḥ putro viśvarūpo mahātapāḥ \|\| 121 \|\|*

His sons were Ajaikapād, Ahirbudhnya, Tvaṣṭṛ and wise Rudra. Tvaṣṭṛ’s own son was the great ascetic Viśvarūpa. 121

## \|\| 1.15.122 \|\|

*haraś ca bahurūpaś ca tryambakaścāparājitaḥ \|*  
*vṛṣākapiś ca śambhuś ca kapardīraivataḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 122 \|\|*

Hara, Bahurūpa, Tryambaka, Aparājita, Vṛṣākapi, Śaṃbhu, Kapardin, Raivata, 122

## \|\| 1.15.123 \|\|

*mṛgavyādhaś ca śarvaś ca kapālī ca mahāmune \|*  
*ekādaśaite kathitā rudrāstribhuvaneśvarāḥ \|\| 123 \|\|*

Mṛgavyādha, Śarva and Kapālin: these, great sage, are said to be the eleven Rudras, lords of the threefold worlds. 123

## \|\| 1.15.124 \|\|

*śataṃ tvekaṃ samākhyātaṃ rudrāṇāmamitaujasām \|*  
*kāśyapasya tu bhāryā yāstāsāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu \|*  
*aditirditirdanuścaivāriṣṭā ca surasā khasā \|\| 124 \|\|*

A total of one hundred Rudras of immeasurable energy are mentioned. 124

## \|\| 1.15.125 \|\|

*surabhir vinatā caiva tāmrā krodhavaśā irā \|*  
*kadrurmuniś ca dharmajña tadapatyāni me śṛṇu \|\| 125 \|\|*

I’ll now tell you, knower of virtue, about the offspring of Aditi, Diti, Danu, Ariṣṭā, Surasā, Svasā, Surabhi, Vinatā, Tāmrā, Krodhavaśā, Irā, Kadru and Muni. 125

## \|\| 1.15.126 \|\|

*pūrvamanvantare śreṣṭhā dvādaśāsansurottamāḥ \|*  
*tuṣitā nāma te 'nyonyamūcurvaivasvatentare \|\| 126 \|\|*

In the previous Manvantara, there were twelve excellent celestial beings known as the Tuṣitas. When the beginning of the Vaivasvata Manvantara was approaching, 126

## \|\| 1.15.127 \|\|

*upasthite 'tiyaśasaścākṣuṣasyāntare manoḥ \|*  
*samavāyīkṛtāḥ sarve samāgamya parasparam \|\| 127 \|\|*

And the period of the previous glorious Manu, Cākṣuṣa, was drawing to a close, having come together, the assembled Tuṣitas conferred with one another: 127

## \|\| 1.15.128 \|\|

*āgacchata drutaṃ devā aditiṃ saṃpraviśya vai \|*  
*manvantare prasūyāmastannaḥ śreyo bhavediti \|\| 128 \|\|*

‘Come quickly, fellow deities. If we enter Aditi’s womb and are born in the next Manvantara, it will be to our advantage.’ 128

## \|\| 1.15.129 \|\|

*evamuktvā tu te sarve cākṣuṣasyāntare manoḥ \|*  
*mārīcātkaśyapājjātā adityā dakṣakanyayā \|\| 129 \|\|*

So saying, they were born in the period of Manu Vaivasvata as the sons of Mārīca Kaśyapa and Aditi, Dakṣa’s daughter. 129

## \|\| 1.15.130 \|\|

*tatra viṣṇuś ca śakraś ca jajñāte punar eva hi \|*  
*aryamā caiva dhātā ca tvaṣṭā pūṣā tathaiva ca \|\| 130 \|\|*

As such, Viṣṇu, mighty Indra, Āryaman, Dhātṛ, Tvaṣṭṛ, Pūṣan, 130

## \|\| 1.15.131 \|\|

*vivasvānsavitā caiva mitro varuṇa eva ca \|*  
*aṃśurbhagaścātitejā ādityā dvādaśa smṛtāḥ \|\| 131 \|\|*

Vivasvat, Savitṛ, Mitra, Varuṇa, Aṃśa and Bhaga are recognised as the twelve magnificent sons of Aditi, the Ādityas. 131

## \|\| 1.15.132 \|\|

*cākṣuṣasyāntare pūrvamāsanye tuṣitāḥ surāḥ \|*  
*vaivasvate 'ntare te vai ādityā dvādaśa smṛtāḥ \|\| 132 \|\|*

Those who had previously been the divine Tuṣitas in the Cākṣuṣa Manvantara are the twelve Ādityas in the current Vaivasvata period. 132

## \|\| 1.15.133 \|\|

*saptaviṃśati yāḥ proktāḥ somapatnyotha suvratāḥ \|*  
*sarvā nakṣatrayoginyastannāmnaścaiva tāḥ smṛtāḥ \|*  
*tāsāmapatyānyabhavandīptānyamitatejasām \|\| 133 \|\|*

The twenty-seven daughters of Dakṣa who are described as the faithful wives of Soma, and whose energy was beyond measure, also brought forth brilliant offspring. 133

## \|\| 1.15.134 \|\|

*ariṣṭanemipatnīnāmapatyānīha ṣoḍaśa \|\| 134 \|\|*

The wives of Ariṣṭanemi bore sixteen children. 134

## \|\| 1.15.135 \|\|

*bahuputrasya viduṣaścatastrovidyutaḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 135 \|\|*

The daughters of prudent Bahuputra are remembered as the four lightning deities, the Vidyuts. 135

## \|\| 1.15.136 \|\|

*pratyaṅgirasajāḥ śreṣṭhā ṛco brahmarṣisatkṛtāḥ \|\| 136 \|\|*

The patron deities of Vedic verses, the excellent Ṛces, honoured by brahminical sages, were born to Aṅgiras. 136

## \|\| 1.15.137 \|\|

*kṛśāśvasya tu devarṣerdevapraharaṇāḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 137 \|\|*

The sons of the godly sage Kṛśāśva were the Devapraharaṇas, patron gods of heavenly weapons. 137

## \|\| 1.15.138 \|\|

*ete yugasahasrānte jāyante punareva hi \|*  
*sarve devagaṇāstāta trayastriṃśattu chandajāḥ \|*  
*teṣāmapīha satataṃ nirodhotpattirucyate \|\| 138 \|\|*

These thirty-three divine hosts are born again of their own volition at the end of a thousand ages, my dear, but even they are said to be ever subject to birth and death. 138

## \|\| 1.15.139 \|\|

*yathā sūryasya maitreya udayāstamanāviha \|*  
*evaṃ devanikāyāste sambhavanti yugeyuge \|\| 139 \|\|*

Just as the sun rises and sets in this world, Maitreya, these hosts of deities are born again in every aeon. 139

## \|\| 1.15.140 \|\|

*dityā putradvayaṃ jajñe kaśyapāditi naḥ śrutam \|*  
*hiraṇyakaśipuścaiva hiraṇyākṣaś ca durjayaḥ \|\| 140 \|\|*

We’ve heard that Kaśyapa had two sons with Diti, Hiraṇyakaśipu and Hiraṇyākṣa. 140

## \|\| 1.15.141 \|\|

*siṃhikā cābhavatkanyā vipracitteḥ parigrahaḥ \|\| 141 \|\|*

Diti also had a daughter, Siṃhikā, who married Vipracitti. 141

## \|\| 1.15.142 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipoḥ putrāścātvāraḥ prathitaujasaḥ \|*  
*anuhlādaś ca hlādaś ca prahlādaścaiva buddhimān \|*  
*saṃhlādaś ca mahāvīryā daityavaṃśavivardhanāḥ \|\| 142 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu had four outstanding sons, Anuhlāda, Hlāda, virtuous Prahlāda and brave Saṃhlāda, who increased the lineage of the Daityas, Diti’s offspring. 142

## \|\| 1.15.143 \|\|

*teṣāṃ madhye mahābhāga sarvatra samadṛgvāśī \|*  
*prahlādaḥ paramāṃ bhaktiṃ ca uvāca janārdane \|\| 143 \|\|*

Among them, great Prahlāda looked on all things with equanimity and was perfectly devoted to Janārdana. 143

## \|\| 1.15.144 \|\|

*daityendradīpito vahniḥ sarvāṅgopacito dvija \|*  
*na dadāha ca yaṃ vipra vāsudeva hṛdi sthite \|\| 144 \|\|*

In former times, the Daitya king lit a fire and heaped it round Prahlāda, brahmin, but the flames did him no harm, as Vāsudeva dwelt in his heart. 144

## \|\| 1.15.145 \|\|

*mahārṇavāntaḥ salile sthitasya calato mahī \|*  
*cacāla calatā yasya pāśabaddhasya dhīmataḥ \|\| 145 \|\|*

When that wise child, bound up with ropes, shivered at the bottom of the sea, the whole world trembled. 145

## \|\| 1.15.146 \|\|

*na bhinnaṃ vividhaiḥ śastrair yasya daityendrapātitaiḥ \|*  
*śarīramadrikaṭhinaṃ sarvatrācyutacetasaḥ \|\| 146 \|\|*

The Daitya king hurled several weapons at Prahlāda, but he was never harmed, his body firm as a mountain and his heart set on eternal Viṣṇu. 146

## \|\| 1.15.147 \|\|

*viṣānalojjvalamukhā yasya daityapracoditāḥ \|*  
*nāntāya sarpapatayo babhūvururutejasaḥ \|\| 147 \|\|*

Mighty serpent-lords, sent by the Daitya king, their jaws ablaze with flames and venom, couldn’t kill him. 147

## \|\| 1.15.148 \|\|

*śailair ākrāntadehopi yaḥ smaranpuruṣottamam \|*  
*tatyāja nātmanaḥ prāṇān viṣṇusmaraṇadaṃśitaḥ \|\| 148 \|\|*

Even mountain ranges were piled upon his body, but, recalling the highest spirit, he didn’t die, protected by the thought of Viṣṇu. 148

## \|\| 1.15.149 \|\|

*patantamuccādavaniryamupetya mahāmatim \|*  
*dadhāra daityapatinā kṣiptaṃ svarganivāsinā \|\| 149 \|\|*

The Daitya king, the denizen of heaven, cast that prudent boy down from a lofty palace, but the Earth embraced him before he hit the ground. 149

## \|\| 1.15.150 \|\|

*yasya saśoṣako vāyurdehe daityendrayojitaḥ \|*  
*avāpa saṃkṣayaṃ sadyaścittasthe madhusūdane \|\| 150 \|\|*

The king of Daityas sent a wind to scorch his body, but it was baffled as Prahlāda’s heart was always set on Madhusūdana. 150

## \|\| 1.15.151 \|\|

*viṣāṇabhaṅgamunmattā madahāniṃ ca diggajāḥ \|*  
*yasya vakṣaḥsthale prāptā daityendrapariṇāmitāḥ \|\| 151 \|\|*

The raging guardian elephants of the quarters sent by the Daitya king broke their tusks on Prahlāda’s broad chest, which robbed them of their arrogance. 151

## \|\| 1.15.152 \|\|

*yasya cotpāditā kṛtyā daityarājapurohitaiḥ \|*  
*babhūva nāntāya purā govindāsaktacetasaḥ \|\| 152 \|\|*

With all their spells the king’s own priests could not destroy Prahlāda then, as his heart was fixed on Govinda. 152

## \|\| 1.15.153 \|\|

*śambarasya ca māyānāṃ sahasramatimāyinaḥ \|*  
*yasminprayuktaṃ cakreṇa kṛṣṇasya vitathikṛtam \|\| 153 \|\|*

The thousand phantoms set on him by Śambara the sorcerer were rendered harmless by Kṛṣṇa’s discus. 153

## \|\| 1.15.154 \|\|

*daityendrasūdopahṛtaṃ yasya hālāhalaṃ viṣam \|*  
*jarayāmāsa matimānavikāramamatsarī \|\| 154 \|\|*

The poison known as hālāhala administered by the chefs of the Daitya king had no effect on wise and selfless Prahlāda. 154

## \|\| 1.15.155 \|\|

*samacetā jagatyasminyaḥ sarveṣveva jantuṣu \|*  
*yathātmani tathānyeṣāṃ paraṃ maitraguṇānvitaḥ \|\| 155 \|\|*

His mind was equally well-disposed to every creature in this world, he treated others as he would himself, filled with the virtue of benevolence. 155

## \|\| 1.15.156 \|\|

*dharmātmā satyaśauryādiguṇānāmākaraḥ paraḥ \|*  
*upamānamaśeṣāṇāṃ sādhūnāṃ yaḥ sadābhavat \|\| 156 \|\|*

He was the soul of virtue, a brilliant font of purity, truth and other qualities, ever standing as a paragon for all good people. 156

## Chapter 16 - Maitreya asks about Prahlāda

## \|\| 1.16.1 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*kathito bhavatā vaṃśo mānavānāṃ mahātmanām \|*  
*kāraṇaṃ cāsya jagato viṣṇureva sanātanaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

You’ve described the origin of humanity, great sage, and everlasting Viṣṇu, the universal cause. 1

## \|\| 1.16.2 \|\|

*yattvetadbhagavānāha prahlādaṃ daityasattamam \|*  
*dadāha nāgnirnāstraiś ca kṣuṇṇastatyāja jīvitam \|\| 2 \|\|*

But who’s this excellent Daitya, Prahlāda, whom you mentioned and who didn’t die when burned with fire or struck by weapons? 2

## \|\| 1.16.3 \|\|

*jagāma vasudhā kṣobhaṃ yatrābdhisalile sthite \|*  
*pāśaurbaddhe vicalati vikṣiptāṅgaiḥ samāhatā \|\| 3 \|\|*

The whole earth shook when he lay beneath the ocean, even though he was tied with bonds, and the firm ground trembled when he moved his limbs. 3

## \|\| 1.16.4 \|\|

*śailair ākrāntadehopi na mamāra ca yaḥ purā \|*  
*tvāya cātīva māhātmyaṃ kathitaṃ yasya dhīmataḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Buried under mountains in former times, he didn’t die. You’ve said so much about the greatness of this prudent being. 4

## \|\| 1.16.5 \|\|

*tasya prabhāvamatulaṃ viṣṇorbhaktimato mune \|*  
*śrotumicchāmi yasyaitaccaritaṃ dīptatejasaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

I want to hear about the peerless power of that devotee of Viṣṇu, sage, and about the deeds of this being of immeasurable energy. 5

## \|\| 1.16.6 \|\|

*kiṃnimittamasau śastrair vikṣipto ditijemuna \|*  
*kimarthaṃ cābdhisalile vikṣipto dharmatatparaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Why did the sons of Diti attack him with weapons, sage, and why was he, intent on virtue, cast into the sea? 6

## \|\| 1.16.7 \|\|

*ākrāntaḥ parvataiḥ kasmāddaṣṭaścaiva mahoragaiḥ \|*  
*kṣiptaḥ kimadriśikharāktiṃ vā pāvakasañcaye \|\| 7 \|\|*

Why was he crushed by mountains and bitten by great serpents? Why cast from heights and thrown into a fire? 7

## \|\| 1.16.8 \|\|

*digdantināṃ dantabhūmiṃ sa ca kasmānnirūpitaḥ \|*  
*saśoṣakonilaścāsya prayuktaḥ kiṃ mahāsuraiḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Why was he made a target for the tusks of guardian elephants, and why did great demons send a wind to scorch him? 8

## \|\| 1.16.9 \|\|

*kṛtyāṃ ca daityaguravo yuyujus tatra kiṃ mune \|*  
*śambaraścāpi māyānāṃ sahasraṃ kiṃ prayuktāvān \|\| 9 \|\|*

Why did the Daitya’s gurus perform those rituals, sage, and why did Śambara loose a thousand phantoms at him? 9

## \|\| 1.16.10 \|\|

*hālāhalaṃ viṣamaho daityasūdair mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*kasmāddattaṃ vināśāya yajjīrṇaṃ tena dhīmatā \|\| 10 \|\|*

Why did the Daitya’s chefs administer the virulent poison hālāhala to dispatch that great and prudent being, which he rendered harmless? 10

## \|\| 1.16.11 \|\|

*etatsarvaṃ mahābhāga prahlādasya mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*caritaṃ śrotumicchāmi mahāmāhātmyasūcakam \|\| 11 \|\|*

I want to hear all about the deeds of noble Prahlāda, blessed sage, which demonstrate his special virtue. 11

## \|\| 1.16.12 \|\|

*na hi kautūhalaṃ tatra yaddaityaurnihato hi saḥ \|*  
*ananyamanaso viṣṇo kaḥ samartho nipātane \|\| 12 \|\|*

No wonder the Daityas didn’t kill him, for who can harm a person whose heart is set on Viṣṇu? 12

## \|\| 1.16.13 \|\|

*tasmindharmaparenityaṃ keśavārādhanodyate \|*  
*svavaṃśaprabhavair daityaiḥ kṛto dveṣotidupkaraḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

He was bent on virtue and always lauded Keśava, but Daityas, his own kinsfolk, showed him extreme antipathy. 13

## \|\| 1.16.14 \|\|

*dharmātmani mahābhāgo viṣṇubhakte vimatsare \|*  
*daitayaiḥ prahṛtaṃ kasmāttanmamākhyātumarhasi \|\| 14 \|\|*

A pious, fortunate and selfless devotee of Viṣṇu—why was he attacked by Diti’s sons? You must explain all this to me. 14

## \|\| 1.16.15 \|\|

*praharanti mahātmāno vipakṣā api neddaśe \|*  
*guṇaiḥ samanvite sādhau kiṃ punaryaḥ svapakṣajaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Noble beings would never strike a virtuous foe like him, filled as he was with goodness, let alone a person born among one’s allies. 15

## \|\| 1.16.16 \|\|

*tadetatkathyatāṃ sarvaṃ vistarānmunipuṅgava \|*  
*daityeśvarasya cāritaṃ śrotumicchāmyaśeṣataḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

You must therefore describe all this in detail, best of sages, as I want to hear about the deeds of this Daitya prince. 16

## Chapter 17 - Prahlāda instructs the Daitya boys

## \|\| 1.17.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*maitreya kṣūyatāṃ samyak caritaṃ tasya dhīmataḥ \|*  
*prahlādasya sadodāracaritasya mahātmanaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Listen carefully, Maitreya, to the deeds of the great and prudent Prahlāda, whose actions were always noble. 1

## \|\| 1.17.2 \|\|

*diteḥ putro mahāvīryo hiraṇyakaśipuḥ purā \|*  
*trailokyaṃ vaśamāninye brahmaṇo varadarpitaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

In former days, Diti’s son, brave Hiraṇyakaśipu, received a boon from Brahmā and brought all three worlds under his control. 2

## \|\| 1.17.3 \|\|

*indratvamakaroddaityaḥ sa cāsītsavitā svayam \|*  
*vāyuragnirapāṃ nāthaḥ somaścābhūnmahāsuraḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

The Daitya usurped the authority of Indra, and then assumed the functions of the Sun, Wind, Fire, Varuṇa, lord of waters, the Moon, 3

## \|\| 1.17.4 \|\|

*dhanānāmadhipaḥ so 'bhūt sa evāsītsvayaṃ yamaḥ \|*  
*yajñabhāgānaśeṣāṃstu sa svayaṃ bubhuje 'suraḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Kubera, lord of riches, and Yama, ruler of the underworld. That demon then partook of every portion of the sacrifice. 4

## \|\| 1.17.5 \|\|

*devāḥ svargaṃ parityajya tartrāsān munisattama \|*  
*viceruravanau sarve bibhrāṇā mānuṣīṃ tanum \|\| 5 \|\|*

The gods fled heaven, fearing him, best of sages, and wandered the world in human form. 5

## \|\| 1.17.6 \|\|

*jitvā tribhuvanaṃ sarvaṃ trailokyaiśvaryadarpitaḥ \|*  
*upagīyamāno gandharvair bubhuje viṣayānpriyān \|\| 6 \|\|*

After the Daitya conquered all three worlds, he grew arrogant because of his universal sovereignty, and, while gandharvas sang his praises, he indulged his favourite senses. 6

## \|\| 1.17.7 \|\|

*pānāsaktaṃ mahātmānaṃ hiraṇyakaśipuṃ tadā \|*  
*upāsāṃcakrire sarve siddhagandharvapannagāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

All the siddhas, gandharvas and nāgas waited on mighty Hiraṇyakaśipu, who loved to drink. 7

## \|\| 1.17.8 \|\|

*avādayan jaguścānye jayaśabdaṃ tathāpare \|*  
*daityarājasya purataścakruḥ siddhā mudānvitāḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

In the presence of the Daitya lord, joyful siddhas provided music, some sang, while others cried, ‘Be victorious!’ 8

## \|\| 1.17.9 \|\|

*tatra pranṛttapsarasi sphāṭikābhramaye 'suraḥ \|*  
*papau pānaṃ mudā yuktaḥ prāsāde sumanohare \|\| 9 \|\|*

Apsarases danced in that delightful crystal palace in the clouds, where the demon, filled with pleasure, spent time drinking. 9

## \|\| 1.17.10 \|\|

*tasya putro mahābhāgaḥ prahlādo nāma nāmataḥ \|*  
*papāṭha bālapāṭhyāni gurugehaṅgator'bhakaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu had a gifted son, Prahlāda by name, a lad who studied subjects suited to young children in his guru’s home. 10

## \|\| 1.17.11 \|\|

*ekadā tu sa dharmātmā jagāma guruṇā saha \|*  
*pānāsaktasya purataḥ puturdaityapatestadā \|\| 11 \|\|*

One day, that pious youth, accompanied by his teacher, came before the Daitya lord, his drunken father. 11

## \|\| 1.17.12 \|\|

*pādapraṇāmāvanataṃ tamutthāpya pitā sutam \|*  
*hiraṇyakaśipuḥ prāha prahlādamamitaujasam \|\| 12 \|\|*

When Prahlāda bowed to touch Hiraṇyakaśipu’s feet, his father lifted up this child of boundless energy and spoke these words. 12

## \|\| 1.17.13 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*paṭhyatāṃ bhavatā vatsa sārabhūtaṃ subhāṣitam \|*  
*kālenaitāvatā yatte sadodyoktena śikṣitam \|\| 13 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Recite for me, dear boy, your favourite verse that you’ve carefully learned to date. 13

## \|\| 1.17.14 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*śrūyatāṃ tāta vakṣyāmi sārabhūtaṃ tavājñayā \|*  
*samāhitamanā bhūtvā yanme cetasyavasthitam \|\| 14 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Listen well, dear father, and as you command, I’ll diligently recite the most important thing that occupies my heart: 14

## \|\| 1.17.15 \|\|

*anādimadhyāntamajamavṛddhikṣayamacyutam \|*  
*praṇatosmyantamantānaṃ sarvakāraṇakāraṇam \|\| 15 \|\|*

‘I bow to him who has no start, no middle and no ending; who is unborn, unageing, undying and unchanging; destroyer and maintainer of the universe; the cause of every cause.’ 15

## \|\| 1.17.16 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*etanniśamya daityendraḥ sakopo raktalocanaḥ \|*  
*vilokya tadguruṃ prāha sphuritādharapallavaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the eyes of the Daitya king grew red with fury. He glared at his son’s guru and berated him, his bud-like lower lip alive with rage. 16

## \|\| 1.17.17 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipuruvāca*  
*brahmabandho kimetatte vipakṣastutisaṃhitam \|*  
*asāraṃ grāhito bālo māmavajñāya durmate \|\| 17 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Brahmin, what insulting nonsense in praise of my arch rival have you taught this boy, you fool? 17

## \|\| 1.17.18 \|\|

*gururuvāca*  
*daityeśvara na kopasya vaśamāgantumarhasi \|*  
*mamopadeśajanitaṃnāyaṃ vadati te sutaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

The guru:

Your anger is misplaced, my lord, as your son didn’t learn this from me. 18

## \|\| 1.17.19 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipuruvāca*  
*anuśiṣṭosi kenedṛgvatsa brahlāda kathyatām \|*  
*mayopadiṣṭaṃ netyeṣa prabravīti gurustava \|\| 19 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Who taught you this, Prahlāda? Tell me, boy, because your guru says it wasn’t him. 19

## \|\| 1.17.20 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*śāstā viṣṇuraśeṣasya jagato yo hṛdi sthitaḥ \|*  
*tamṛte paramātmānaṃ tāta kaḥ kena śāsyate \|\| 20 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Viṣṇu, teacher of all the world, is fixed within my heart, father. Apart from him, the highest spirit, what remains to be taught by anyone? 20

## \|\| 1.17.21 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*koyaṃ viṣṇuḥ sudurbuddhe yaṃ bravīṣi punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*jagatāmīśvarasyeha purataḥ prasabha mama \|\| 21 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Who’s this Viṣṇu you prattle on about before me, silly boy? I’m the lord of the threefold worlds! 21

## \|\| 1.17.22 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*na śabdagocare yasya yogidhyeyaṃ paraṃ padam \|*  
*yato yaś ca svayaṃ viśvaṃ sa viṣṇuḥ parameśvaraḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Ascetics may contemplate his highest state, but it cannot be described. From him arises everything, and he is everything. He is Viṣṇu, the highest lord. 22

## \|\| 1.17.23 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*parameśvarasaṃjñojña kimanyo mayyavasthite \|*  
*tathāpi martukāmastvaṃ prabravīṣi punaḥ punaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

How can anyone be called the ‘highest lord’ while I am here, you foolish boy? If you want to die, just mention him again. 23

## \|\| 1.17.24 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*na kevalaṃ tāta mama prajānāṃ sa brahmabhūto bhavataś ca viṣṇuḥ \|*  
*dhātā vidhātā parameśvaraś ca prakīda kopaṃ kuruṣe kimartham \|\| 24 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Viṣṇu, the Absolute, is creator, disposer and highest lord, not just of me and all the world, but of you as well, father. Be merciful to me. What use is anger? 24

## \|\| 1.17.25 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*praviṣṭaḥ ko 'sya hṛdaye durbuddheratipāpakṛt \|*  
*yenedṛśānyasādhūni vadatyāviṣṭamānasaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Which evil being has occupied the heart of this foolish child? He’s talking nonsense as if possessed. 25

## \|\| 1.17.26 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*na kevalaṃ maddhṛdayaṃ sa viṣṇurākramya lalokānakhilānavasthitaḥ \|*  
*sa māṃ tvadāndīś ca pitaḥsamastānsamastaceṣṭāsu yunakti sarvagaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Viṣṇu not only occupies my heart but fills all three worlds. He’s the universal being who enjoins me, you, father, and other beings to undertake all actions. 26

## \|\| 1.17.27 \|\|

*hiraṇyakiśipur uvāca*  
*niṣkāsyatāmayaṃ pāpaḥ śāsyatāṃ ca gurorgṛhe \|*  
*yojito durmatiḥ kena vipakṣaviṣayastutau \|\| 27 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Take this wicked boy away and punish him in his teacher’s home. Who’s encouraged this stupid child to falsely praise my enemy? 27

## \|\| 1.17.28 \|\|

*śrīpāraśara uvāca*  
*ityukto 'sau tadā daityair nīto gurugṛhaṃ punaḥ \|*  
*jagrāha vidyamaniśaṃ guruśuśrūṣaṇodyataḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After the king had spoken thus, the Daityas took Prahlāda to his teacher’s home, where he kept up his schooling, while serving his guru diligently. 28

## \|\| 1.17.29 \|\|

*kālotīteti mahati prahlādamasureśvaraḥ \|*  
*samāhūyābravīdrāthā kācitputraka gīyatām \|\| 29 \|\|*

Some time later, the demon king summoned Prahlāda again. ‘Sing something for me, son,’ he said to him. 29

## \|\| 1.17.30 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*yataḥ pradhānapuruṣau yataścaitaccarācaram \|*  
*kāraṇaṃ sakalasyāsya sa no viṣṇuḥ prasīdatu \|\| 30 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

May Viṣṇu, from whom both matter and spirit arise, from whom this world of moving and unmoving things ensues, and who is the cause of everything, show mercy to us all. 30

## \|\| 1.17.31 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*durātmā vadhyatāmeṣa nānenārthosti jīvatā \|*  
*svapakṣahānikartṛtvādyaḥ kulāṅgāratāṃ gataḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Kill this wicked child—his life is worthless! Because he’ll bring ruin on his family, he’s like a glowing ember in a house. 31

## \|\| 1.17.32 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityājñaptāstatastena pragṛhītamahāyudhāḥ \|*  
*udyatāstasya nāśāya daityāḥ śatasahasraśaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

Parāśara:

At the king’s command, hundreds of thousands of Daityas, weapons in hand, prepared to kill the boy. 32

## \|\| 1.17.33 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*viṣṇuḥ śastreṣu yuṣmāsu mayi cāsau vyavasthitaḥ \|*  
*daiteyāstena satyena mākramantvāyudhāni me \|\| 33 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Just as Viṣṇu occupies your swords, as well as you and me, you Daityas, by this same truth, may all your weapons be rendered harmless! 33

## \|\| 1.17.34 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tatastaiḥ śataśo daityaiḥ śastroghair āhatopi san \|*  
*nāvāca vedanāmalpāmabhūccaiva punarnavaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Even though the Daityas in their hundreds struck him with their awful weapons, Prahlāda felt not the slightest pain and was continually restored. 34

## \|\| 1.17.35 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*durbuddhe vinivartasva vairipakṣastavāhataḥ \|*  
*abhayaṃ te prayacchāmi mātimūḍhamatirbhava \|\| 35 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

You foolish child, don’t praise our rivals! You’ll have nought to fear from me if you renounce these stupid thoughts. 35

## \|\| 1.17.36 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*bhayaṃ bhayānāmapahāriṇa sthite manasyanante mama kutra tiṣṭhati \|*  
*yasminsmṛte janmajarāntakādibhayāni sarvāmyapayānti tāta \|\| 36 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

When the everlasting lord, who dispels all anxiety, abides within my heart, what fear have I? Calling him to mind, father, all concerns of birth, old age and death are gone. 36

## \|\| 1.17.37 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*bhobhoḥ sarpāḥ durācāramenamatyantadurmatim \|*  
*viṣajvālākulair vakraiḥ sadyo nayataḥ saṃkṣayam \|\| 37 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Come, serpents! With your jaws that spew those venomous flames, dispatch at once this ill-mannered wretch. 37

## \|\| 1.17.38 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktāste tataḥ sarpāḥ kuhakāstakṣakādayaḥ \|*  
*adaśanta samasteṣu gātreṣvativiṣolbaṇāḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

Parāśara:

At the king’s command, Takṣaka and other sly poisonous snakes struck Prahlāda’s every limb. 38

## \|\| 1.17.39 \|\|

*sa tvāsaktamatiḥ kṛṣṇe daśyamāno mahoragaiḥ \|*  
*na vivedātmano gātraṃ tatsmṛtyāhlādasusthitaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

But his mind was fixed on Kṛṣṇa, and even when those mighty snakes assailed him, unaware of his own body and filled with bliss, he recalled the deity. 39

## \|\| 1.17.40 \|\|

*sarpā ūcuḥ*  
*daṃṣṭrā viśīrṇā maṇyaḥ sphuṭanti phaṇeṣu tāpo hṛdayeṣu kaṃpaḥ \|*  
*nāsya tvacaḥ svalpamapīha bhinnaṃ prasādhi daityeśvara kāryamanyata \|\| 40 \|\|*

The serpents:

Our fangs have snapped and our crest jewels cracked, our hoods are sore and our hearts all tremble, but we’ve made not the slightest mark on this child’s skin. Assign some other task to us, your majesty. 40

## \|\| 1.17.41 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur upāca*  
*he diggājāḥ saṃkaṭadantamiśra ghnatainamasmadripupakṣabhinnam \|*  
*tajjā vināśāya bhavanti tasya yathāraṇeḥ prajvalito hutāśaḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Come, you guardian elephants of the quarters with your massive tusks. Strike down this boy who’s been seduced by enemies. One’s progeny can cause one’s own destruction, just like a fire that burns the forest where it was kindled. 41

## \|\| 1.17.42 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tataḥ sa diggajair bālo bhūbhṛcchikharasannibhaiḥ \|*  
*pātito dharaṇīpṛṣṭhe viṣāṇair vāvapīḍitaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The elephants of the quarters, towering like great mountain peaks, threw Prahlāda to the ground and gored him with their tusks. 42

## \|\| 1.17.43 \|\|

*smaratastasya govindamibhadantāḥ sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*śīrṇā vakṣasthalaṃ prāpya sa prāha pitaraṃ tataḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

Because his mind was set on Govinda, their tusks broke into a thousand pieces when they touched his chest. The boy then addressed his father: 43

## \|\| 1.17.44 \|\|

*dantā gajānāṃ kuliśāgraniṣṭhurājha śīrṇā yadetena balaṃ mamaitat \|*  
*mahāvipattāpavināśano 'yaṃ janārdanānusmaraṇānubhāvaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

These elephants’ tusks with tips as hard as diamonds snapped, not because of my own strength, but because I remembered Janārdana, the great destroyer of death and sin. 44

## \|\| 1.17.45 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*jvālyatāmasurā bahnirapasarpata diggajāḥ \|*  
*vāyo samedhayāgniṃ tvaṃ dahyatāmeṣa pāpakṛt \|\| 45 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Get those elephants out of here! Light a fire, you demons, and Vāyu, fan the flames to burn this wretch! 45

## \|\| 1.17.46 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*mahākāṣṭhacayasthaṃ tamasurendrasutaṃ tataḥ \|*  
*prajvālya dānavā vahniṃ dadahuḥ svāminoditāḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

Parāśara:

At their lord’s command, the Dānavas covered the son of the demon king with a towering pile of kindling and lit a fire to burn him. 46

## \|\| 1.17.47 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*tātaiṣa vahniḥ pavaneritopi na māṃ dahatyatra samantatoham \|*  
*paśyāmi padmāstaraṇāstṛtāni śītāni sarvāṇi diśāṃ mukhāni \|\| 47 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Father, this wind-fanned fire can do no harm, as all around on every side I see cooling prospects spread with lotuses. 47

## \|\| 1.17.48 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*atha daityeśvaraṃ procurbhārgavasyātmajā dvājāḥ \|*  
*purohitā mahātmānaḥ sāmnā saṃstūya vāgminaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The brahmin sons of Bhārgava, those wise and eloquent priests, praised their patron, the Daitya king, with soothing words: 48

## \|\| 1.17.49 \|\|

*purohitā ūcuḥ*  
*rājanniyamyatāṃ kopo bālepi tanaye nije \|*  
*kopo devanikāyeṣu teṣu te śapalo yataḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

The priests:

Sire, don’t be angry with this boy, your son, because your fury will bear fruit among the gathered hosts of gods. 49

## \|\| 1.17.50 \|\|

*(tataḥ purohitair ukto hiraṇyakaśipuḥ svakaiḥ)*  
*tathātathainaṃ bālaṃ te śāsitāro vayaṃ nṛpaḥ \|*  
*yathā vipabhanāśāya vinītaste bhaviṣyati \|\| 50 \|\|*

We’ll be your son’s instructors, and we’ll teach him to destroy your rivals. 50

## \|\| 1.17.51 \|\|

*bālatvaṃ sarvadoṣāṇāṃ daityarājāspadaṃ yataḥ \|*  
*tatotra kopamatyarthaṃ yoktumarhasi nārbhake \|\| 51 \|\|*

Childhood is a time of many errors, Daitya king. Don’t be angry with the boy. 51

## \|\| 1.17.52 \|\|

*na tyakṣyati hareḥ pakṣamasmākaṃ vacanādyadi \|*  
*tataḥ kṛtyāṃ vadhāyāsya kariṣyāmo 'nivartinīm \|\| 52 \|\|*

If, after our instruction, he still cleaves to Hari’s cause, we’ll then take steps to bring about his downfall. In this we will not fail. 52

## \|\| 1.17.53 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evamabhyarthitastais tu daityarājaḥ purohitaiḥ \|*  
*daityair niṣkāsayāmāsa putraṃ pāvakasaṃcayāt \|\| 53 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After the priests of the Daitya king petitioned him with these words, he ordered the demons to pull his son out of the fire. 53

## \|\| 1.17.54 \|\|

*tato gurugṛhe vālaḥ sa vasanbāladānavān \|*  
*adhyāpayāmāsa muhurupadeśāntare guroḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

Later, residing in his guru’s home, during breaks between his teacher’s lessons, Prahlāda often gave instruction to the other demon boys. 54

## \|\| 1.17.55 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*śrūyatāṃ paramārtho me daiteyā ditijātmajāḥ \|*  
*na cānyathaitanmantavyaṃ nātra lobhādikāraṇam \|\| 55 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Listen, Daityas, Diti’s grandsons, while I speak about the highest goal. Nothing else is worthy of consideration or desire. 55

## \|\| 1.17.56 \|\|

*janma bālyaṃ tataḥ sarvo jantuḥ prāpnoti yauvanam \|*  
*avyāhataiva bhavati tatonudivasaṃ jarāḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

Every living being experiences birth, childhood and youth, and then in time old age will surely come. 56

## \|\| 1.17.57 \|\|

*tataś ca sṛtyumabhyeti janturdaityeśvarātmajāḥ \|*  
*pratyakṣaṃ dṛsyate caitadasmākaṃ bhavatāṃ tathā \|\| 57 \|\|*

After that, you sons of the Daitya lord, a creature faces death, as you and I all clearly see. 57

## \|\| 1.17.58 \|\|

*mṛtasya ca punarjanma bhavatyetacca nānyathā \|*  
*āgamo 'yaṃ tathā yacca nopādānaṃ vinodbhavaḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

Each being who dies will be reborn, and according to our sacred texts it must be thus, but there is no existence without a cause. 58

## \|\| 1.17.59 \|\|

*garbhavāsādi yāvattu punarjanmopapādanam \|*  
*saha stāvasthakaṃ tāvadduḥkhamevāvagamyatām \|\| 59 \|\|*

As long as pregnancy results in birth, the pain of every new existence will be felt. 59

## \|\| 1.17.60 \|\|

*kṣuttṛṣṇopaśamaṃ tadvac chītādyupaśamaṃ mukham \|*  
*manyate bālabuddhitvādduḥkhameva hi tatpunaḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

People foolishly believe that the end of thirst and hunger, and the absence of cold and other irritations, will lead to happiness, but these are in truth merely further forms of pain. 60

## \|\| 1.17.61 \|\|

*atyantastimitāṅgānāṃ vyāyāmena sukhaiṣiṇām \|*  
*bhrāntijñānāvṛtākṣāṇāṃ duḥkhameva sukhāyate \|\| 61 \|\|*

Just as those with aching limbs seek comfort when they stretch, even a slap enlivens those whose minds have wandered. 61

## \|\| 1.17.62 \|\|

*kva śarīramaśeṣāṇāṃ śleṣmādīnāṃ mahācayaḥ \|*  
*kva kāntiśobhāsaundaryaramaṇīyādayo guṇāḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

How ill-matched are the physical body, a mass of phlegm and other humours, and sought-for qualities such as beauty and a pleasant scent. 62

## \|\| 1.17.63 \|\|

*māṃsāsṛkpūyaviṇmūtrasnāyumajjāsthisaṃhatau \|*  
*dehe cetprītimān mūḍho bhavitā narakepyasau \|\| 63 \|\|*

The fool who delights in a body composed of blood and muscle, pus and faeces, urine, sinews, bones and marrow will surely go to hell. 63

## \|\| 1.17.64 \|\|

*agnoḥ śītena toyasya tṛṣā bhaktasya ca kṣudhā \|*  
*kriyate sukhakartṛtvaṃ tadvilomasya cetaraiḥ \|\| 64 \|\|*

Fire gives comfort when one is cold, water when thirsty and food when hungry, but in other circumstances, they have the opposite effect. 64

## \|\| 1.17.65 \|\|

*karoti he daityasutā yāvanmātraṃ parigraham \|*  
*tāvanmātraṃ sa evāsya duḥkhaṃ cetasi yacchati \|\| 65 \|\|*

As long as you persist with marriage, sons of Daityas, such miseries will weigh upon your hearts. 65

## \|\| 1.17.66 \|\|

*yāvataḥ kurute jantuḥ sambandhānmanasaḥ priyān \|*  
*tāvantosya nikhanyante hṛdaye śokaśaṅkavaḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

As many relatives as a being holds dear, so many painful arrows will pierce his heart. 66

## \|\| 1.17.67 \|\|

*yadyadgṛhe tanmanasi yatra tatrāvatiṣṭhataḥ \|*  
*nāśadāhopakaraṇaṃ tasya tatraiva tiṣṭhati \|\| 67 \|\|*

Wherever a person journeys, he always worries that his goods at home are lost or burned or stolen. 67

## \|\| 1.17.68 \|\|

*janmanyatra mahadduḥkhaṃ mriyamāṇasya cāpi tat \|*  
*yātanāsu yamasyograṃ garbhasaṃkramaṇeṣu ca \|\| 68 \|\|*

Birth is attended by great pain and, for the dying, there are the awful torments under Yama’s rod. Then comes the pain of passing through another womb. 68

## \|\| 1.17.69 \|\|

*garbheṣu sukhalośopi bhavadbhir anumīyate \|*  
*yadi tatkathyatāmevaṃ sarvaṃ duḥkhamayaṃ jagat \|\| 69 \|\|*

If you conclude there’s little pleasure in the embryonic state, then you must admit that this whole world consists of woe. 69

## \|\| 1.17.70 \|\|

*tadevamatidukhānāmāspadetra bhavārmave \|*  
*bhavatāṃ kathyate satyaṃ viṣṇurekaḥ parāyaṇaḥ \|\| 70 \|\|*

I’ll therefore speak truthfully to you: here in the abode of suffering, the ocean of existence, Viṣṇu is your only refuge. 70

## \|\| 1.17.71 \|\|

*mā jānīta vayaṃ bālā dehī deheṣu śāśvataḥ \|*  
*jarāyauvanajanmādya dharmā dehasya nātmanaḥ \|\| 71 \|\|*

Do not think: ‘We’re only boys, and the embodied being that inhabits our physical form is everlasting. Birth, youth and old age are the way of the body, not the soul.’ 71

## \|\| 1.17.72 \|\|

*bālohaṃ tāvadicchāto yatiṣye śreyase yuvā \|*  
*yuvāhaṃ vārdhake prāpte kariṣyāmyatmano hitam \|\| 72 \|\|*

Or: ‘While I’m a boy, I’ll do as I wish, but when I become a youth I’ll strive for some improvement.’ Or: ‘I’m a young man now, but I’ll do something to benefit my soul when I’m older.’ 72

## \|\| 1.17.73 \|\|

*vṛddho 'haṃ mama kāryāṇi samastāni na gocare \|*  
*kiṃ kariṣyāmi mandātmā samarthena na yatkṛtam \|\| 73 \|\|*

Or: ‘Now I’m old, those things I should have done are out of reach. I didn’t do them then when I was able; how can I do them now I’m slowing down?’ 73

## \|\| 1.17.74 \|\|

*evaṃ durāśayā kṣiptamānasaḥ puruṣaḥ sadā \|*  
*śreyaso 'bhimukhaṃ yāti na kādācitpipāsitaḥ \|\| 74 \|\|*

One whose mind is always filled with such vain thoughts will not improve himself, even though he yearns to do so. 74

## \|\| 1.17.75 \|\|

*bālye kriḍanakāsaktā yovane viṣayonmukhāḥ \|*  
*ajñā nayantyaśaktyā ca vārdhakaṃ samupasthitam \|\| 75 \|\|*

Children love their toys; young people seek out sensual pleasures, but before they know it, relentless age creeps up on them. 75

## \|\| 1.17.76 \|\|

*tasmādbālye vivekātmā yateta śreyase sadā \|*  
*bālyayauvanavṛdvādyair dehabhāvair asaṃyutaḥ \|\| 76 \|\|*

A discerning being should always strive to improve himself while young, without regard to boyhood, youth, old age or other states. 76

## \|\| 1.17.77 \|\|

*tadetadvo mayākhyātaṃ yadi jānīta nānṛtam \|*  
*tadasmatprītaye viṣṇuḥ smaryatāṃ bandhamuktidaḥ \|\| 77 \|\|*

This, then, is what I have to tell you. If you believe that it is true, then out of your affection for me, remember Viṣṇu, who will free you from the bonds of life. 77

## \|\| 1.17.78 \|\|

*prayāsaḥ smaraṇe kosya smṛto yacchati śobhanam \|*  
*pāpakṣayaś ca bhavati smaratāṃ tamaharniśam \|\| 78 \|\|*

What’s so hard about remembering him? When called to mind, he blesses you, so think about him day and night and all your sins will be forgiven. 78

## \|\| 1.17.79 \|\|

*sarvabhūtasthite tasminmatirmaitrī divāniśam \|*  
*bhavatāṃ jāyatāmevaṃ sarvakleśānprahāsyatha \|\| 79 \|\|*

Let your love and thoughts both day and night be on him who dwells within all creatures, and in doing so you’ll leave your woes behind. 79

## \|\| 1.17.80 \|\|

*tāpatrayeṇābhihataṃ yadetadakhilaṃ jagat \|*  
*tadā śocyeṣu bhūteṣu dveṣaṃ prājñaḥ karoti kaḥ \|\| 80 \|\|*

As threefold suffering afflicts the world, which wise person would be angry with other creatures deserving of our pity? 80

## \|\| 1.17.81 \|\|

*atha bhadrāṇi bhūtāni hīnaśaktirahaṃ param \|*  
*mudaṃ tadāpi kurvita hānirdveṣaphalaṃ yataḥ \|\| 81 \|\|*

If other beings enjoy good fortune, but I’m powerless to enjoy it too, I should be as happy as they, since my misfortune results from anger I expressed before. 81

## \|\| 1.17.82 \|\|

*baddhavairāṇi bhūtāni dveṣaṃ kurvanti cettataḥ \|*  
*suśocyānyatimohena vyāptānīti manīṣiṇām \|\| 82 \|\|*

As beings bound by hatred feel anger in their hearts, the wise should pity them because they’re filled with great delusion. 82

## \|\| 1.17.83 \|\|

*ete bhinnadṛsāṃ daityā vikalpāḥ kathitā mayā \|*  
*kṛtvābhyupagamaṃ tatra saṃbhepaḥ śrūyatāṃ mama \|\| 83 \|\|*

I’ve described the ideas of those who perceive the deity and creation as distinct from one another. Now listen, if you demon boys are willing, to a summary of the other view. 83

## \|\| 1.17.84 \|\|

*vistāraḥ sarvabhūtasya viṣṇoḥ sarvamidaṃ jagat \|*  
*draṣṭavyamātmavattasmādabhedena vicakṣaṇaiḥ \|\| 84 \|\|*

This whole world is but an aspect of universal Viṣṇu. The wise see everything as one and as no different from themselves. 84

## \|\| 1.17.85 \|\|

*samutsṛjyāsuraṃ bhāvaṃ tasmādyayaṃ yathā vayam \|*  
*tathā yatnaṃ kariṣyāmo yathā prāpsyāma nirvṛtim \|\| 85 \|\|*

Casting off our demonic natures, let us—you and I together—strive to gain the highest bliss. 85

## \|\| 1.17.86 \|\|

*yā nāgninā na cārkeṇa nendunā na ca vāyunā \|*  
*pajanyavaruṇābhyāṃ vā na siddherna ca sākṣasaiḥ \|\| 86 \|\|*

This bliss will never be undone by fire, the sun, the moon, Vāyu, Parjanya, Varuṇa, the siddhas, rākṣasas, 86

## \|\| 1.17.87 \|\|

*na yakṣair na ca daityendrair noragair na ca kinnaraiḥ \|*  
*na manuṣyair na paśubhir doṣair naivātmasambhavaiḥ \|\| 87 \|\|*

Yakṣas, Daitya kings, nāgas, kiṃnaras, humans, animals, afflictions born of our own natures, 87

## \|\| 1.17.88 \|\|

*jvārākṣirogātīsāraplīhagulmādikais tathā \|*  
*dveṣorṣyāmatsarādyaurvā rāgalobhādibhiḥ kṣayam \|\| 88 \|\|*

Fevers, eye diseases, dysentery, diseases of the spleen and other illnesses, anger, envy, greed, passion, avarice or any other thing. 88

## \|\| 1.17.89 \|\|

*na cānyair nīyate kaiścinnityā yātyantanirmalā \|*  
*tāmāpnotyamalenyasya keśave hṛdayaṃ naraḥ \|\| 89 \|\|*

Nothing can rob us of this joy, as it’s the everlasting, purest state attained by one who sets his heart on stainless Keśava. 89

## \|\| 1.17.90 \|\|

*asārasaṃsāravivartaneṣu mā yāta toṣaṃ prasabhaṃ bravīmi \|*  
*sarvatra daityāḥsamatāmupaita samatvamārādhanamacyutasya \|\| 90 \|\|*

I beseech you, Daityas, to give up satisfaction. Be equally disposed in every situation, because equanimity is a form of worship of unfailing Viṣṇu. 90

## \|\| 1.17.91 \|\|

*tasminprasanne kimihāstyalabhyaṃ dharmārthakāmair alamalpakāste \|*  
*samāśritādbrahyataroranantānniḥ saṃśayaṃ prāpstatha vai mahatphalam \|\| 91 \|\|*

When Viṣṇu is gratified, what in this world is out of reach? Virtue, wealth and pleasure will all mean nought to you. 91

## \|\| 1.17.92 \|\|

*sanskrit not found \|\| 92 \|\|*

Great is the reward you’ll surely win if you seek refuge beneath the everlasting tree of knowledge. 92

## Chapter 18 - Prahlāda instructs his gurus

## \|\| 1.18.1 \|\|

*śrīpāraśara uvāca*  
*tasyaitāṃ dānavāśceṣṭāṃ dṛṣṭvā daityapaterbhayāt \|*  
*ācacakhyuḥ sa covāca sūdānāhūya satvaraḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

When the Dānavas saw what Prahlāda was doing, fearing the Daitya lord, they informed Hiraṇyakaṣipu, who called his cooks at once and addressed them thus. 1

## \|\| 1.18.2 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*he sūdāḥmama putrosāvanyoṣāmapi durmatiḥ \|*  
*kumārgadeśiko duṣṭo hanyatāmavilambitam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Come, cooks. Because this perverse and wicked son of mine is leading other boys astray, you must poison him at once. 2

## \|\| 1.18.3 \|\|

*hālāhalaṃ viṣaṃ tasya sarvabhakṣeṣu dīyatām \|*  
*avijñātamasau pāpo hanyatāṃ mā vicāryatām \|\| 3 \|\|*

Without his knowledge, poison his food with hālāhala. This wretch must die as soon as possible! 3

## \|\| 1.18.4 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*te tathaiva tataścakruḥ prahlādāya mahātmane \|*  
*viṣadānaṃ yathājñāptaṃ pitrā tasya mahātmanaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Just as his mighty father ordered, the cooks administered that poison to noble Prahlāda. 4

## \|\| 1.18.5 \|\|

*hālāhalaṃ viṣaṃ ghoramanantoccāraṇena saḥ \|*  
*abhimantrya sahānnena maitreya bubhuje tadā \|\| 5 \|\|*

But Prahlāda sanctified the virulent hālāhala by reciting the name of imperishable Viṣṇu, Maitreya, as he swallowed it with his food. 5

## \|\| 1.18.6 \|\|

*avikāraṃ sa tadbhuktvā prahlādaḥ svasthamānasaḥ \|*  
*anantakhyātinirviryajarayāmāsa tadviṣam \|\| 6 \|\|*

Prahlāda consumed that poison with no ill effects and, remaining calm, he digested the terrible dose rendered harmless by Viṣṇu’s name. 6

## \|\| 1.18.7 \|\|

*tataḥ sūdā bhayatrastā jīrṇaṃ dṛṣṭvā mahadviṣam \|*  
*daityeśvaramupāgamya praṇipatyedamabruvan \|\| 7 \|\|*

Seeing that Prahlāda had consumed it, the fearful cooks came before the Daitya lord and, falling at his feet, reported what had happened. 7

## \|\| 1.18.8 \|\|

*sūdā ūcuḥ*  
*daityarāja viṣaṃ dattamasmābhir atibhīṣaṇam \|*  
*jīrṇaṃ tena sahānnena prahlāhena sutena te \|\| 8 \|\|*

The cooks:

Daitya king, your son, Prahlāda, was able to digest the terrible poison that we fed him with his other food. 8

## \|\| 1.18.9 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*tvaryatāṃ tvaryatāṃ he he sadyo daityapurohitāḥ \|*  
*kṛtyāṃ tasyavināśāya utpādayata mā ciram \|\| 9 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Hurry! Hurry! Come at once, you Daitya priests. Create an apparition to destroy him without delay! 9

## \|\| 1.18.10 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*sakāśamāgamya tataḥ prahlādasya purohitāḥ \|*  
*sāmapūrvamathocuste prahlādaṃ vinayānvitam \|\| 10 \|\|*

Parāśara:

But the household priests then came to Prahlāda, spoke soothingly to him and gave him this advice: 10

## \|\| 1.18.11 \|\|

*purohitā ūcuḥ*  
*jātastrailokyavikhyāta āyuṣmanbrahmaṇaḥ kule \|*  
*daityarājasya tanayo hiraṇyakaśiporbhavāt \|\| 11 \|\|*

The priests:

You were born in Brahmā’s lineage, renowned in all three worlds, as the son of the Daitya king, Hiraṇyakaśipu. May you live long! 11

## \|\| 1.18.12 \|\|

*kindevaiḥ kimanantena kimanyena tavāśrayaḥ \|*  
*pitā te sarvalokānāṃ tvaṃ tathaiva bhaviṣyasi \|\| 12 \|\|*

What good to you are other gods? What good is this so-called imperishable being or anyone else, when your father is the refuge of all the worlds, as you will be one day? 12

## \|\| 1.18.13 \|\|

*tasmātparityajaināṃ tvaṃ vipakṣastavasaṃhitām \|*  
*ślāghyaḥ pitā samastānāṃ gurūṇāṃ paramo guruḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Don’t venerate our enemy. One’s father is the highest guru, so they say. 13

## \|\| 1.18.14 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*evam etan mahābhāgāḥ ślāghyametanmahākulam \|*  
*mariceḥ sakalepyasmin trailokye nā nyāthā vadet \|\| 14 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

‘What you lucky priests have said is true: this great lineage of Marīci is praised in all three worlds, and no-one would say otherwise. 14

## \|\| 1.18.15 \|\|

*pitā ca mama sarvāsmiñjagatyuktṛṣṭaceṣṭitaḥ \|*  
*etadapyavagacchāmi satyamatrāpi nānṛtam \|\| 15 \|\|*

My father has performed prodigious feats throughout the universe. I understand all this and certainly it is true. 15

## \|\| 1.18.16 \|\|

*gurūṇāmapi sarveṣāṃ pitā paramako guruḥ \|*  
*yaduktaṃ bhrāntistatrāpi svalpāpi hi na vidyate \|\| 16 \|\|*

One’s father is indeed the highest guru. I’ve not the slightest doubt about your words. 16

## \|\| 1.18.17 \|\|

*pitā gururna sandehaḥ pūjanīyaḥ pūyatnataḥ \|*  
*tatrāpi nāparādhyāmītyevaṃ manasi se sthitam \|\| 17 \|\|*

One’s father is indeed a guru to be honoured with devotion. I know in my heart that I must never offend against this principle. 17

## \|\| 1.18.18 \|\|

*yattvetatkimanantenetyuktaṃ yuṣmābhir idṛsam \|*  
*ko bravīti yathānyāyyaṃ kiṃ tu naitadvacorthavat \|\| 18 \|\|*

But when you ask things like, “What good is this imperishable being?”, who can say that you’re correct? Your questions make no sense.’ 18

## \|\| 1.18.19 \|\|

*ityuktvā so 'bhavanmaunī teṣāṃ goravayantritaḥ \|*  
*prahasya ca punaḥ prāha kimanantena sādhviti \|\| 19 \|\|*

So saying, Prahlāda fell silent in deference to the priests, but added with a laugh, ‘What good is this imperishable being? An excellent question indeed! 19

## \|\| 1.18.20 \|\|

*sādhu bhoḥ kimanantena sādhu bho guruvo mama \|*  
*śrūyatāṃ yadanantena yadi khedaṃ na yāsyatha \|\| 20 \|\|*

I repeat: an excellent question! I salute you! What good is this imperishable being? Again: an excellent question! I salute you, gurus. Hear what this everlasting being means to me. I hope this won’t upset you. 20

## \|\| 1.18.21 \|\|

*dharmārthakāmamokṣāś ca puruṣārthā udāhṛtāḥ \|*  
*catuṣṭayamidaṃ yasmāttasmāktiṃ kimidaṃ vaca \|\| 21 \|\|*

Virtue, wealth, love and liberation are said to be the goals of life. Your questions about the deity from whom these four arise make no sense to me. 21

## \|\| 1.18.22 \|\|

*marīcimiśrair dakṣādyais tathaivānyair anantataḥ \|*  
*dharma prāptas tathā cānyair arthaḥ kāmastathāparaiḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Marīci, Dakṣa and the other sages learned virtue from this eternal being. Similarly, others acquired wealth, yet others, love. 22

## \|\| 1.18.23 \|\|

*tantattvavedino bhūtvā jñānadhyānasamādhibhiḥ \|*  
*avāpurmuktimapare puruṣā dhvastabandhanāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Some, understanding his true nature through spiritual insight, meditation or realisation, won liberation once their bonds to life were all undone. 23

## \|\| 1.18.24 \|\|

*sampadaiśvaryamāhātmya-jñānasaṃtatikarmaṇām \|*  
*vimukteścaikato labhyaṃ mūlamārādhanaṃ hareḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Worship of Hari is the singular root cause of prosperity, influence, greatness, knowledge, progeny, activity and liberation. 24

## \|\| 1.18.25 \|\|

*yato dharmārthakāmākhyaṃ muktiścapi phalaṃ dvijāḥ \|*  
*tenāpi kiṅkimityevamanantena kimucyate \|\| 25 \|\|*

Since virtue, wealth, love and liberation all result from praising him, brahmins, how could you doubt the good of this imperishable being? 25

## \|\| 1.18.26 \|\|

*kiṃ cāpi bahunoktena bhavanto guravo mama \|*  
*vadantu sādhu vāsādhu viveko 'smākam alpakaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

Why say more, respected gurus? Tell me if I’m right or not, as my ability to discriminate is weak.’ 26

## \|\| 1.18.27 \|\|

*bahunātra kimuktena sa eva jagataḥ patiḥ \|*  
*sa kartā ca vikartā ca saṃhartā ca hṛdi sthitaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

The priests:

We saved you from being burnt alive, you foolish child, thinking that you’d stop this nonsense, but we failed to see how unwise you were. 27

## \|\| 1.18.28 \|\|

*sa bhoktā bhojyamapyevaṃ sa eva jagadīśvaraḥ \|*  
*bhavadbhir etatkṣantavyaṃ bālyāduktaṃ tu yanmayā \|\| 28 \|\|*

If you do not follow our advice and free yourself from the grip of folly, ignorant boy, we’ll conjure up an apparition to destroy you. 28

## \|\| 1.18.29 \|\|

*purohitā ūcuḥ*  
*dahyamānastvamasmābhir agninā bāla rakṣitaḥ \|*  
*bhūyo na vakṣyasītyevaṃ naiva jñātosyabuddhimān \|\| 29 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Who is saved or killed by whom? This whole world of moving and unmoving things destroys and saves itself. 29

## \|\| 1.18.30 \|\|

*yadāsmadvacanānmohagrāhaṃ na tyakṣate bhavān \|*  
*tataḥ kṛtyāṃ vināśāya tava sṛkṣyāma durmate \|\| 30 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After Prahlāda had spoken thus, the priests of the Daitya king were furious and conjured up an awful apparition. Her mouth ablaze with rings of fire, 30

## \|\| 1.18.31 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*kaḥ kena hanyate jantur jantuḥ kaḥ kena rakṣyate \|*  
*hanti rakṣati caivātmā hyasatsādhu samācaran \|\| 31 \|\|*

She scorched the earth with the touch of her feet. With her spear, that raging phantom struck Prahlāda on the chest. 31

## \|\| 1.18.32 \|\|

*karmaṇā jāyate sarvaṃ karmaiva gatisādhanam \|*  
*tasmātsarvaprayatnena sādhu karma samācaret \|\| 32 \|\|*

But when the flaming weapon reached the young boy’s heart, it burst into a hundred shards and fell upon the ground. 32

## \|\| 1.18.33 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktāstena te kruddhā daityarājapurohitāḥ \|*  
*kṛtyāmutpādayāmāsurjvālāmālojjvalākṛtim \|\| 33 \|\|*

The heart in which unfailing Hari dwells could resist the thunderbolt of Indra, to say nothing of a spear. 33

## \|\| 1.18.34 \|\|

*atibhīmā samāgamya pādanyāsakṣatakṣitiḥ \|*  
*śulena sādhu saṃkruddhā taṃ jaghānāśu vakṣasi \|\| 34 \|\|*

The apparition set upon the sinless boy by the wicked Daitya priests now struck out at them and vanished in an instant. 34

## \|\| 1.18.35 \|\|

*tattasya hṛdayaṃ prāpya śūlaṃ bālasya dīptimat \|*  
*jagāma khaṇḍitaṃ bhūmau tatrāpi śatadhā gatam \|\| 35 \|\|*

Seeing the priests scorched by the ghoul, the noble child ran towards them crying, ‘Eternal Kṛṣṇa, save them!’ 35

## \|\| 1.18.36 \|\|

*yatrānapāyī bhagavān hṛdyāste haririśvaraḥ \|*  
*bhaṅgo bhavati vajrasya tatra śūlasya kā kathā \|\| 36 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

You pervade most everything, Janārdana, and are the form and author of the world. Save these priests from this spell-born fire, so difficult to endure. 36

## \|\| 1.18.37 \|\|

*āpāpe tatra pāpaiś ca pātitā daityāyājakaiḥ \|*  
*tāneva sā jaghānāśu kṛtyā nāśaṃ jagāma ca \|\| 37 \|\|*

Since you are Viṣṇu, guru of the world who abides in all, spare these priests. 37

## \|\| 1.18.38 \|\|

*kṛtyayā dahyamānāṃstanvilokya sa mahāmatiḥ \|*  
*trāhi kṛṣṇetyananteti vadannabhyavapadyata \|\| 38 \|\|*

As I meditate on you, universal and eternal Viṣṇu, and even though I think they’ve sided with my enemy, spare these brahmins’ lives. 38

## \|\| 1.18.39 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*sarvavyāpin jagadrūpa jagatstraṣṭarjanārdana \|*  
*pāhi viprānimānasmādduḥ sahānmantrapāvakāt \|\| 39 \|\|*

If I regard as friendly those who came to kill me, fed me poison, tried to burn me, sent elephants to crush me and snakes to bite me, 39

## \|\| 1.18.40 \|\|

*yathā sarveṣu bhūteṣu sarvavyāpī jagadguruḥ \|*  
*viṣṇureva tathā sarve jīvantvete purohitāḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

And if I’m free from sin, then, by that same truth, spare the lives of these demon priests today. 40

## \|\| 1.18.41 \|\|

*yathā sarvagataṃ viṣṇuṃ manyamānonapāyinam \|*  
*cintayāmyaripakṣepi jīvantvete purohitāḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After Prahlāda spoke these words, all the afflicted brahmins rose unharmed and addressed him with respect. 41

## \|\| 1.18.42 \|\|

*ye hantumāgatā dattaṃ yorviṣaṃ yair hutāśanaḥ \|*  
*yair diggajair ahaṃ kṣuṇṇo daṣṭaḥ sarpaiś ca yair api \|\| 42 \|\|*

The priests:

May you lead a long and stainless life, dear boy, imbued with strength and courage, and be blessed with sons and grandsons, wealth and power. 42

## \|\| 1.18.43 \|\|

*teṣvahaṃ mitrabhāvena samaḥ pāposmi na kvacit \|*  
*yathā tenādya satyena jīvantvasurayājakāḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, the priests approached the Daitya king to tell him what had happened, sage. 43

## Chapter 19 - Prahlāda’s torments continue

## \|\| 1.19.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*hiraṇyakaśipuḥ śrutvā tāṃ kṛtyāṃ vitathīkṛtām \|*  
*āhūya putraṃ papraccha prabhāvasyāsya kāraṇam \|\| 1 \|\|*

When Hiraṇyakaśipu heard that the phantom had been foiled, he summoned his son to ask about the origin of his strength. 1

## \|\| 1.19.2 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*prahlāda suprabhāvosi kimetatte viceṣṭitam \|*  
*etanmantrādijanitamutāho sahajaṃ tava \|\| 2 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Your endurance is amazing, Prahlāda. How do you do it? Is it the result of some mantra or were you born with it? 2

## \|\| 1.19.3 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvacā*  
*evaṃ pṛṣṭastadā pitrā prahlādo 'surabālakaḥ \|*  
*praṇipatya pituḥ pādāvidaṃ vacanamabravīt \|\| 3 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Questioned by Hiraṇyakaśipu, the Daitya boy Prahlāda fell at his father’s feet and gave him this reply: 3

## \|\| 1.19.4 \|\|

*na mantrādikṛtaṃ tāta na ca naisargiko mama \|*  
*prabhāva eṣa sāmānyo yasya yasyācyuto hṛdi \|\| 4 \|\|*

‘My strength does not arise from a mantra or anything else, father, nor was I born with it, but it’s common to all who hold in their hearts the everlasting deity. 4

## \|\| 1.19.5 \|\|

*anyeṣāṃ yo na pāpāni cintayatyātmano yathā \|*  
*tasya pāpāgamastāta hetva bhāvānna vidyate \|\| 5 \|\|*

A being who wishes no harm on others, as he wouldn’t wish it on himself, father, will undergo no harm, as harm will have no cause. 5

## \|\| 1.19.6 \|\|

*karmaṇā manasā vāca paripīḍāṃ karoti yaḥ \|*  
*tadvījaṃ janma phalati prabhūtaṃ tasya cāśubham \|\| 6 \|\|*

The harm one brings upon another by word or thought or deed becomes the seed of one’s next birth and will result in pain. 6

## \|\| 1.19.7 \|\|

*so 'haṃ na pāpa micchāmi na karomi vadāmi vā \|*  
*cintayansarvabhūtasthamātmanyapi ca keśavam \|\| 7 \|\|*

I wish no harm on anyone, nor do I act or speak unkindly, thinking Keśava exists in every being, as he does in me. 7

## \|\| 1.19.8 \|\|

*śārīraṃ mānasaṃ duḥkhaṃ daivaṃ bhūtabhavaṃ tathā \|*  
*sarvatra śubhacittasya tasya me jāyeta kutaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

As my mind is well disposed to everything, how can I experience bodily or mental suffering sent by heaven or by earth? 8

## \|\| 1.19.9 \|\|

*evaṃ sarveṣu bhūteṣu bhaktiravyabhicāriṇī \|*  
*kartavyā paṇḍitair jñātvā sarvabhūtamayaṃ harim \|\| 9 \|\|*

Knowing that Hari is at the heart of all, the wise should show unwavering devotion towards all beings.’ 9

## \|\| 1.19.10 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*iti śrutvā sa daityendraḥ prāsādaśikhare sthitaḥ \|*  
*krodhāndhakāritamukhaḥ prāha daiteyakiṅkarān \|\| 10 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing his son say this, the face of the Daitya king, who was standing on the palace roof, grew black with fury as he cried out to his demon minions: 10

## \|\| 1.19.11 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*durātmā kṣipyatāmasmātprāsādācchatayojanāt \|*  
*giripṛṣṭhe patatvasmin śilābhinnāṅgasaṃhatiḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

‘Throw this wicked child off the roof—it’s a hundred leagues above the ground! Drop him on that mountain so his body’s smashed upon the rocks.’ 11

## \|\| 1.19.12 \|\|

*tatastaṃ cikṣupuḥ sarve bālaṃ daiteyadānavāḥ \|*  
*papāta so 'py adhaḥ kṣipto hṛdayenodvahahnarim \|\| 12 \|\|*

The Daitya servants threw the boy from the palace roof, but after they had done so and even as he fell, Prahlāda still held Hari in his heart. 12

## \|\| 1.19.13 \|\|

*patamānaṃ jagaddhātrī jagaddhātari keśave \|*  
*bhaktiyuktaṃ dadhārainamupasaṃgamyamaijinī \|\| 13 \|\|*

As he reached the ground, the Earth, supporter of the world, caught him as he fell, filled as he was with devotion to Keśava, protector of the universe. 13

## \|\| 1.19.14 \|\|

*tato vilokya taṃ svasthamaviśirṇasthipañjaram \|*  
*hiraṇyakaśipuḥ prāha śambaraṃ māyināṃ varam \|\| 14 \|\|*

Seeing Prahlāda unharmed and without a single broken bone, Hiraṇyakaśipu spoke to Śambara, his most powerful sorcerer: 14

## \|\| 1.19.15 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*nāsmābhiḥ śakyate hantumasau durbuddhibālakaḥ \|*  
*māyāṃ vetti bhavāṃstasmānmāyayainaṃ niṣūdaya \|\| 15 \|\|*

‘We can’t kill this wretched child, but you know magic, so you can do it!’ 15

## \|\| 1.19.16 \|\|

*śambara uvāca*  
*sūdayāmyeva daityaindra paśya māyābalaṃ mama \|*  
*sahasramatra māyānāṃ paśya koṭiśataṃ tathā \|\| 16 \|\|*

Śambara:

I’ll kill him, Daitya king. Just watch the power of my spells. Behold these thousand apparitions, and here are another million! 16

## \|\| 1.19.17 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tataḥ sa sasṛje māyāṃ prahlāde śambaro 'suraḥ \|*  
*vināśamicchandurbuddhiḥ sarvatra samadarśini \|\| 17 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Then the wicked demon Śambara, desiring Prahlāda’s ruin, loosed the apparitions upon the child who was equally kind to all. 17

## \|\| 1.19.18 \|\|

*samāhitamatirbhūtvā śambarepi vimatsaraḥ \|*  
*maitreya so 'pi prahlādaḥ sasmāra madhusūdanam \|\| 18 \|\|*

But Prahlāda mastered his thoughts, Maitreya, and, free from anger even towards the sorcerer, he contemplated Viṣṇu, Madhu’s nemesis. 18

## \|\| 1.19.19 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tato bhagavatā tasya rakṣārthaṃ cakramuttamam \|*  
*ājagāma samājñaptaṃ jvālāmāli sudarśanam \|\| 19 \|\|*

The brilliant discus, Sudarśana, wreathed in flames and summoned by the lord, appeared to save the boy. 19

## \|\| 1.19.20 \|\|

*tena māyāmahasraṃ tacchambarasyāśugāminā \|*  
*bālasya rakṣatā dehamekaikaṃ cāriśodhitam \|\| 20 \|\|*

The speeding weapon destroyed each and every one of the thousand apparitions raised by Śambara, to defend the child. 20

## \|\| 1.19.21 \|\|

*saṃśoṣakaṃ tathā vāyuṃ daityendrastvidamabravīt \|*  
*śīgrameṣa mamādeśāddurātmā nīyatāṃ kṣayam \|\| 21 \|\|*

The Daitya king then addressed the drying wind, ‘I order you to kill this wicked child at once.’ 21

## \|\| 1.19.22 \|\|

*tathetyuktvā tu so 'py enaṃ viveśa pavano laghu \|*  
*śītotirukṣaḥ śoṣāya taddehasyātiduḥ sahaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

‘So be it,’ replied the raging wind as it filled the boy. Cold and harsh, the undaunted wind strove to dry his body. 22

## \|\| 1.19.23 \|\|

*tenāviṣṭamathātmānaṃ sa buddha datyabālakaḥ \|*  
*hṛdayena mahātmānaṃ dadhāra dharaṇīdharam \|\| 23 \|\|*

The Daitya lad, knowing he’d been beset by wind, set his heart on mighty Viṣṇu, foundation of the world. 23

## \|\| 1.19.24 \|\|

*hṛdayasthastatastasya taṃ vāyumati bhīṣaṇam \|*  
*papau janārdhanaḥ kruddhaḥ sa yayau pavanaḥ kṣayam \|\| 24 \|\|*

Furious Janārdana, abiding in Prahlāda’s heart, imbibed the wind and caused it to be still. 24

## \|\| 1.19.25 \|\|

*kṣīṇāsu sarvamāyāsu pavane ca kṣayaṃ gate \|*  
*jagāma so 'pi bhavanaṃ kuroreva mahāmatiḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

When all the apparitions had departed and the wind was overcome, wise Prahlāda then returned to his guru’s home. 25

## \|\| 1.19.26 \|\|

*ahanyahanyathācāryo nītiṃ rājyaphalapradām \|*  
*grāhayāmāsa taṃ bālaṃ rājñāmusanasā kṛtām \|\| 26 \|\|*

Each day, his teacher taught the boy good governance as devised for rulers by the sage Uśanas, so that they might govern well. 26

## \|\| 1.19.27 \|\|

*gṛhītanītiśāstraṃ taṃ vinītaṃ ca yadā guruḥ \|*  
*mene tadainaṃ tatpitre kathayāmāsa śikṣitam \|\| 27 \|\|*

When Prahlāda’s guru judged that he had mastered all these texts, he informed Hiraṇyakaśipu that his son was now well-schooled. 27

## \|\| 1.19.28 \|\|

*ācārya uvāca*  
*gṛhītanītiśāstraste putro daityapate kṛtaḥ \|*  
*prahlādastattvato vetti bhārgaveṇa yadīritam \|\| 28 \|\|*

The master:

I’ve taught your son, Prahlāda, the science of governance, Daitya king, and now he truly understands the principles of Uśanas, Bhṛgu’s scion. 28

## \|\| 1.19.29 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*mitreṣu varteta kathamarivargeṣu bhūpatiḥ \|*  
*prahlāda triṣu lokeṣu madhyastheṣu kathaṃ caret \|\| 29 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

How should a king behave towards his friends and foe, Prahlāda? What should he do in the three times of decrease, increase and stability? How should he treat those neutral parties? 29

## \|\| 1.19.30 \|\|

*kathaṃ mantri ṣvamātyeṣu bāhyoṣvābhyantareṣu ca \|*  
*cāreṣu pauravargeṣu śaṅkiteṣvitareṣu ca \|\| 30 \|\|*

How should he behave towards his councillors and ministers, those responsible for affairs inside the palace and outside it, informants and his citizens, those whom he suspects and those he trusts? 30

## \|\| 1.19.31 \|\|

*kṛtyākṛtyavidhānañca durgāṭavikasādhanam \|*  
*prahlāda kathyatāṃ samyak tathā kaṇṭakaśodhanam \|\| 31 \|\|*

Relationships that one should seek or shun, the means of handling strongholds, forest tribes and thorny issues—describe in detail ways to manage each of these, Prahlāda. 31

## \|\| 1.19.32 \|\|

*etaccānyacca sakalamadhītaṃ bhavatā yathā \|*  
*tathā me kathyatāṃ jñātuṃ tavecchāmi manogatam \|\| 32 \|\|*

Tell me this and other things you’ve learned, as I’d like to hear your thoughts. 32

## \|\| 1.19.33 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*praṇipatya pituḥ pādau tadā praśrayabhūṣaṇaḥ \|*  
*prahlādaḥ prāha daityendraṃ kṛtāñjalipuṭas tathā \|\| 33 \|\|*
