Parāśara:

Prahlāda bowed at his father’s feet, wearing respect like an ornament, and, standing before the Daitya king, he spoke with folded palms. 33

## \|\| 1.19.34 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*mamo padiṣṭaṃ sakalaṃ gurumā nātra saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*gṛhītantu mayā kintu na sadetanmataṃmama \|\| 34 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

My guru taught all this to me, no doubt, and I’ve taken it all in, but I do not think that it’s correct. 34

## \|\| 1.19.35 \|\|

*sāma copapradānaṃ ca bhedadaṇḍau tathāparau \|*  
*upāyāḥ kathitāḥ sarve mitrādināṃ ca sādhane \|\| 35 \|\|*

He described conciliation, generosity, subversion and aggression as strategies for dealing with one’s allies and with others. 35

## \|\| 1.19.36 \|\|

*sarvabhūtātmake tāta jagannāthe jaganmaye \|*  
*paramātmani govindai mitrādīṃstāta mā kruddhaḥ \|*  
*sādhyābhāve mahābāho sādhanaiḥ kiṃ prayojanam \|\| 36 \|\|*

But I see neither friend nor foe—don’t be angry, father—you’re a strong-armed warrior yourself, but if there’s nothing to be achieved, why bother with the means? 36

## \|\| 1.19.37 \|\|

*sarvabūtātmake tāta jagannāthe jaganmaye \|*  
*paramātmani govinde mitrāmitrakathā kutaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

As Govinda is at the heart of all creation, the universal lord and highest being who incorporates the world, father, why speak of friend and enemy? 37

## \|\| 1.19.38 \|\|

*tvāyyasti bhagavān viṣṇurmayi cānyatra cāsti saḥ \|*  
*yatastatoyaṃ mitraṃ me śatruśceti pṛthaktutaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

Because Lord Viṣṇu is in you, in me and every other thing, what’s the difference between my friend and him I call my enemy? 38

## \|\| 1.19.39 \|\|

*tad ebhir alam atyarthaṃ duṣṭārambhoktivistaraiḥ \|*  
*avidyāntargatair yatnaḥ kartavyastāta śobhene \|\| 39 \|\|*

What use are boring talks on dubious subjects based on ignorance, father, when we should strive for virtue? 39

## \|\| 1.19.40 \|\|

*vidyābuddhiravidyāyāmajñānāntāta jāyate \|*  
*bālo 'gniṃ kiṃ na khadyotam asureśvara manyate \|\| 40 \|\|*

The idea of what forms knowledge arises from our ignorance, father, and a lack of understanding, demon king. Does not a child mistake a firefly for a fire? 40

## \|\| 1.19.41 \|\|

*tatkarma yan na bandhāya sā vidyā yā vimuktaye \|*  
*āyāsāyāparaṃ karma vidyānyā śilpanaipuṇam \|\| 41 \|\|*

Action that doesn’t lead to bondage is true knowledge that sets us free. All other action is wasted effort; all other knowledge merely artful cleverness. 41

## \|\| 1.19.42 \|\|

*tadetadavagamyāhamasāraṃ sāramuttamam \|*  
*niśāmaya mahābhāga praṇipatya bravīmi te \|\| 42 \|\|*

I therefore hold that all of this is vain. Now listen to the highest goal. Bowing at your feet, blessed monarch, I’ll describe it. 42

## \|\| 1.19.43 \|\|

*na cintayati ko rājyaṃ ko dhanaṃ nābhivāñchati \|*  
*tathāpi bhāvyamevaitadubhayaṃ prāpyate naraiḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

Who doesn’t worry about sovereignty? Who doesn’t hanker after wealth? People may have both in the life that follows this. 43

## \|\| 1.19.44 \|\|

*sarva eva mahābhāga mahattvaṃ prati sodyamāḥ \|*  
*tathāpi puṃsāṃ bhagyāni nodyamā bhūtihetavaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

Everyone strives for greatness, fortunate king, but fate, not effort, will determine their success. 44

## \|\| 1.19.45 \|\|

*jaḍānāmavivekānāmaśūrāṇamapi prabho \|*  
*bhāgyabhojyāni rājyāni santyanītimatāmapi \|\| 45 \|\|*

Even fools, cowards, the undiscerning and those who know nought of governance, lord, win kingdoms because of fate. 45

## \|\| 1.19.46 \|\|

*tasmādyateta puṇyeṣu ya icchenmahatīṃ śriyam \|*  
*yatitavyaṃ samatve ca nirvāṇamapi cecchatā \|\| 46 \|\|*

One who desires the greatest glory should therefore strive for virtue, and one who yearns for liberation should aspire to equanimity. 46

## \|\| 1.19.47 \|\|

*devā manuṣyāḥ paśavaḥ pakṣivṛkṣasarīsṛpāḥ \|*  
*rūpametadanantasya viṣṇor bhinnam iva sthitam \|\| 47 \|\|*

Deities, mortals, animals, birds, plants and creeping creatures are all forms of everlasting Viṣṇu, yet all seem to stand apart from him. 47

## \|\| 1.19.48 \|\|

*etadvijānatā sarvaṃ jagatsthāvarajaṅgamam \|*  
*draṣṭavyamātmavadviṣṇur yato 'yaṃ viśvarūpadhṛk \|\| 48 \|\|*

One who understands all this perceives the world of moving and unmoving things as no different from himself, and as one with Viṣṇu, whose form is universal. 48

## \|\| 1.19.49 \|\|

*evaṃ jñāte sa bhagavānanādiḥ parameśvaraḥ \|*  
*prasīdatyacyutastasminprasanne kleśasaṃkṣayaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

When this is understood, the beginningless lord, the imperishable supreme master, is pleased and, when he’s pleased, all afflictions cease. 49

## \|\| 1.19.50 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*etacchrutvā tu kopena samutthāya varāsanāt \|*  
*hiraṇyakaśipuḥ putraṃ padā vakṣasyatāḍayat \|\| 50 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, Hiraṇyakaśipu leapt from his throne in a rage and kicked his son in the chest. 50

## \|\| 1.19.51 \|\|

*uvāca ca sa kopena sāmarṣaḥ prajyalanniva \|*  
*niṣpiṣya pāṇinā piṇiṃ hantukāmo jagadyathā \|\| 51 \|\|*

The impetuous king seemed to blaze with anger and wrung his hands together as if he intended to destroy the entire world as he spoke. 51

## \|\| 1.19.52 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*he vipracitte he rāho he balaiṣa mahārṇave \|*  
*nāgāpāśair daḍhair baddhā kṣipyatāṃ mā vilaṃbyatām \|\| 52 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

Come, Vipracitti! Come, Rāhu! Come, Bali! Bind this boy with serpent-ropes and throw him in the ocean. Don’t delay! 52

## \|\| 1.19.53 \|\|

*anyathā sakalā lokās tathā daiteyadānavāḥ \|*  
*anuyāsyanti mūḍhasya matamasya durātmanaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

If not, all this world, including Dānavas and Daityas, will start to follow this stubborn fool’s ideas. 53

## \|\| 1.19.54 \|\|

*bahuśo vāri tosmābhir ayaṃ pāpastathāpyareḥ \|*  
*stutiṃ karoti duṣṭānāṃ vadha evopakārakaḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

We’ve tried everything to stop him, but the wicked child keeps singing praises of our foe. Getting rid of evildoers is a social service! 54

## \|\| 1.19.55 \|\|

*parāśara uvāca*  
*tataste satvarā daityā baddhā taṃ nāgabandhanaiḥ \|*  
*bharturājñāṃ puraskṛtya cikṣipuḥ salilārṇave \|\| 55 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The Daityas then quickly bound Prahlāda with serpent-ropes and, with their master’s orders foremost in their minds, they cast him in the sea. 55

## \|\| 1.19.56 \|\|

*tataścacāla calatā prahlādena mahārṇavaḥ \|*  
*udvelobhūtparaṃ kṣobhamupetya ca sama ntataḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

But when Prahlāda hit the water, the ocean was set in motion and threw up a towering wave, causing floods in all directions. 56

## \|\| 1.19.57 \|\|

*bhurlokamakhilaṃ dṛṣṭvā plāvyamānaṃ mahāmbhasā \|*  
*hiraṇyakaśipurdaityānidamāha māhamate \|\| 57 \|\|*

Seeing the whole world inundated, great sage, Hiraṇyakaśipu addressed the Daityas. 57

## \|\| 1.19.58 \|\|

*hiraṇyakaśipur uvāca*  
*daiteyāḥ sakalaiḥ śailair atraiva varuṇālaye \|*  
*niśchidraiḥ sarvaśaḥ sarvaiś cīyatāmeṣa durmatiḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu:

‘Cover this wicked child in the ocean, Varuṇa’s realm, with all the rocks you Daityas find. Don’t leave a single gap. 58

## \|\| 1.19.59 \|\|

*nāgnirdahati naivāyaṃ śastraiś chinno na coragaiḥ \|*  
*kṣayaṃ nīto na vātena na viṣeṇa na kṛtyayā \|\| 59 \|\|*

Fire didn’t burn him; weapons, serpents, poison, wind and magic all failed to harm him. 59

## \|\| 1.19.60 \|\|

*na māyābhir na caivoccatpātito na ca diggajaiḥ \|*  
*bālotiduṣṭacittoyaṃ nānenārthosti jīvatā \|\| 60 \|\|*

Neither apparitions, nor falling from a height, nor elephants of the quarters could destroy this evil-minded child, but there’s no point in allowing him to live. 60

## \|\| 1.19.61 \|\|

*tadeṣa toyamadhye tu samākrānto mahīdharaiḥ \|*  
*tiṣṭhatvabdasahasrāntaṃ prāṇānhāsyati durmatiḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

Let’s keep him in the water under heaps of rocks for a thousand years—that should finish off the wretch!’ 61

## \|\| 1.19.62 \|\|

*tato daityādānavāś ca parvatais taṃ mahodadhau \|*  
*ākramya cayanaṃ cakruryojanāni sahasraśaḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

The Daityas and the Dānavas then piled mountains on Prahlāda underwater, raising a heap that soared a thousand leagues. 62

## \|\| 1.19.63 \|\|

*sa citaḥ parvaterantaḥ samudrasya mahāmatiḥ \|*  
*tuṣṭāvāhnika velāyāmekāgramatiracyutam \|\| 63 \|\|*

But at the time of his daily rites, from below the heap of rocks on the ocean floor, wise Prahlāda intently worshipped Viṣṇu who never fails. 63

## \|\| 1.19.64 \|\|

*prahlā uvāca*  
*namaste puṇḍarīkākṣa namaste puruṣottama \|*  
*namaste sarvalokātmannamaste tigmacakriṇe \|\| 64 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

I bow to you, lotus-eyed deity. I bow to you, supreme being. I bow to you, heart of the world. I bow to you, wielder of the flaming discus. 64

## \|\| 1.19.65 \|\|

*namo brahmaṇyadevāya gobrāhmaṇahitāya ca \|*  
*jagaddhitāya kṛṣṇāya govindāya namonamaḥ \|\| 65 \|\|*

I bow to the deity of the pious, patron of the cattle and of brahmins, benefactor of the world. To Kṛṣṇa and to Govinda, I bow and bow again. 65

## \|\| 1.19.66 \|\|

*brahmatve sṛjate viśvaṃ sthitau pālayate punaḥ \|*  
*rudrarūpāya kalpānte namastubhyaṃ trimūrtaye \|\| 66 \|\|*

In the form of Brahmā, you create the world. While it endures, you keep it. At the aeon’s end, you take the form of Rudra. I bow to you, deity of threefold forms. 66

## \|\| 1.19.67 \|\|

*devā yakṣāḥ surāḥ siddhā nāgā gandharvakinnarāḥ \|*  
*piśācā rākṣasāścaiva manuṣyāḥ paśavas tathā \|\| 67 \|\|*

Gods, yakṣas, demigods, siddhas, nāgas, gandharvas, kiṃnaras, piśācas, rākṣasas, humans and animals, 67

## \|\| 1.19.68 \|\|

*pakṣiṇasthāvarāścaiva pipīlikasarīsṛpāḥ \|*  
*bhūmyāpognirnabhovāyuḥ śabdaḥ sparśas tathā rasaḥ \|\| 68 \|\|*

Birds, inanimate things, ants and crawling creatures, earth, water, fire, sky, wind, sound, touch, taste, 68

## \|\| 1.19.69 \|\|

*rūpaṃ gandho mano buddhirātmā kālas tathā guṇāḥ \|*  
*eteṣāṃ paramārthaś ca sarvametattvamacyuta \|\| 69 \|\|*

Form, smell, mind, intellect, ego, time and the qualities known as guṇas—you are the highest sense of these, as well as being all of these, eternal Viṣṇu. 69

## \|\| 1.19.70 \|\|

*vidyāvidye bhavānsatyamasatyaṃ tvaṃ viṣāmṛte \|*  
*pravṛttaṃ ca nivṛttaṃ ca karma vedoditaṃ bhavān \|\| 70 \|\|*

You are knowledge and you are ignorance, truth and falsehood, poison and nectar, the practice of rites and contemplation. You are the rituals determined by the Vedas. 70

## \|\| 1.19.71 \|\|

*samastakarmabhoktā ca karmopakaraṇāni ca \|*  
*tvameva viṣṇo sarvāṇi sarvakarmaphalaṃ ca yat \|\| 71 \|\|*

You are the beneficiary of all these acts and the means of their achievement. You are all this, Viṣṇu, and the result of every ritual. 71

## \|\| 1.19.72 \|\|

*mayyanyatra tathānyeṣu bhūteṣu bhuvaneṣu ca \|*  
*tavaivāvyāptiraiśvaryaguṇasaṃsūcikī prabho \|\| 72 \|\|*

Your presence, Lord, which manifests power and virtue, is in me, in others, in all beings and all the worlds. 72

## \|\| 1.19.73 \|\|

*tvāṃ yoginaścintayanti tvāṃ yajanti ca yājakāḥ \|*  
*havyakavyabhugekastvaṃ pitṛdevasvarūpadhṛk \|\| 73 \|\|*

Ascetics contemplate you, and those who offer sacrifices sacrifice to you. You alone enjoy oblations intended for gods and spirits, as you are one with them. 73

## \|\| 1.19.74 \|\|

*rūpaṃ mahatte sthitamatra viśvaṃ tataś ca sūkṣmaṃ jagadetadīśa \|*  
*rūpāmi sarvāṇi ca bhūtabhedāsteṣvantarātmākhyamatīva sūkṣmam \|\| 74 \|\|*

Everything’s encompassed in your great form, imperishable Lord, from which is born your subtle form, this world and other subtle forms, the fundamental elements and that which is known as the subtlest essence of them all. 74

## \|\| 1.19.75 \|\|

*tasmācca sūkṣmādiviśeṣaṇānāmagocare yatparamātmarūpam \|*  
*kimapyacintyaṃ tava rūpamasti tasmai namaste puruṣottamāya \|\| 75 \|\|*

The form that is the highest goal beyond description of gross or subtle, something that surpasses thought, is also a form of yours. I bow to you in the form of the highest spirit. 75

## \|\| 1.19.76 \|\|

*sarvabhūteṣu sarvātmanyā śaktiraparā tava \|*  
*guṇāśrayā namastasyai śāśvatāyai sureśvara \|\| 76 \|\|*

You who are at the heart of everything, I bow to your other form, the universal power in every being, abode of threefold guṇas, Lord of gods. 76

## \|\| 1.19.77 \|\|

*yātītagocarā vācāṃ manāsāṃ cāviśeṣaṇā \|*  
*jñānijñānaparicchedya tāṃ vande sveśvarīṃ parām \|\| 77 \|\|*

I praise that supreme and heavenly power that lies beyond the reach of words and is not encompassed by the mind, but is perceptible to the wisdom of the wise. 77

## \|\| 1.19.78 \|\|

*oṃ namo vāsudevāya tasmai bhagavate sadā \|*  
*vyatiriktaṃ na yasyāsti vyatiriktokhilasya yaḥ \|\| 78 \|\|*

Oṃ! May I always bow to Vāsudeva, Lord. Nothing is distinct from him, but he’s distinct from everything. 78

## \|\| 1.19.79 \|\|

*namastasmai namastasmai namastasmai mahātmane \|*  
*nāma rūpaṃ na yasyaiko yostitvenopalabhyate \|\| 79 \|\|*

I bow to him. I bow to him. I bow to that great being who has neither name nor form, and who alone is understood by the fact of his existence. 79

## \|\| 1.19.80 \|\|

*yasyāvatārarūpāmi samarcanti divaukasaḥ \|*  
*apaśyantaḥ paraṃ rūpaṃ namastasmai mahātmane \|\| 80 \|\|*

I bow to that great being whose earthly forms are worshipped by the denizens of heaven, being blind to his supreme form. 80

## \|\| 1.19.81 \|\|

*yontastiṣṭhannaśoṣasya paśyatīśaḥ śubhāśubham \|*  
*taṃ sarvasākṣiṇaṃ viśvaṃ namasye parameśvaram \|\| 81 \|\|*

I bow to him who dwells in all, the lord who sees both good and evil, Viṣṇu, the universal witness and supreme master. 81

## \|\| 1.19.82 \|\|

*namostu viṣṇave tasmai yasyā bhinnamidaṃ jagat \|*  
*dhyeyaḥ sa jagatāmādyaḥ sa prasīdatu me 'vyayaḥ \|\| 82 \|\|*

Let us all bow down to Viṣṇu, who is no different from this world. May we all regard him as the first in every realm. May that imperishable being be favourable to me. 82

## \|\| 1.19.83 \|\|

*yatrotametatprotaṃ ca viśvamakṣaramavyayam \|*  
*ādhārabhūtaḥ sarvasya sa prasīdatu me hariḥ \|\| 83 \|\|*

May Hari, the universe’s warp and weft, known as the everlasting deity and support of all creation, be favourable to me. 83

## \|\| 1.19.84 \|\|

*oṃ namo viṣṇave tasmai namastasmai punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*yatra sarvaṃ yataḥ sarvaṃ yaḥ sarvaṃ sarvasaṃśrayaḥ \|\| 84 \|\|*

Oṃ! I bow to Viṣṇu. I bow again and again to him in whom all this exists, from whom all this arises, who is all this, yet stands apart from it. 84

## \|\| 1.19.85 \|\|

*sarvagatvādanantasya sa evāhamavasthitaḥ \|*  
*mattaḥ sarvamahaṃ sarvaṃ mayi sarvaṃ sanātane \|\| 85 \|\|*

Because that eternal being pervades all things, I am him. All this is born from me, I am all this, all this is in me, the everlasting being. 85

## \|\| 1.19.86 \|\|

*ahamevākṣayo nityaḥ paramātmātmasaṃśrayaḥ \|*  
*brahmasaṃjño 'hamevāgre tathānte ca paraḥ pumān \|\| 86 \|\|*

I myself am indestructible and eternal, I’m the abode of the heart of the heart of everything. I’m the one called brahman, the Absolute, the highest being at the start and at the end. 86

## Chapter 20 - Prahlāda achieves liberation

## \|\| 1.20.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃ saṃcintayanviṣṇumabhedenātmano dvija \|*  
*tanmayatvamavāpyagrya mene cātmānamacyutam \|\| 1 \|\|*

Thus contemplating Viṣṇu as unified with himself, brahmin, Prahlāda felt that he’d achieved the highest state of oneness with that eternal being. 1

## \|\| 1.20.2 \|\|

*visasmāra tathātmānaṃ nānyatkiñcidajānata \|*  
*ahamevāvyayo 'nantaḥ paramātmetyacintayat \|\| 2 \|\|*

He forgot himself and knew of nothing else while thinking, ‘I am indeed the unchanging, unending, highest spirit.’ 2

## \|\| 1.20.3 \|\|

*tasya tadbhāvanāyogātkṣīṇapāpasya vai kramāt \|*  
*śuddhentaḥkaraṇe viṣṇustasthau jñānamayocyutaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

As the result of Prahlāda’s practice of cultivating the deity and being cleansed in time of all his sins, eternal Viṣṇu, knowledge itself, filled his unsullied heart. 3

## \|\| 1.20.4 \|\|

*yogaprabhāvātprahlāde jāte viṣṇumaye 'sure \|*  
*calatyuragabandhais tair maitreya truṭitaṃ kṣaṇāt \|\| 4 \|\|*

When the Daitya Prahlāda, now one with Viṣṇu through the power of his practice, moved his limbs, Maitreya, those serpent-bonds all snapped at once. 4

## \|\| 1.20.5 \|\|

*bhrantagrāhagamaḥ sormiryayau kṣobhaṃ mahārṇavaḥ \|*  
*cacāla ca mahī sarvā saśailavanakānanā \|\| 5 \|\|*

The boundless undulating ocean, with its surging hosts of monsters, was agitated, and all the world with mountains, groves and forests began to quake. 5

## \|\| 1.20.6 \|\|

*sa ca taṃ śailasaṃghātaṃ daityair nyastamathopari \|*  
*utkṣipya tasmātsalilānniścakrāma mahāmatiḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Casting off the rocks the Daityas heaped on him, wise Prahlāda rose from the ocean depths. 6

## \|\| 1.20.7 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā ca sa jagadbhū6yo gaganādyupalakṣaṇam \|*  
*prahlādosmīti sasmāra punarātmānamātmani \|\| 7 \|\|*

Beholding the world, the sky and everything else, he remembered who he was once more. 7

## \|\| 1.20.8 \|\|

*tuṣṭāva ca punardhāmānanādiṃ puruṣottamam \|*  
*ekāgramatiravyagro yatavākvāyamānasaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Wise Prahlāda, firm and focused, sang further praises of the highest being who has no start, his body, voice and mind restrained. 8

## \|\| 1.20.9 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*oṃnamaḥ paramārthāya sthūla sūkṣmakṣarākṣara \|*  
*vyaktāvyaktakalātīta sakaleśa nicañjana \|\| 9 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Oṃ! I bow to you, highest of all goals, gross and subtle, perishable and imperishable, visible and invisible, who transcends division, lord of all, free from faults, 9

## \|\| 1.20.10 \|\|

*guṇāñjana guṇādhāra nirguṇātman guṇasthita \|*  
*mūrtāmūrtamahāmūrte sūkṣmamūrte sphuṭāsphuṭa \|\| 10 \|\|*

Bestower of qualities, source of qualities, free from qualities, abiding in qualities, formed and formless, gross and subtle, distinct and indistinct. 10

## \|\| 1.20.11 \|\|

*karālasaumyarūpātmanvidyāvidyāmayācyuta \|*  
*sadasadrūpasadbhāva sadasadbhāvabhāvana \|\| 11 \|\|*

You are fearsome and benign, knowledge and ignorance, eternal Lord, you are existence and nonexistence, and the cause of both, 11

## \|\| 1.20.12 \|\|

*nityānityaprapañcātmanniṣprapañcāmalāśrita \|*  
*ekāneka namastubhyaṃ vāsudevādikāraṇa \|\| 12 \|\|*

Permanent and impermanent, you are manifold elements and the unstained dwelling place of elements non-manifold. You are one and you are many. I bow to you, Vāsudeva, the primary cause of everything, 12

## \|\| 1.20.13 \|\|

*yaḥ sthūlasūkṣmaḥ prakaṭaḥ prakāśo yaḥ sarvabhūto na ca sarvabhūtaḥ \|*  
*viśvaṃ yataścaitadaviśvaheto namostu tasmai puruṣottamāya \|\| 13 \|\|*

Gross and subtle, evident and manifest. You are all creation and yet distinct from it. Everything arises from you, yet you are distinct from the cause of everything. I bow to you, the highest being. 13

## \|\| 1.20.14 \|\|

*parāśara uvāca*  
*tasya taccetaso devaḥ stutimitthaṃ prakṛrvataḥ \|*  
*āvirbabhūva bhagavān pītāmbaradharo hariḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Parāśara:

While Prahlāda sang this praise, his mind on Viṣṇu, Lord Hari appeared before him, clad in yellow robes. 14

## \|\| 1.20.15 \|\|

*sasambhramas tam ālokya samutthāyākulākṣaram \|*  
*namostu viṣṇavetyetavdyājahārāsa kṛddvija \|\| 15 \|\|*

Seeing him, Prahlāda was thrown into confusion, brahmin. He stood up and stammered repeatedly, ‘I bow to Viṣṇu!’ 15

## \|\| 1.20.16 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*deva prapannārtihara prasādaṃ kuru keśava \|*  
*avalokanadānena bhūyo māṃ pāvayācyuta \|\| 16 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Lord, you take away the pain we feel. Be merciful to me, Keśava. Purify me further, eternal god, by looking down on me. 16

## \|\| 1.20.17 \|\|

*śrībhagavān uvāca*  
*kurvataste prasannohaṃ bhaktimavyabhicāriṇīm \|*  
*yathābhilaṣito mattaḥ prahlāda vrīyatāṃ varaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

The Lord:

I’m pleased with your unwavering devotion, Prahlāda. You may ask for anything you desire. 17

## \|\| 1.20.18 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*nātha yonisahasreṣu yeṣu yeṣu vrajāmy aham \|*  
*teṣu teṣvacyutābhaktiracyutāstu sadā tvayi \|\| 18 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Master, in whichever of myriad wombs I am reborn, may I always have undying faith in you. 18

## \|\| 1.20.19 \|\|

*yā prītiravivekānāṃ viṣayeṣvanapāyinī \|*  
*tvāmanusmarataḥ sā me hṛdayānmāpasarpatu \|\| 19 \|\|*

May that constant joy the undiscerning find in objects of the senses never leave my heart while contemplating you. 19

## \|\| 1.20.20 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*mayi bhaktistavāstyeva bhūyo 'py evaṃ bhaviṣyati \|*  
*varastu mattaḥ prahlāda vrīyatāṃ yastavepsitaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

The Lord:

You are indeed my devotee, Prahlāda, and will be so in future. Now ask for something else that you desire. 20

## \|\| 1.20.21 \|\|

*prahlāda uvāca*  
*mayi dveṣānubandhobhūtsaṃstutāvudyate tava \|*  
*matpitustatkṛtaṃ pāpaṃ deva tasya pramaśyatu \|\| 21 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

As I was intent on praising you, my father was enraged. Lord, forgive the sins that he’s committed. 21

## \|\| 1.20.22 \|\|

*śastrāṇi pātitānyaṅge kṣipto yaccāgnisaṃhatau \|*  
*daṃśitaścoragair dattaṃ yadviṣaṃ mama bhojane \|\| 22 \|\|*

Weapons struck my body, I was thrown into a fire, I was bitten by his serpents and my food was laced with poison, 22

## \|\| 1.20.23 \|\|

*baddhā samudre yatkṣipto yaccito 'smi śiloccayaiḥ \|*  
*anyāni cāpyasādhūni yāni pitrā kṛtāti me \|\| 23 \|\|*

I was bound and cast into the ocean where mountains were heaped upon me. These and other wicked deeds my father has committed. 23

## \|\| 1.20.24 \|\|

*tvayi bhaktimato dveṣādaghaṃ tatsaṃbhavaṃ ca yat \|*  
*tvatprasādātprabho sadyastena mucyatu me pitā \|\| 24 \|\|*

All this he did as I, your devotee, had angered him, so by your mercy, Lord, set my father free at once. 24

## \|\| 1.20.25 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*prahlāda sarvametatte matprasādādbhāviṣyati \|*  
*anyacca te varaṃ daghni vrīyatāmasurātmaja \|\| 25 \|\|*

The Lord:

By my mercy all will be as you desire, Prahlāda. I grant you yet another boon, so make a wish, you demon boy. 25

## \|\| 1.20.26 \|\|

*prahalāda uvāca*  
*kṛtakṛtyosmi bhagavanvare ṇānena yattvayi \|*  
*bhavitrī tvatprasādena bhaktiravyabhicāriṇī \|\| 26 \|\|*

Prahlāda:

Now you’ve granted me my wishes, I’ve done all I had to do, Lord. By your mercy, may my devotion never waver. 26

## \|\| 1.20.27 \|\|

*dharmārthakāmaiḥ kiṃ tasya muktistasya kare stitā \|*  
*samastajagatāṃ mūle yasya bhaktiḥ sthirā tvayi \|\| 27 \|\|*

What are virtue, wealth or love to one with freedom in his hands and whose faith in you, the root of all the worlds, is firm? 27

## \|\| 1.20.28 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca*  
*yathā te niścalaṃ ceto mayi bhaktisamanvitam \|*  
*tathā tvaṃ matprasādena nirvāṇamparamāpsyasi \|\| 28 \|\|*

The Lord:

Since your steady heart is filled with faith in me, you’ll reach final liberation with my blessing. 28

## \|\| 1.20.29 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktvāntardadhe viṣṇus tasya maitreya paśyataḥ \|*  
*sa cāpi punarāgamya vavande caraṇau pituḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, Viṣṇu withdrew from sight before Prahlāda’s eyes, Maitreya. The lad then approached his sire and paid homage at his feet. 29

## \|\| 1.20.30 \|\|

*taṃ pitā mūrdhnyupāghrāya pariṣvajya ca pīḍitam \|*  
*jīvasītyāha vatseti bāṣpārdranayanodvija \|\| 30 \|\|*

Hiraṇyakaśipu kissed Prahlāda’s forehead and embraced the boy who had endured so much. ‘You’re alive, my son!’ he cried as tears were glistening in his eyes, brahmin. 30

## \|\| 1.20.31 \|\|

*prītimāṃścābhavattasminnanutāpī mahāsuraḥ \|*  
*gurupitroścakāraivaṃ śuśrūṣāṃ so 'pi dharmatam \|\| 31 \|\|*

That great demon, now regretful, showed affection to his son, and Prahlāda, who knew virtue, duly served his guru and his father. 31

## \|\| 1.20.32 \|\|

*pitaryuparatiṃ nīte narasiṃha svarūpiṇā \|*  
*viṣṇunā so 'pi daityānāṃ maitreyābhūtpatistataḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

After Hiraṇyakaśipu was destroyed by Viṣṇu in the form of Narasiṃha, Maitreya, Prahlāda became the Daityas’ lord. 32

## \|\| 1.20.33 \|\|

*tato rājyadyutiṃ prāpya karmaśuddhikarīṃ dvīja \|*  
*putrapautrāṃś ca subahūn avāpyaiśvaryameva ca \|\| 33 \|\|*

Having attained the glory of sovereignty through his pure deeds, brahmin, he had many sons and grandsons, and enjoyed great supremacy. 33

## \|\| 1.20.34 \|\|

*kṣīṇādhikāraḥ sa yadā puṇyapāpavivirjitaḥ \|*  
*tadā sa bhagavaddhyānātparaṃ nīrvāṇamāppavān \|\| 34 \|\|*

When Prahlāda shed his worldly duties and transcended notions of good and evil, while contemplating the lord intently, he finally accomplished liberation. 34

## \|\| 1.20.35 \|\|

*evaṃ prabhāvo daityo 'sau maitreyāsīn mahāmatiḥ \|*  
*prahlādo bhagavadbhakto yaṃ tvaṃ mām anupṛcchasi \|\| 35 \|\|*

Such was the power of the prudent Daitya, Prahlāda, devotee of the lord, Maitreya, about whom you questioned me. 35

## \|\| 1.20.36 \|\|

*yastvetaccārītaṃ tasya prahlādasya mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*śṛṇoti tasya pāpāni sadyo gacchanti saṃkṣayam \|\| 36 \|\|*

The sins of one who hears the deeds of this great being are washed away at once. 36

## \|\| 1.20.37 \|\|

*ahorātrakṛtaṃ pāpaṃ prahlādacaritaṃ naraḥ \|*  
*śṛṇvan paṭhaṃś ca maitreya vyapohati na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

Hearing or narrating this account will free a man from misdeeds done by day or night, Maitreya. 37

## \|\| 1.20.38 \|\|

*paurmamāsyāmamāvāsyāmaṣṭamyāmatha vā paṭhan \|*  
*dvādaśyāṃ tadāprantoti gopradānaphalaṃ dvija \|\| 38 \|\|*

Reciting it when the moon is full or new, or on the eighth or twelfth day of the lunar month, brahmin, confers the same reward as the donation of a cow. 38

## \|\| 1.20.39 \|\|

*prahlādaṃ sakalāpatsu yathā rakṣitavānhariḥ \|*  
*tathā rakṣati yastasya śṛṇoti caritaṃ sadā \|\| 39 \|\|*

Just as Hari shielded Prahlāda in his trials, he’ll protect a person who listens to this tale. 39

## Chapter 21 - Kaśyapa’s descendants and the origin of the Maruts

## \|\| 1.21.1 \|\|

*prahlādaputra āyuṣmāñchibirbāṣkala eva ca \|*  
*virocanastu prāhladirbalirjajñe virocanāt \|\| 1 \|\|*

The sons of Prahlāda’s brother Saṃhlāda were Āyuṣmant, Śibi and Bāṣkala. Prahlāda’s son was Virocana, and Virocana’s son was Bali. 1

## \|\| 1.21.2 \|\|

*baleḥ putraśataṃ tvasīdbāṇajyeṣṭhaṃ mahāmune \|*  
*hiraṇyākṣasutāścāsansarva eva mahā balā \|\| 2 \|\|*

Bali had one hundred sons, great sage, of whom Bāṇa was the eldest. The sons of Hiraṇyakaśipu’s brother Hiraṇyākṣa were all mighty beings: 2

## \|\| 1.21.3 \|\|

*jharjharaḥ śakuniścaiva bhūtasantāpanas tathā \|*  
*mahānābho mahābāhuḥ kālanābhastathāparaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Jharjhara, Śakuni, Bhūtasaṃtāpana, strong-armed Mahānābha and also Kālanābha. 3

## \|\| 1.21.4 \|\|

*abhavandanupūtrāś ca dvimūrdha śaṃbaras tathā \|*  
*ayomukhaḥ śaṅkuśirāḥ kapilaḥ śaṅkaras tathā \|\| 4 \|\|*

The sons of Kaśyapa’s third wife, Danu, were Dvimūrdhan, Śaṃkara, Ayomukha, Śaṅkuśiras, Kapila, Śambara, 4

## \|\| 1.21.5 \|\|

*ekacakro mahābāhustārakaś ca mahābalaḥ \|*  
*svarbhānurvṛṣaparvā ca puloma ca mahābalaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Strong-armed Ekacakra, mighty Tāraka, Svarbhānu, Vṛṣaparvan and powerful Puloma. 5

## \|\| 1.21.6 \|\|

*ete danoḥ sutāḥ khyātā vipracittiś ca viryavān \|\| 6 \|\|*

These, along with heroic Vipracitti, were the famous sons of Danu. 6

## \|\| 1.21.7 \|\|

*svarbhānostu prabhā kanyā śarmiṣṭhā vārṣaparvaṇī \|*  
*upadānī hayaśiraḥ prakhyātā varakanyakāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Prabhā was the daughter of Svarbhānu. Śarmiṣṭhā was the daughter of Vṛṣaparvan, as were Upadānavī and Hayaśiras—all renowned and brilliant women. 7

## \|\| 1.21.8 \|\|

*vaiśvānarasute cobhe pulomā kālakā tathā \|*  
*ubhe sute mahābhāge mārīcestu parigrahaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Danu’s son Vaiśvānara had two daughters, Pulomā and Kālakā, both of whom were highly fortunate and married Mārīci. 8

## \|\| 1.21.9 \|\|

*tābhyā putrasahasrāṇi ṣaṣṭirdānavasattamāḥ \|*  
*paulomāḥ kālake yāś ca mārīcatanayāḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

These two had sixty thousand sons with Mārīci, the excellent Dānavas known as the Paulomas and Kālakeyas. 9

## \|\| 1.21.10 \|\|

*tato 'pare mahāvīryā dāruṇā stvatinirghṛṇā \|*  
*siṃhikāyāmathotpannā vipracitte sutās tathā \|\| 10 \|\|*

Other mighty, fearsome and pitiless sons were born to Vipracitti and Siṃhikā: 10

## \|\| 1.21.11 \|\|

*tryaṃśaḥ śalyaś ca balavān nabhaścaiva mahābalaḥ \|*  
*vātāpī namuciścaiva ilvalaḥ khasṛmas tathā \|\| 11 \|\|*

Tryaṃśa, powerful Śalya, Nabha the strong, Vātāpi, Namuci, Ilvala, Khasṛma, 11

## \|\| 1.21.12 \|\|

*āndhako narakaścaiva kālanābhastathaiva ca \|*  
*svarbhānuś ca mahāvīryo vaktrayodhī mahāsuraḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Andhaka, Naraka, Kālanābha, heroic Svarbhānu and the great demon Vaktrayodhin. 12

## \|\| 1.21.13 \|\|

*ete vai dānavāḥ śreṣṭhā danuvaṃśavivardhanāḥ \|*  
*eteṣāṃ putrapautraś ca śataśo 'tha sahasraśaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

These were the best of the Dānavas who swelled the lineage of Danu and whose sons and grandsons numbered in the hundreds and thousands. 13

## \|\| 1.21.14 \|\|

*prahlādasya tu daityasya nivāta kavacāḥ kule \|*  
*samutpannāḥ sumahatā tapasā bhāvitātmanaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

The Nivātakavacas, purified through austere penances, also arose in the family of the Daitya Prahlāda. 14

## \|\| 1.21.15 \|\|

*ṣaṭ sutāḥ sumahāsattvāstāmrāyāḥ parikīrtitāḥ \|*  
*śukī śyenī ca bhāsī ca sugrīvī śucigṛddhrakā \|\| 15 \|\|*

The six daughters of Kaśyapa’s fourth wife, Tāmrā, renowned as mighty beings, were Śukī, Śyenī, Bhāsī, Sugrīvī, Śuci and Gṛdhrikā. 15

## \|\| 1.21.16 \|\|

*śukī śukānajanayadulūkapratyulūkikān \|*  
*śyenī śyenāṃs tathā bhāsī bhāsāngṛrdhyāṃś ca gṛddhryapi \|\| 16 \|\|*

Śukī gave rise to parrots, Ulūkī to crows, the natural enemies of owls, while Śyeni gave rise to hawks, Bhāsī to kites and Gṛdhrī to vultures. 16

## \|\| 1.21.17 \|\|

*śucyau dakānpakṣigaṇānsugrīvī tu vyajāyata \|*  
*aśvānuṣṭrāngardabhāṃ ś ca tāmrāvaṃśaḥ prakīrtitaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Śuci brought forth waterbirds, and Sugrīvī gave rise to horses, camels and donkeys. All these are known as the lineage of Tāmrā. 17

## \|\| 1.21.18 \|\|

*vinatāyāstu dvau putrau viratau garuḍāruṇau \|*  
*suparṇaḥ patatāṃ śreṣṭho dāruṇaḥ pannagāśanaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Kaśyapa’s fifth wife, Vinatā, bore two sons, famed Garuḍa and Aruṇa. Fair-winged Garuḍa, foremost of flying creatures, was the merciless devourer of serpents. 18

## \|\| 1.21.19 \|\|

*surasāyāṃ sahasraṃ tu sarpāṇāmamitaujasām \|*  
*anekaśirasāṃ brahman khecarāmāṃ mahātmanām \|\| 19 \|\|*

His sixth wife, Surasā, gave rise to a thousand great many-headed snakes of boundless energy, brahmin, which coursed the sky. 19

## \|\| 1.21.20 \|\|

*kāndraveyāstu balinaḥ sahasramamitaujasaḥ \|*  
*suparṇavaśāgā brahman jajñire naikamastakāḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

His seventh wife, Kadru, gave rise to the Kādravas, another thousand mighty serpents subject to fair-winged Garuḍa, brahmin, similarly many-headed. 20

## \|\| 1.21.21 \|\|

*teṣāṃ pradhānabhūtāstu śeṣavāsukitakṣakāḥ \|*  
*śaṅkhaśveto mahāpadmaḥ kambalāśvatarau tathā \|\| 21 \|\|*

Chief among these were Śeṣa, Vāsuki, Takṣaka, Śaṅkaśveta, Mahāpadma and both Kambala and Aśvatara, 21

## \|\| 1.21.22 \|\|

*elāputras tathā nāgaḥ karkoṭakadhanañjayau \|*  
*ete cānye ca bahavo dandaśukā viṣolbaṇā \|\| 22 \|\|*

Elāputra, Nāga, Karkoṭaka and Dhanaṃjaya—these and many other serpents full of venom. 22

## \|\| 1.21.23 \|\|

*gaṇaṃ krodhavaśaṃ viddhi tasyāḥ sarve ca daṃṣṭriṇaḥ \|*  
*sthalajāḥ pakṣiṇobjāś ca dāruṇāḥ piśitāśanāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

You also need to know about the host descended from Kaśyapa’s eighth wife, Krodhavaśā: whether born on land, flying on the wing or living in water, all were pitiless carnivores with sharp teeth. 23

## \|\| 1.21.24 \|\|

*krodhāttu piśācāṃś ca janayāmāsa mahābalān \|*  
*gāstu vai janayāmāsa surābhīrmahiṣīs tathā \|*  
*irāvṛkṣalatāvallīstṛmajatīś ca sarvaśaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

His ninth wife, Surabhi, gave rise to cattle and buffalo, and Irā to varieties of trees and vines, grass and creepers everywhere. 24

## \|\| 1.21.25 \|\|

*svasā tu yakṣarakṣāṃsi munirapsarasas tathā \|*  
*ārīṣṭā tu mahāsattvān gandharvānsamajījanat \|\| 25 \|\|*

His tenth wife brought forth yakṣas and rākṣasas; his eleventh, Muni, bore apsarases; and his twelfth, Ariṣṭā, gave birth to the great gandharvas. 25

## \|\| 1.21.26 \|\|

*ete kaśyapadāyādāḥ kīrtitāḥ sthāṇujaṅgamāḥ \|*  
*teṣāṃ putrāś ca pautrāś ca śataśo 'tha sahasraśaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

These, then, are the renowned descendants of Kaśyapa, both moving and unmoving, whose sons and grandsons number in the hundreds of thousands. 26

## \|\| 1.21.27 \|\|

*eṣa manvantare sargo brahmansvārociṣesmṛtaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

This creation took place in the second Manvantara, known as Svārociṣa, brahmin. In the present Manvantara, the Vaivasvata, while Brahmā was engaged in the great sacrifice initiated by Varuṇa, 27

## \|\| 1.21.28 \|\|

*vaivasvate ca mahati vāruṇe vitate kṛtau \|*  
*juhvānasya brahmaṇo vai prajāsarga ihocyate \|\| 28 \|\|*

The so-called Prajāsarga or ‘progeny creation’ took place. In the age before this, Brahmā brought forth the Seven Sages from his mind. 28

## \|\| 1.21.29 \|\|

*pūrvaṃ yatra tu saptarṣinutpannānsaptamānasān \|*  
*putratve kalpayāmāsa svayameva pitāmahaḥ \|*  
*gandharvabhogidevānāṃ dānavānāṃ ca sattama \|\| 29 \|\|*

Now he brought them to the world as his own sons, and he himself became the grandsire of the gandharvas, serpents, deities and Dānavas, best of sages. 29

## \|\| 1.21.30 \|\|

*ditirvinaṣṭaputrā vai toṣayāmāsa kāśyapam \|*  
*tayā cārādhitaḥ samyakkāśyapastapatāṃ paraḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

Diti, who later lost her sons, pleased Kaśyapa, and that foremost of ascetics, being fully gratified, then granted her a wish. 30

## \|\| 1.21.31 \|\|

*vareṇa cchandayāmāsa sā ca vavre tato varam \|*  
*putramindravadhārthāya samarthama mitaujasam \|\| 31 \|\|*

She asked him for a son of boundless power, one capable of slaying Indra. 31

## \|\| 1.21.32 \|\|

*sa ca tasmai varaṃ prādādbhāryāyai munisattamaḥ \|*  
*dattvā ca varamatyugraṃ kaśyapastāmuvāca ha \|\| 32 \|\|*

That foremost sage allowed his wife her wish, and, having done so, Kaśyapa calmly said to her, 32

## \|\| 1.21.33 \|\|

*śakraṃ putro nihantā te yadi garbhaṃ śaracchatam \|*  
*samāhitātiprayatā śaucinī dhārayiṣyasi \|\| 33 \|\|*

‘Your son will conquer Indra if you’re pure and steadfast and bear the infant in your womb for a hundred years with perfect love.’ 33

## \|\| 1.21.34 \|\|

*ityevasuktvā tāṃ devīṃ saṃgataḥ kaśyapo muniḥ \|*  
*dadhāra sā ca taṃ garbhaṃ samyakchocasamanvitā \|\| 34 \|\|*

Having addressed that heavenly woman, sagacious Kaśyapa departed, and Diti, filled with faultless purity, then conceived a child. 34

## \|\| 1.21.35 \|\|

*garbhamātmavadhārthāya jñātvā taṃ maghavānapi \|*  
*śuśrūṣustāmathāgacchadvinayādamarādhipaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

Mighty Indra, king of gods, knowing that the child was destined to destroy him, respectfully approached the woman to wait upon her. 35

## \|\| 1.21.36 \|\|

*tasyā ścaivāntaraprepsuratiṣṭhatpākaśāsanaḥ \|*  
*ūne varṣaśate cāsyā dadarśāntaramātmanā \|\| 36 \|\|*

Indra, chastiser of the demon Pāka, remained in Diti’s presence while waiting for a chance to thwart her, and, in the ninety-ninth year, the self-possessed deity saw an opportunity. 36

## \|\| 1.21.37 \|\|

*akṛtvā pādayoḥ śaucaṃ ditiḥ śayanamāviśat \|*  
*nidrāṃ cāhārayāmāsa tasyāḥ kukṣiṃ praviśya saḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

Diti lay down to sleep one day, but forgot to wash her feet, and Indra entered her womb. 37

## \|\| 1.21.38 \|\|

*vajrapāṇirmahāgarbhaṃ cicchedātha sa saptadhā \|*  
*sampīḍyamāno pajreṇa sa rurodādidāruṇam \|\| 38 \|\|*

With his vajra in his hand, he cut the mighty foetus into seven pieces. Sliced by Indra’s weapon, the infant wailed most piteously. 38

## \|\| 1.21.39 \|\|

*mā rodīriti taṃ śakraḥ punaḥpunarabhāṣata \|*  
*so 'bhavatsaptadhā garbhastamidraḥ kupitaḥ punaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

‘Don’t cry! Mā rodīḥ!’ Indra said repeatedly, and, even though the foetus was already cut in seven, the deity’s anger grew. 39

## \|\| 1.21.40 \|\|

*ekaikaṃ saptadhā cakre vajreṇārividāriṇā \|*  
*maruto nāma devāste babhūvurativeginaḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

Indra, scourge of enemies, then used his vajra to cut each piece into seven more. These became in time the speeding deities of the wind. 40

## \|\| 1.21.41 \|\|

*yaduktaṃ vai bhagavatā tenaiva maruto 'bhavan \|*  
*devā ekonapachcāśatsahāyā vajrapāṇinaḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

Because mighty Indra said mā rodīḥ, they were called the Maruts, forty-nine in all, deity-companions to the bearer of the vajra. 41

## Chapter 22 - Brahmā appoints sovereigns; Nature of the Absolute

## \|\| 1.22.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*yadābhiṣiktaḥ sa pṛthuḥ pūrvaṃ rājye maharṣibhiḥ \|*  
*tataḥ krameṇa rājyāni dadau lokapitāmahaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

When Pṛthu had been anointed sovereign of the earth by the foremost sages, Brahmā, grandsire of the world, bestowed the other sovereignties in order. 1

## \|\| 1.22.2 \|\|

*nakṣatragrahaviprāṇāṃ vīrudhāṃ cāpyaśeṣataḥ \|*  
*somaṃ rājye dadhadbrahma yajñānāṃ tapasāmapi \|\| 2 \|\|*

To Soma he gave sovereignty over the stars, planets, brahmins, all plant life, as well as sacrifices and austerities. 2

## \|\| 1.22.3 \|\|

*rājñāṃ vaiśravaṇaṃ rājye jalānāṃ varuṇaṃ tathā \|*  
*ādityānāṃ patiṃ viṣṇuṃ vasūnāmatha pāvakam \|\| 3 \|\|*

He gave Vaiśravaṇa sovereignty over kings, and Varuṇa over waters. He made Viṣṇu lord of Ādityas and shining Agni lord of Vasus. 3

## \|\| 1.22.4 \|\|

*prajāpatīnāṃ dakṣaṃ tu vāsavaṃ marutāmapi \|*  
*daityānāṃ dānavānāṃ ca prahlādamadhipaṃ dadau \|\| 4 \|\|*

He made Dakṣa lord of patriarchs, Indra lord of Maruts and Prahlāda lord of Daityas and Dānavas. 4

## \|\| 1.22.5 \|\|

*pitṝṇāṃ dharmarājānaṃ yamaṃ rājye 'bhyaṣecayat \|*  
*airāvataṃ garjedrāṇāmaśoṣāṇāṃ patiṃ dadau \|\| 5 \|\|*

He caused Yama, king of justice, to be anointed lord of pitṛs, the ancestral spirits, and made Airāvata lord of all the elephant kings. 5

## \|\| 1.22.6 \|\|

*patatriṇāṃ tu guruḍaṃ devānāmapi vāsavam \|*  
*uccaiḥśravasamaśvānāṃ vṛṣabhaṃ tu gavāmapi \|\| 6 \|\|*

He made Garuḍa king of birds, Indra king of gods, Uccaiḥśravas lord of horses and Vṛṣabha king of cattle. 6

## \|\| 1.22.7 \|\|

*mṛgāṇāṃ caiva sarveṣāṃ rājye siṃhaṃ dadau prabhuḥ \|*  
*śeṣaṃ tu dandaśūkānāmakarotpatimavyayaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

He made Śeṣa lord of serpent kings, the lion the king of beasts and caused the sacred fig to be anointed king of forest trees. 7

## \|\| 1.22.8 \|\|

*himālayaṃ sthāvaraṇāṃ munīnāṃ kapilaṃ munim \|*  
*nakhināṃ deṣṭriṇāṃ caiva mṛgāṇāṃ vyāghramīśvaram \|\| 8 \|\|*

Having thus shared out sovereignties, Brahmā, lord of patriarchs, immediately appointed guardians for each direction. 8

## \|\| 1.22.9 \|\|

*vanaspatīnāṃ rājāneḥ plakṣamaivābyaṣecayat \|*  
*evamevānyajātīnāṃ prādhānyenākarotprabhūn \|\| 9 \|\|*

He caused the king Sudhanvan, son of the patriarch Vairāja, to be anointed guardian-king of the east. 9

## \|\| 1.22.10 \|\|

*evaṃ vibhajya rājyāni diśāṃ pālānanantaram \|*  
*prajāpatipatirbrahmā sthāpayāmāsa sarvataḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

The son of the patriarch Kardama, Śaṅkhapada by name, was anointed king of the south. 10

## \|\| 1.22.11 \|\|

*pūrvasyāṃ diśi rājānaṃ vairājasya prajāpateḥ \|*  
*diśāpālaṃ sudhanvānaṃ sutaṃ vai so 'bhyaṣecayat \|\| 11 \|\|*

He had the great immortal Ketumant, son of Rajas, anointed king of the west. 11

## \|\| 1.22.12 \|\|

*dakṣiṇasyāṃ diśi tathā kardamasya prajāpateḥ \|*  
*putraṃ śaṅkhapadaṃ nāma rājānaṃ so 'bhyaṣecayat \|\| 12 \|\|*

Hiraṇyaroman, son of the patriarch Parjanya, was anointed doughty king of the north. 12

## \|\| 1.22.13 \|\|

*paścimasyāṃ diśi tathā rajasaḥ putramacyutam \|*  
*ketumantaṃ mahātmānaṃ rājānaṃ so 'bhyaṣaicayat \|\| 13 \|\|*

Even today, this whole world with its forests and seven continents is protected by these beings, realm by realm, in accord with virtue. 13

## \|\| 1.22.14 \|\|

*tathā hiraṇyaromāṇaṃ parjanyasya prajāpateḥ \|*  
*udīcyāṃ diśi durdharṣaṃ rājānamabhyaṣecayat \|\| 14 \|\|*

All these and other sovereigns, invested with authority for the preservation of the world by mighty Viṣṇu, best of sages, 14

## \|\| 1.22.15 \|\|

*tair iyaṃ pṛthivī sarvā saptadvīpā sapattanā \|*  
*yathāpradeśamadyāpi dharmataḥ paripālyate \|\| 15 \|\|*

As well as those to come in future and lords of creation in times gone by, are aspects of Viṣṇu, who himself is all creation, best of brahmins. 15

## \|\| 1.22.16 \|\|

*ete sarve pravṛttasya sthitau viṣṇormahātmanaḥ \|*  
*vibhūtibhūtā rājāno ye cānye munisattama \|\| 16 \|\|*

Kings of gods and kings of Daityas, lords of Dānavas and flesh-eating demons, 16

## \|\| 1.22.17 \|\|

*ye bhaviṣyanti ye bhūtāḥ sarve bhūteśvarā dvija \|*  
*te sarve sarvabhūtasya viṣṇoraṃśā dvijottama \|\| 17 \|\|*

Lords of beasts and winged creatures, and lords of humankind and snakes and nāgas, 17

## \|\| 1.22.18 \|\|

*ye tu devādhipatayo ye ca daityādhipās tathā \|*  
*dānavānāṃ ca ye nāthā ye nāthāḥ piśitāśinām \|\| 18 \|\|*

Lords of trees and mountains and planets, those of the present, past and future—all these arise as aspects of Viṣṇu, who is all creation. 18

## \|\| 1.22.19 \|\|

*paśunāṃ ye ca patayaḥ patayo ye ca pakṣiṇām \|*  
*manuṣyāṇāṃ ca sarpāṇāṃ nāgānāmadhipāś ca ye \|\| 19 \|\|*

None other than Hari, lord of all, wise sage, has the power to preserve the world. 19

## \|\| 1.22.20 \|\|

*vṛkṣāṇāṃ parvatānāṃ ca grahāṇāṃ cāpi ye 'dhipāḥ \|*  
*atītā vartamānāś ca ye bhaviṣyanti cāpare \|*  
*te sarve sarvabhūtasya viṣṇoraṃśasamudbhāvāḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

He creates this universe and, once it’s made, he is the everlasting being who preserves it. In the form of the destroyer, he destroys it, filled in turn with energy, purity and the other quality, darkness. 20

## \|\| 1.22.21 \|\|

*na hi pālanasāmarthyamṛte sarveśvaraṃ harim \|*  
*sthitaṃ sthitau mahāprājña bhavatyanyasya kasyacit \|\| 21 \|\|*

Janārdana appears in four aspects during the creation of the world, another four during its preservation and yet another four during final dissolution. 21

## \|\| 1.22.22 \|\|

*sṛjatyeṣa jagatsṛṣṭau sthitau pāti sanātanaḥ \|*  
*hanti caivāntakatvena rajaḥsattvādisaṃśrayaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

During creation, one aspect is Brahmā, the manifest form of the unmanifest being. The second is the patriarch Marīci and the others. 22

## \|\| 1.22.23 \|\|

*caturvibhāgaḥ saṃsṛṣṭau caturdhā saṃsthitaḥ sthitau \|*  
*pralayaṃ ca karotyante caturbhedo janārdanaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Time is the third aspect, and all other creatures are the fourth. Thus, he exists in four aspects during the creation of the world, having the quality of energy. 23

## \|\| 1.22.24 \|\|

*ekenāṃśena brahmasau bhavatyavyaktamūrtimān \|*  
*marīcimiśrāḥ patayaḥ prajānāñcānyabhāgaśaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

In one aspect, Viṣṇu preserves creation; in another, he manifests as Manu and the patriarchs; and in yet another takes the form of Time. 24

## \|\| 1.22.25 \|\|

*kālastṛtīyastasyāṃśaḥ sarvabhūtāni cāparaḥ \|*  
*itthaṃ caturdhā saṃsṛṣṭau vartatesau rajoguṇaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Manifesting in yet another aspect within all creatures, he comprises all the world. Taking on the quality of purity, he becomes puruṣottama, the highest spirit of the universe. 25

## \|\| 1.22.26 \|\|

*ekāṃśenāsthito viṣṇuḥ karoti pratipālanam \|*  
*manvādirūpaścānyena kālarūpo 'paroṇa ca \|\| 26 \|\|*

Assuming the quality of darkness at the time of dissolution, the birthless lord manifests one aspect in the form of Rudra. 26

## \|\| 1.22.27 \|\|

*sarvabhūteṣu cānyena saṃśthitaḥ kurute sthitim \|*  
*sattvaṃ guṇaṃ samāśritya jagataḥ puruṣottamaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

Another aspect of Viṣṇu exists in the forms of Agni, Yama and the other gods. The third appears in the form of Time, and yet another is all creatures. 27

## \|\| 1.22.28 \|\|

*āśritya tamaso vṛttimantakāle tathā punaḥ \|*  
*rudrasvarūpo bhagavānekāṃśena bhavatyajaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Thus, this great deity exists in four aspects during the destruction of the world. This set of four aspects is called sārvakālikī, ‘universal dissolution’, brahmin. 28

## \|\| 1.22.29 \|\|

*agnyantakādirūpeṇa bhāgenānyena vartate \|*  
*kālasvarūpo bhāgo yaḥsarvabhūtāni cāparaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

Brahmā, Dakṣa and the other patriarchs, Time and other creatures—these are manifestations of Hari’s power and are the cause of the creation of the world. 29

## \|\| 1.22.30 \|\|

*vināśaṃ kurvatastasya caturdhaivaṃ mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*vibhāgakalpanā brahman kathyate sārvakālikī \|\| 30 \|\|*

Viṣṇu is Manu and the progenitors, Time and other beings, brahmin. These are also manifestations of his power, and he himself is the cause of the preservation of the world. 30

## \|\| 1.22.31 \|\|

*brahma dakṣādayaḥ kālastathai vākhilajantavaḥ \|*  
*vibhūtayo hareretā jagataḥ sṛṣṭihetavaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

Rudra, Time, Yama, the other deities and all creatures are the fourfold manifestations of Janārdana’s power for the destruction of the universe. 31

## \|\| 1.22.32 \|\|

*viṣṇurmanvādayaḥ kālaḥ sarvabhūtāni ca dvija \|*  
*sthiternimittabhūtasya viṣṇoretā vibhūtayaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

At the beginning of the world, in the middle period and until its final dissolution, brahmin, the processes of creation are undertaken by the arranger Viṣṇu through the agency of Marīci, the patriarchs and other living creatures. 32

## \|\| 1.22.33 \|\|

*rudraḥ kālāntakādyāś ca samastāścaiva jantavaḥ \|*  
*caturdhā pralāyāyaitā janārdanavibhūtayaḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

In the beginning, Brahmā creates the world, then Marīci and the foremost patriarchs bring forth progeny unhindered, as do other creatures. 33

## \|\| 1.22.34 \|\|

*jagadādau tathā madhye sṛṣṭirāpralayā dvija \|*  
*dhātrā marīcimiśraiś ca kriyate jantubhis tathā \|\| 34 \|\|*

But Brahmā cannot undertake creation without Time, brahmin, nor can the patriarchs or any other beings. 34

## \|\| 1.22.35 \|\|

*brahma sṛjatyādikāle marīcipramukhāstataḥ \|*  
*utpādayantyapatyāni jantavaś ca pratikṣaṇam \|\| 35 \|\|*

Thus, these four aspects of Viṣṇu, god of gods, Maitreya, are essential for creation and destruction of the world. 35

## \|\| 1.22.36 \|\|

*kālena na vinā brahma sṛṣṭiniṣpādako dvija \|*  
*na prajāpatayaḥ sarve na caivākhilajantavaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

Anything brought forth by a living being, brahmin, is entirely Hari’s body. 36

## \|\| 1.22.37 \|\|

*evameva vibhāgoyaṃ stitāvapyupadiśyati \|*  
*caturdā tasya devasya maitreya pralaye tathā \|\| 37 \|\|*

Whatever destroys a creature, whether moving or unmoving, anywhere, Maitreya, is the destructive form of Janārdana, who brings an end to all. 37

## \|\| 1.22.38 \|\|

*yatkiñcitsṛjyate yena sattvajātena vai dvija \|*  
*tasya sṛjyasya sambhūto tatsarvaṃ vai harestanuḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

Janārdana is creator of the world, preserver, the world itself, its destroyer and universal lord. 38

## \|\| 1.22.39 \|\|

*hanti yāvacca yatkiñcitsattvaṃ sthāvarajaṅgamam \|*  
*janārdanasya tadraudraṃ maitreyāntakaraṃ vatuḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

He exists in threefold forms during its creation, preservation and destruction, acting in accord with each of these three qualities, but his ultimate state is a higher one beyond all these. 39

## \|\| 1.22.40 \|\|

*evameṣa jagatstraṣṭā jagatpātā tathā jagat \|*  
*jagadbhakṣayitā devaḥ samastasya janārdanaḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

That fourfold form of the highest spirit consists of true knowledge, is all-pervading, unequalled and is comprehended by Viṣṇu himself alone. 40

## \|\| 1.22.41 \|\|

*sṛṣṭhisthityantakāleṣu tridhaivaṃ saṃpravartate \|*  
*guṇapravṛtyā paramaṃ padaṃ tasyāguṇaṃ mahat \|\| 41 \|\|*

Maitreya:

Sage, duly describe for me the four forms of the Absolute, which is called the highest state. 41

## \|\| 1.22.42 \|\|

*tacca jñānamayaṃ vyāpi svāsaṃvedyamanaupamam \|*  
*catuṣprakāraṃ tadapi svarūpaṃ paramātmanaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The way to reach a goal is called the means, Maitreya, and that which one desires to reach is the goal itself. 42

## \|\| 1.22.43 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*catuḥprakāratāṃ tasya brahmabhūtasya he mune \|*  
*mamācakṣva yathānyāyaṃ yaduktaṃ paramaṃ padam \|\| 43 \|\|*

For an ascetic who yearns for liberation, breath control and other practices are the means, and his goal is the highest Absolute, from which there’s no return. 43

## \|\| 1.22.44 \|\|

*śrīparāsara uvāca*  
*maitreya kāraṇaṃ proktaṃ sādhanaṃ sarvavastuṣu \|*  
*sādhyaṃ ca vastvabhimataṃ yatsādhayitumātmanaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

The knowledge on which ascetics rely for achieving liberation is discrimination. This, sage, is the first aspect of the Absolute as knowledge. 44

## \|\| 1.22.45 \|\|

*yogino muktikāmasya prāṇāyāmādisādhanam \|*  
*sādhyaṃ ca paramaṃ brahma punarnavartateyataḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

The second aspect of the Absolute is the knowledge of causes, great sage, as it pertains to ascetics whose goal is liberation from all suffering. 45

## \|\| 1.22.46 \|\|

*sādhanālambanaṃ jñānaṃ muktaye yogināṃ hi yat \|*  
*sa bhedaḥ prathamastasya brahmabhūtasya vai mune \|\| 46 \|\|*

Knowledge of the inseparability and non-duality of both goal and means I call the third aspect. 46

## \|\| 1.22.47 \|\|

*yuñjataḥ kleśamuktyarthaṃ sādhyaṃ yadbrahma yoginaḥ \|*  
*tadālambanavijñānaṃ dvitīyo 'ṃśo mahāmune \|\| 47 \|\|*

Knowledge that overcomes distinctions between the first three aspects, great sage, and reveals the true form of the Self, 47

## \|\| 1.22.48 \|\|

*ubhayostvavibhāgena sādhyasādhanayorhi yat \|*  
*vijñānamadvaitamayaṃ tadbhāgonyo mayoditaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

Which is free from action, inexpressible, omnipresent, unequalled, has self-understanding as its object and simply exists without characteristics, 48

## \|\| 1.22.49 \|\|

*jñānatrayasya vai tasya viśeṣo yo mahāmune \|*  
*tannirākaraṇadvārā darśitātmasvarūpavat \|\| 49 \|\|*

Which is tranquil, fearless, pure, difficult to comprehend and independent—that is called the highest state of Viṣṇu in the form of knowledge. 49

## \|\| 1.22.50 \|\|

*nirvyāpāramanākhyeyaṃ vyāptimātramanūpamam \|*  
*ātmasambodhaviṣayaṃ sattāmātramalakṣaṇam \|\| 50 \|\|*

Those ascetics who achieve absorption in that state by dispelling ignorance will never take root in worldly existence, brahmin, unlike seeds sown in a ploughed field. 50

## \|\| 1.22.51 \|\|

*praśāntamabhayaṃ śuddhaṃ durvibhāvyamasaṃśrayam \|*  
*viṣṇorjñānamayasyoktaṃ tajjñānaṃ brahmasaṃjñitam \|\| 51 \|\|*

Such is the nature of the pure, eternal, universal, undecaying, faultless supreme state called Viṣṇu. 51

## \|\| 1.22.52 \|\|

*tatra jñānanirodhena yogino yānti ye layam \|*  
*saṃsārakarṣaṇoptau te yānti nirbījatāṃ dvijā \|\| 52 \|\|*

The ascetic who reaches that highest Absolute knows no return. Transcending concepts of vice and virtue, he is free from suffering and is completely pure. 52

## \|\| 1.22.53 \|\|

*evaṃprakāramamalaṃ nityaṃ vyāpakamakṣayam \|*  
*samastaheyarahitaṃ viṣṇvākhyaṃ paramaṃ padam \|\| 53 \|\|*

Two states of the Absolute abide in all creation: one with form and the other without. One may perish, the other is eternal. 53

## \|\| 1.22.54 \|\|

*tadbrahma paramaṃ yogī yato nāvartate punaḥ \|*  
*śrayatyapuṇyoparame kṣīṇakleśe 'tinirmale \|\| 54 \|\|*

The eternal is the highest Absolute, while the perishable form is all this world. Just as moonlight spreads in all directions though the moon itself stands still, so, too, does the power of the highest Absolute fill all the world. 54

## \|\| 1.22.55 \|\|

*dve rūpe brahmaṇastasya mūrta cāmūrtameva ca \|*  
*kṣarākṣarāsvarūpe te sarvabhūteṣvavasthite \|\| 55 \|\|*

In that same regard, just as the intensity of light depends on distance from its source, Maitreya, the power of the Absolute is likewise. 55

## \|\| 1.22.56 \|\|

*akṣaraṃ tatparaṃ brahma kṣaraṃ sarvamidaṃ jagat \|*  
*ekadeśasthitasyāgnerjyotstrā vistāriṇi yathā \|*  
*parasya brahmaṇaḥ śaktistathodamakhilaṃ jagat \|\| 56 \|\|*

Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva are the primary forms of the Absolute and are imbued with its power. Next come the lesser deities, Maitreya, and then Dakṣa and the other patriarchs, 56

## \|\| 1.22.57 \|\|

*tatrāpyāsannadūratvād bahutvasvalpatāmayaḥ \|*  
*jyotsnābhedosti tacchaktestadvanmaitreya vidyate \|\| 57 \|\|*

After these come humankind, cattle, wild beasts, birds and things that creep, each inferior to the one before, finally followed by trees and shrubs and all the others. 57

## \|\| 1.22.58 \|\|

*ba3hmaviṣṇuśivā brahmanpradhānā brahmaśaktayaḥ \|*  
*tataś ca devā maitreya nyūnā dakṣādayastataḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

Thus, this whole world, although imperishable and eternal, best of sages, appears and disappears as if subject to birth and death. 58

## \|\| 1.22.59 \|\|

*tato manuṣyāḥ paśavau mṛgapakṣisarīsṛpāḥ \|*  
*nyūnānyūnatarāścaiva vṛkṣagulmādayas tathā \|\| 59 \|\|*

Viṣṇu, who comprises all power, is the highest form of the Absolute. This is the form on which ascetics first meditate at the beginning of their practices. 59

## \|\| 1.22.60 \|\|

*tadetadakṣaraṃ nityaṃ jaganmunivarākhitam \|*  
*āvirbhāvatirobhāva-janmanāśavikalpavat \|\| 60 \|\|*

Perfect identity with the deity arises in an untroubled heart, fully one with the divine, which itself is dependent on the divine, sage, and which is endowed with the seed of the divine. 60

## \|\| 1.22.61 \|\|

*sarvaśakti mayo viṣṇuḥ svarūpaṃ brahmaṇaḥ param \|*  
*mūrtaṃ yadyogibhiḥ purvaṃ yogārambheṣu cintyate \|\| 61 \|\|*

Viṣṇu is superior to all those with power. He is identical with the Absolute and is the Absolute in form. Hari consists entirely of the Absolute, blessed sage. 61

## \|\| 1.22.62 \|\|

*sālambano mahāyogaḥ sabījo yatrasaṃsthitaḥ \|*  
*manasyavyāhate samyagyuñjatāṃ jāyate mune \|\| 62 \|\|*

This whole world without exception is imbued with him, sage. It arises from him, abides in him and is indeed identical with him. 62

## \|\| 1.22.63 \|\|

*sa paraḥ paraśaktīnāṃ brahmaṇaḥ samanantaram \|*  
*mūrtaṃ brahma mahābhāga sarvabrahmamayo hariḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

Viṣṇu comprises all that’s perishable and imperishable. He is the lord who bears this whole world of spirit and matter in the form of his ornaments and his weapons. 63

## \|\| 1.22.64 \|\|

*yatra sarvamidaṃ protamotaṃ caivākhitaṃ jagat \|*  
*tato jagajjagatyasminsa jagaccakhilaṃ mune \|\| 64 \|\|*

Maitreya:

Kindly explain how Lord Viṣṇu thus upholds the world. 64

## \|\| 1.22.65 \|\|

*kṣarākṣaramayo viṣṇurbibhartyakhilamīśvaraḥ \|*  
*puruṣāvyākṛtamayaṃ bhūṣaṇāstrasvarūpavat \|\| 65 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Having bowed down to mighty Viṣṇu, who’s beyond all measure, I’ll relate to you what the sage Vasiṣṭha once told me. 65

## \|\| 1.22.66 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*bhūṣaṇāstrasvarūpasthaṃ yaccaitadakhilaṃ jagat \|*  
*bibharti bhagavanviṣṇu stanmamākhyātumarhasi \|\| 66 \|\|*

Lord Hari wears the unblemished essence of the world, free from stains and qualities, as the Kaustubha jewel on his breast. 66

## \|\| 1.22.67 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*namaskṛtvāprameyāya viṣṇave prabhaviṣṇave \|*  
*kathayāmi yathākhyātaṃ vasiṣṭhena mamā bhavat \|\| 67 \|\|*

Eternal Viṣṇu wears all primal matter in the whorl of hair, Śrīvatsa, on his chest. Primal intellect abides in Mādhava as his mace. 67

## \|\| 1.22.68 \|\|

*ātmānamasya jagato nirlepamaguṇāmalam \|*  
*bibharti kaustubhamaṇisvarūpaṃ bhagavānhariḥ \|\| 68 \|\|*

The lord bears twofold individuation, consisting of the elements and the senses, in the form of his conch and bow. 68

## \|\| 1.22.69 \|\|

*śrīvatsasaṃsthānadhara manante na samāśritam \|*  
*pradhānaṃ buddhirapyāste gadarūpeṇa mādhave \|\| 69 \|\|*

Viṣṇu carries primal mind, fickle and swifter than the wind, in the form of the discus he holds in his hand. 69

## \|\| 1.22.70 \|\|

*bhūtādimindriyādiṃ ca dvidhāhaṅkāramīśvaraḥ \|*  
*bibharti śaṃkharūpeṇa śārṅgarūpeṇa ca sthitam \|\| 70 \|\|*

The garland of fundamental elements, consisting of the elements themselves and their causes, is the fivefold wreath of victory, Vaijayantī, worn by the mace-bearing deity, brahmin. 70

## \|\| 1.22.71 \|\|

*calatsvarūpamatyantaṃ javenāntāritānilam \|*  
*cakrasvarūpaṃ ca mano dhatte viṣṇukare sthitam \|\| 71 \|\|*

Janārdana bears all the faculties of perception and action in the form of his many arrows. 71

## \|\| 1.22.72 \|\|

*pañcarūpā tu yā mālā vaijayantī gadābhṛtaḥ \|*  
*sā bhūtahetusaṃghātā bhūtamālā ca vai dvija \|\| 72 \|\|*

The excellent sword of surpassing brilliance borne by the unfailing deity is knowledge: this is wisdom that may be sheathed in ignorance. 72

## \|\| 1.22.73 \|\|

*yānīndriyāṇyaśeṣāṇi buddhikarmātmakāni vai \|*  
*śararūpāṇyaśeṣāṇi tāni dhatte janārdanaḥ \|\| 73 \|\|*

In this way, primal spirit, matter, intellect, individuation, the elements, mind, all the senses, knowledge, ignorance and all the world, Maitreya, abide in Hṛṣīkeśa. 73

## \|\| 1.22.74 \|\|

*bibharti yaccāsiratnamacyutotyantanirmalam \|*  
*vidyāmaya tu tajjñānamavidyākośasaṃsthitam \|\| 74 \|\|*

Hari, who is formless and consists of illusion, adopts this form with weapons and adornments for the benefit of all living beings. 74

## \|\| 1.22.75 \|\|

*itthaṃ pumānpradhānaṃ ca buddhyahaṅkārameva ca \|*  
*bhūtāni ca hṛṣīkeśe manaḥ sarvendriyāṇi ca \|*  
*vidyāvidye ca maitreya sarvametatsamāśritam \|\| 75 \|\|*

The supreme lotus-eyed deity carries primal matter with its permutations, spirit and the universe. 75

## \|\| 1.22.76 \|\|

*astrabhūṣaṇasaṃsthānasvarūpaṃ rūpavarjitaḥ \|*  
*bibhārti māyārūposau śreyase prāṇināṃ hariḥ \|\| 76 \|\|*

That which is knowledge and that which is ignorance, that which exists and that which does not—all this abides in eternal Madhusūdana, lord of all creation, Maitreya. 76

## \|\| 1.22.77 \|\|

*savikāraṃ pradhānaṃ ca pumāṃścaivākhilaṃ jagat \|*  
*bibharti puṇḍahīkākṣastadevaṃ parameśvaraḥ \|\| 77 \|\|*

The lord, boundless and eternal Hari, appears in the form of time consisting of subseconds, seconds, minutes, days, seasons, half-years and years. 77

## \|\| 1.22.78 \|\|

*yā vidyā yā tathāvidyā yatsadyaccāsadavyayam \|*  
*tatsarvaṃ sarvabhūteśe maitreya madhusūdane \|\| 78 \|\|*

The lord upholds the seven realms of earth, sky, heaven, Mahar, Jana, Tapas and Satya, best of sages. 78

## \|\| 1.22.79 \|\|

*kalākāṣṭhānimeṣādidinartvayanahāyanaiḥ \|*  
*kālasvarūpo bhagavānapāpo hariravyayaḥ \|\| 79 \|\|*

Hari’s form is the essence of the world, he is firstborn of all firstborn, foundation of all knowledge and stands alone. 79

## \|\| 1.22.80 \|\|

*bhūrlokotha bhuvarlokaḥ svarloko munisattama \|*  
*maharjanastapaḥ satyaṃ sapta lokā ime vibhuḥ \|\| 80 \|\|*

The lord, master of all, who has neither end nor body, abides in the manifold forms of creation as gods, mortals, beasts and others. 80

## \|\| 1.22.81 \|\|

*lokātmamūrtiḥ sarveṣāṃ pūrveṣāmapi pūrvajaḥ \|*  
*ādhāraḥ sarvavidyānāṃ svayameva hariḥ sthitaḥ \|\| 81 \|\|*

The Ṛg, Yajur, Sāma and Atharva Vedas, the histories, the lesser Vedic texts and the declarations of the Upaniṣads, 81

## \|\| 1.22.82 \|\|

*devamānuṣapaśvādiśvarūpair bahubhiḥ sthitaḥ \|*  
*tataḥ sarveśvaro 'nanto bhūtamūrtiramūrtimān \|\| 82 \|\|*

The Vedic supplements, Manu and other legal texts, lyrics, treatises, narratives, any texts for recital, 82

## \|\| 1.22.83 \|\|

*ṛco yajūṃṣi sāmāni tathaivātharvāṇāni vai \|*  
*itihāsopavedāś ca vedānteṣu tathoktayaḥ \|\| 83 \|\|*

Poetry, speech and song are all the body of great Viṣṇu in the form of sound. 83

## \|\| 1.22.84 \|\|

*vedāṅgāni samastāni manvādigaditāni ca \|*  
*śāstrāṇyaśeṣāṇyākhyānāny anuvākāś ca ye kvacit \|\| 84 \|\|*

All things with and without form, here and elsewhere, are his body. 84

## \|\| 1.22.85 \|\|

*kāvyālāpāś ca ye kecidrītakānyakhilāni ca \|*  
*śabdamūrtidharasyaitad vapurviṣṇor mahātmanaḥ \|\| 85 \|\|*

‘I am Hari. All this is Janārdana. There is no cause or effect other than him’—one whose mind is set on this will never be subjected to repeated rebirths. 85

## \|\| 1.22.86 \|\|

*yāni mūrtāny amūrtāni yānyatrānyatra vā kvacit \|*  
*santi vai vastujātāni tāni sarvāṇi tadvapuḥ \|\| 86 \|\|*

Such is the first part of this purāṇa, brahmin, which I’ve accurately recalled for you. Hearing this, you’ll be freed from all misdeeds. 86

## \|\| 1.22.87 \|\|

*ahaṃ hariḥ sarvamidaṃ janārdano nānyattataḥ kāraṇakāryajātam \|*  
*īdṛṅmano yasya na tasyaṭa bhūyo bhavodbhāvā dvandvagadā bhavanti \|\| 87 \|\|*

A person acquires as much merit from listening to this, Maitreya, as from bathing in Puṣkara Lake on the full-moon day in the month of Kārttika for twelve consecutive years. 87

## \|\| 1.22.88 \|\|

*ityaiṣa teṃśaḥ prathamaḥ purāṇasyāsya vai dvija \|*  
*yathāvatkathito yasmindhṛte pāpaiḥ pramucyate \|\| 88 \|\|*

To a person who hears this, the wish-granting gods bestow the status of a deity, sage, ancestral spirit, gandharva, yakṣa and the rest. 88
